# Architecture

A per-file map of what exists and why. Read this before any non-trivial change —
it is the fastest way to find the module that already does the thing you are
about to write, and it records the reasoning behind choices that look arbitrary
from the code alone.

Companion documents: [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) for the dated record of *why*
each feature landed the way it did, and [../CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) for the
working rules, security boundaries, and testing conventions.

**Maintaining this file is part of the change.** It was generated and kept
current by the Glaze app's agent up to 2026-08-06; that agent no longer works on
this codebase, so from here it is hand-maintained like any other source file.
A change that adds a service, moves a boundary, or invalidates something below
should update the relevant entry in the same commit.

## Overview

- **App Name:** Test Recorder
- **Purpose:** Record interactions on any website and turn them into runnable Playwright tests.
- **Features:**
  - Prompt for a URL, open it in a dedicated browser window, and record every interaction as a test step.
  - Captures clicks, text input (fill), selects, checkboxes, Enter/Escape key presses, and navigation.
  - Pause/resume recording; add assertions (element visible / contains text) by clicking elements in the recorded page.
  - Generates a `@playwright/test` spec on close; stored in a test library.
  - Runs a selected test locally with the bundled Playwright (installs the browser on first run), streaming live output. **Step highlighting during runs:** `__GLAZE_STEP__:` JSON markers on stdout carry each step's begin/end plus the spec line number, written by **two** emitters that between them cover every step (see `step-marker.ts` for the one spelling of the prefix and the parser): the custom Playwright reporter (`step-reporter-source.ts` → `step-reporter.mjs` written next to the specs) for `pw:api` **and `expect`** steps located in the spec, and the capture fixture's action wrapper for the actions Playwright attributes to the fixture file instead. They cannot overlap — installing that wrapper is exactly what moves a step's location off the spec — and between them a run reports every executed step including the one that failed. The runner (`playwright-runner.ts`) builds a line→stepIndex map from the spec, strips the markers from visible output, and pushes `runner:step` events; the recorder store tracks per-step `stepStatus` ("running"/"passed"/"failed") in `RunInfo`, and on `runner:done` runs the map through `settleStepStatus` so nothing is left `running` — a stop is a SIGKILL and the reporter dies with its open step unclosed, which used to leave that row spinning after the run had finished (non-zero exit settles it `failed`, zero settles it `passed`; steps that never began stay absent); `StepRow` accepts a `runStatus` prop and shows an accent-tinted background + spinner (running), green tint + check (passed), or red tint + X (failed) with a status icon on the right. The failed highlight was enhanced to `bg-support-red/15 ring-1 ring-inset ring-support-red/40` (15% red background + 40% red ring) with red description text and a larger `size-4` X icon so the failed step clearly stands out. `TestDetailView` passes `runInfo.stepStatus[i]` to each row. Only applies to app-generated specs; an imported script never loads the fixture and has no step map, and a marker on a run with no map is now **stripped rather than passed through** (a hand-edited spec the scanner can't map would otherwise print raw markers into the Output panel). Guarded by `check:step-progress` (source-level) and `e2e/step-progress.spec.ts` (real Playwright, both modes) — the two writers exist only inside the Playwright CLI and a Playwright worker, so nothing else can ask them what they report. **Both writers report the attempt as of R24a** (`result.retry` in the reporter, `testInfo.retry` in the fixture), and the runner keys its per-step outcome map by it — flat, the passing attempt overwrote the failing one step for step and `buildReplay` computed `failedIndex === null`, so the replay of a run that went red said nothing had gone wrong. The replay is built from attempt 0's outcomes. The attempt is deliberately NOT on the `runner:step` payload yet: the live highlight has nothing to show for it until retries exist (R24).
  - "Edit in Trainer" reopens an existing test's trainer window preloaded with its recorded steps so the user can extend it; stopping regenerates the script from the combined steps and updates the same record (preserves `createdAt`). When a test has manual script edits (`scriptEdited`), the "Edit in Trainer" button opens a confirmation dialog ("Edit in Trainer") with three options: "Save & continue" (primary accent — persists any unsaved script draft, then opens the trainer), "Continue without saving" (left-aligned destructive — opens the trainer, letting later regeneration overwrite the edits), and Cancel. On arrival, all trainer controls stay disabled (status "Loading page…") until the browser window finishes loading its first page (`pageReady` on `RecorderState`); the trainer then just shows the loaded page (the `goto`/navigation) and waits — it does **not** auto-replay the recorded steps (the old auto-`replayAll` on load was removed because it "flew through" the whole test and could wedge the backend). The user replays on demand with **"Replay from current step"** (see the replay bullet below). Row highlighting still uses the `StepRow runStatus` mechanism driven by `recorder:replayStep`. **Status states:** the trainer's top-bar `Status` badge reads "Loading page…" (before `pageReady`), then **"Running"** whenever ANY replay is executing (a store-level `executing` flag covering single-step / from-current replays, OR the streamed `replayRun.running`), else "Paused" / "Editing" / "Recording". While running, ALL step-editing controls are locked (`controlsDisabled = !pageReady || running` gates the replay button, assert menu, Add step, AI steps, pause/resume, every `StepRow` action, and the cursor-gap inserts) — so "Editing" only appears when the user is actually free to record or change steps. **Loading modal + timeout:** while the training browser opens, a full-area loading overlay (spinner + "Opening training browser…" + URL + copy) shows on the main window. The training window is force-shown on the earliest of `ready-to-show` / `dom-ready` / a **creation-relative `SHOW_FALLBACK_MS` (1.5s) timer** — the timer is the key fix for the "first click does nothing on a cold start" bug: on the first recorder window after app launch the WebView subsystem cold-starts and its readiness events lag past 10s, which used to leave the window hidden and falsely trip the timeout. Now the window always appears ~1.5s after creation regardless of those events. `LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` (15s) only bounds `pageReady` (how long controls stay disabled waiting for the first page), decoupled from window-opening. The load-failure path (`!shown || destroyed`) is now a genuine "couldn't open" case (window destroyed) — it logs a failed `RunRecord` to Stats and shows the "Couldn't open the training browser" dialog ("Try again" / "Check Stats"). **Exit confirmation:** the toolbar button is labeled **"Save Test"** in Training mode (editing an existing test, `state.editing`) or **"Generate Test"** in Creation mode (new test). With no live steps (`liveSteps.length === 0`) it saves/exits directly (no warning). With unsaved steps it opens a two-button `Dialog` — "Save & Exit" (finalize + close) or "Discard Edits" (discard steps + close via `recorder:discardExit`) — showing the step count.
  - **mabl-style Trainer (editable step list):** the live trainer step list is fully mutable — drag to reorder, click a value/text to inline-edit it, delete, and an insert cursor (click the gap between steps) that controls where new captured/added steps land. A "+ Add step" menu inserts manual steps without touching the page: Assertion, Wait (duration or wait-for-element), Go to URL, Press key, Find element, Set viewport. Locator-requiring steps (assertion, wait-for-element, press-on-element, find-element) use the same "Target Element" crosshair picker as the per-step "Refine selector" flow (now grouped under a kebab "Step utilities" dropdown beneath the row, alongside the "Continue on Failure" toggle — see Recent History) — the user picks an element in the training browser and chooses one of its candidate locators (best-first) instead of typing a CSS selector by hand. Assertions expanded from visible/has-text to 13 kinds (visible/hidden/contains-text/exact-text/enabled/disabled/checked/unchecked/value/attribute/count/page-URL/page-title) with a Hard/Soft toggle (`expect.soft`). A per-step ▶ replay previews that one step in the live window (best-effort synthetic events, not a real Playwright run). A **"Replay from current step"** button (formerly "Replay from start") replays SLOWLY from the currently selected step (or index 0 if none is selected) through the END of the list — a settle + pause between steps (`REPLAY_SETTLE_MS`/`REPLAY_STEP_DELAY_MS` in `recorder-service.ts`) so the user can watch — streaming each step's result + verbose logs live to the debug panel's Console tab via a new `recorder:replayLog` push (`{phase:"start"|"step"|"done"}`). A hard failure stops the run; soft failures continue; if/endif honored. Each step's injected script is raced against `REPLAY_STEP_TIMEOUT_MS` (8s) via `execWithTimeout` so a step that triggers a navigation (or hangs) fails cleanly instead of wedging the run with controls disabled. Capture is suppressed during the run (`withCaptureSuspended` — see below), the training window is focused before the first step, and both trainer windows show **Replaying** and lock their controls off the broadcast `RecorderState.replaying` rather than each window's own local flag. The running step is auto-selected so the highlight + Step-details tab follow along, and each step gets an **ephemeral pass/fail outline** as it finishes — `.step-replay-pass`/`.step-replay-fail` in `renderer/styles.css`, a green/red one-shot fade held for `REPLAY_FLASH_MS`(2.5s, matching the per-row replay tint) and driven by the store's `replayFlash` map off the `recorder:replayStep` END event. Same outline mechanism as the AI-added `.step-new` glow and mutually exclusive with it (both draw an `outline`; the flash wins while it lasts). Pinned by `check:step-glow`. An "AI steps" button asks the local LLM for a structured `Step[]` from a natural-language flow and inserts editable steps into the list; the dialog has an optional "Refine selector" picker (same crosshair pick as step rows) that captures an element's locator and passes it to the LLM as context so generation can target the exact element the user pointed at. The AI generates only the steps AFTER navigation — Step 1 ("Navigate to URL") is a test-level setting the user controls in the Step 1 URL editor, so the prompt tells the model not to emit a `goto` step (assume the page is already loaded at the starting URL), and the dialog defensively filters out any `goto` the model still emits before inserting. **Tabbed debug panel (`DebugPanel` in `recording-view.tsx`, h-56):** a `TabsRoot` (controlled `value`) with two tabs. **Console** — the live "test results" stream: each replayed step's pass/fail header + verbose formatted log lines (timestamp · level · message) as the run progresses, a status line (`Running… n/total` / `Done — p/r passed` / `Stopped at step n`) and a **% hit rate** pill (passed÷ran). An **Auto-scroll checkbox (checked by default)** gates the `ScrollArea autoScrollToBottom`; unchecking lets the user scroll manually. The Console auto-selects on "Replay from current step". **Per-step "Debug with AI":** each FAILED step's error block in the Console shows a Sparkles button that opens `StepAiDebugDialog` (`renderer/main/ai-debug-panel.tsx`) — a leaner sibling of the full-run `AiDebugDialog` that streams a focused LLM diagnosis of that single step (step label + Playwright-style locator via `locatorToPrompt` + error + verbose logs) via `buildStepDebugMessages` (`renderer/lib/llm-prompts.ts`). Reuses `useLlmChat`, `ModelPicker`, `CodeBlock`, `parseResponse`, `friendlyError` (exported from `ai-debug-panel.tsx`). No "Apply to script" (these are editable steps, not a script) — just diagnosis + optional corrected-expression snippet + copy. Auto-starts once per unique failed step; Stop/Regenerate buttons. **Step details** — the selected step's element-locator summary + persisted replay diagnostics (the previous per-step panel content) plus the hit-rate pill and a clear (✕) button. Clicking a step row still selects it (accent ring). Per-step diagnostics are persisted per-test in `userData/recorder/debug-logs.json` (via `recorder-debug-store.ts`); the store loads them on session open and listens for `recorder:debugLogs` pushes; the live Console stream is driven separately by `recorder:replayLog` (ephemeral, held in the recorder store's `replayRun`).
  - Script tab supports direct in-app editing (Edit/Save/Cancel) of the generated spec text, independent of the recorded steps. **IDE-style script views:** both the read-only `ScriptView` and the editable `ScriptEditor` (in `renderer/main/script-view.tsx`) render with a line-numbered gutter + regex syntax highlighting (keywords/strings/comments/numbers/decorators) using the design system's `--color-token-*` variables (blue keywords, red strings, gray-italic comments) so they adapt to light/dark. The editor overlays a transparent `<textarea>` on a highlighted `<pre>` (shared font metrics + padding for exact alignment, synced scroll, explicit white caret since `text-transparent` hides the caret in WebKit). Both views use `text-mono font-mono` (13px/18px) — slightly larger than the old `text-small-mono` (11px) for a more legible IDE feel.
  - Import existing Playwright tests: the sidebar + opens a native menu with "Train manually" (the original recorder flow), "Generate from prompt", "Select from files" (pick a local folder), and "From URL" (clone a git repo). Each matched `*.spec.ts`/`*.test.ts` file is parsed into `Step[]` (via `spec-parser.ts`); the verbatim file is kept as the runnable script and the test is `scriptEdited`. Imported tests (`sourceDir` set) are **Script-only** in the detail view — the verbatim file is the source of truth, so the Steps tab is not shown even when steps parsed. Tests created in the app show **both** Steps and Script tabs (Steps primary when there are steps).
  - **Generate test from prompt (phase 3):** the sidebar + menu's "Generate from prompt" opens `GenerateTestDialog` — a separate window where the user describes a test in natural language and the local LLM generates a complete Playwright spec. Pre-selectable common options: test name, starting URL, execution speed (Fast/Medium/Slow, persisted on the `TestRecord`), and browser viewport (Default/Desktop/Laptop/Tablet/Mobile presets — passed into the prompt as a hint). The dialog shows prompt-formatting tips, streams the response with the same formatted rendering as the AI debug panel (prose + fenced code cards), and when the model returns a complete applyable spec (contains `import` and `test(`) a "Create test" button persists it via `tests:createFromPrompt` (creates a `TestRecord` whose `steps` are `parseSpecDetailed`'s reading of the generated source, `scriptEdited: true`, the generated source as the script file) then navigates to it. **The step list is only as good as what the parser can read back**, and a model left to its own devices writes JavaScript, not a step sequence: locators bound to `const`s, data read out of the page and asserted with `expect(value)`, several statements inside one `test.step(…)`. (Since 2026-08-22 the generator itself wraps every statement in a `test.step` with a phrase title, and the parser consumes a wrapper whole — so the prompt now asks for one statement per wrapper rather than none.) `GENERATE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` therefore states the round-trippable vocabulary explicitly (see `llm-prompts.ts`) — without it a generated test arrives with a near-empty Steps tab (or none at all: the tab is hidden at zero steps) and an "Edit in Trainer" that offers to regenerate the good script from those two steps.
  - **Visual testing (roadmap complete, Phases 0–5 + post-roadmap):** an optional per-run "Capture screenshots" toggle (default off) next to Run test, plus a "Visual" sidebar tab (grouped with Stats). When the toggle is on, a run of an app-generated test **captures a screenshot after each page action** and persists it under `userData/recorder/artifacts/<testId>/<runId>/<stepIndex>.png` + `manifest.json` + `replay.json` (retained for the newest 10 runs per test). The **Visual tab is a screenshot-replay viewer**: pick a captured run and scrub its steps in a timeline, seeing the screenshot + pass/fail per step with the failure point highlighted (auto-jump + red callout + timeline marker). **Phase 3 adds page-level visual diffing:** each captured step's screenshot is compared against a **pinned baseline** (keyed by `Step.id`; first captured run seeds it, later runs diff against it — never the previous run). A step exceeding a per-test threshold is flagged "visual change detected" (orange badge/callout/timeline marker), with a Current/Baseline/Diff view toggle and a **per-step** "Accept New Baseline" to re-pin an intentional change (the run-level "Accept All & Re-baseline" was deliberately removed on 2026-08-04 — see DECISIONS; `visual:acceptRun` and `api.visual.acceptRun` remain in place, unused by the UI). Diffing is pure-JS (pixelmatch); it degrades to "can't compare" on size mismatch/corrupt images rather than false-flagging. **Phase 4 adds freeform step notes:** any step in the replay timeline can carry one freeform text note (an "Add note" affordance in the step-detail row, editable/clearable), shown inline while scrubbing and marked on the timeline with a message-square icon. **Phase 5 (final)** wired the tab to the real views (done in Phase 2), added a one-path end-to-end regression check (`npm run check:visual-pipeline`), kept retention at 10 (validated against real disk usage), and fixed a retention bug that could delete the pinned baseline. **Post-roadmap additions:** (1) **Ignore/mask regions** — `TestRecord.visualMasks` stores normalized 0–1 rectangles; `diffPngBuffers` paints a solid fill into both images before pixelmatch so masked pixels compare equal. The Visual tab's "Ignore regions" button toggles a `cursor-crosshair` overlay (`MaskLayer` in `visual-view.tsx`) over the screenshot; drag to draw a dashed orange rectangle. A mask with `stepId: null` applies to every step; the "Apply to all steps" switch controls this. The diff badge shows a dashed-square icon when regions were excluded. IPC: `visual:getMasks`/`setMasks`. (2) **Capture-overhead measurement** — the capture fixture times each `page.screenshot()` and writes `captureMs`/`shotCount` into `manifest.json`; the runner stores them on `RunRecord`. Stats shows a "Capture overhead" panel (above the pass/fail chart) with per-run screenshot time, per-screenshot mean, and captured-vs-uncaptured duration. Hidden until at least one instrumented capture run exists. (3) **Age-based artifact retention** — `pruneRuns` takes an optional `maxAgeMs` on top of the count cap; a run survives only if it satisfies both. New setting `artifactRetentionDays` (0 = off, max 365) in Settings → "Delete screenshots older than". Pinned baselines (`baseline/` dir) are excluded from both rules. (4) **Element-scoped (component-level) diffing** — records the acted-on element's bounding box (`elementRect` in `capture-fixture-source.ts`) at capture time; a Page/Element `SegmentedControl` in the step-detail row (only for steps with `step.rect`) toggles between page-wide and element-cropped comparison. The baseline stores its own rect, so each side is cropped by geometry from its own run. IPC: `visual:getElementSteps`/`setElementStep`. Steps whose baseline predates the geometry feature show no toggle; if the user toggles Element on a step whose baseline lacks geometry, the diff reports "the pinned baseline predates element geometry — accept a new baseline to enable it". (5) **Local run notifications** — a macOS notification (`run-notifier.ts` via the SDK `Notification` API) fires when a run fails or flags a visual change; clean runs stay quiet. Gated by Settings → "Notify when a run has problems" (`notifyOnRunIssues`). The macOS notification is local only; external delivery is a SEPARATE, opt-in feature — see the webhook-alerts bullet below (added later; earlier notes calling external alerting "deferred" are stale).
  - **Accessibility checks (axe), gated separately from screenshots:** a per-test **"Check accessibility"** toggle beside "Capture screenshots" (global default `RecorderSettings.defaultA11yChecks`, off) makes the capture fixture inject `axe-core` via `addInitScript` (once per document, resolved from the same `node_modules` the Playwright CLI came from) and run it after each captured action. Gated independently of screenshots because a11y is useful without them and costs far more — `a11yMs`/`a11yChecks` are timed and reported separately from `captureMs` for exactly that reason, and Stats shows the two apart. Violations ride the SAME manifest entries as screenshots (one correlation, in `buildReplay`, so a result can never be attributed to the wrong step), compacted to rule id + impact + help + node targets, capped at 25 violations × 5 nodes. `enrichWithA11y` then diffs them against the test's accepted baseline (`a11y-diff.ts`, pure). **Identity is rule id AND node target** — keying on the rule alone would make accepting one low-contrast label accept every future contrast failure on the page. The baseline lives on the `TestRecord` (`a11yBaseline`), NOT under the artifacts tree, which makes "retention pruned my baseline" structurally impossible rather than a rule to remember; accept-step / accept-run / reset are in `a11y-baseline-ops.ts`, joined 2026-08-19 by the rule-grained pair the Accessibility view added: `acceptRuleA11y` (one rule, everywhere it currently fires — only that rule's keys, over the same `selectLatestA11yRuns` selection the rollup reads) and `revokeA11yRule` (un-accept one rule for one test, the per-item undo `resetA11yBaseline` was too blunt for). **Reported, never fatal** — nothing here touches `runStatus`, pinned by a source-level assertion in `a11y-diff.test.ts`. **Four surfaces, in decreasing order of how much the user has to go looking** (renumbered 2026-08-19, when accessibility LEFT the Visual view for the dedicated Accessibility view — see `renderer/main/a11y-view.tsx`): (1) an **"Accessibility" tab on the test itself** (`a11y-panel.tsx`, last tab, after Heals; hidden for imported tests like Variables/Heals), badged with the unaccepted-step count — it describes the most recent run that CHECKED (`latestA11yRun`), NOT the most recent run, so a later run with the toggle off can't blank the results and read as "fixed"; it carries per-step and whole-run Accept, a **dismiss** on the "not accepted yet" banner, plus **"Reset accepted"**, which is the only UI that has ever reached `a11y:resetBaseline`. The dismiss writes `RunReplay.dismissedNotices` (`run-notice-ops.ts`); it used to share that flag with the Visual view's findings banner, but that banner was retired on 2026-08-19 (the counts became panel-header chips and the accepts moved to the tool band), so this tab is now the only surface that writes it. Only the orange banner is dismissible: the red "completed none" one reports a BROKEN CHECK rather than a finding about the page, and letting that be waved off is how a silently broken axe fixture hides for another few months. All four states of that banner slot centre their copy, since they swap as a run's verdict changes and centring one would look like the banner jumping alignment on its own. (2) A `describeA11yOutcome` line in the run's Output panel. (3) The **Accessibility view** (`/a11y`, `a11y-view.tsx`) — the suite-wide, rule-centric surface: the Triage board over `a11y:rollup`, rule-wide accept (`a11y:acceptRule`), rule-scoped issue filing, and the Baseline tab with per-rule revoke (`a11y:revokeRule`) and the trend strip. (4) The Stats board's Accessibility tile and category dashboard. The Visual view shows NOTHING about accessibility since 2026-08-19 — its banner, header chip, per-step badge, frame-rail mark and run-list marker are all gone, because a finding surfaced in two places teaches the user to check both. **Both (1) and (2) state the number of COMPLETED checks**, because zero of them is a fault and must never read as a clean page (see DECISIONS 2026-08-06). The violation rendering (`a11y-violations.tsx`: `A11yBadge`, `A11yViolationList`, `IMPACT_TONE`) is shared by the tab and the Accessibility view — two hand-written copies would eventually disagree about which issues matter. **`IMPACT_TONE` maps axe's four impacts onto THREE ranks** (critical → red, serious → amber, moderate and minor → no hue), because colour in this design means outcome and a finding with nothing urgent to report must not borrow one; the impact WORD is the chip's text, so the two neutral ones are still told apart by reading them. On `check:sdk-retired`'s list alongside `visual-view.tsx`, since a component rendered by a retired surface has SDK imports that surface's own entry cannot see. Coverage: `a11y-diff.test.ts`, `a11y-format.test.ts`, `a11y-panel.test.tsx` (one test per situation it must tell apart), `capture-fixture-a11y.dom.test.ts` (the shipped axe callback, evaluated the way Playwright evaluates it), the a11y section of `check:visual-pipeline`, and the tab/run-list markers in `test-detail-view.test.tsx` / `visual-view.test.tsx`.
  - Right-click a test in the sidebar for a context menu: "Reveal in Finder" (highlights the spec file), "Remove from Sidebar" (hides the test from the sidebar view WITHOUT deleting its record or script file — `hidden: true` on the `TestRecord`; the file stays on disk for later restoration), "Duplicate Test" (see the next bullet), and "Adjust Test Speed" submenu with a 3-stop Slow/Medium/Fast slider that sets a Playwright `slowMo` delay for that test's runs (0/400/1200ms).
  - **Duplicate a test** (sidebar right-click → "Duplicate Test"): a new `TestRecord` and a new spec file carrying everything that describes the test — steps (with their ids), variables, datasets, tags, flow flags, visual masks/thresholds/element-scoped steps, and every run preference (speed, browser, headless, timeout, capture/a11y/logs toggles) — and **none of its history**: no runs, screenshots, pinned visual baselines, step notes, Auto-Heal journal or AI debug sessions (all of those live in their own stores keyed by test id, so a fresh id starts empty without anything being deleted), and no `a11yBaseline` (accepted violations describe the ORIGINAL's runs; a copy carrying them would report a clean page it has never been run against). Named `<original> [n]` with the lowest free n from 2 up, counting **hidden** tests so a collision can't be invisible. **Stored secret VALUES are copied** (backend-only, via `testSecretsStore.copyTest`) so the copy actually runs — the handler then refreshes the redaction snapshot, or the copy's password is a value redaction was never told about. A generated spec is regenerated so its `test("…")` carries the new name; a hand-edited or imported one is copied word for word (same trade-off `tests:rename` already makes), and an imported test's whole `scripts/imported/<id>/` sandbox comes across so its relative imports still resolve. **The record is REBUILT from `DUPLICATED_FIELDS`, never spread** — same reasoning as `normalizeRawStep`; `check:duplicate-test` fails while any `TestRecord` field is absent from both that list and `DROPPED_FIELDS`. A warning dialog appears **only when something runner/trainer-related is active** (variables, stored secrets, cookie/capture/`runFlow` steps, datasets, being a flow, hand-edited or imported) — a prompt that always appears is one nobody reads. Files: `main/services/duplicate-test.ts`, `renderer/lib/duplicate-warnings.ts`, `renderer/main/duplicate-test-dialog.tsx`.
  - **Auto-Heal (resilient replay):** during "Replay from current step", when a step's locator fails to resolve, a backend Auto-Heal engine probes the live page for alternative locator candidates using all available context — the step's action/text, freshly generated candidate locators, and hints from past runs' debug logs — retrying per the user's settings. If a candidate makes the step pass, it's auto-applied to the step (marked with a green "Healed ✓" badge in the Console); otherwise the ranked candidates surface in the Console as a menu where the user can pick any suggested target to apply to the step. Settings (Settings → Auto-Heal) control retry count and per-attempt timeout, and let the user disable Auto-Heal.
  - **Refine Selector (per-step element picker):** each editable trainer step row that has a locator shows a crosshair "Refine selector" icon (between the ▶ Run/replay and ✕ Delete icons). Clicking it pauses the session and enters a pick mode in the training browser — hovering draws a blue bounding box over components and clicks are intercepted (page doesn't respond). Clicking a component captures its locator candidates (test-id / role / label / placeholder / text / CSS / XPath, best-first), computed CSS, and attributes; a "Refine Selector" review dialog (showing which step is being refined) lets the user pick the best locator and **update that step's locator** (Update selector), then resumes recording. Operates at the step level — it refines an existing step rather than inserting a new one.
  - **Right-click test-tools menu in the training browser:** right-clicking in the recorder window pops a native macOS menu (the window loads an external page we don't own, so the menu is a backend `Menu.popup` from `@glaze/core/backend`, not in-page React). The element under the cursor is resolved via `PICK_AT_POINT_SCRIPT` (`elementFromPoint` + inlined `DOM_HELPERS` → `buildPicked` + the element's current text/value), so menu items are **pre-targeted at that element**. Menu layout: a disabled header showing the target (e.g. `button#submit`) or "No element here"; **Assert element ▸** (visible/hidden/contains-text/exact-text/enabled/disabled/checked/unchecked/value/attribute/count — text/value kinds prefill the element's current text); **Assert page ▸** (URL path is… / URL contains… / URL ends with… / URL is… / title is… / title contains… — URL path is leads, as the robust default); **Wait ▸** (for element visible / for element hidden / for duration…); **Refine selector for this element**; **Add step ▸** (Go to URL / Press key / Set viewport / Find element). Picking an item pushes a `recorder:contextAction` event to the main window (`ContextAction` payload: kind + assert?/waitMode? + picked + prefillText/prefillValue), which opens the existing Add-step dialog **prefilled** (locator from the picked element, assert kind / wait mode / text / value seeded) so the user can tweak before inserting at the cursor. Fully additive — the "+ Add step" menu and assert-mode toolbar stay. Coordinate mapping: context-menu `x/y` are DIP; `elementFromPoint` uses CSS client coords, adjusted by `wc.getZoomFactor()` when not 1.
  - **Trainer window URL bar setting:** the recording/editing window's native title bar shows the current page's live URL by default ("Recording — https://…", updated on every navigation) as a stand-in address bar. A Settings toggle ("Show URL bar in training window") lets the user turn this off, reverting to a static title; persisted in `recorder-settings.json`, applied per-session at `recorder:start`.
  - **Default run speed (New Recording dialog + Settings):** the New Recording dialog has a "Run speed" `SegmentedControl` (Slow/Medium/Fast) that reads and writes a persisted `defaultRunSpeed` on `RecorderSettings` (`recorder-settings.json`, **slow by default**). New recordings seed their per-test `speed` from this default in `recorder-service.finalize()`, so the sidebar "Adjust Test Speed" override still works per-test. Settings shows the same control under "Default run speed" so the choice is visible and changeable there too. Same `SegmentedControl` idiom as `GenerateTestDialog`'s execution-speed picker.
  - **Window size preset (New Recording dialog + Settings):** the New Recording dialog has a "Window size" `Select` (Default / Desktop 1280×800 / Laptop 1440×900 / Tablet 768×1024 / Mobile 390×844, from `renderer/lib/viewport-presets.ts`) that reads and writes a persisted `defaultWindowSize` on `RecorderSettings`, mirrored in Settings under "Default window size". The preset does two things at once, and both are needed: the training window OPENS at that PAGE size (`useContentSize`), and the size is recorded as the session's first `viewport` step so the generated spec calls `setViewportSize` BEFORE `goto` and the test REPLAYS at the size it was recorded at. Sizing only the window would make the preset cosmetic — a test recorded at 390×844 would run at Playwright's own 1280×720 and meet a desktop layout it never saw. Re-opening a test in the trainer takes its size from `recordedViewport(steps)` instead of the dialog, and adds no second viewport step.
  - **Window resize as a test step (`viewport`):** a resize can be inserted anywhere in a test, not only at the start. Both "+ Add step → Set viewport" pickers (the trainer's `add-step-dialog.tsx` and the detail view's `edit-steps-view.tsx`) offer the shared presets plus **Custom…** width/height, clamped by `clampViewportAxis` to the same 200–4000 range `normalizeViewport` enforces on the backend, so a typed size is never silently rewritten. A resize row is inline-editable as one `WIDTHxHEIGHT` field (`parseSizeDraft` in `step-row.tsx` accepts `x`, `×` or `,`; an unparseable draft commits NOTHING, because a resize with only a width is not a smaller edit but a broken step) and is **replayable on its own** — the one exclusion from the per-step ▶ that was dropped. It composes with the rest of the step model like any other step: disabled, continue-on-failure, inside an `if` block, inlined through a `runFlow`, reordered by drag, round-tripped by `spec-parser.ts`. **Trainer replay actually resizes the window** (`resize-service.ts`, dispatched from `runStep` alongside `cookie` steps) rather than reporting "not previewable" — see DECISIONS 2026-08-06. **Every executed resize logs its dimensions:** the generated spec emits a `console.log` after `setViewportSize` reporting the requested size and what `page.viewportSize()` actually returned (so it lands in the run's `.log`), the trainer streams requested/window/page sizes into the Console tab, and a manual drag-resize of the training window logs its settled page size (`resized`, not `resize` — one line per deliberate change, not one per drag frame).
  - **Scroll as a test step (`scroll`), and scroll depth recorded as context:** Playwright actions auto-scroll but ASSERTIONS do not, and content a page renders lazily (virtualized lists, IntersectionObserver gates) is not in the DOM at all until the scroll that reveals it — so an assertion recorded against it passed in the trainer (the user had already scrolled the live page) and failed every run. The capture script now tracks the window's scroll position and, when a locator-bearing step is captured more than `SCROLL_CAPTURE_MIN_PX` (100px, either axis) from the last recorded position, pushes an explicit `scroll` step ahead of it (single point: `push()` in `capture-script.ts`; window scroll only — inner containers are not recorded, see DECISIONS 2026-08-19). Two forms, both visible rows and both round-tripped by `spec-parser.ts`: **to an element** emits `locator.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` (native, reports through the step reporter), **to a position** emits `glazeScrollTo(page, x, y)` — a runtime helper in `glaze-runtime-source.ts` that scrolls incrementally with a beat between rounds so lazy loaders see real scroll progression and can extend the page (a single jump clamps at the CURRENT document height); one awaited line per step, same rule as `glazeCapture`. Manual entry: the trainer composer's "+ Add step → Scroll" (element via the crosshair picker, or x/y), and the detail view's "Scroll to position" (position only — that dialog has no live page to pick from). The position row is inline-editable as one `x, y` field (`parseScrollDraft` in `step-row.tsx`; 0 is a legitimate offset on both axes, so it does not reuse `clampViewportAxis`). The injected replayer mirrors both forms (strict-mode included for the element form) so trainer and run agree — pinned end-to-end by the lazy-render rows in `e2e/assert-parity.spec.ts`, and at the ingest boundary by `check:step-ingest` (scroll offsets arrive from the page's OWN scroll position, which the page controls).
  - **Local LLM integration (phase 1 — backend foundation):** connect to a local Ollama or LM Studio server (loopback, private, no API key). Settings has an "AI provider" section to pick the provider, run a "Test connection" (reports Online/Offline + available models), and choose a default model. The server URL is **editable** (an `Input` pre-filled with the saved override or the provider default; saved on blur via `setConfig({ baseUrls: { [provider]: override } })`, clearing the override when it matches the default). Available models **auto-populate** based on the selected provider + URL — a `useEffect` on `[provider, baseUrl]` calls `llm.status` and renders the model list + Online/Offline badge without a manual "Test connection" click; the button remains as a manual refresh.
  - **Debug with AI (phase 2):** when a run fails, a "Debug with AI" button appears in the run Output panel. Opens a dialog that auto-sends the test's script + failing run output to the configured local LLM and streams back a diagnosis + suggested fix, with Regenerate/Stop/Copy response controls. Generating test steps in the trainer is **also built** (`generate-steps-dialog.tsx` + `buildGenerateStepsMessages`, behind the trainer's "AI steps" button) — earlier notes calling it deferred are stale.
  - **Cross-browser runs:** test runs can execute on **Chromium, Firefox, or WebKit**. Resolution order is explicit choice → the test's saved `TestRecord.runBrowser` → the global `RecorderSettings.defaultRunBrowser` (default `chromium`). Picked per-test from a `Select` in the test detail toolbar (persisted via `tests:setBrowser`) and globally in Settings. The Playwright CLI's `--browser=<engine>` flag selects it (the generated `playwright.config.ts` defines no `projects`, so `--browser` is honored rather than overridden). Each engine downloads on its own first use. **Trainer is unaffected** — it records in the app's own WebView. `RunRecord.runBrowser` records which engine a run used (absent on runs predating this — they all ran on Chromium).
  - **Batch (suite) runs:** a **Batch** view (sidebar → Views) runs many tests back to back. Pick any subset of the library, set batch-level headless/capture/browser options, and watch per-test progress plus a pass/fail summary. Tests run **strictly sequentially** — the runner is keyed by testId so it *would* run several at once, but each run spawns its own Playwright process and browser (parallel would contend for CPU and make headed runs fight over the screen), and sequential keeps the per-run event stream (`runner:output`/`step`/`done`, keyed by `runId === testId`) unambiguous for a renderer that shows one live run. Each test still writes its own `RunRecord`, so a batch shows up in Stats as ordinary runs — the batch is a driver, not a new kind of history. Batch-level options are deliberately **not** persisted per test (a suite run is a one-off choice; writing it back would rewrite every test's saved preference). **Batches are persisted** to `batch-history.json` write-through (after every per-test transition, so a crash mid-batch keeps what it collected), and the view shows a "Previous batches" list plus falls back to the most recent stored batch when nothing is live.
  - **Webhook alerts (the only AUTOMATIC outbound data path):** when a run fails, a step changes visually, or a batch finishes with failures, the app can POST a short summary to a user-supplied URL (works with Slack/Discord incoming webhooks, or anything else). **Off by default and the toggle can't be enabled until a URL is saved** — nothing leaves the machine otherwise. **Summary only:** test name, status, the failing step's LABEL, counts, duration, browser. Run logs are never sent, because they routinely contain page content, URLs with session tokens, and values typed during recording (passwords, card numbers). Best-effort with a 10s timeout — a webhook that is slow/down/wrong is logged and swallowed, never affecting the run it reports on. A batch sends ONE alert for the suite; per-run alerts are suppressed for batched runs. The URL is treated as a bearer credential (see storage below), not a setting. **Accuracy note:** this is the only *unattended* egress, NOT the only egress — with the Claude provider selected, "Debug with AI" sends the test script + the failing run's output to `api.anthropic.com` (user-initiated, per click). Don't describe the app as "nothing leaves the machine"; the honest framing is "nothing leaves automatically, and the alert payload never contains logs".
  - **Cookie management in the trainer:** a **Cookies** tab in the trainer's bottom panel (beside Console / Step details) lists the cookies visible to the current page and can create, update and delete them — plus Clear all. Goes through `webContents.session.cookies`, NOT injected `document.cookie`: an httpOnly cookie (exactly the kind you paste in to skip a login while recording) is invisible to page JS by definition, and `document.cookie` can't faithfully set Domain/Secure/SameSite either. Each change also does a second, **separable** thing — with "Add to test as a step" ticked (default ON) it records a `cookie` step so a real run reproduces the state. Default-on because the failure mode of forgetting is a test that passes in the trainer and fails on a run with nothing pointing at the missing cookie; still opt-out because always recording would bake an expiring auth token into the committed spec.
  - **Paginated Stats lists:** the raw-log search results render 50 rows at a time behind a shared `Pager` (Prev / "Page 2 of 9" / Next, plus a "51–100 of 412" range); the run-history table and Step health render **25** (`DENSE_PAGE_SIZE`), because both carry two lines per row and several numeric columns and fifty of those is a wall rather than a list. `Pager` computes its own counts, so a caller paging at a non-default size **must pass `size` too** — a mismatch reports too few pages and strands real rows behind a Next button that never enables. Step health pages over the SORTED rows, not the received ones: sorting one page of 25 out of 200 ranks the wrong set while the header claims otherwise; it renders nothing for a single page. Rules live in `renderer/lib/paginate.ts` because the failure modes read as "my data disappeared" rather than as errors: an empty list is "page 1 of 1" (never "of 0"), and **a page number that outlives its list** — you're on page 5, a filter narrows the table to 20 rows — clamps to a page with real rows. Each list also resets to page 1 when its own inputs change (filters for the table, the debounced query for search), with clamping in render as the backstop.
  - **Batch run order:** rows in the Batch view can be dragged into a run order (grip handle on hover, reusing the trainer step list's drag pattern). Persisted as test ids in `RecorderSettings.batchOrder`, so a curated suite survives navigation and restart. `applyOrder` guarantees **every test appears exactly once**: stored ids first in order, then everything else in library order — a newly added test lands at the END rather than reshuffling a curated suite, deleted ids are ignored, and a duplicate in a corrupt stored order can't duplicate a row. Reordering commits **by id, not visible index**, because a tag filter makes the visible rows a subsequence of the real order. `selectedIds` derives from the ordered list, which is what makes the order real rather than cosmetic. A **"Reset order"** button appears only when a custom order is actually in effect (`isCustomOrder`); it clears the stored order and lets the drift effect rewrite plain library order, so one code path produces the default.
  - **Test tags:** free-form grouping labels on `TestRecord.tags`, edited from the sidebar's right-click → **Edit Tags…**. The dialog offers tags already used elsewhere as one-click toggles — free-form tags rot fast when typing is the only affordance ("checkout" vs "check-out" both get created and neither groups anything). The Batch view's tag cluster (`tag-cluster.tsx`: a chip per tag with its count, plus **All** and **Untagged**) filters the checklist; selection is stored by test id and select-all/none act only on the visible set, so switching filters never drops what you already ticked. **Normalization is backend-only** (`normalizeTags`) — the renderer posts raw strings and re-renders what comes back, so the two sides can't disagree.
  - **Deleting a tag** happens on that cluster, from an X on each tag chip (never on All/Untagged, which aren't tags) behind a confirm that states how many tests carry it. **Creating** stays in the sidebar dialog — that's where you know which test you're labelling; deleting is library-wide, and the cluster is the only place the whole vocabulary is on screen with counts. One backend call (`tests:deleteTag` → `testStore.removeTag`), not a renderer loop over `tests:setTags`: a loop rewrites `tests.json` once per test and can strand the tag on half the library. Removal is case-insensitive (`smoke`/`Smoke` are one chip, so both must go or the chip returns) and includes **hidden** tests (`list()` hides them, so a tag left on one is invisible until it's unhidden — at which point a deleted tag reappears). The toast reports the BACKEND's count, which can exceed the confirm dialog's preview for exactly that reason.
  - **Stats run-history filtering:** the run table filters on three ANDed axes — status (All/Passed/Failed/Baselines), tag (Headed/Headless/Screenshots, plus one entry per browser engine), and test. Filters apply to the **table only**; the summary cards and pass/fail chart keep describing the whole history, so narrowing the table never silently redefines "pass rate".
  - **Conditional logic layer (if/endif blocks):** the trainer can wrap steps in an IF/THEN block so a test skips steps and continues gracefully when a condition isn't met (e.g. a pop-up that only appears to some users). "+ Add step → Add condition (if)" inserts an empty `if`/`end if` pair at the cursor; the user drags steps between them. Condition kinds: element state (visible/hidden/exists/enabled/disabled/checked/unchecked — via the crosshair element picker) and page state (URL contains / title contains — a substring input). `script-generator.ts` emits `if (await <predicate>) { … }` with depth-aware indentation (real Playwright runs branch past the body when false); `spec-parser.ts` round-trips those `if (…) {` / `}` blocks back to `if`/`endif` steps (verified by `spec-parser.check.ts` sections 5–6). The in-app replay preview (`step-replayer.ts` + `recorder-service` replayAll/replayFromStart) evaluates the condition and skips the whole block when false, so the preview matches a real run. Deleting either `if` or `end if` removes its matching partner (unwraps the block). The step list nests block bodies visually (indent per `computeStepDepths`); `if`/`end if` rows use a purple badge.
  - **Conditional waits ("Wait until"):** the "Add wait" dialog offers three INDEPENDENT properties as checkboxes — **An element** (the original locator wait), **Wait until** (a predicate), **A duration** — and one submit emits one `wait` step per ticked box, in that order (duration last, as a settle pad). Separate steps rather than one compound step, so each stays individually reorderable/editable/deletable and the data model gains no new shape. Predicates (`WaitUntilKind`, a deliberate SUPERSET of `ConditionKind` kept as its own type and allowlist): element visible/hidden/exists/enabled/disabled/checked/unchecked/contains-text/has-value/count, plus page URL-contains / title-contains. `Step.timeoutMs` (default 10s) is emitted as `{ timeout: N }` and is a bare-numeral field, so it goes through the same `int()`/`num()` treatment as `count`. **Codegen splits two ways** (`waitLine` in `script-generator.ts`): visible/hidden/exists have a native waiting API and emit `locator.waitFor({ state: … })`; every other predicate has no retrying primitive except `expect`, so it emits the same call the matching assertion would and is disambiguated by a trailing **`// wait until` marker** — see the DECISIONS entry. `spec-parser.ts` preserves that marker through `stripComments` and routes marked `expect`s to `wait` steps (`spec-parser.check.ts` sections 10–12). The replay preview polls the predicate every 100ms up to the step's timeout, **capped at 5s** like the fixed-duration wait, so a 60s wait can't look like a hung trainer. The trainer step row inline-edits the timeout where a plain wait inline-edits its duration; `describeWait` phrases the step rather than showing the generated call (which carries the marker and the options object).
  - **CSS assertions + element pseudo-states:** a `css` assert kind (`expect(L).toHaveCSS(prop, value | RegExp)`, `is exactly` / `contains`) and a `state` step type that puts an element into `:hover` / `:focus` / `:active` first, so the assertion after it measures the styled state rather than the resting one. **Real input, not a forced pseudo-class** — CDP's `CSS.forcePseudoState` is Chromium-only and runs here execute on all three engines, while `locator.hover()` moves a real virtual mouse everywhere. `ElementState` has four members that each compile to exactly ONE awaited statement (`hover`, `focus`, `press` → `page.mouse.down()`, `release` → `page.mouse.up()`), because `generateSpecDetailed`'s line map and `buildStepLineMap`'s await-counting fallback both assume one statement per step. The two pseudo-states needing more than one call are **compositions the Add-step dialog emits as several ordinary rows** (`renderer/lib/element-states.ts` → `buildStateSteps`): `:focus-visible` = a plain `press`-Tab step then `focus`; `:active` = `hover`, `press`, `release` with the assertion dragged between the last two, the `if`/`end if` idiom. Order is the meaning and is invisible once inserted, so it is pinned in `element-states.test.ts` rather than in the dialog (whose native-menu `Select` jsdom cannot drive). `press`/`release` carry NO locator — `mouse.down` acts wherever the preceding `hover` put the pointer. **Trainer preview drives a real cursor** (`main/services/input-service.ts`, dispatched from `runStep` beside `cookie`/`viewport`): the page resolves the locator and reports the element's centre `point`, the window sends `mouseMove`/`mouseDown`/`mouseUp` through `webContents.sendInputEvent`. A synthetic `mouseover` would fire handlers, apply no style and report success — certifying the thing it failed to test. A press with no preceding hover is REFUSED rather than defaulted to `(0,0)` (a real click on the page corner), `releaseHeldMouse` runs in `withCaptureSuspended`'s `finally` so an aborted replay can't leave the training window in a drag, and a lone `press` has no per-step ▶ for the same reason. Expected values are **picked from the element's live computed values** in the dialog, because Playwright compares the COMPUTED value (`red` never matches `rgb(255, 0, 0)`); those values are read with the user's cursor on the element, so they already include `:hover` and the UI says so. Everything is KEBAB-case end to end — `getPropertyValue` answers `""` for a camelCase name on both sides — and `CSS_ASSERT_PROPS` is interpolated into both injected scripts from one constant (the `page-actions.ts` idiom), pinned by `check:css-assertions`. `cssProp` is shape-checked at the boundary (`isCssPropName`) AND re-checked in the generator, since `recorder:updateStep` copies without re-normalizing. **Two pre-existing bugs fixed on the way:** `captureMethod` now classifies `state` steps, without which the first hover in a capture run left its manifest entry unconsumed and every later step silently lost its screenshot/geometry/a11y/diff; and the parser's unclassified-statement fallback now matches NESTED `page.a.b(...)` calls, which previously produced neither a step nor a skip and simply vanished on resync. `.hover()`/`.focus()` also parse now, fixing the old wart where importing a spec that hovered flagged it permanently `stepsDiverged`. Limits, stated: `page.mouse.*` is on a nested object no fixture patches, so `press`/`release` produce no capture screenshot or crawl settle; `::before`/`::after` need `toHaveCSS`'s 1.60 `pseudo` option, available since the 2026-08-22 move to 1.62.1 but not yet used; `:focus-visible` rides the browser's keyboard-modality heuristic and is the least robust of the four.

  - **Debug with AI prompt engineering:** the system prompt and dynamic context sent to the local LLM are grounded in this app's actual conventions (locator style rules matching `script-generator.ts`, plain-text output since the response renders in a `<pre>` not a markdown viewer), plus per-test context (imported vs. recorder-generated script, playback speed / slowMo delay) so the model reasons about the right root causes instead of generic advice.
  - **Debug with AI response persistence:** the streamed response survives closing and reopening the dialog. `AiDebugDialog` stays mounted in `TestDetailView` (controlled by `open`), so `useLlmChat` state persists. The auto-start effect keys on `${testName}:${output.length}` and no longer resets that key on close — reopening for the same run shows the prior response; a new run (different output length) re-fires.
  - **Debug with AI formatted output + Apply to script:** the LLM response is parsed into prose + fenced ```code``` segments; code blocks render as distinct bordered cards (language label + per-block copy). The system prompt now asks for a 1-2 sentence diagnosis followed by the COMPLETE corrected spec in a single fenced block. When streaming finishes and that block looks like a full, applyable spec (contains `import` and `test(`), an "Apply to script" button (with a confirm dialog) writes it back via `tests:updateScript`. If the script was too long to fit in the prompt (head-truncated), the prompt asks for changed lines only, so no partial file is offered for apply. The apply confirm now shows a **line-level diff** (`renderer/lib/line-diff.ts`, LCS-based, no dep) of the current script vs. the corrected spec — added lines green-tinted with `+`, removed lines red-tinted with `-`, unchanged dimmed — plus a `+N / -M lines` summary in the description. `tests:updateScript` re-parses the steps from the new script via `parseSpec` (for non-imported tests) so the Steps tab reflects the applied script; `onApplyScript` invalidates both the `script` and `test` queries to re-render. Since 2026-08-06 `onApplyScript` also snapshots the steps **before** the write (the only moment the previous list still exists anywhere), diffs them against the record the handler returns (`renderer/lib/diff-steps.ts`) and glows the added rows in the Steps tab — the diff comes off the return value rather than a refetch, which would race the highlight. The Steps/Script tabs are controlled so an apply that deletes the last step moves off the Steps tab instead of stranding the user on a pane whose trigger has disappeared. **Since 2026-08-12 the apply also says who made it**: `onApplyScript(source, origin)` carries a `ScriptChangeSource` into `tests:updateScript`, which journals it (`script-change-store.ts`) so the change shows up in the Heals tab with a diff and a Revert. The panel's own Apply sends `reviewed: true` (it is only reachable behind the diff) and the auto-apply path in `ai-debug-store.tsx` sends `reviewed: false` — which is what files an unread fix in the review queue rather than in history. The model comes from the SESSION (`AiDebugSession.model`, stamped at `startStream`), not from the current setting: the auto-apply path runs long after the panel that chose the model is gone, and a follow-up can be sent to a different model than the one that wrote the fix being applied.
  - **Debug with AI dialog title shows the model name:** the `AiDebugDialog` title is "Debugging with {model}" (the configured default model from `llm:getConfig`), falling back to "Debugging with AI" if no model is selected or the config fetch fails. **The model name is clickable** (`ModelPicker` in `ai-debug-panel.tsx`): clicking opens a floating list of available models (fetched via `llm:status` for the configured provider); scrolling the wheel or arrow keys cycles the highlighted entry (wrapping), Enter or click confirms the selection — persisted via `llm:setConfig({ model })` so future prompts use it — and Escape closes. The current model is marked with a check icon and the highlight seeds there when the list opens. Falls back to the plain string title when no models are available.
  - **"Thinking with {model}" streaming placeholder:** all three LLM dialogs (`AiDebugDialog`, `GenerateTestDialog`, `GenerateStepsDialog`) fetch the configured default model via `llm:getConfig` on open and show `Thinking with {model}` in the status badge and `Thinking with {model}…` in the empty-content placeholder while streaming — falling back to `Thinking` / `Thinking…` when no model is configured. Replaces the prior generic `Generating` / `Generating…` text.

  - **Navigation containment in the training window (a replay ESCAPED to the system browser):** Glaze routes cross-origin main-frame navigations to the SYSTEM BROWSER by default, and the trainer's interception had holes — so a "Replay from current step" left the training window and drove a different browser the user had open through the whole test. Root cause: **`will-redirect` had no listener at all.** It fires instead of `will-navigate` when a server 302s a navigation elsewhere, so a click that began same-origin escaped the moment the site redirected cross-origin — routine on a Shopify checkout. Secondary holes, all fail-OPEN: `if (!details.isMainFrame) return` meant a missing field disabled the entire guard, and `if (!/^https?:/.test(url)) return` let `mailto:`/`tel:`/custom app schemes hand off to the OS mid-replay. `main/services/recorder-navigation.ts` now owns the decision as a PURE function that **fails closed** — nothing proceeds unless positively proven safe, and the only two accepted proofs are "same-document" (cannot leave the page) and "we issued it ourselves" (`selfLoad`). `isMainFrame === false` (strictly) allows a sub-frame nav; an ABSENT field is treated as main-frame and contained, because a mis-loaded iframe is recoverable and someone's real browser being driven through a test is not. Non-http schemes are BLOCKED, not passed through. `GUARDED_NAVIGATION_EVENTS` (will-navigate / will-redirect / will-frame-navigate) are attached by iteration so adding one wires it, with `isDuplicateContainment` suppressing the overlapping fires that would otherwise loadURL the same page repeatedly. `preventDefault` happens FIRST on every containment path and a throw from it is logged as an error. Blocks push `recorder:navigationBlocked` → a toast, because when this protection fails the damage happens in another application where the app can neither see nor undo it. Coverage: `recorder-navigation.test.ts` (22 tests, mostly on the shapes that used to return early, incl. an exhaustive sweep asserting no unowned main-frame http nav is ever allowed) + `npm run check:recorder-navigation`, a SOURCE-level check that the events are wired, that the old fail-open patterns cannot return, that `setWindowOpenHandler` always denies, and that **no main-process file calls `openExternal`** — the other way out of the app.

  - **Sticky trainer panel (docked beside the training browser):** an opt-in narrow window (`RecorderSettings.trainerPanelEnabled`, **default false**) carrying the mabl-shaped essentials — status, the live step list (drag / inline-edit / delete / insert-cursor), the tile strip (Assert / Add / Replay / AI — always folded at 360px, no carets: four tiles share ~332px and a caret is the difference between a name and an ellipsis) over the reserved context band (`.gl-panelwin-context`, where the armed banners and the replay-status band retired to, and where Hard/Soft became reachable from this surface for the first time — `soft` was hard-coded `false` in this view's assert handler its whole life), and Save/Discard — pause/resume is the header's Recording/Paused chip itself, which is a real button here as in the main trainer — pinned to the right edge of the training browser. Docking **shrinks the browser** by the panel width so nothing is ever covered; the panel then FOLLOWS the browser on move and resize, and a ⤢ button undocks it (giving the width back) and re-docks. The main window's `RecordingView` is unchanged and stays live: both are renderings of the same session, which is safe only because the backend owns step ordering and broadcasts the whole list (`recorder:steps`) after every mutation. Console / Step details / Cookies deliberately stay in the main window — at ~360px a tabbed log pane is unreadable, and the panel's job is the loop you repeat hundreds of times per test. Files: `main/services/panel-dock.ts` (pure geometry), `main/windows/trainer-panel-window.ts` (window + follower), `renderer/trainer/` (`index.tsx` → `trainer-window.html`, `trainer-panel-view.tsx`). Guarded by `check:trainer-panel`. **Drawn in the `--gl-*` theme layer since 2026-08-12** (`.gl-panelwin*` in `screens.css`) — it was the last surface in the app still on the component library's stock classes, which mattered because it docks EDGE TO EDGE with `RecordingView` and the two were rendering one session in two designs; the status chip in particular was the SDK's `error` variant (red — the failed-run colour) for "Recording". Reviewable at `?view=trainer-panel` in the browser preview. The training browser sets `acceptFirstMouse` (see `recorder-service.ts`) because this panel is `alwaysOnTop`: without it macOS spends every click returning to the page on window activation, and in a recorder that is a step that silently never gets recorded.

  - **Experimental: keep a running AI debug job across a re-run** (`RecorderSettings.keepRunningAiDebugJobs`, default FALSE, in Settings' Experimental section). The blank-slate rule cancels and discards a test's AI debug session when the run changes, which also destroys a job still mid-answer — painful with a slow local model. With this on, a session that is STILL STREAMING survives instead, flagged `superseded`: it stops colouring the run panel's icon (that lookup is run-scoped), is reachable only from the global chip where it reads "· previous run", and opens with a callout saying its diagnosis describes output no longer on screen. **A FINISHED session is discarded either way** — work in progress is what the setting protects; a stale answer is precisely what the blank-slate rule exists to remove. Once flagged `superseded` a session is exempt from further discards, including when it completes: clearing it the moment its answer arrived would defeat the entire opt-in. Reopening it for the run it actually belongs to clears the flag.

  - **Blank slate between runs + the icon-state suite:** a session is now DROPPED from the store the moment the run changes (`TestDetailView` effect calling `discard`), not merely reset when reopened. Resetting-on-open left it reachable from the global chip, which restores via `expand()` and never goes through `openSession`'s reset — so the previous run's answer kept reappearing. Run identity is `recordId ?? "t<RunInfo.startedAt>"`, stable for the run's WHOLE life; the earlier `hash(output)` changed on every streamed line, so a session opened mid-run looked like a different run one chunk later. `useAiDebugStatus(key, runKey?)` additionally scopes the icon so a session from a previous run can't colour it (a session with no runKey — persisted before the field existed — matches anything, or every restored diagnosis would be blanked). **`renderer/main/ai-debug-icons.test.tsx` is THE icon-state suite** and CLAUDE.md now requires extending it whenever the AI debug feature changes: a wrong icon colour is silent (the panel works perfectly while the icon lies) and it is acted on — walking away from a finished answer, or waiting on a dead one. It covers every status on the run panel, transitions without a reload, across-tests isolation, across-runs scoping, and the chip's aggregate.
  - **Response pane overflow:** `flex-1 + min-h-0` alone made the pane fill but stopped it CLIPPING — the dialog body is `max-h-[70vh]` (a max, not a definite height), so `viewportClassName="h-full"` resolves against an auto height and bounds nothing, and a long reasoning stream ran straight out through the bottom of the dialog. Both panes now carry `min-h-0 flex-1 max-h-[56vh]` with a matching viewport max-height: flex-1 fills, the max-height clips.

  - **A session belongs to ONE run, not to a test:** `AiDebugSession.runKey` records which execution the session describes (the artifact `recordId`, or a hash of the run output before one exists). `openSession` RESETS the session when the runKey differs — clearing content, status, error and `scriptHash`, and cancelling any request still answering about the old run (its chunks route by session key, so a survivor would stream the previous run's answer into the new session). Regression this fixes: sessions were keyed `run:<testId>` only, so re-running a test and reopening the panel showed the PREVIOUS run's diagnosis, its diff, and a stale-script warning about a diagnosis never requested for that run. The pre-refactor dialog had reset itself on `output.length` changing; lifting state into the store dropped that. Reopening the SAME run still restores everything — that's the feature. A restored session with no runKey (persisted before this existed) is never treated as stale, or every restored diagnosis would be wiped on first open. The reset works by issuing a fresh `startedAt` via a **strictly monotonic** `nextStamp()` — the dialog's draft, thread and fulfilment counters all key on startedAt, and `Date.now()`'s millisecond resolution would let a same-tick reset silently reuse the previous run's UI state (it also gives "stop the oldest stream" a defined answer between ties).

  - **AI dialog response pane fills the window, with an auto-scroll toggle:** the streamed response used to size itself to its content and sit in the top half of an otherwise empty dialog, and a reasoning model's thinking was additionally boxed in its own `max-h-40` scroller — so a long "thinking" phase showed ~9 lines mid-panel with black space below it. The response `ScrollArea` in BOTH dialogs is now `min-h-0 flex-1` + `viewportClassName="h-full"` (min-h-0 is the load-bearing half: a flex child otherwise refuses to shrink below its content, so the parent overflows instead of the child scrolling), its content wrapper is `min-h-full`, and the reasoning `<pre>` lost its nested scroller so thinking and answer scroll together — which also puts the thinking within reach of auto-scroll. **Auto-scroll is on by default** (a response you have to chase is one you stop watching) with an `AutoScrollToggle` beside Stop/Regenerate/Minimize; in `AiDebugDialog` the preference lives in the session DRAFT so switching it off survives minimize/restore (the dialog is unmounted while minimized, so component state would silently re-enable the yank). `check:ai-debug-scroll` now validates TWO shapes — constrained (`max-h-[Nvh]` + matching `viewportClassName`) and fill (`flex-1` + `min-h-0` + bounded viewport) — and asserts at least two panes use the fill shape, which is what caught the regression when reverted.

  - **Console + network capture, offered to the AI only when it ASKS:** a new per-test **"Record console & network"** toggle (beside "Capture screenshots", **default OFF**, global default `RecorderSettings.defaultRecordLogs`) makes the capture fixture subscribe to `console`/`pageerror`/`request`/`response`/`requestfailed` and write `console.json` + `network.json` into the run's artifact dir. Each entry records the ACTION INDEX current at the time, so a failure can be correlated with what the page was doing. Gated independently of screenshots and a11y for the same reason those are gated from each other — this one persists page-controlled text and request URLs, so it must never be bought by asking for pictures. App-generated tests only (an imported spec never gets the fixture). **Two-layer scrubbing, because neither layer can do the other's job:** the fixture runs in Playwright's process and cannot reach the encrypted secret store, so it does the STRUCTURAL work at write time — a header **allowlist** (`log-capture-source.ts`; content/caching/CORS/tracing names kept, everything else recorded BY NAME with the value `<omitted>` so a header's presence stays visible, failing closed rather than denylisting) and masking of credential-bearing URL query parameters — while `artifactStore.readLogs` applies `redactWithSnapshot` on the way out, the last point before the data can reach a UI or a prompt. A `recordAllHeaders` setting is the explicit escape hatch — with exactly one exception, `HEADER_NEVER_RECORD`, checked BEFORE the `allowAll` branch: the three Shopify signature headers are a credential THIS APP injected, and `recordAllHeaders` is documented as the user's opt-out for headers the PAGE sends. The line stops there and does not grow to cover `authorization`/`cookie`, which are what the escape hatch is for. Per-run head+tail caps (100+400 each) bound a polling page, and the dropped count is reported rather than silently swallowed. Helpers live as a raw JS string (`LOG_CAPTURE_HELPERS`) interpolated into the fixture — the `DOM_HELPERS` idiom — and `check:log-capture` evaluates THAT EXACT STRING, since testing a re-implementation would miss the only risk that matters. `check:run-logs` covers the read side incl. secret redaction and corrupt files. **The request protocol:** the debug system prompt gains `LOG_REQUEST_PROTOCOL` ONLY when the run actually recorded logs (`DebugContext.logsAvailable`), telling the model it may end a reply with a ```glaze-request fenced block (`{"need":["console","network"],"why":"…"}`). `parseLogRequest` is deliberately strict — exact fence tag, valid JSON, known `need` values — because the two failure directions are asymmetric: a miss is harmless, while a false positive arms a button offering to ship page data on the strength of the model writing "you should check the console logs", an utterly ordinary sentence. **Fulfilment is never automatic and takes two explicit clicks:** the card shows what was asked and why; "Show me what it would send" FETCHES and summarizes (`buildLogPayload` — failures first, then the tail, capped at 60 lines each, headers only on failed requests, with counts + a token estimate and an explicit note of what was left out); only then does a "Send this data" button exist. Declining is recorded so the model isn't re-asked, and `MAX_LOG_FULFILMENTS` (3) plus per-`need` de-duplication stop a loop. The payload labels itself PAGE-CONTROLLED and untrusted, because console text is attacker-influenceable and the model's output can be applied to the user's script. When a run recorded nothing the card says how to turn recording on and re-run, rather than reporting an empty log — which would read as "the page was silent". `runner:done` now carries `recordId` (the artifact id) since `runId` is the TEST id that every run shares. Coverage: `ai-log-request.test.ts` (mostly what must NOT parse), `ai-log-payload.test.ts`, `check:log-capture`, `check:run-logs`, and the request-card flow in `ai-debug-integration.test.tsx`.
  - **A third thing to ask for: `structure` (2026-08-10).** The protocol's vocabulary was `console` and `network`, so a model facing an ambiguous locator — the one failure it genuinely cannot diagnose from the output, because a strict-mode violation says ten elements matched and nothing about what they are — had no value to name and fell back to prose, asking the user to paste in a screenshot or the HTML by hand. `need:"structure"` answers it from data already on disk: `heal-failures.json`, which run-time Auto-Heal already writes for every step it could not rescue, and which the heal fixture already produces for a strict-mode violation (`isResolveFailure` matches that string). No new capture. The payload renders each candidate through `locatorToPrompt`, so what the model gets back is a ranked list of addressable locators to PICK from rather than an element it must then invent a way to reach. `LOG_REQUEST_PROTOCOL` (a const) became `logRequestProtocol(available)` (a function), because the two sources are independent settings — a run can have Auto-Heal data and no console recording — and advertising a value the app cannot answer costs the user a round trip to be told no. The panel likewise splits availability by SOURCE: a request for both when only one exists sends the half it has, with the section header built from what was actually fetched (a `Console (0 recorded):` header over an empty fence is a claim that the page was silent). **The protocol had never reached a model.** `logsAvailable` was computed in `test-detail-view.tsx` and passed into the panel, and the panel never forwarded it to `buildDebugMessages` — so the conditional branch appending the instructions was dead for the whole life of the feature, while its parser, card, fulfilment cap and decline state all worked. The feature failed in the shape of a bad model. Now covered by an assertion on what is actually SENT, not on what the builder returns when asked directly.

  - **LLM errors carry a KIND, not just a message:** the renderer appends the "and here's where to fix it" hint, and it used to choose which hint by regex-matching the message text. That misfires the moment a message legitimately contains a trigger word, and it shipped twice. The visible failure: the empty-response message reads "…so this is **not a connection or timeout** problem", which matched `/timeout/` and had "Open Settings → AI provider to check the connection" appended — one paragraph both denying and asserting a connection fault. `LlmErrorKind` (`no-model` | `auth` | `model-unavailable` | `provider` | `empty-response` | `connection`, in `main/services/llm/types.ts`, mirrored in `renderer/lib/llm-types.ts`) is now decided by the backend code that KNOWS and travels on the `llm:error` payload. `describeHttpFailure` returns `{message, kind}` together so the two can't be re-derived from each other and drift; `ProviderError` carries its kind; `friendlyError(message, kind?)` switches on it exhaustively (a new kind is a compile error). **`empty-response` and `provider` get NO hint** — the request completed and nothing is misconfigured, so any pointer invites changing a setting that was never the problem. Backend messages no longer hard-code "Settings → …" paths, since the "where to fix it" half is the renderer's job. The text-based `guessKind` survives only for errors with no kind (sessions persisted before this existed) and is ordered so "completed normally / not a connection" wins over the transport words it contains. `AiDebugSession.errorKind` persists it so a restored error still renders the right hint. Regression coverage: `renderer/lib/llm-errors.test.ts` (both routed-by-kind and the legacy guess), `llm-service.test.ts` (kind asserted end-to-end for empty-response / connection / no-model), `provider-errors.test.ts` (a 4xx/5xx with a body is NEVER classified `connection`, however much the quoted body sounds like one).

  - **Empty-response diagnosis (streams that end with no answer):** a stream can finish cleanly having emitted zero tokens. The old message guessed — "It may have hit a token limit, or the prompt may have been too long for its context window" — which made a COMPLETED request look like a dropped connection, and was simply wrong for a prompt that measured ~780 tokens. `runChat` now records evidence while streaming (`eventCount`, the last `finish_reason`, and the set of delta fields that carried a value) and `describeEmptyResponse` (`main/services/llm/provider-errors.ts`) turns it into a specific message: `finish_reason: "length"` → token limit + raise the context length; zero events → "sent no data at all"; events but no text → "ended its turn… the request completed normally, so this is not a connection or timeout problem", pointing at a chat-template mismatch. Every message names the model, the provider, and the prompt size (`approxTokens`, ~4 chars/token) so it can be compared against a context-length setting. The high-value branch is **unknown delta fields**: a model streaming its answer under a key we don't read (`KNOWN_DELTA_FIELDS` = content/reasoning_content/reasoning/role) is otherwise indistinguishable from a model that said nothing, so that case is reported as OUR bug, naming the fields. The facts are also `logger.warn`'d. Covered end-to-end in `llm-service.test.ts` (real SSE lines through `runChat`) plus unit tests in `provider-errors.test.ts`.
  - **ANSI stripping on run output:** Playwright's `line` reporter redraws with cursor-up/erase-line (`ESC[1A ESC[2K`) and colours failures. Those bytes reached three consumers: the Output panel rendered them as visible mojibake, the log file kept them, and they were sent VERBATIM to the model in the Debug-with-AI prompt, spending context on cursor movements. `stripAnsi` is applied in `emitOutput` (`playwright-runner.ts`) — the same single choke point where secret redaction happens, and for the same reason: per-consumer stripping means getting it right in three places forever. Marker extraction (`__GLAZE_STEP__:`) happens upstream of `emitOutput`, so it is unaffected. The regex is built with `String.fromCharCode(27)` because `no-control-regex` rejects an inline ESC literal and blanket-disabling that rule would cover future additions too. Checked by `main/services/strip-ansi.test.ts` (including that `[chromium]`/`[1/1]`/array syntax survive — a regex that ate a leading `[` would silently corrupt every log line).
  - **Model list is fetched once per provider, not per dialog open:** `useModelPicker` used to call `llm:status` every time an AI dialog opened, which after minimize/restore put a `GET /v1/models` on the wire on each restore — noise that appears in the provider's log right beside an in-flight completion and reads like reopening the window is interfering with the stream. It now refetches only when the configured provider actually changes.

  - **Readable LLM provider errors:** a non-2xx chat response used to reach the AI panel as `Chat request failed (HTTP 400). {"error":"Failed to load model \"…\".
