Error: LM Link connection closed"}` — the one sentence that mattered buried in a status code and JSON escaping, with no hint what to do. `main/services/llm/provider-errors.ts` (pure) decodes it: `extractProviderMessage` handles the shapes these providers actually send (`{error: string}` for Ollama/LM Studio, `{error:{message}}` for OpenAI-compatible/Anthropic, `{message}`, `{detail}`, plain text), returning null for JSON with no message field so the caller falls back to the status rather than dumping an object, and clamping an HTML error page to 300 chars. `describeHttpFailure` leads with the actionable sentence and keeps the provider's own words after it. The **model-unavailable** case is called out specifically because it's the commonest local-provider failure — the model picker lists every model the server knows about, but a local server only serves what it can load — with a per-provider fix (`ollama pull <model>` / "Load it in LM Studio"). Errors are thrown as a **`ProviderError`** so `llm-service`'s catch distinguishes a decoded provider failure from a TRANSPORT failure **by type, not by text**: a decoded message quotes the provider verbatim, so a body mentioning e.g. a "network error while fetching weights" would match the `abort|timeout|econnrefused|fetch failed|network` patterns and get replaced with "Make sure LM Studio is running" — sending the user after a server that is up and answering. `renderer/lib/llm-errors.ts` adds the model-picker hint and checks that branch BEFORE the connection branches for the same reason. Covered by `main/services/llm/provider-errors.test.ts` + `renderer/lib/llm-errors.test.ts`.

  - **Minimizable AI debug sessions:** any "Debug with AI" job — the full-run `AiDebugDialog` and the trainer's per-step `StepAiDebugDialog` — can be **minimized** back into its icon and picked up later. A session is keyed (`run:<testId>` / `step:<testId>:<stepIndex>`) and lives in a global `AiDebugProvider` (`renderer/main/ai-debug-store.tsx`) mounted in `RootView` ABOVE the router, because `TestDetailView` is a route component and `RootView` swaps the whole outlet for `RecordingView` while recording — a session owned by a dialog died on navigation, and its backend request was left running with nobody holding the `requestId` to cancel it (billed tokens on the Claude provider). **Icon colour is the status** (`renderer/lib/ai-debug-status.ts`, the single mapping): since B9, cyan = ready for input (`idle`/`cancelled`/`interrupted`), amber + pulse = model thinking, phos = finished and ready for review, red = failed — the same four meanings the pre-redesign contract had (blue/orange/green/red), in the redesign's vocabulary. `toneFor` returns the palette **tone as data** alongside a semantic class (`.gl-ai-busy`, never `.gl-ai-amber`), which is the substantive change: the colour used to be a Tailwind string and jsdom has no computed styles worth trusting, so the only assertable thing was the LABEL — a wrong colour with a right label passed everything. The panel also carries a **"Sending" strip** (`describeSending` in `llm-prompts.ts`), itemising what leaves the machine before a prompt goes to a hosted provider; it is derived from the same `ctx` the prompt is built from, with a drift test that fails if the builder attaches a payload the strip does not name. Each state also carries a distinct `title`/`aria-label` — colour alone isn't accessible and isn't assertable in jsdom. Coloured icons appear in the run Output panel (`run-output.tsx`, now shown whenever a session exists — it used to render only for a FAILED run, so a minimized job vanished the moment the test was re-run and passed), on each failed trainer Console step, and on a **global chip** (`ai-debug-chip.tsx`, bottom-right) which is the only affordance that survives navigation and the trainer; it shows the aggregate status by ATTENTION priority (error > done > streaming > interrupted > cancelled > idle — a finished answer must never hide behind a still-running job) and lists sessions when there are several. `AiDebugHost` (in `ai-debug-panel.tsx`) renders whichever session is expanded, so restoring works from anywhere; dialog dismissal (Esc / close / outside click) MINIMIZES, and discarding is a separate explicit button that cancels the request. **Persistence:** sessions are stored in `userData/recorder/ai-debug-sessions.json` (`main/services/ai-debug-store.ts`, versioned envelope, atomic tmp+rename, capped at 20 newest-first, corrupt/unknown-version reads as empty) — but only the OUTPUT survives a restart, never the job: `llm-service`'s in-flight map dies with the process, so `reconcileInterrupted()` at startup (`main/index.ts`, mirroring `batch-history-store`) rewrites a session left as `streaming` into `interrupted` rather than restoring a permanently-orange icon. A RENDERER-only reload is different — the backend request is still alive — so hydration re-adopts a `streaming` session after confirming it with the new `llm:isActive` IPC. The prompt `messages` are deliberately NOT persisted (they embed the script and run output verbatim; they're rebuildable from live data via `buildDebugMessages`); the answer is, so Stats → "Manage data" gained **"Delete AI debug history…"**. Writes are throttled (2s while streaming, always flushed on a terminal transition and on minimize) and streamed chunks are coalesced at 60ms, with **two separate contexts** (metadata vs. content) so a token doesn't re-render every icon in the app. **Concurrency:** at most `MAX_ACTIVE_STREAMS` (2) concurrent streams — local providers serialize requests, so a third would sit "thinking" without progressing and the colour would be lying; the refusal offers "Stop the oldest and send". Restarting a session cancels its own previous request and never counts against its own cap. `scriptHash` (FNV-1a) is stamped at SEND time — NOT on open, which would erase the signal — so reopening a session whose script was edited while minimized shows a **stale-script warning** above the diff, and the diff always recomputes against the CURRENT script. Pure rules live in `renderer/lib/ai-debug-sessions.ts` (keys, hashing, staleness, capacity, aggregate status, pruning that never evicts a live session); coverage is `ai-debug-status.test.ts`, `ai-debug-sessions.test.ts`, `ai-debug-store.test.tsx`, `ai-debug-chip.test.tsx`, `ai-debug-integration.test.tsx`, `run-output.test.tsx`, plus `npm run check:ai-debug-store`. Views wrap in the provider via `renderer/__tests__/ai-debug-harness.tsx`. **Completion announcements (2026-08-08):** a session reaching `done`/`error` while its dialog is NOT expanded raises a sonner toast with a **Review** action that restores the dialog, and pings `aiDebug:notifyDone` → `main/services/ai-debug-notifier.ts` for a macOS notification. The SETTING gate (`notifyOnAiDebugDone`, default off, Alerts pane) lives backend-side so a renderer bug cannot post banners the user turned off; the renderer fires unconditionally. A user-initiated cancel announces nothing. **Opt-in auto-accept** (`autoAcceptAiDebugFixes`, default off, AI pane → Experimental) applies a finished run-session's corrected script by itself, but only when `hashScript(currentScript)` still equals the session's send-time `scriptHash` — an edit made while the model was thinking wins, and the answer falls back to the review toast saying so. It reuses the same `onApplyScript` path as the manual Apply, so the applied steps glow identically.

  - **Stats view + run-history log database:** a separate "Stats" sidebar nav item (top of the sidebar, above the test list) routes to `/stats` (`StatsView`). Every completed run is persisted to a **log database**: `playwright-runner.ts` accumulates the visible console output per run and, on `runner:done`, appends a `RunRecord` (metadata: testId/testName/url/status/exitCode/startedAt/finishedAt/durationMs) to `userData/recorder/run-history.json` and writes the raw output to `userData/recorder/logs/<runId>.log` (via `run-history-store.ts`), then pushes `runs:changed`. The Stats view shows: summary cards (total/pass rate/passed/failed — counted from `runs:totals`, i.e. **every run ever**, not the length of the capped index; where the two differ the Total runs card says how many are still stored, and the drill rows below keep counting the retained runs because they open lists of exactly those), a dependency-free **pass/fail-over-time** stacked bar chart (green passed / red failed, bucketed by day, fixed to the last 7 calendar days inclusive of today — days with no runs show as a zero bar rather than being skipped), a **run-history table** (Table component; row click opens a raw-log inspector Dialog with copy — columns Test / Status / **Browser** / Started / Tags / Duration / Log, where Browser is an icon-only `BrowserIcon` and baseline rows show a dash; the Tags badge alongside it carries the mode icon plus the engine's glyph with NO engine name, since the column now owns that fact and the name in both places was pure duplication), and a **raw-log search** box (debounced `runs:searchLogs` — case-insensitive across all `.log` files, returns match count + snippet; clicking a result opens its log). Cleanup via a "Manage data" native menu: **Reset stats (keep logs)** clears `run-history.json` but keeps the `.log` files on disk, **Delete stats & logs** removes both, **Delete logs by date…** opens a Dialog with two `NativeDatePicker` (From/To) → `runs:deleteRange` deletes records + logs whose run started in the inclusive range, and **Reveal logs folder in Finder**. Design note: "stats" = the RunRecord index (charts/table); "logs" = the raw `.log` files — deleted independently by design.

## Current State

### Key files
- `main/recorder/capture-script.ts` — Injected page script (as a string) + `DRAIN_SCRIPT` + `DRAIN_PICKED_SCRIPT`. Refine Selector: `ATTR_REFINE`/`ATTR_PICKED` attrs; while `refineMode()` is on, `onOver` draws a fixed bounding-box overlay (`data-pw-refine-box`, pointer-events:none) and `onClick` captures the element (candidates via `candidatesFor` incl. new `xpathFor`, computed `cssPropsOf`, `attrsOf`) into `ATTR_PICKED` then clears refine mode — checked BEFORE the isPaused/assert branches so the page can't respond. Computes Playwright-style locators and pushes steps out of the page. **Since 2026-08-13 the script is built per session (`buildCaptureScript(nonce)`) and capture state lives in the recorder's ISOLATED WORLD, not on `<html>`** (`window.__glCapture` = `{doc, seq, queue}`): world 1999 is the same world on every `executeJavaScriptInIsolatedWorld` call and dies with the document, so it is both the right lifetime and somewhere the page cannot reach — an attribute-based install marker lied, because a page can strip every attribute off `<html>`. Its presence IS the install guard, which is what makes the backend's re-injection safe (state and listeners are created together, so they cannot disagree, and a second copy would double every later step). `push` EMITS each step (see `capture-channel.ts`) before queueing it; click/change/keydown/pointerdown listeners are registered on `window` as well as `document` in the capture phase (a router that stops propagation used to hide exactly the clicks that change route) and deduplicated per dispatch by Event identity; a pointerdown on an activatable element is recorded from `pagehide` if the click never arrives, with a guard both ways so the rescue and the real click cannot both record it. **Not `beforeunload`** — it fires mid-click and recorded every routed click twice. Covered by `capture-egress.dom.test.ts` and `e2e/click-navigation.spec.ts`. Exports mode attr names (`data-pw-paused/-assert/-assert-soft/-refine`, still on `<html>` because the BACKEND writes them) AND `DOM_HELPERS` — a shared string of pure DOM helper functions (cssEscape/txt/isUniqueId/labelFor/accName/roleOf/cssPath) interpolated into both this script and the replayer so locator semantics can't drift. **`roleOf` is a transcription of Playwright's whole element-to-role table (2026-08-22)** — `GL_IMPLICIT_ROLES`, kept parallel to `kImplicitRoleByTagName` in playwright-core's injected script, conditional entries (header/footer outside a landmark, section/form only when named, `alt=""` as presentation, th by scope, td by the table's role) as functions — where it used to know five tags and an `<h1>` had no role at all. Three rules travel with it: `accName` takes a name from CONTENT only for Playwright's name-from-content roles (`GL_NAME_FROM_CONTENT`; alt for an image), because `getByRole("listitem", { name })` verifies unique here and matches nothing in a run; `bareRoleOk` keeps content and structural roles (`GL_BARE_ROLE_SKIP`) out of the NAMELESS candidate list in all three candidate builders (`candidatesFor`, `locatorCandidates`, Auto-Heal's copy), since `getByRole("paragraph").nth(7)` is the positional path with a better name; and the role scan's selector is DERIVED from the table (`roleScanSelector`), so a tag cannot be in one and missing from the other. The browser's own answer for every entry is `e2e/assert-parity.spec.ts`; the fast half is `locator-uniqueness.dom.test.ts`. **The exact text candidate (2026-08-22):** `candidatesFor`, `locatorCandidates` and Auto-Heal's copy push `{ k: "text", v, exact: true }` right after the substring candidate — built from `pwExact(el)` (textContent, never innerText: `getByText` reads textContent and a CSS `text-transform` would produce a value that never matches) — so a unique substring still records as it always did and the whole-string, case-sensitive form is tried before any generated path. The text arm of `matchesForBase` swaps `pwHas` for `pwIs` when `exact` is set and changes nothing else; `pickLocator`'s indexed fallback and `locatorFor`'s rebuild carry the flag. `Locator.exact` survives `normalizeLocator` only as a literal `true` on `k: "text"`. `assertMode()` now accepts the 8 click-pickable assert kinds; assert capture reads the soft flag. **Since 2026-08-12 the chosen locator is verified against the page before it is recorded** (`UNIQUENESS_HELPERS`, `MAX_UNIQUENESS_SCAN`): `locatorFor` builds the same preference order as before but hands it to `pickLocator`, which asks `matchesFor` how many elements each candidate resolves to and takes the first that identifies THIS element — `found[0] === el`, not merely `found.length === 1`, because a wrapper whose text comes from its child matches exactly one element that is not it. `matchesFor` mirrors Playwright's DEFAULT string semantics (case-insensitive substring, whitespace normalized; the text engine's smallest-matching-element rule) — exact-string comparison would under-count and ship the strict-mode violation it exists to prevent. When nothing is unique the best candidate carries `Locator.nth`, and the list ends in an xpath on every path so the choice is total. Scans `textContent`, never `innerText`, because this is on the click path and `innerText` forces layout. Covered by `locator-uniqueness.dom.test.ts`. **Element context (2026-08-14).** `CONTEXT_HELPERS` defines `ctxFilter(list, ctx)` — the ONE implementation of what a user's pinned context narrows to — and it is included INTO `UNIQUENESS_HELPERS` so every existing caller of `matchesFor` honours context without being changed: `pickLocator`, the replayer, the heal probe. `matchesFor` is now `ctxFilter(matchesForBase(loc), loc.ctx)`; `matchesForBase` is the old body, split out because a container and an `and` predicate are themselves locators and never carry a context of their own (that is where the recursion stops). Each clause mirrors the Playwright expression `locatorExpr` emits: `within` is a STRICT descendant (`Node.contains` counts self, so the identity check is what says so), `withinHasText` uses `pwHas` on the container's own text, `and` intersects. Applied BEFORE `nth`, matching the emitted chain. `PICKED_HELPERS` is the picker's whole surface hoisted into one shared string — `describeEl`/`xpathFor`/`candidatesFor`/`attrsOf`/`neighborTextOf`/`buildPicked` plus the new signal builders — because `PICK_AT_POINT_SCRIPT` carried a hand-maintained TRANSCRIPTION of four of those, so a right-click pick would have offered different disambiguation from a left-click one. `contextSignalsFor(el, base)` offers every property that could tell the element apart (scoping ancestors via `scopeLocatorFor`, a full `data-*`/`aria-*` sweep via `attrSignalsOf`, per-class rows via `classSignalsOf`) each PRICED with `matchesFor` — the same oracle `pickLocator` trusts. `semanticBaseFor` decides what those counts are relative to, and it is deliberately NOT `pickLocator`'s answer: `cssPath` emits `:nth-of-type` and is unique by construction, so `pickLocator` almost always finds one of them unique and `nth` is essentially never written — pricing against it would report every signal as narrowing 1 → 1 and the picker would never open, while the recorder silently recorded a generated path. `buildCountScript(loc)` is the live readout's counterpart, returning -1 (never 0) when it cannot count. Covered by `element-context.dom.test.ts` and `e2e/context-parity.spec.ts`. **Shadow DOM (2026-08-22).** `evTarget(e)` resolves every handler's target through `composedPath()[0]` rather than `e.target`, because a document-level listener sees an event that crossed a shadow boundary RETARGETED to the host — so a click inside a web component recorded as a click on the component. `scanAll` (in `UNIQUENESS_HELPERS`, therefore shared with the heal probe and the replayer) descends into open shadow roots, bounded by `MAX_SHADOW_ROOTS`, because Playwright's selector engines pierce them and an oracle that does not scores a runnable locator as matching nothing. CLOSED roots and the XPath arm are deliberately left alone — Playwright reaches neither, so excluding them is what keeps trainer and run agreeing. `deepElementFromPoint` (in `DOM_HELPERS`) is the same fix for the two paths that ask what is under the cursor: the right-click pick and the drag-release target. Covered by `shadow-dom-capture.dom.test.ts`; see DECISIONS 2026-08-22.
- `main/recorder/capture-channel.ts` — **How a captured step leaves the page, and how the backend decides which arrivals are new.** Pure (no electron/fs/IPC), imported by the injected script's builder and by `recorder-service.ts`. The page emits each step as `console.debug("__GOODLOOKS_STEP__" + …)` INSIDE the click's own dispatch, and the backend reads it off `webContents`'s `console-message` — so a click that navigates cannot lose its step to the document being destroyed, which is what the DOM queue + 250ms poll did (six clicks along a chain of routes, zero steps recorded; see DECISIONS 2026-08-13). Chosen over `fetch`/`sendBeacon`, which a page's CSP can forbid. `parseCaptureMessage(message, nonce)` rejects everything an ordinary site prints and authenticates the rest against the SESSION's nonce, which lives only in the isolated world's closure — so this channel, unlike the queue, cannot be forged by the page (steps are still rebuilt by `normalizeRawSteps`). `parseDrainPayload` reads the backup channel's `{d, installed, q}`. `CaptureLedger` is what makes running two channels safe: it admits each `(doc, seq)` once and IN CAPTURE ORDER, holding a step that arrives ahead of a gap and releasing it after `GAP_GRACE_MS` (1.2s) — a plain seen-set would admit a late-recovered step after ones captured behind it, which is a test that does the right things in the wrong sequence and reports nothing. Covered by `capture-channel.test.ts` + `check:capture-egress`.
- `main/recorder/types.ts` — Backend types: `Step`, `RawStep`, `Locator`, `TestRecord`, `RecorderState`, `AssertKind` (16 kinds, incl. `css` — reads `Step.cssProp` (kebab-case) / `Step.cssMatch`, expected value in `Step.value`), `ElementState` (`hover`/`focus`/`press`/`release`, for `state` steps) + `CSS_ASSERT_PROPS` + `isCssPropName`, `ConditionKind` (9 kinds — visible/hidden/exists/enabled/disabled/checked/unchecked/urlContains/titleContains, used by `if` steps via `Step.cond`), `LocatorKind` (incl. `xpath`), `StepType` (incl. `wait`/`viewport`/`if`/`endif`/`state`). `RecorderSettings.defaultWindowSize` (`{width,height} | null`) persists the New Recording dialog's window-size preset. Also `initialCursor(editing, steps)` — where a session's insert cursor opens (its counterpart for a session already under way is `cursorPastReplayed` in `recorder-service.ts`): just past the NAVIGATION, found with `findIndex(type === "goto")` rather than assumed to be index 0, because a test recorded at a window-size preset opens with a `viewport` step ahead of it (covered by `initial-cursor.test.ts`). Also `StepStructure`/`StepMatch` + `normalizeStepStructures` / `normalizeStepMatches` / `buildStepStructures` — the rebuild for the page structure recorded around a failing step (`heal-failures.json` + `step-matches.json`, joined on step index into one record per step), a THIRD page-authored channel: the probe runs inside the page, so every description and locator it reports is chosen by the site, and it now feeds an LLM prompt whose answer is one click from the user's script. Read off disk rather than off the queue attribute, which changes nothing — `writeHealFailures` persists the fixture's JSON verbatim. Capped by `MAX_STRUCTURE_STEPS`/`MAX_STRUCTURE_CANDIDATES`; an out-of-range score is zeroed rather than clamped, since a made-up 1 would sort a hostile candidate to the top of a list the model reads as ranked. Pinned by `check:step-ingest` §9–11. `matches` (what the locator LITERALLY resolved to) and `candidates` (what Auto-Heal thought RESEMBLED the element) answer different questions and either can exist alone — an ambiguous locator that then healed writes matches and no heal failure — so the join is a union, and neither side invents the other's fields. Mirror: `renderer/lib/recorder-types.ts` (also exports `RawStep`).
- `main/recorder/window-size.ts` — the size a recording is made at, as pure functions (no SDK imports, so it's directly testable). `normalizeViewport(input)` turns an untrusted `{width,height}` from IPC or the settings file into a clamped integer size or `null` (min/max `MIN_VIEWPORT`/`MAX_VIEWPORT`); a half-specified size is `null` rather than a guess. `recordedViewport(steps)` reads the size an existing test was recorded at from its FIRST `viewport` step (a later one is a mid-test resize, not the size the run starts at), skipping steps whose stored numbers are unusable. Used by `recorder-service.start` for both paths and by `recorder-settings-store` to validate the persisted default. Covered by `main/recorder/window-size.test.ts`, which also pins the `recorder-service` wiring at source level (viewport step BEFORE goto, `useContentSize`) the way `trainer-window-gate.test.ts` does.
- `main/recorder/recording-shortcut.ts` — the ⌘R / Ctrl+R recording-paused toggle, as two pure functions: `isRecordingToggleInput(input)` (the chord — keyDown, no auto-repeat, no ⇧/⌥, so ⌘⇧R stays force reload) and `recordingToggleAction(state)` (what it DOES: `fallthrough` with no session — the chord still belongs to View → Reload; `consume` for a live session that is loading, replaying or refine-armed — the key must never mean "reload the window" while a session lives, and replay/refine own `session.paused`; `toggle` otherwise). Pure and structural, because both decisions must be testable without an Electron window; the attachment (`attachRecorderShortcuts` in recorder-service.ts) is where the main process wins the chord off the Reload accelerator with `preventDefault()`. Wired to the MAIN window (main/index.ts) and the TRAINER PANEL (on open) — never to the training page, where ⌘R keeps meaning "reload the site" and is recorded as a reload step. Covered by `recording-shortcut.test.ts` (both tables) and `e2e/recorder-shortcuts.spec.ts` (who wins the chord, on the real main window AND the real panel — only a real menu, webContents and key event can say).
- `main/services/recorder-service.ts` — Owns the recording `BrowserWindow`. **The window does not load the page itself.** As of the URL strip it holds two `WebContentsView`s: `pageView` (the untrusted site, carrying the per-session in-memory `partition`, the navigation guards, the denied `openExternal` permission and the capture injection) and `chromeView` (`recorder-chrome.html`, the app-owned URL bar). `pageWc()` is the ONLY way to reach the page — `recWindow.webContents` renders nothing now, so a call left on it is a guard protecting nothing or a drain returning no steps, and neither throws. `check:recorder-views` fails the build if the direct form reappears. `destroyViews()` runs on every path that clears `recWindow`, because a view's webContents is not owned by the window and would otherwise outlive it. `layoutViews()` re-lays both views on every resize (view bounds are absolute; nothing re-flows them). `stripHeight()` is `scaled(URL_STRIP_HEIGHT)` or 0 when `showUrlBar` is off, snapshotted per session. `focusTrainingPage()` focuses the window AND the page view — the window has two focusable webContents now, and focusing only the window can leave a `press` step typing into the (read-only, therefore silent) URL bar; pinned by `check:replay-suspend`. `broadcastTrainingUrl()` pushes `recorder:trainingUrl` to the strip and updates `session.liveUrl`, on `did-navigate`, `did-navigate-in-page` (SPA `pushState` — the case a `did-navigate`-only bar misses entirely), `did-redirect-navigation` and the loading transitions; `page-title-updated` is `preventDefault()`ed, which is what the old title-bar URL bar lost to. `assertUrl(kind)` opens a URL assertion prefilled via `shared/url-assert.mjs`. It opens the window at the session's size — the New Recording dialog's preset (`start({viewport})`, recorded as the session's FIRST `viewport` step so the test replays at it) for a new recording, or `recordedViewport(steps)` for an editing session, falling back to `DEFAULT_WINDOW_WIDTH`/`_HEIGHT` when there's no size. A preset sizes the PAGE (`useContentSize: true`) **plus `stripHeight()`**, since the recorded viewport step is what `page.setViewportSize` replays at and the window's content box now holds the strip as well — without the offset the page renders shorter than the step, the spec and the run all claim, and nothing measures it. Replayed `viewport` steps go through `pageResizeHost()`, an adapter that adds the strip on the way in and subtracts it on the way out so `resize-service.ts` stays unaware of it. **Capture ingest has two channels and one boundary.** The primary is a `console-message` listener on the page's webContents: the step arrives the instant it is captured, before the navigation that click started can destroy the document holding it (see `capture-channel.ts`). The backup is the old drain — injects on `dom-ready`, polls (250ms) and drains on `will-navigate`/`did-navigate`. Both go through `ingestCapture` → `captureLedger.admit` → `recordCaptured`, the ONLY `normalizeRawSteps` call site in the file, because a second channel is a second capture boundary to forget. `sweepCapture` (on the poll) releases anything stuck behind a gap; `stopPolling` resets the ledger and logs the per-session channel tally, which is the only way a silently-dead primary channel would be noticeable. A drain that reports no capture state RE-INJECTS, so a document that never got the script (a missed `dom-ready`, a `document.write`) stops being a session that silently records nothing. `session.captureNonce` (per session, `randomUUID`) is interpolated into every injection and authenticates the console channel. Pinned by `check:capture-egress` and `e2e/click-navigation.spec.ts`. Pause/resume/assert write DOM attributes; finalize on window close generates+saves the spec. `session.steps` is fully mutable with a `cursor`: `addStep` inserts at the cursor; `insertStep`/`reorderStep`/`updateStep`/`setCursor`/`deleteStep` mutate it, and every change broadcasts the whole list via `recorder:steps`. `deleteStep` on an `if`/`endif` also removes its matching partner (`matchingBlockIndex`, nesting-aware) so blocks stay balanced. `replayAll`/`replayFromStart` skip a conditional block whose `if` condition returns `met===false` (jump to matching `endif`); `if`/`endif` never count as failures. `replayStep(id)` pauses capture, injects `buildReplayScript(step)`, builds a `DebugEntry` (with verbose `logs`), persists it via `recorderDebugStore`, pushes `recorder:debugLogs`, and returns the entry. `replayFromStart()` replays steps from index 0, stopping after the first success (or on first failure), persisting each replayed step's `DebugEntry` along the way; returns `{ok, stoppedAtIndex, error?}`. `replayFromCurrent(startIndex)` (backs the "Replay from current step" button) replays SLOWLY from `startIndex` through the end (settle + pause per step), streaming `recorder:replayLog` (`start`/`step`/`done`) + `recorder:replayStep`, persisting each `DebugEntry`; stops on a hard failure (soft continues), honors if/endif, returns `{ok, ranCount, passedCount, failedAtIndex, error?}`. All four replay paths run their injected script through `execWithTimeout(wc, script, REPLAY_STEP_TIMEOUT_MS=8000)` so a hanging/navigating step fails cleanly. **All four also run inside `withCaptureSuspended(body)`** — the single place a replay suspends capture: it sets `paused`+`replaying`, pushes `applyStateAttributes()` (the page gates on the `data-pw-paused` ATTRIBUTE, so the field alone does nothing), `broadcastState()`s so the OTHER trainer window also locks its controls, focuses the training window (a `press` step types into whatever the OS considers focused), waits `REPLAY_FOCUS_SETTLE_MS`(300ms), and restores the PRIOR pause state in a `finally` (replaying while the user had deliberately paused must not resume recording). Replaced four hand-rolled copies of the pause/restore dance, none of which focused or broadcast. `replayStep` now also emits `recorder:replayStep` begin/end, so a single-step preview gets the same row treatment as a multi-step run. Guarded by `check:replay-suspend`. The `resized` logger is gated on `session.replaying`, since a replayed `viewport` step resizes the window through the same host API and would otherwise log as a manual drag. The old auto-`replayAll`-on-load was removed from the renderer; `replayAll` remains but is unused. `start()` force-shows the recorder window on the earliest of `ready-to-show`/`dom-ready`/`SHOW_FALLBACK_MS`(1.5s) timer (cold-start first-click fix); `LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS`(15s) only bounds `pageReady`; the `!shown||destroyed` branch is now a genuine "couldn't open" failure. `getDebugLogs(testId)`/`clearDebugLog(stepId)` read/remove persisted entries (IPC: `recorder:getDebugLogs`/`recorder:clearDebugLog`). Refine Selector: `startRefine()` pauses + sets `refineMode`, `endRefine()` clears it and resumes; the poll also runs `drainPicked()` which reads `ATTR_PICKED`, pushes `recorder:picked`, and drops the overlay. `applyStateAttributes()` also writes `ATTR_REFINE` and shows the crosshair cursor for refine mode. **`Session.variables` + `addVariable()` (2026-08-17)** carry the test's `${name}` vocabulary through the session rather than reading it off the record, because a NEW recording HAS no record — `testId` is a UUID nothing has been saved under, so `tests:setVariables` would answer "Test not found" for precisely the test being trained. Seeded from the record when editing, broadcast in `RecorderState` so both trainer windows agree, and folded back by `mergeSessionVariables` at finalize (upsert by name, keeping anything only the record has — a variable added on the Variables tab in the other window while the trainer was open must survive a save the user thinks is about steps). `addVariable` THROWS its rejections rather than returning silently: this one is a form submit with a visible field, so "that isn't a valid identifier" has somewhere to be said. A secret's value is the exception to "session only" — it goes straight to `testSecretsStore` (keyed by test id, needing no record) the moment it is typed, since there is nowhere else for plaintext to wait; `session.wroteSecrets` records that, and `discardExit` clears the orphan when the session had no record, but never for an "Edit in Trainer" discard. **`runStep` resolves `${name}` before it builds the replay script**, via `resolveStepForReplay` — the injected replayer acts on exactly what it is handed, so a step carrying a reference would type the reference, and the per-step ▶ is the first thing anyone does after making one. **Secrets included**: their values come from `testSecretsStore.valuesFor`, the same read that feeds a real run's environment, because the page is where a password has to end up (see DECISIONS 2026-08-17 for why the first version refused them and why that lasted a day). A declared secret with no STORED value is still refused rather than filled with `""`. What does not follow the value is the trainer's own output: `maskValues` puts every resolved value back behind `****` in the result's error and log lines before anything downstream sees them — the Console tab, `debug-logs.json`, and the prompt "Debug with AI" builds from the step. It reuses `redact` with a placeholder rather than reimplementing the longest-first ordering. The right-click test-tools menu gains a top-level **"Use variable…"** (a `fill` `ContextAction`, deliberately not inside the "Add step" submenu, and carrying NO prefill — a password box's current value is the last thing that should default into a new variable). **`verifyAndInsertSteps(raws, label)` (2026-08-22)** is the generate-steps dialog's insert path: it normalizes the model's steps through `normalizeRawSteps`, then under `withCaptureSuspended` runs each with `runStep` against the live page and `insertStep`s it only once it has worked, stopping at the first that has not; a step the replayer declines (`ok` with a note) is inserted and reported `unchecked`, never `ran`. What lands is wrapped lazily in a `group`/`endGroup` pair named `label` (the prompt) — the group row is written on the first step that works, so a run whose first step fails leaves the list untouched. Returns a `VerifiedStepsResult` (`inserted` counts proposed steps, not markers). Over `recorder:verifySteps`. Proven by `e2e/verified-steps.spec.ts`; see DECISIONS 2026-08-22. **Basic auth (2026-08-22):** a `login` handler on the training webContents answers SERVER auth challenges (never proxy) with `TestRecord.basicAuth.username` + the secret `passwordVar` names, resolved from `test-secrets-store` at challenge time — the same credentials the run's emitted `test.use({ httpCredentials })` reads from env, so a wall that blocks one blocks neither. No `basicAuth` → Electron's default (cancel) and the 401 page shows, exactly as before. **`tryOneStep` + `tryStep` (2026-09-01, PR 3):** the per-step body of `verifyAndInsertSteps` extracted into ONE gate — normalize → `runStep` against the live page → insert only on success, with an `onInsert` hook for the caller's lazy group-open — so the trainer agent is a second CALLER rather than a second gate; `verifyAndInsertSteps` loops over it unchanged (its whole-loop `withCaptureSuspended` stays), and the public `tryStep` suspends around a single try, pinned beside the replay methods in `check:replay-suspend` because an unsuspended verified try records the step it just inserted. Two agent probes live here too because this service owns page access: `agentPageSummary()` (the bounded interactables inventory from `agent/page-summary.ts`, normalized through its own ingest; null on any failure — the agent plans blinder, it does not crash) and `agentProbeStep(raw)` (the Auto-Heal probe reused as recovery evidence, each candidate's locator REBUILT through `normalizeRawStep` before it reaches a prompt, capped at six).
- `main/services/agent/` — **the trainer agent (2026-09-01, PR 3 of the bar plan): drives the live recording session toward a typed goal, one verified step at a time.** `trainer-agent-service.ts` is the loop, dependency-injected like insights (`createTrainerAgentService(deps)` pure against the deps, real bindings at the bottom): states `idle | planning | acting | recovering | awaiting-user | done | stopped`; a per-run event log pushed as `agent:event` (`AgentEventRecord`, seq-ordered so a late-opening window seeds from `agent:getRun` and applies pushes on top); a user-turn MAILBOX drained between steps and before every planning turn (`say()` queues, `stop()` flips the flag every stage checks) so a redirection lands before the next step, never mid-flight; a consecutive-failure budget (3) that parks the run in `awaiting-user` — the park loop returns only on a message, a stop or a dead session, never on its poll tick, or the turn budget would burn while the user was away; hard caps of 12 inserted steps and 10 model turns; every insert through `recorderService.tryStep` (ONE verify gate), serialized so a proposal accept can never interleave with the loop's own try; what lands bracketed lazily `group`/`endGroup` under the goal. Assertion proposals become CARDS resolved by `resolveProposal` — accepting TRIES the assertion on the live page through the same gate, and one that does not hold reports "didn't hold" instead of inserting. The model call is `llmService.completeJson` on the CHAT role slot (structured output against `AGENT_TURN_SCHEMA`; the step objects stay loose in the schema because `normalizeRawStep` at the gate is the real validator), with the insights belt-and-braces redaction before send. `agent-prompts.ts` builds the turn (goal, live URL, `describeStep` tail, the bounded inventory, user redirections, failure evidence — no logs, no headers, no raw HTML; the inventory and evidence are marked untrusted page DATA) and derives its vocabulary lists from `main/recorder/types.ts` constants so they cannot drift from what the boundary accepts; `normalizeAgentTurn` rebuilds the model's answer from named keys with the per-turn caps. `page-summary.ts` is the probe: up to 40 visible interactables (tag/role/aria-label/capped text/id/testid/placeholder/type, NEVER a value — a live form holds passwords), script + `normalizePageSummary` ingest pair. IPC: `agent:start/say/stop/resolveProposal/getRun` in handlers/index.ts, all params `unknown` for the service to judge. Unit-tested against stubbed deps (state machine, retry budget, mailbox, budgets, proposals); the assembled authority is `e2e/agent-loop.spec.ts`, which scripts an Ollama-protocol endpoint via `llm:setConfig`'s `baseUrls` override. **`suggestion-service.ts` (2026-09-01, PR 4) is the AI suggestion strip's engine — the trainer's ONE unattended LLM send**, and the reason `check:agent-egress` exists: after each captured step (via `recorder-service.ts`'s `onCaptureRecorded` hook, which carries no payload — a listener learns the list moved, never what the page sent), a 2.5s debounce fires one `completeJson` on the INSTANT role slot carrying the `describeStep` tail plus the bounded page inventory, and offers up to two next steps as chips. Gated by `RecorderSettings.aiSuggestionsEnabled` (OFF by default — the Recording pane's always-visible `risk` disclosure is the consent), and the flag is read at SEND time and re-checked after the model answers, so a toggle flipped off mid-debounce or mid-completion wins; silent on any LLM error, while the agent runs, and when the session is gone; a generation nonce drops an answer for a page that has moved on. Suggestions are OFFERS: the raw step never crosses to the renderer (`suggest:changed` pushes `{id,label}` only), a step type outside the service's own allowlist (never `fill`/`press` — their values would be invented) is refused whatever the prompt said, and accepting sends the id back over `suggest:accept` where the step runs through `recorderService.tryStep` — inserted only once it has worked on the live page, whatever the flag says now (accepting is attended). `suggest:accept/dismiss/get` in handlers/index.ts; the strip renders as violet `gl-context-chip-ai` chips in both trainers' `BarContextZone` idle slot, beside the mechanical next-action chip, with one X dismissing all shown offers. `check:agent-egress` pins the whole directory the way `check:insights-egress` pins the report: source scan (no logs/scripts/fs/header values, and page-summary never reads a field's `.value`), planted-secret behavior through both prompt builders, and the wiring (redact→`redactWithSnapshot` before `deps.completeJson` in BOTH services, the double flag re-check, the store binding, the false default). See DECISIONS 2026-09-01.
- `main/services/recorder-debug-store.ts` — Persists per-test replay debug logs to `userData/recorder/debug-logs.json` keyed by testId; each test holds a `DebugEntry[]` (latest attempt per step, capped at 200). `get`/`append`/`remove`/`clear`.
- `main/services/overlay-rule-store.ts` — the standing overlay dismissal rules, as a flat JSON index at `userData/recorder/overlay-rules.json` (the failure-reason-store shape). Keyed by HOST, not by test: a pop-up is a property of the site, and a copy on every test that visits it is a copy that drifts. `createRule` derives the host from the page's URL so the renderer never has to agree about what a host is, enforces `MAX_RULES_PER_HOST` (a store that accepts what enforcement ignores is a store that lies), and refuses a target that will not normalize. `updateRule` renames and enables/disables only — the TARGET is deliberately not editable, because a locator comes from the picker where it was priced against a real page, and a retyped one is a selector nobody ever saw match. See DECISIONS 2026-08-22. **Since 2026-09-01 a rule is gated, not always-on.** Rules shipped without a toggle because a host with none armed nothing, so there was nothing to forget; that stopped holding when the app began shipping handlers of its own. `armedPopupRulesFor` (`shared/popup-presets.mjs`) arms the host's rules only while the test's *Handle pop-ups* option — `TestRecord.handlePopups`, with `RecorderSettings.defaultHandlePopups` underneath — is on, beside the built-in presets. The store itself is unchanged: the switch lives on the test and in Settings → Overlay rules, never on the rule, and "off" means the taught rules are not armed either — half a switch would leave a test that asserts on the banner watching it vanish anyway. See DECISIONS 2026-09-01.
- `shared/dismiss-fixture-source.mjs` (it was `main/services/dismiss-fixture-source.ts` until 2026-08-25 — the move has its own bullet under `shared/` below) — the run-time half of overlay rules, as a raw JS string written beside the specs (`glaze-dismiss.mjs`, the settle-fixture idiom). Since 2026-09-01 what it arms is decided by `armedPopupRulesFor` in `shared/popup-presets.mjs` — the host's taught rules plus the built-in Klaviyo/DataGrail handlers, or nothing when the test's *Handle pop-ups* option is off. Installs the shared watcher into EVERY document via `context.addInitScript` — **not** an action patch like the other fixtures, because the overlays it exists for do not wait for a step (ritual.com's Klaviyo modal arrives ~8s in, so a wrapper would catch it on the action it had already blocked). The script it injects carries the app's OWN `DOM_HELPERS` + `UNIQUENESS_HELPERS`, with the click-path cap raised to `UNCAPPED_SCAN`, so a rule resolves through the same `matchesFor` the trainer uses. Rules arrive one pair of env vars each (`GLAZE_DISMISS_<i>_LABEL`/`_TARGET`), never a blob. Dismissals are counted through a `context.exposeBinding` (page-level until 2026-09-02, when a second tab's dismissals went uncounted) rather than a page global — the watcher dies with its document, so a tally on `window` reports whatever the LAST page happened to dismiss, reliably nothing. Never fails a run; diagnostics on stderr only.
- `shared/tabs-fixture-source.mjs` (2026-09-02) — **the run follows the newest tab**, as a raw JS string written beside the specs (`glaze-tabs.mjs`) and installed FIRST by the capture fixture under `GLAZE_FOLLOW_TABS`, which both runners set for every app-generated spec and never for an imported one. The trainer records a LINEAR journey (`keepInWindow` and the window-open handler force every navigation into its one window), so a run has to follow or a `_blank` click strands every later step on the opener. The `page` a spec gets is a Proxy over the ACTIVE page — the newest open one, falling back on close; locator factories return RECIPES materialized against the active page when they ACT (`.filter().nth()` extend the recipe, a lazy argument to `.and()`/`filter({has})` is materialized too); the exported `expect` resolves a page or recipe receiver at CALL time, because Playwright's page matchers bind once (`page.mainFrame().waitForURL`) and never re-resolve; a context init script hooks `window.open` and capture-phase clicks on targeted anchors/forms and reports through `__glTabIntent`, so the next action or assertion WAITS for a tab the page said is coming (an anchor's page arrives after the click resolves — the spike lost that race every time without the signal); and an action or assertion whose bound page CLOSED underneath it (`isClosed()`, never the error text) is retried once on the new active page. The assertion wrapper announces its own step through the capture fixture's marker hooks, because once `expect` goes through this file the reporter files the step under it and its file guard drops it. Every new page writes a `tab` marker to stdout (`{event:"tab", count, attempt}` — `shared/step-marker.mjs` hands it back BESIDE the transitions as `tabs`, since it carries no line) plus a `[glaze-tabs] New Tab Opened (#N)` line to stderr; the runner forwards it as `runner:tab {count, afterIndex}` and Step details renders `> New Tab Opened (#N)` under the step that opened it (`run-output.tsx`, `data-gl="tab-event"`). The per-page halves of the other fixtures follow the page through `perPage`: capture's `patchPageActions` + `installLogCapture`, heal's `tagHealFactories` (and its probe/rebuild now run on `loc.page()`), settle's `installSettleOnPage`, the user stylesheet's `installUserPageOn`; axe and the dismissal counter moved to the context. Manifest and log entries carry `page: n` for a later tab only, so a manifest predating tabs reads as it did; `buildReplay` carries it onto `ReplayStep.tab` in the same pass as the screenshot and rect, and the Visual tab's step row shows a "Tab 2" chip (`data-gl="visual-tab"`, `tabChipLabel`). Both runners write `RunRecord.tabsOpened` off the same markers (absent when none opened), the ingest gate carries it, and the MCP's `triage_run` reports it with a note that a step after that point acted on the newest tab — its screenshot, console and network describe that document. `e2e/tab-follow.spec.ts` is the authority (ten rows: anchor, `window.open`, `noopener`, form, a self-closing popup, a `_blank` download, a ten-round race, assertion progress, capture evidence on the second tab, **a heal on the second tab** — the row R49's shape would have hidden — and a control with following OFF); `main/services/tabs-fixture.test.ts` executes the string against a fake context, `replay-builder.test.ts` pins the `tab` join. Design: `docs/plans/multi-tab.md`.
- `main/services/step-replayer.ts` — `buildReplayScript(step)` builds an injected IIFE (reusing `DOM_HELPERS`) that resolves the step's locator (getByRole/TestId/Label/Placeholder/Text via attribute/role/text scans; css via querySelector; xpath via `document.evaluate`) and performs the action or evaluates the assertion, returning `{ok,error?,met?,logs}` where `logs` is an ordered `DebugLogLine[]` (timestamp · level · message) with verbose detail: locator kind/value + match count, resolved element tag/id/class, action taken, assertion actual-vs-expected, errors/thrown. For an `if` step it evaluates the condition (`evalCondition`) and returns `met` (whether the block body should run); `endif` is a no-op. For a `wait` step carrying `waitUntil`, `runWaitUntil` POLLS `evalWaitUntil` every 100ms and returns a Promise — capped at 5s regardless of the step's own timeout (`PREVIEW_WAIT_CAP_MS`), for the same reason the fixed-duration wait is capped: the trainer UI awaits this call, and honouring a 60s timeout here would read as a hang. The log says when it capped, so a preview timeout isn't mistaken for a real one. Best-effort synthetic-event PREVIEW, not Playwright's engine — but no longer a preview with **its own opinions**. It embeds `shared/step-semantics.mjs` (the tables as JSON, `matchesValue`/`isVisibleByRect` as source), and resolves locators through `UNIQUENESS_HELPERS.matchesFor` — the same engine the capture script uses to judge uniqueness — rather than the private resolver it used to carry, which was exact where Playwright is substring (`label`/`placeholder`) and length-sorted with no containment filter where Playwright is deepest-match (`text`). `resolveOne(loc)` is the single-element path and enforces what a run enforces: it honours `loc.nth` (previously ignored ENTIRELY, so the preview acted on a different element than the generated `.nth(k)` addresses) and returns a **strict-mode violation** for an ambiguous locator instead of silently taking the first match — the most common real Playwright failure, and the one the trainer was structurally incapable of showing. A click additionally runs `occludedBy(el)`, an `elementFromPoint` hit test standing in for Playwright's "receives events" actionability check, and names what is covering the element (a cookie banner over the button is the everyday case a human dismisses by reflex and never records); it returns null rather than a failure when it cannot tell, since jsdom has no hit testing. It also refuses to count an element's own SHADOW HOST as an occluder, walking the `getRootNode().host` chain to tell them apart: `elementFromPoint` stops at the host, so taken literally every control in a web component is covered by the component it lives in — and on the pages this check exists for, the consent modal IS the host, so the banner would be reported as covering its own Accept button. `select` verifies the option exists (`el.value = x` sets `selectedIndex` to -1 and reported success, where `selectOption` does not accept it), a missing attribute is distinguished from an empty one, and `enabled`/`checked` consult `aria-disabled`/`aria-checked` so a div-with-a-role stops reporting the opposite of its state. See DECISIONS 2026-08-13. **A framed step is REFUSED here (2026-08-22):** this script runs in the top document and cannot resolve a `frameLocator` chain, so a step whose `locator`/`toLocator` carries a `frame` returns `ok:false` with an iframe reason rather than acting on the wrong document.
- `main/services/auto-heal.ts` — the **Auto-Heal engine**. `healStep(wc, step, stepIndex, pastEntries, settings)` probes the live page (via an injected `buildHealProbeScript` that reuses `DOM_HELPERS` + the capture script's candidate-generation logic) for alternative locator candidates when a step's original locator fails to resolve. Uses all available signals: the step's action/text/expected context, generated candidate locators, and hints extracted from past-run debug entries (`extractPastHints` — candidates that `matchedPastRun` are ranked/flagged). Retries up to `autoHealRetries` (clamp 1–10) with a per-attempt `autoHealAttemptTimeoutMs` (clamp 1000–30000) via `execWithTimeout`. Returns a `HealResult` (`candidates` best-first, `attempts`, `ok`, `autoApplied`, `appliedLocator?`, `error?`); de-dups candidates by locator identity. Does NOT itself re-run the step — the caller (`tryHeal` in `recorder-service.ts`) applies the best candidate and re-runs. **Element context (2026-08-14).** `resolveAllFor` — a fourth near-copy of `matchesFor`, written against the same Playwright semantics and sharing no code with it — is GONE; `identifiesOnly` calls `matchesFor` instead. Collapsing it was a precondition rather than tidiness: context has to be honoured by whatever decides "does this locator identify one element", and two implementations is one to forget. `withCtx(loc)` puts the failing step's context on every candidate, which is the load-bearing half — it is what `identifiesOnly` judges uniqueness against (so a locator hopelessly ambiguous page-wide qualifies when the user has said which card) and what the applied heal carries, since a heal REPLACES the locator and would otherwise drop the user's disambiguation. `collectElements` also `ctxFilter`s, which is redundant for correctness (verified by reverting it) and kept for cost: everything downstream is per-element and scans the document at UNCAPPED_SCAN. **Framed steps are excluded (2026-08-22):** `healStep` declines a step whose locator carries a `frame` without probing, and `buildHealMap` builds no probe for one — the probe is a top-document query and cannot reach inside a frame. **Exact text (2026-08-22):** the probe's `candidatesFor` carries the exact text candidate like the recorder's, but proposes it only where the substring form did not identify the element — otherwise it is a second row resolving the same set, and under `MAX_CANDIDATES` it cost another element its place. `sameLocator` and the outer dedup key both include `exact`.
- `main/services/ai-debug-history-store.ts` — the **AI debug history** (`userData/recorder/ai-debug-history.json`), added 2026-08-17. The counting half of a deliberate split: `ai-debug-store.ts` holds the model's ANSWER, which quotes the script and run output, so it keeps twenty and is hard-deleted with its test; this holds the FACTS of one ATTEMPT and no content at all — ids, trigger, provider, model, status, `errorKind`, timings, `firstTokenMs`, prompt/answer SIZES. That absence is the whole design: it is what lets these rows outlive both the twenty-session cap and the test, so a deleted test TOMBSTONES its rows (`markTestDeleted`, as run history does) instead of erasing them and making a lifetime total walk backwards. An ATTEMPT and not a session, because re-sending after a connection error is a second wait and a second chance at an answer. Written TWICE per attempt (sent, then settled) by `ai-debug-store.tsx` through `aiDebug:record`, never per token; the open row is what makes an attempt the app died during visible at all, and `reconcileInterrupted()` at startup ends it at the last moment it was known alive — its first token — rather than at `Date.now()`, which would charge a week of downtime to a forty-second session. `backfillFrom` seeds it ONCE from whatever sessions survive, so an existing user's board does not open at zero; what it cannot recover (provider, first token) is stamped null rather than guessed, because the local-versus-hosted split is exactly the figure a guess would corrupt. `normalizeAiDebugHistoryRecord` (in `main/recorder/types.ts`) rebuilds every record field by field on the way in AND on the way out, so neither a renderer holding a fuller object nor a hand-edited file can put content into it. Capped at 2000, cleared with the sessions by `aiDebug:clear`.
- `main/services/live-page-service.ts` — **the Script IDE's live page** (Phase 2, 2026-08-23): a Playwright browser the EDITOR owns, launched headed from the main process through a dynamic `import("playwright")` (external to the main bundle in `scripts/build-main.mjs`; `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` is set to the app's browsers directory before the import, and `ensureBrowserInstalled` installs the bundled CLI's build first). Not the trainer: the detail view is unmounted for the whole of a recording session, and the trainer's `matchesFor` is a model of Playwright's resolution where this is Playwright. Three questions, answered by the engine itself: `countMany(locators)` — how many elements each locator MODEL matches (the model is rebuilt by `normalizeLocator`, spelled by the generator's own `locatorExpr`, and evaluated with `new Function("page", …)` — the same text the generator would put in a spec, with `q()` between every user value and the source); `highlight(locator | null)` — `locator.highlight()`, null clears; `pick()` — `page.pickLocator()` (1.59+), answered as Playwright spells it AND as the app models it (`parseLocatorExpression` in `spec-parser.ts`, the same reader a spec gets). One live page per app; `livePage:changed` pushes status (open, url, picking, closedReason) and the detail view also writes each call's answer into the `["live-page"]` query so the window that asked never waits on the broadcast. In the editor: `LineInlay`s (`script-editor-cm.tsx`, a `Decoration.widget` field) show "1 match" / "no match" (red) / "N matches" (amber) at the end of each parsed step's statement, from `tests:previewScript`'s new `stepList`; the caret's step is outlined in the page 150 ms after it settles; **Pick locator** (editing only) inserts `page.<locatorExpr>` at the caret through the handle's `insertAtCaret`, or Playwright's spelling verbatim when the parser has no model for it; **Record here** starts a trainer session for the test with `recorder:setCursor` just past the caret's step. `check:live-page` drives the real engine over a served page (counts by testid, role, role+name, label, text, css; highlight; close); `live-page-service.test.ts` drives the contract against a fake browser.
- `shared/browser-install.mjs` — **which browser directories a run needs** (2026-08-22): `expectedBrowserDirs(browsersJson, engine)` reads the bundled CLI's `playwright-core/browsers.json` for the revision it launches (two directories for chromium — the browser and the headless shell) and `browserInstalledIn(entries, engine, expected)` checks the user's `recorder/browsers` listing against it, falling back to the old engine-prefix rule only when the manifest cannot be read. Used by `isBrowserInstalled` in both `playwright-runner.ts` and `mcp/run-tests.mjs`; the prefix rule each carried accepted the 1.53 build after the 1.62 upgrade, so every run failed at launch. Tests in `main/services/browser-install.test.ts`.
- `main/services/script-check.ts` — the **pre-save script check** (2026-08-22): `runListCheck` (pure apart from fs + spawn; what `check:script-check` drives against the real CLI in a temp dir), `parseListReport` (the reporter's file → `ScriptCheckResult`, with the draft renamed away and `No tests found` dropped when a load error explains it), `draftPathFor` (same directory as the spec, still matching Playwright's default `testMatch`, never indexed by the store), and `checkTestScript`, the app-facing entry that resolves the CLI, config, runtime and reporter the way a run does. Exports the runner's `resolvePlaywright`/`ensureConfig`/`ensureRuntime`/`baseEnv`/`writeIfChanged` were opened up for it rather than copied. Mirror types in `renderer/lib/recorder-types.ts`; the preview bridge answers `tests:checkScript` with a bracket-balance heuristic so the failed-check UI can be seen in `dev:web`.
- `shared/propagation.mjs`'s **near-miss arm** (2026-09-01, the plan's Later item 3) — a target whose locator is keyed DIFFERENTLY from the donor's old one but is pinned on the same identifier: `css=[data-testid="pay-now"]` or `xpath=//*[@data-testid="pay-now"]` beside a donor's `testid=pay-now`. All three break together when the identifier goes, and only the exactly-keyed one got a proposal before. **The obvious reading of "fuzzy matching" is the wrong one and quietly so**: propose wherever the elements resemble each other, and you rewrite locators that still work — two tests can address one button through a testid and through a role+name, and renaming the testid breaks only the first. So `nearMissLocator` answers "does this locator DEPEND on what changed", never "is this the same element": some identifying value of the donor's old locator (`v`/`name`/`placeholder`/`label`/`alt`/`title` — never `role`, which is a category, not an identity; never under three characters) appears in one of the target's as a WHOLE TOKEN, so `pay-now` does not match `pay-nowhere`. Sameness of element is corroborated separately and REQUIRED — `fingerprint-key-match` or `fingerprintsSimilar`, without which the arm refuses — because a label and the input it names share an identifier. A near miss is **suggest-only**: `autoApplyEligible` is false for every one whatever the confidence, since its step's locator is not the one that was fixed. It carries `match: "near-miss"` (stored, mirrored to the renderer, chipped in the Heals view) and the `near-miss-selector` reason code. Seeds work unchanged — `seedsForTest` keys on the TARGET's own locator, so a near-miss seed fires only when that locator actually fails. The dedupe triple moved to the target's key at the same time: identical for an exact match, and keying on the donor's would have made every sweep re-mint a near-miss proposal it already had.
- `main/services/propagation-store.ts` — **cross-test propagation proposals** (`userData/recorder/propagations.json`), PR 3 of `docs/plans/preemptive-updates.md`. The third journal, and the third separate file for the script-change reason: the heal journal's five consumers key on stepId/runId and would count a proposal as a healed step. An entry is one proposed rewrite of one target step — the TARGET's own `fromLocator` (the undo and the staleness check, nth and context preserved), the `toLocator`, the donor refs, reason codes and confidence — with the heal journal's lifecycle plus `dismissed`/`superseded`/`stale`. Rules carried over on purpose: cap 50/test pruning settled-first never pending (pending is the review queue AND, when `applied`, the only undo); normalized on the way OUT as well as in (the file sits in userData, the locators are page-authored once removed, and the MCP/CLI runner reads this file to seed heal maps), an entry that fails normalization DROPPED, not repaired; one writer, whole file. `check:propagation` pins the lifecycle, the pruning rule, the normalization drops, and — source-level with a positive control — that none of the heal journal's five consumers ever reads this file.
- `main/services/propagation-service.ts` — **the propagation engine's app half**: event-driven glue (no timer) around `shared/propagation.mjs`. Sweeps run when donors can have appeared — after `collectRunHeals` journals a run's heals, after a trainer heal, after `heals:accept`, after a manual locator fix (the `tests:updateSteps` / `recorder:updateStep` HANDLERS diff before/after per step id; `recorder:applyHeal` travels its own channel and the service's own apply writes through `testStore` directly, which is what makes donor feedback loops impossible by construction) — plus one idempotent catch-up sweep ~5s after launch. `applyEntry` is THE ONE apply path (the IPC accept and the auto-apply pass share it): the `heals:accept` write shape plus refusals for settled/stale (the target's key moved — marked `stale`, never applied), script-edited, imported and live-recording targets, with the recording probe INJECTED from `main/index.ts` because recorder-service calls `noteTrainerHeal` back into this module and an import both ways would be a cycle (until init, every apply is refused). Auto-apply runs only under `autoHealApply: "apply"`, re-checks the guards live, and a refused apply just stays pending — the review queue is the fallback, never an error. **An auto apply is a write, not a decision** (2026-09-01): it lands via `markApplied` as pending+`applied` — the amber "Applied, unreviewed" state every review count surfaces — and only a person's accept settles it; `applyEntry` recognises the step already carrying `toLocator` (Keep, and a bulk apply that swept one up, settle without rewriting), the sweep skips applied entries, and the shared stale rule holds an applied entry while its step still carries the fix. A `push` dep (injected beside the recording probe, wired to `propagations:changed`) announces every store change so all windows' counts refresh; missing, it costs freshness, never a write. `seedsFor` hands the runner a test's pending fixes keyed by heal key; `propagateFixes` (default on, `recorder-settings-store`) is a real kill switch — off computes no proposals and emits no seeds. Every trigger is best-effort: nothing here may throw into a run's teardown or an edit's save.
- `main/services/script-change-store.ts` — the **script-change journal** (`userData/recorder/script-changes.json`), added 2026-08-12. The heal journal's sibling: a heal swaps ONE locator on ONE step, this records a rewrite of the whole spec — an AI-debug fix, or a hand edit in the Script tab. Both go through `tests:updateScript`, which overwrites the file and re-parses the steps wholesale, so before this store the previous script existed nowhere afterwards and there was no undo; the "Apply AI debug fixes automatically" setting says in its own copy that "your script can change without you reading the change first". Same shape and same rules as `heal-journal-store.ts` (flat JSON under userData, prune SETTLED entries first because a pending one is the undo for a change already made), plus three of its own: **a no-op is not a change** (`before === after` records nothing — the Script tab's Save fires either way); **sources over `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` are dropped and the entry flagged `truncated`**, which every Revert control must then disable rather than writing an empty file over a working spec; and a **cap of 50 per test** rather than 200, because each entry carries two copies of a file. `normalizeScriptChangeOrigin` is the boundary check for the renderer's `origin` payload — REBUILT, not filtered, the same rule as `normalizeRawStep`; `by` must be one of the two literals, `model` is stripped of control characters and capped at 80 chars, and only an explicit `reviewed: false` means "nobody looked", so a malformed payload cannot fill the review queue. **`reviewed` decides the starting status, and that is the split the whole feature turns on**: a change the user watched land is `accepted` (settled history), one that landed while the AI-debug job was minimized is `pending` (the review queue). **Deliberately NOT folded into `heal-journal.json`** — see DECISIONS 2026-08-12.
- **Deleting a test** (`tests:delete` in `main/handlers/index.ts`) draws one line: anything that NAMES the test or holds its CONTENT goes; the arithmetic stays. Gone: the record, the generated spec, the imported sandbox, artifacts + pinned baselines, annotations, encrypted secrets, the heal journal, the script-change journal, AI-debug sessions, recorder debug logs, the raw run `.log` files, and the test's ids in `batchOrder`/`batchTestOptions`. KEPT: the `RunRecord`s themselves, tombstoned with `testDeleted: true` and their `testName` replaced by `DELETED_TEST_NAME` — so pass rate, the daily chart and capture-overhead totals don't lurch when a test is removed, while every surface that names a test (`runHistoryStore.listLive()`, the Stats table + test filter, log search, Stability, MCP `list_runs`/`get_run_log`) drops them. `batchHistoryStore.markTestDeleted` does the same to a stored batch's rows so its summary still adds up. Stats shows a "N from deleted tests counted above, not listed" note whenever the two numbers differ. The handler also emits `runs:changed`, which it previously didn't — Stats reads run history, not the library, so without it the deleted test stayed on screen. Covered by the `tests:delete` block in `main/handlers/handlers.test.ts`, which asserts each store SEPARATELY: one combined "it's clean" check would pass vacuously the day a new per-test store is added and left out.
- `main/services/batch-runner.ts` — **batch (suite) runs**. `createBatchRunner(deps)` returns `{start, stop, getState}`; `batchRunner` is the app singleton. `start({testIds, captureArtifacts?, runHeadless?, browser?, concurrency?})` queues the tests and runs them **one at a time by default** (`concurrency` absent or 1 — the pre-existing behaviour), or up to `concurrency` at once, awaiting each via `playwrightRunner.waitFor(runId)`, emitting `batch:progress` per transition and `batch:done` at the end. Edge cases it pins: a test deleted after queueing is **skipped** (not fatal); a test already running (`waitFor` → null) is **skipped**, not reported as a bogus failure; a failing test never aborts the batch **unless its own `onFailure` says `stopRoutine`**; `stop()` kills **every** running test and skips the rest while keeping earlier results; only one batch runs at a time (a second `start` returns `{alreadyRunning: true}` with the in-flight id). `summarize(state, now)` is pure (an all-skipped batch is NOT `ok`). **Parallelism is lane-based.** `buildLanes(queue)` partitions the expanded queue into one lane per distinct `testId`; lanes run concurrently up to the limit, entries WITHIN a lane stay sequential. That is not a nicety: the runner keys a live run by `testId` (`runId === testId`), so two entries for one test in flight together make `start()` decline the second with `alreadyRunning` and the row is reported **skipped** — i.e. a dataset sweep would silently run one row instead of all of them. `buildQueue` dedupes the selection so a test's entries are always contiguous, which is what makes flattened lane order identical to queue order and therefore makes `concurrency: 1` the same execution as the old sequential loop. **Two things now multiply a test's entries** — dataset rows and the per-row engine multi-select (`perTest`, validated in the `batch:run` handler by rebuilding each element and filtering `browsers` through `RUN_BROWSERS`, which validates/dedupes/orders in one pass). Both expand *engine-major inside the test*, never across tests, so contiguity — and therefore the lane invariant — survives; the cost is that one test on three engines runs them serially. A test absent from `perTest` falls back to the batch-wide `browser`/`runHeadless`, which is the MCP path. `BatchTestResult.browser` (optional, so `batch-history.json` needs no migration) is what makes three results for one test distinguishable. `clampBatchConcurrency(requested, lanes)` (in `main/recorder/types.ts`) bounds the request by the lane count and `MAX_BATCH_CONCURRENCY` (16); it is applied in the `batch:run` handler AND the runner. `currentIndex` is now **derived** as the lowest-index running entry (-1 idle) rather than assigned by the loop, because it is persisted and pre-parallel `BatchRecord`s are still read back. On completion it fires TWO things through injected seams: `alert` (the opt-in outgoing webhook, failures only) and `notify` (a local macOS notification via `run-notifier`, gated on `notifyOnBatchDone`, default ON, and firing on **success too** — a batch is a job you walk away from). The per-run notification is correspondingly suppressed for batched runs via `shouldNotifyRun`, so a batch with eight failures posts one notification rather than eight. **`notify` steps (2026-08-13).** A barrier may carry a message as well as (or instead of) a pause; the runner fires it through the injected `notifyStep` dep BEFORE pausing, and routing lives on the far side of that seam so the runner never learns which channels exist. `desktop` goes to `run-notifier` (local, and deliberately NOT gated on `notifyOnBatchDone` — that setting turns off a courtesy, this is a step somebody put in a job). `webhook` goes to `alert-service` as a `routineNotify` alert, which keeps the app's single egress single. The message is STATIC text the user typed, never interpolated: see DECISIONS. `check:alerts` pins that its `detail` carries no run data and that `redactPayload` scrubs a secret pasted into it. **Barriers (2026-08-13).** `params.barriers` plus a `segment` on each queue entry is how a `wait` step executes without a second engine: the runner groups its lanes BY SEGMENT, drains one segment's pool, joins, pauses via the injected `wait` dep, then moves on. No barriers = one segment = the exact pool that was there before, which is what `batch:run`, the MCP's `run_batch` and every pre-`wait` Routine get. A test cannot straddle a barrier (the store collapses duplicate testIds routine-wide), so the lane and segment partitions cannot disagree. `wait` is INJECTED so `check:batch-runner` can prove the join without the check actually sleeping, and the real one polls in slices so Stop ends a pause promptly rather than after the full duration. `BatchState.waitingUntil` is set while sitting in one and cleared in a `finally` — without it a pause is indistinguishable from a hang. There is deliberately NO early `break` on `cancelled` in the segment loop: `runEntry` already refuses a cancelled entry and marks it skipped, and breaking would leave segments past the barrier persisted at "queued" forever, which `summarize` counts as nothing at all. **Branches (2026-08-14).** A barrier may carry a `branch` instead of (or as well as) a pause: `{id, on, thenSegment, elseSegment}`. Both sides are already in the queue — the plan put each on its own segment — so taking a branch is one pass marking the PENDING entries of the losing segment `skipped`, with a note saying which way it went and why (a row reading `Skipped` with no reason looks like the runner lost it). Queueing both up front rather than appending the chosen side is deliberate: write-through, the summary, `currentIndex` and the view's row lookup all assume the list is decided at start. The condition reads `results` for a failure, so it answers about the run SO FAR — which is why it sits at a join. **Groups and `skipGroup` (2026-08-13).** A queue entry carries the `groupId` `routineRunPlan` stamped it with, and a `skipGroup` failure marks the rest of THAT group skipped — PENDING entries only. That guard does two jobs: an entry already running belongs to a lane that started before the failure (killing it would make `skipGroup` a `stopRoutine`), and an entry already FINISHED keeps its result, because overwriting a green run with "skipped" loses a run that happened. `runEntry` returns early on an already-skipped entry — without that the mark is cosmetic and the worker runs the row anyway. An entry with no group continues. **A step's failure policy (2026-08-13).** A queue entry carrying `onFailure: "stopRoutine"` that fails does exactly what `stop()` does — kills everything in flight, skips everything not started, keeps what finished. Anything gentler would be a second meaning of "stop", and with lanes running concurrently there is no rest-of-the-queue left to merely not start. The FIRST failure owns it: two lanes can fail in the same tick, and a later one rewriting the attribution would make the note, the alert and the notification all name a test that stopped nothing. The failure-to-START branch triggers it too (a step that could not begin did not do its job). `BatchState` gains `stoppedBy: "user" | "failure"` and `stoppedByTest`, both optional so every pre-existing record still means what it meant; they reach `alert` and `notify`, which is the point — a scheduled routine's desktop notification is often all the user sees of it, and "Batch stopped" for a run nobody touched reads as somebody having intervened. **Deps are injected (`BatchDeps`)** purely so `check:batch-runner` can drive the queue against a fake runner — including asserting runs never overlap, that a swept test's rows never overlap *each other*, and that `stop()` kills all of them — with no browsers or child processes.
- `main/services/script-generator.ts` — `generateSpec(steps)` → spec source; `describeStep` for display. Handles all step/assert kinds: `assertLine` maps each `AssertKind` to its `expect`/`expect.soft` call (url/title are page-level); `wait` → `waitLine` (a `waitUntil` predicate first, else `waitForTimeout`/`waitFor` exactly as before, so tests already on disk regenerate byte-identically); `viewport` → `setViewportSize`; xpath locator → `locator("xpath=…")`. Conditional logic: `conditionExpr(step)` builds the `if` predicate per `ConditionKind` (element → `.isVisible()/.isHidden()/(count())>0/.isEnabled()/.isDisabled()/.isChecked()/!(…isChecked())`; page → `page.url().includes(…)`/`(await page.title()).includes(…)`); `if` → `if (<expr>) {`, `endif` → `}`, and `generateSpec` tracks a depth counter so block bodies indent one level deeper. `describeCondition(step)` gives readable phrasing for the UI. Mirror in `renderer/lib/describe-step.ts` (also exports `computeStepDepths` for list indentation — keep both in sync). **The page-level assert kinds and the two page-level wait predicates all build their matcher argument with `textMatchExpr` from `shared/step-semantics.mjs`**, so anchoring (`^…$` exact, `…$` ends-with, bare substring) and the `i` flag are read from one table rather than decided per call site: `url` emitting a bare string meant an EXACT whole-URL check against a path-shaped prefill, i.e. an assertion that could not pass, while the `urlContains` wait in the same file emitted the correct escaped RegExp the whole time. `title` stays the plain-string (exact) form because that is what its "Page title is" label promises; `titleContains` is the substring counterpart. `containsExpr` does the same job for an `if` condition, which compiles to a JS `if (…)` and so has no matcher to carry the semantics — it emits `page.url().toLowerCase().includes("…".toLowerCase())`, lowering at RUN time rather than folding the literal, so re-parsing does not rewrite the value the user typed. An empty expected value **refuses to generate** (`urlEndsWith` with no value emitted `/$/i`, which every URL satisfies), and any step that produces no line now emits an `// UNGENERATABLE STEP — <describeStep>: <reason>` comment via `ungeneratableReason(step)` instead of vanishing from the spec while remaining in the step list. **`locatorExpr` emits the user's pinned context as a chain (2026-08-14):** `page.<container>[.filter({hasText})].<target>[.and(page.<pred>)]…[.nth(k)]`. `.nth()` stays LAST because it indexes whatever precedes it — emitted before the context clauses it would index the unnarrowed set, so an indexed step with a container would silently mean a different element than the trainer showed. Every context string goes through `q()`. Guarded by `check:locator-roundtrip` (everything written here can be read back) and `e2e/context-parity.spec.ts` (the chain selects the same elements the trainer's resolver does). **iframes (2026-08-22):** `root(loc)` returns `page` or `page.frameLocator(<sel>)` per hop and replaces the five hard-coded `"page."` locator prefixes; `locatorExpr` is otherwise unchanged (Playwright pierces open frames on its own), so a locator with no `frame` regenerates byte-identically. An `and` predicate is emitted with the target's frame, since it resolves in the same one. Proven by `e2e/frame-parity.spec.ts`. **Exact text (2026-08-22):** a text locator with `exact === true` emits `getByText(v, { exact: true })` — a literal `true` only, because the flag crosses the capture boundary and a truthy string reaching here would emit an option the trainer never graded. Mirrored in `describe-step.ts` and `formatLocator`, diffed by `describe-mirror.test.ts` and `locator-prompt-parity.test.ts`. **Basic auth (2026-08-22):** a record carrying `basicAuth` emits `test.use({ httpCredentials })` into the preamble, password read from `process.env.GLAZE_SECRET_<passwordVar>` so the value never lands in source; `passwordVar` re-gated via `isValidVariableName` at emission, username through `q()`. The spec parser skips `test.use` in `extractTestBodies` (it matched the test-call scan and doubled every step on re-parse). Proven end to end by `e2e/basic-auth.spec.ts`.

- `main/services/input-service.ts` — The native half of a `state` step: moves the training window's REAL pointer via `webContents.sendInputEvent` so `:hover`/`:active` genuinely apply in the trainer preview.
