  - `cli/args.mjs` — pure parsing, and three decisions a generic argument library has no opinion about. **An unknown flag is a REFUSAL**: a pipeline passing `--fail-fast` to a build that does not have it must not run the suite anyway and report green. **There is NO default selector**, deliberately unlike `run_batch`, which runs everything visible when given nothing — right for a tool an agent calls with an explicit intent, wrong for a CLI where a misspelt flag would run the entire library on someone's runner. And **two selectors is a refusal, not a precedence**: `selectTests` has one, so it would run something, just not necessarily what the person who wrote both flags expected. `--help` is scanned for FIRST, before anything can be rejected.
  - `cli/exit.mjs` — the 0/1/2/3 contract (R2), as a table plus `exitCodeFor`. **Code 2 is the one that matters**: a selector matching nothing produces a summary shaped exactly like a clean pass, so without its own number a renamed selector leaves a pipeline green while testing nothing. Folding it into 1 is wrong differently — that reads as "your tests fail" and sends someone to the tests rather than the selector. An unrecognised refusal returns 3, never 0. A SKIPPED test does not fail the run (one secret-bearing test would otherwise redden every suite it sits in) and the command says so in words instead.
  - Unit tests are `main/services/cli-exit.test.ts`, **not** beside the code: vitest's node project takes `main/**`, `mcp/**` and `renderer/lib/**`, so a test file under `cli/` matches neither project and would pass by never running. `check:cli-exit` is the end-to-end half — it spawns the real binary against a throwaway store, which is what proves the file parses, its imports resolve from `bin/`, and the async entry point actually sets an exit code.
  - `good-looks install <engine> [--with-deps]` (R11) unpacks into the LIBRARY's own browsers directory. Playwright's ordinary install writes a machine-wide cache, so `npx playwright install` leaves an engine no run here can find; `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` is what makes the two agree, and it comes from the same `browsersDir()` the runner launches from. It uses the BUNDLED CLI, so the revision installed is by construction the revision `isBrowserInstalled` expects — reaching for a Playwright on the caller's PATH is how the 1.62 upgrade broke installs. It is a **no-op when the engine is there** (an install step runs on every CI job) and it **re-asks `isBrowserInstalled` after a successful install**, because `playwright install` can exit 0 having unpacked a revision the runner will not launch, and reporting success then makes "install it, and it is still not installed" a loop with no error in it. `check:runner-config` pins that the browsers directory is derived exactly once. **Both runners REPAIR the `node_modules` link beside the specs** rather than only creating a missing one (2026-08-26): it is absolute, names whichever install wrote it, and outlives that install — so a library copied to a runner carries a dangling one, and two installs on one machine carry a live one pointing at the wrong tree. Either way the spec resolves a SECOND `@playwright/test` and collection dies with a message naming four causes, none of them this. The app learned it while adopting a legacy data directory; the MCP's copy was the pre-fix version until this, which is the drift `check:mcp-parity` section 16 now asserts against both sources, with a behavioural half in `check:cli-exit`.
  - **`--dry-run` (R9)** reports what WOULD run and stops, spawning nothing and recording nothing — mabl's `?preview=true`, whose docs give the silently-empty pipeline its own troubleshooting section. It plans through the SAME `selectTests` + `buildQueue` the real run uses rather than describing them, because a dry run computed by different code answers a different question, which is worse than not answering: it would be trusted. Two placement decisions carry it. It answers **before** the Playwright and browser checks, since "what would this run?" is asked on a fresh CI container that has no browser yet — the browser's state is reported as a fact rather than a refusal, while the real run still stops. And it answers **after** selection, so an empty selection is still exit 2: a dry run that matches nothing has found the bug it was run to look for. It reports the RESOLVED pace (every test since R18 is unpinned, so the record's field would print nothing) and names the tests a run would skip for secrets, since finding that out should not require running the suite. `run_batch` takes the same `dryRun`, over the same function.
  - **`--junit <path>` (R1, 2026-08-26)** writes a JUnit XML report of the runs THAT INVOCATION produced, and `cli/junit.mjs` is the first emit path outside `main/`. The app's `emitReportTo` is unreachable from here — it imports `@shell/backend`, the metrics DB, the run-history store and `redactWithSnapshot`, none of which exist in plain `.mjs` — so the pure emitters are called directly and everything the app does around them is done again on this side's terms. **The scope is a security boundary, not a convenience**: the report is built from `outcome.results` in memory and reads no run history, because this process redacts with the values IT resolved through `resolveCiSecrets`, and a run the APP produced took its secrets from an encrypted store it cannot open. A report scoped to "every run of this test" would include one, and the file would look exactly like a file that had been redacted. The module takes secret VALUES and never a redactor — the same rule `emitReportTo` follows, since a caller that could pass one could pass an identity function. `--junit` with `--dry-run` is a refusal: a dry run performs none, and both an empty file and no file leave a pipeline reading something that never says anything. `check:emit-redaction` covers `cli/` and `mcp/` as of this change, which is plan §3.5's rule that every new egress path extends its scan set in the same commit.
  - **`--base-url <url>` and `--var <name=value>` (2026-08-26)** — R5's remaining half. Its premise was refuted on 2026-08-22 (see DECISIONS): `use.baseURL` resolves RELATIVE navigations only and a recorded `goto` is absolute, so a per-run base URL *"moves imported suites and nothing else"*. `--base-url` therefore ships for exactly that case — an imported suite whose stored `record.baseUrl` a CI run needs to override — resolving override → record (R16's environment layer slots between them, which is why it is a resolution and not a `??`), travelling as `PW_BASE_URL`, and **recorded on the RunRecord** so two runs against two environments are not indistinguishable afterwards. **A run reports when the override could not apply, and names the remedy**: declaring the site address as a variable (`origin-variable.ts`) is what moves a recorded test. `--var` is what makes that remedy reachable at all — on the unattended path a declared variable's value could previously come only from a dataset row, so the app's own answer was unusable from CI. An explicit `--var` layers over a row's value for the same name, and a name no selected test declares is reported, because it is substituted nowhere and is almost always a typo.
  - **`action.yml` + `.github/workflows/action-selftest.yml` — the GitHub Action (R14, 2026-08-26).** A COMPOSITE action, and that is not a style choice: `package.json` is `private: true` with no `files` field, so `npm install good-looks` does not exist, and publishing an Electron app's whole dependency closure to run a Node CLI would be thirty packages a run never loads. GitHub checks this repository out at `${{ github.action_path }}` before the first step, which is exactly the `PROJECT_ROOT` (`mcp/..`) that `mcp/run-tests.mjs` resolves `node_modules` from — so one targeted `@playwright/test` install there is the whole bootstrap. Measured at **three packages and 19 MB**. The Playwright version is READ from `package.json`, never written in the action: `shared/browser-install.mjs` answers "is the browser there" from the installed Playwright's `browsers.json`, so the CLI, the installer and the detector must be looking at one tree. **Every input reaches bash as an environment variable, never spliced through `${{ }}`** — an action input is attacker-controlled the moment a workflow passes it a PR title or a branch name, and `${{ }}` is substituted *before* bash parses the line, so a value carrying `; rm -rf …` is the next command rather than an argument; the same boundary this repo draws for a branch name and an imported project. `check:github-action` pins those two plus flag parity with `cli/args.mjs` (an unknown flag is a refusal there, so a rename would turn every run through the action into exit 3) and INPUT parity with the two documents that describe it — `docs/GITHUB-ACTION.md` and `docs/CI-GUIDE.md`, the second being the in-app manual, so an input missing there is one a user of the app cannot discover at all. That drift is what `retries` did on the day R24 landed. `action-selftest.yml` is the behavioural half — it uses the action the way a stranger would against a library built from nothing whose `scriptPath` is another machine's, which is the one configuration no local check can stand up. User-facing docs are `docs/GITHUB-ACTION.md`.
  - `bin/` and `cli/` are in `build.files`, beside `mcp/` and for R15's reason: a packaged `.app` is where someone who installed a `.app` has the code.
- `shared/base-url.mjs` (+ `.d.mts`) — **the one place a base URL becomes trusted** (2026-08-26, R5). A parsed `playwright.config`, the app's own field and the CLI's `--base-url` all go through `normalizeBaseUrl`. It lived in `main/services/imported-config.ts` while both ways in were main-process; R5 added a third in plain `.mjs`, and a transcribed copy of a validator fails in one direction — the CLI accepting a URL the app refuses. It rejects a non-string, anything containing `${` (an un-interpolated reference would resolve as a literal hostname) and any scheme but http(s) (`file://` would point a run at the local disk), and returns the PARSED href so one URL has one spelling. `imported-config.ts` imports and re-exports it — a bare `export … from` forwards the name without binding it, and the functions in that file call it. `check:mcp-parity` §17 asserts there is no second copy and that the run both resolves through it and records the result.
- `shared/ci-secrets.mjs` + `shared/secret-redaction.mjs` (+ `.d.mts`) — **how a secret reaches an unattended run, and why that is safe** (2026-08-25, R7). `resolveCiSecrets(test, {env, fileValues})` resolves each declared secret from `GOOD_LOOKS_SECRET_<TESTID>__<NAME>` then a `--secrets-file` map, and returns `{env, values, missing}`. **`values` is the load-bearing half**: the plan's requirement is that whatever supplies a secret also feeds the redaction, or the run log becomes a credential store — a secret is typed INTO the page and comes back out in a Playwright error, an assertion diff or a content dump. `executeTest` resolves ONCE and uses both halves, which `check:ci-secrets` pins scoped to that function. The env name carries the TEST ID because two tests can each declare `PASSWORD` and mean different credentials, and a library-wide name hands one test's staging password to another's production login — a failure with no error message, since the run succeeds against the wrong account. An empty value is MISSING rather than supplied (the old failure was empty strings typed into a login form, failing several steps later); the refusal names the variables to set and never a value. `secret-redaction.mjs` is the app's own `redact` lifted out so both strip by one rule — its longest-first ordering is what stops a secret that prefixes another leaving the longer one's tail beside a `[redacted]` label. `runEnv` still mints no `GLAZE_SECRET_*` key: it is shared with the app's runner, and the injection belongs to the caller that also holds the values for redaction.
- `shared/run-fixtures.mjs` (+ `.d.mts`) — **what gets written beside a spec before it runs, and which of it an unattended run gets** (2026-08-25, R8). Two questions in one file because they drifted the moment there were two runners. `ALWAYS_WRITTEN` is the spec runtime and the step reporter; `CAPABILITY_FIXTURES` is capture + heal + settle + signature + user-page + dismiss + tabs (2026-09-02), written as a SET because the capture fixture IMPORTS the other six (a partial set is an import error, not a disabled feature); `redirectToCaptureFixture` rewrites a spec's `@playwright/test` specifier and nothing else, so line numbers — and therefore the step line map and every screenshot's step attribution — survive. **`glaze-runtime.mjs` was the bug this ends**: a generated spec using any helper *imports* it, so it is a dependency rather than a feature, and only `playwright-runner.ts` wrote it. An MCP or CLI run therefore worked exactly when the app had happened to run that test on the same machine first, and failed at module load on every fresh CI container — reported as "no tests found", never as a missing fixture. `CI_FIXTURE_POLICY` is the per-capability decision R8 asks for in writing, `why` column included: signature headers are off for a reason that will stop being true (→ R7); overlay dismissal has been ON since R51 moved the locator engine into `shared/` and, since 2026-09-01, follows the test's own *Handle pop-ups* option through the same `armedPopupRulesFor` the app calls; and Auto-Heal is **suggest only** — run-time healing on, writeback off by construction, since this process has no writeback code and a writeback would edit a `tests.json` that dies with the container. Pinned by `check:ci-fixtures`, which revert-testing showed had one vacuous ordering assertion before it had five real ones.
- `mcp/store.mjs` — **the app's JSON stores, read and written from plain Node** (2026-08-25). `createStore(dataDir)` returns `listTests`, `listRuns`, `saveRunRecord`, `listBatches`, `readFailureReasons`, `listRoutines`, `readSettings`, `readSignatures`, `readOverlayRules` and `saveBatchRecord`. Every one of these was module-private inside `server.mjs`; the extraction is what makes them reachable by anything that is not an MCP tool (the CLI, R3/R4). **A factory rather than a module of free functions**, because all of them are bound to one `dataDir` and threading it through each call site is a parameter nobody reads and anybody can pass wrong — `server.mjs` destructures the result into the names it already used, so no call site there changed. The envelope traps stay documented where they bite: `routines.json` is `{version, routines[]}` and reading it as an array reports an empty library to every user who has one, and `shopify-signatures.json` is read for the plaintext register only (host + expiry), never a header value.
- `mcp/run-tests.mjs` — **the run routine, as something a caller can call** (2026-08-25). `createRunner({dataDir, store})` returns `findPlaywrightCli`, `isBrowserInstalled`, `executeTest` and `runSelection`. Three of those moved verbatim out of `server.mjs`. `runSelection` did not: the batch driver **returned MCP tool content** (`{content:[{type:"text"}], isError}`) at every exit, so a caller that is not an MCP tool could not use it at all — which is the real reason §3.3a of the runner plan is its own change rather than a step inside the CLI's first commit. It now returns a RESULT: `{ok:false, reason:"unknown-browser"|"no-match"|"no-playwright"|"no-browser", …}` for the four refusals, each carrying the fact the message was built from (`how` names the selector that actually applied, in the order `selectTests` resolves them), and `{ok:true, batchId, browser, parallel, missing, summary, fixturesSkipped, results}` for a run that happened. `runBatchTool` in `server.mjs` is now purely the renderer of that result, producing byte-identical text and the same `isError` rule; the CLI turns the same result into an exit code. **`executeTest` resolves the run's pace through `resolveRunSpeed` and RETURNS it** (2026-08-25): it read `test.speed ?? "fast"`, which R18 turned into a real divergence when recordings stopped stamping their speed — every test recorded since then ran at `fast` here and at the user's default in the app. Returning it is what stops `run_test` printing a pace the run did not go at. Pinned by `check:mcp-parity` §15, over every call site. Write-through persistence, the queue expansion through `shared/batch-queue.mjs`, the secret-variable skip and the pool all moved with it unchanged. Pinned by `check:mcp-parity` (whose source oracle now reads all three files) and `check:runner-config`.
- `main/services/metrics-store.ts` — opens `userData/recorder/metrics.db` and fills it. Three rules, each load-bearing: **never fatal** (a derived cache of files that still exist, so every method swallows its own errors and degrades to "no metrics"); **never loaded eagerly** (`node:sqlite` is imported *dynamically* inside a try — a static import would throw at module load on a runtime without it and take the backend down); and **roll up before pruning** — a launch-ordering guarantee too: the startup sweep in `main/index.ts` runs inside `whenReady`, after `metricsStore.init()` and the preflight registration, because pruning skips an unregistered preflight rather than waiting for one, and until 2026-08-18 the sweep ran at module scope and deleted the "older than N days" population unrolled (`check:metrics-db` §7 pins the order against the entry file's AST; `check:retention` §9 covers the preflight seam itself). Three ingest points: run completion (`playwright-runner.ts`), the prune preflight, and the MCP's own runs. Verified against the host's pinned runtime (`node-v24.14.1`), which is what makes `node:sqlite` safe here — the packaged backend does NOT run on the user's `PATH` node. **`triage(runId)`** is the impure gathering around `shared/triage.mjs` (evidence rows, the `TRIAGE_COHORT` sibling window, the failing step's history), kept here so its two callers — the `runs:triage` handler answering the run panel, and the runner's failure-reason auto-assignment at run end — cannot assemble the inputs differently. Null when metrics are unavailable or the run has no rows, per rule 1.
- `mcp/metrics.mjs` — the MCP's half of the same database. Two processes write one file, which is safe because WAL + a busy timeout keeps them from blocking each other and every write is idempotent. Never **creates** the database for a read tool and never backfills — that sweep reads every run's artifacts and belongs to whichever process can do it once at startup, not to a tool call an agent is waiting on. `readHandle()` is the read path added with `triage_run`: it opens an EXISTING file (`existsSync` first, no `CREATE_STATEMENTS`, a version mismatch returns null rather than dropping, because dropping is a write). The distinction matters — refusing to *create* is right, but refusing to *open* what the app already built made every read tool useless in a fresh MCP process, which is every MCP process that has not itself run a test.
- **The prune preflight** (`setPrunePreflight` in `artifact-store.ts`) — called for every run directory about to be deleted, before it is deleted. Registered rather than imported: metrics-store reads *through* artifact-store, so the reverse import would be a cycle — and this way a metrics failure cannot break pruning, which is the more important of the two. Retention is where per-step evidence dies, so this is the last moment anything can distil a run into the rows that outlive its screenshots.
- `mcp/artifacts.mjs` — read-only reader for `userData/recorder/artifacts/<testId>/<runId>/`, mirroring the layout `artifact-store.ts` owns (`readReplay`, `readManifest`, `readRunLogs`, `listRunDirs`). Nothing here writes, prunes or seeds a baseline — retention stays the app's business so two processes can't disagree about what it means. `readRunLogs` unwraps the `{ testId, runId, dropped, entries[] }` envelope the capture fixture writes and carries `dropped`/`headersFiltered` through, so a log truncated by the per-run cap says so.
- `mcp/propagations.mjs` — what `list_propagations` reports, as a pure function of `recorder/propagations.json` (the `artifacts.mjs`/`metrics.mjs` shape: server.mjs resolves a data directory and opens a transport at module scope, so nothing in it can be imported by a test — the deciding half lives here, driven by `mcp/propagations.test.ts`). Two jobs. It is an **egress boundary**: entries are REBUILT from named keys, so the next field on `PropagationEntry` does not reach an external MCP client because nobody looked (the `export-bundle.mjs` rule, same reason), and donor refs are reduced to counts/kinds/test names rather than carried whole — a client wanting journal ids has `list_heals`. And it computes `sitesChanging`, the aggregate a chronological list hides: origins where a PENDING proposal waits on **two or more distinct tests**, which is the shape of "a release moved a selector" (`list_heals`'s `chronicSteps` rule, asked about origins). Several proposals inside ONE test is deliberately not that finding, and settled proposals are not counted — a site whose proposals were all dismissed is a question already answered. The status vocabulary it filters by is `PROPAGATION_STATUSES` in `shared/propagation.mjs`, which `propagation-store.ts` also normalizes against: a second spelling would be a status the app writes and the MCP silently reports nothing for.
- `renderer/lib/run-filters.ts` — pure filter predicate + option helpers for the Stats run table (`runMatchesFilters`, `filtersActive`, `testFilterOptions`, `runBrowserOf`, `NO_FILTERS`). Deliberately kept out of `stats-view.tsx` so `check:run-filters` can exercise it without React or the design system. Subtlety it encodes: **baseline-update rows** carry an incidental `status` of "passed", so they are reachable ONLY via status `baseline` and are excluded by every tag filter.
- `renderer/lib/run-derived-cache.ts` — the six query caches a finished run invalidates (`RUN_DERIVED_KEYS`, `invalidateRunDerived`), and the single place that knows the list. **`RecorderProvider` is the only subscriber that may call it** — an invalidation inside a route component only runs while that route is mounted, which is how the Stats board's Stability, Auto-Heal and Visual tiles came to never refresh after a run (`["flake"]` was invalidated by nothing in the app; `["heals"]` only by the user's own accept/revert, although Auto-Heal journals a heal mid-run; `["replays"]` only from inside `VisualView`). Stats' Manage menu calls the same helper, because Reset/Delete rewrites run history wholesale. `["script-changes"]` is deliberately NOT in the list — no run writes one. Guarded by `check:derived-cache` and `run-derived-cache.test.tsx`, which drives a real push bus and counts `queryFn` calls rather than `invalidateQueries` calls.
- `renderer/lib/browser-icons.tsx` — the one place that decides what Chromium/Firefox/WebKit look like. Two maps, because the app draws browsers on two surfaces that cannot share a renderer: `BROWSER_ICONS` (lucide `Chrome`/`Flame`/`Compass`) for the DOM, and `BROWSER_SF_SYMBOLS` (`globe`/`flame`/`safari`) for the SDK's native `Select`, whose options are drawn by AppKit and never enter the DOM — a React node passed as a `SelectItem` child icon there is silently dropped. `<BrowserIcon browser labelled>` renders the DOM glyph with `role="img"` + `aria-label` + `<title>` by default (icon-only usages need both the accessible name and the hover), and `labelled={false}` where the same row already names the engine — the Stats tag badge, whose row has a Browser column, where a labelled icon would have a screen reader announce "Firefox Firefox". Every instance carries `data-browser`, which is the only handle a test has on an unlabelled glyph. Consumers: the Stats run table + tag filter/badge — that is all. **The three browser `Select`s take only `BROWSER_SF_SYMBOLS`** — `SelectValue` renders the selected item's `icon` on the trigger itself, so a `BrowserIcon` beside it draws the same engine twice (it did, in all three, until 2026-08-07). Guarded by `browser-icons.test.tsx`, which pins that both maps are TOTAL over `RUN_BROWSERS` and that no two engines share a glyph — a missing entry renders `undefined` as a component and throws at runtime, in whichever view happened to show that engine first.
- `renderer/main/batch-view.tsx` — the Batch view (route `/batch`). Test checklist (**defaults to none** — every row's ticked/engines/headless state is persisted in `RecorderSettings.batchTestOptions`, and a test with no stored entry resolves from its own `runBrowser` and the global defaults, so a newly recorded test arrives unticked rather than joining the next "Run all" unasked). Each row carries three icon-only engine toggles (multi-select — N engines means N runs) and its own headed/headless toggle; the toolbar keeps capture, concurrency, and a **master** Headless checkbox that overwrites every row. The batch-wide browser `Select` was removed when engines moved into the rows. Live per-test status rows aggregate every result for that test (worst-first), and an end-of-run summary card closes it out. Subscribes to `batch:progress`/`batch:done`, and calls `api.batch.status()` on mount so a batch started before navigating here is picked up mid-flight.
- `renderer/main/tag-cluster.tsx` — the Batch view's tag chips, extracted from `batch-view.tsx` when they gained a delete affordance. `TagCluster` renders All / one chip per tag / Untagged and owns library-wide tag deletion; `TagChip` is a `<span>` wrapping the filter button and an optional trailing slot, because a button inside a button is invalid markup that swallows the inner click. The X is the `AlertDialog`'s own trigger, so there's no open-state to keep in sync. Uses the real design-system utilities (`border-secondary`, `bg-well`, `bg-support-red-10`, `rounded-pill`) rather than the `*-token-*` names used elsewhere in the renderer, which are not utilities the design system defines and generate no CSS.
- `main/services/test-store.ts` — JSON persistence (`userData/recorder/tests.json`) + spec files (`userData/recorder/scripts/<id>.spec.ts`). `removeTag(tag)` strips a tag from every record carrying it — case-insensitively, hidden tests included, in ONE `writeAll` so the deletion can't land on half the library — and returns how many changed. `allNames()` returns every test's name **including hidden ones**, for duplicate-name numbering — `list()` is the wrong input there, since a name taken by a hidden test would be handed out as free.
- `main/services/duplicate-test.ts` — copying a test. `DUPLICATED_FIELDS` / `DROPPED_FIELDS` classify every `TestRecord` field (enforced by `check:duplicate-test`); `inheritedFields` builds the copy's inherited half from the allowlist and deep-clones it (exported so the omissions and the cloning can be asserted — both are invisible through `duplicateTest`, because records are re-read from JSON on every store access); `nextDuplicateName` / `baseNameOf` do the `<base> [n]` numbering; `copyImportedSandbox` mirrors an imported test's directory, skipping anything that isn't a regular file so a symlink is never read through, and asserting containment on both roots and every destination. Secrets are copied by the HANDLER, not here — they're async and every write has to be followed by `refreshSecretSnapshot()`.
- `renderer/lib/duplicate-warnings.ts` — pure. `describeDuplicationWarnings(test, storedSecrets)` returns what to say before duplicating, and returning `[]` is what makes the sidebar duplicate silently; `NOT_COPIED` is the "what a copy never inherits" list the dialog prints.
- `main/services/dataset-csv.ts` — CSV → dataset rows, behind Variables → Datasets → "Import CSV…". The pure half (`parseCsv`, `datasetsFromCsv`) is a real RFC 4180 parser (quoted cells, embedded delimiters/newlines, `""` escapes, BOM, CRLF) with the delimiter sniffed from the header line among comma/semicolon/tab — semicolon is what Excel writes in half the world's locales. Header cells name variables; each data row becomes one `Dataset` named "Row N" continuing the record's numbering. Two honesty rules carry it: columns are refused OUT LOUD with a reason (invalid name, duplicate, or naming a SECRET variable — a row's values travel plaintext in GLAZE_VARS, and the generated header already refuses to let them shadow a secret, so the plaintext would be stored and then ignored), and nothing truncates silently (ragged rows are padded/trimmed but counted; rows past `MAX_DATASETS_PER_TEST` are dropped but flagged). Kept columns the test doesn't declare become plain variables with empty defaults, so imported values are live rather than inert. `importDatasetCsvFile` is the file half (dialog + 5MB cap + fs), exposed as `tests:importDatasetCsv`; the merged list still passes through `normalizeDatasets` — the boundary does not trust this module. Values never reach generated source: a dataset row rides the GLAZE_VARS env JSON at run time. Covered by `dataset-csv.test.ts` (dialect battery + mapping rules, secret-refusal and BOM-strip revert-verified) and the import toasts in `variables-panel.test.tsx`.
- `shared/glaze-runtime-source.mjs` — the spec runtime helper module written next to the specs (`glaze-runtime.mjs`): one awaited line per step is the rule every helper exists for. `glazeCapture` (read a value into V), `glazeScrollTo` (incremental position scroll), **`glazeEmailCode` (2026-08-26)** — the `emailCode` step: poll the configured mailbox for the one-time code a site emailed, apply the shared watermark and code scan (both embedded via `toString()`), and write it into `V`. Through `page.request.fetch`, so it needs no dependency the CLI might lack and honours the run's proxy — a bare fetch would bypass it, which on a proxy-only runner is the difference between a sign-in and an unexplained timeout. `glazeRunStartedAt` and `glazeLastCode` are MODULE scope, which is what makes the watermark mean anything. A rejected token throws immediately rather than waiting out the budget, and the timeout names the address and the rule. And **`glazeA11yGate` (2026-08-19)** — the `a11y` GATE step: inject axe if the per-step capture toggle hasn't (GLAZE_AXE_PATH, else a local axe-core install, else a clear error naming the fix; page.evaluate, so a page CSP can't block it), run it, and THROW on new violations at/above the step's impact floor. The decision logic is `gateFailures` from `shared/a11y-rollup.mjs` embedded via toString() — the step-semantics idiom — so the gate and the app's baseline bookkeeping share one key spelling; accepted keys arrive as GLAZE_A11Y_BASELINE env (flattened from the record's `a11yBaseline` by the runner), never baked into source, so an accept takes effect without regenerating. Outside the app the env is absent and the gate is strictest, the safe direction. The impact floor is enum-guarded at the ingest boundary AND at emission. Covered by `a11y-emission.test.ts` (emission, forged-impact fallback revert-verified, round-trips incl. disabled/continue-on-failure, the gateFailures battery, the keysOf parity pin, the runtime-embedding assertion), and by `check:runtime-boot` for the question none of those can ask. **The local-install branch must never spell `import.meta` (2026-08-21)** — it resolves axe through `await import("axe-core")` and its `.source` string, not `createRequire(import.meta.url)`. Playwright's Babel transform converts a file containing `import.meta` to CommonJS while Node loads `.mjs` as ESM, so the emitted `exports` threw at module scope and took EVERY importer of this file down with it — api, dialog, a11y, aiCheck, totp, echo, scroll and capture steps all reported "No tests found". See DECISIONS 2026-08-21.
- **Generated variables (2026-08-19)** — a fourth VariableKind, `generated`: the record stores `genSpec` (string/email/number/uuid/name) and never a value; the V header calls `glazeGenerate("<spec>", "<name>")` (in `glaze-runtime-source.ts`) BEFORE the GLAZE_VARS spread, so a dataset row can pin the value while secrets stay unshadowable after it. The helper logs each choice to stdout, so the run log answers "which email did this run use?". normalizeVariables strips a generated variable's value (fresh-per-run is the contract); the trainer session holds one sample (recorder-service `sampleGeneratedValue`) so replay shows something real, stripped again on fold-back. genSpec is enum-guarded at the boundary AND at emission. Surfaces: the Variables tab's `Generated` group (its generator picker; the kind itself is chosen on the Add menu as of 2026-09-01) and the trainer's New-variable form (Generated segment). Covered by `generated-variables.test.ts` (header order, forged-spec fallback revert-verified, boundary strip/default, the emitted runtime imported as real JS with per-spec shape checks) and rows in `variables-panel.test.tsx` / `variable-picker.test.tsx`.
- **Which fields interpolate `${name}` — one traversal (2026-08-20)** — `mapInterpolatable(step, fn)` in `main/recorder/types.ts` is the single definition, and it both READS and WRITES on purpose. `interpolatableFields` is derived from it (a scan), `collectVarRefs` scans through it (deriving `varRefs` at `testStore.save`, which is what the Variables tab counts usage from), `resolveStepForReplay` substitutes through it, and `bindFlowStep` in `script-generator.ts` binds a flow call's arguments through it. The shape before this was a `string[]`-returning `interpolatableFields` whose comment asked every writer to keep its own copy of the list in sync — a rule the types could not enforce, and it drifted the moment `api` steps landed: the generator interpolated `apiBody` and the VALUES of `apiHeaders`, and none of the other three sites knew. Two silent failures came out of it, both landing far from the edit — a token referenced only by an `Authorization` header counted as unused, so the panel offered to delete a variable the run still needed and the request then sent the literal text `Bearer ${token}`; and a flow parameter never reached an API step in the flow's body, so a parameterised login flow posted `${password}` verbatim. Header NAMES are deliberately NOT interpolated (the token grammar is re-checked at emission, so a reference there could only produce a header the generator drops). Pinned by `check:variables` §8b, which asserts BOTH halves per field — that the generator interpolates it AND that the traversal sees it — so the pin cannot pass vacuously if the generator stops interpolating. A `${var}` inside a REGEX-backed assertion is handled separately, and no longer stays literal — see **Variables inside regex-backed assertions** below.
- **Variables inside regex-backed assertions (2026-08-20)** — `${var}` now resolves in the seven kinds that embed their value in a `RegExp` (the four URL asserts, `titleContains`, `css` with `contains`, and the two page-level waits). Before this the reference was regex-escaped as literal text, so the RUN could never pass while the TRAINER — which resolves variables before replaying — showed the step green. `shared/step-semantics.mjs` owns both directions: `regexPatternExpr` emits a template whose static chunks are `reEscape`d at generation time and whose references become `glazeReEscape(V.name)`, escaped at RUN time (raw interpolation would let a value holding `a.b` match `aXb`); `regexPatternFromTemplate` is its inverse, read by `spec-parser.ts`. **`urlPathIs` is the exception**: its slash rules read the ENDS of the path and a reference is opaque until the run supplies it, so the whole pattern is built at run time by `glazeUrlPathPattern` — the shared `urlPathPattern`, the same function the replayer calls, which makes the two agree by construction. Both helpers are embedded in `glaze-runtime-source.ts` by `toString()`, never retyped. **Which helper is imported is asked of the emitted BODY**, not predicted from the steps — so the preamble is now built after the body, and `record1` records a body-relative line resolved to an absolute one at the end. Without a declared reference every kind emits exactly what it did before, byte for byte. `describe-step.ts` deliberately takes no variables (the step list shows what the user typed). Covered by `regex-assert-variables.test.ts` (emission, the emitted runtime imported as real JS, round-trip, and the no-variables regression) and by variable-bearing rows in `e2e/assert-parity.spec.ts`, whose harness now carries `vars` and resolves them for the replayer exactly as `runStep` does — it caught a real bug in the first `urlPathIs` implementation.
- **Teardown steps (2026-08-20)** — a `teardown` DIVIDER step: every step below it runs even when a step above it failed. A divider rather than a pair because the block is the whole rest of the test (see DECISIONS 2026-08-20). `script-generator.ts` computes `teardownSplit` in a pre-pass — it decides the base indent of the WHOLE body, hence `baseDepth`, which every block-closing `Math.max` floors at instead of a hard-coded 1. Emission is an error LATCH, not `try/finally`: `glTeardownError` keeps the FIRST error and is rethrown at the end, because an error thrown inside `finally` REPLACES the body's and would make the feature a way of losing failures. Tested `=== undefined`, since a thrown value can be falsy. Refused (as a comment naming the reason, never a broken spec) when nested inside an `if`/`loop` or when a second divider appears; the refusal comment is preserved through `stripComments` so it reads back as the row the user placed and regeneration refuses it again. The parser's three matchers sit ABOVE the continue-on-failure `try {` matcher and the bare-`}` closer — either would otherwise mis-read the latch — and are anchored on the `glTeardownError` identifier, not on the banner comment, which the strip removes. Trainer preview says out loud that it only replays in order (same honesty rule as download/a11y). Surfaces: the Add-step menus in `recording-view.tsx` and `trainer-panel-view.tsx`, the composer's `teardown` case, no replay button and no continue-on-failure toggle on the row. Covered by `teardown-emission.test.ts`, which EXECUTES the emitted body against a fake page — the naive `finally` passes every source-shape assertion and fails only there.
- **Input fidelity: typing mode, per-step timeouts, the reload step (2026-08-21)** — three features sharing one mechanism, the trailing options object the generator builds with `optsExpr`/`timeoutParts`/`callArgs` in `script-generator.ts` (FIXED key order — `force`, `delay`, `timeout` — because the parser reads it back key by key, and an order varying with which fields a step carries would make the same step regenerate differently between saves). **`Step.typeMode`** picks how a `fill` delivers its value: `"fill"` stays the default and every test on disk regenerates byte-identically, `"sequential"` emits `pressSequentially(value, { delay })` — a real keydown/keypress/input/keyup per character, which is the only way an autocomplete, combobox or masked field sees the typing at all. Emitted ON THE SPEC LINE rather than through a runtime helper, and that is not style: `step-reporter-source.ts` drops any action located outside the spec file, so a fill delivered from inside `glaze-runtime.mjs` would be reported by nothing on a plain run. `pressSequentially` is added to `page-actions.ts`'s LOCATOR_ACTIONS so the capture and settle fixtures wrap it. It does NOT clear the field (clearing would cost a second Playwright action, and the capture fixture keys screenshots by ACTION ORDER), so the capture script only chooses it on its own when the field was EMPTY at focus — where the two modes are exactly equivalent — and the step row, the composer and both `describeStep` copies say "appends" out loud. **`Step.timeoutMs` widened** from `waitUntil`-only to every action and assertion; before it, "this button takes twelve seconds" could only be said with a separate wait step, which asserts something the user did not mean and fails as its own step. `page.keyboard.press` is deliberately excluded — it has no such option. **`reload`** is a new StepType (`page.reload()`), handled in `runStep` like `viewport`/`cookie` because the injected replayer runs inside the page it would be reloading; auto-recorded from `before-input-event` on the page webContents (⌘R/Ctrl+R/F5) rather than from `performance.navigation`, which would also record the reloads a SITE does to itself and make the run reload twice. The parser reads all three back and REFUSES an options object it does not model (`parseTrailingOptions` returns null → the statement is unclassified → `stepsDiverged`), because an imported `toBeVisible({ visible: false })` read leniently would come back as its own opposite. Covered by `input-fidelity.test.ts` (emission, round-trip, forged-field guards), `main/recorder/typing-mode.dom.test.ts` (the real capture script's detection and the real replayer's per-character events, both revert-verified), `check:step-ingest` §4d, rows in `describe-step-parity`, and `e2e/input-fidelity.spec.ts` — which runs the emitted lines against a real autocomplete and is the only place "an event per character" can be proved.
- **Variable assertions, variable conditions and the echo step (2026-08-21)** — the half of the variable system that was missing. `capture` steps wrote variables, `api` steps extracted JSON fields into them, datasets swept them and flows bound them, and nothing could ever CHECK one. `AssertKind: "variable"` and `ConditionKind: "variable"` both read `Step.captureVar` (which variable), `Step.compareOp` (how) and `Step.value` (what, interpolatable — so one variable can be compared against another). The vocabulary is `COMPARE_OPS` in **`shared/step-semantics.mjs`** — thirteen operators with `compareValues` as the ONE implementation, embedded into the emitted runtime as `glazeCompare` by `toString()` and called directly by the trainer. Semantics are **RAW** (case-sensitive, no whitespace normalization) and that is a reading rather than a preference: `toBe`/`toContain`/`toMatch` do not normalize, so kinder rules here would be a rule the generated spec does not implement. **The assertion emits REAL MATCHERS on the spec line**, not `expect(helper()).toBe(true)` — Playwright reports an `expect` step located at the spec line (verified for generic matchers, so the run highlights it) and the matcher prints both values, where a wrapped boolean prints "expected false to be true"; `expect(value, message)` makes the variable's name the step's title. starts/ends-with compile to an ANCHORED `regexPatternExpr` pattern because Playwright has no matcher for them; the numeric four wrap BOTH sides in `Number()`, and a non-number becomes NaN, which every one of them reports as a failure naming the value. **`waitUntil` deliberately has no `variable` member**: a wait polls for something another agent changes, and nothing changes a variable while a step waits on it. `echo` writes one line to stdout via `glazeEcho` and can never fail. The trainer's half is **`main/services/variable-step.ts`** — pure, so every branch is exercisable without a window (the `recorder-navigation.ts` argument), and it never injects: comparing two strings the session already holds has nothing page-side about it, and injecting would hand an untrusted page the value. It refuses out loud for a secret and for a not-yet-captured variable, and a CONDITION it cannot evaluate FAILS rather than reporting `met: false` — which would skip the block and read as "the condition did not hold". Covered by `variable-assertions.test.ts` (semantics battery, emission, forged-operator and forged-name guards, round-trip of all thirteen to a fixed point), `variable-step.test.ts` (the trainer's refusals, revert-verified), rows in `describe-step-parity`, and `e2e/assert-parity.spec.ts`, whose variable block compares real Playwright's verdict against `compareValues` for every operator plus the traps (`"10" > "9"`, an unescaped dot, `"".slice(-0)`, case, whitespace) with a coverage guard derived from COMPARE_OPS.
- **Double-click, right-click and drag (2026-08-21)** — the three interactions mabl records that this recorder did not. `dblclick` emits `.dblclick()`; `rightclick` emits `.click({ button: "right" })` — not a method of its own, so the OPTIONS OBJECT is what tells it from an ordinary click in both directions, and `button` is the one option key whose value is a string (only `"right"`, the one the generator writes, reads back). **`drag` is the only step pointing at TWO elements**: `Step.toLocator` alongside `Step.locator`, emitted as `source.dragTo(target)` with both quoted through the same `locatorExpr`. It stays ONE step rather than mabl's hold/release pair because a step emits one awaited statement. Every reader of `Step.locator` was asked whether it meant "the target" or "every target": the generator emits both, the replayer resolves both (a drag that resolved only its source would report success for half a step), and **Auto-Heal heals the SOURCE and never the target** — the map is keyed by locator and probes with the step's FINGERPRINT, which is the source element's, so the target's key is simply absent and the fixture attempts nothing. That falls out with no code and is pinned rather than left to be discovered; the step row says "Refine Selection (drag source)" so nobody believes they refined "the element". **Capture:** `dblclick` and `drag` are captured from the page — drag from the POINTER sequence (not HTML5 drag events, which a pointer-driven app never fires) with mabl's own thresholds, ≥500ms and ≥20px, which is what stops an ordinary click being recorded as a degenerate drag onto itself, and the drop target read from `elementFromPoint` at release rather than `e.target` (a dragged element follows the pointer and would otherwise be its own target). A browser fires two clicks BEFORE `dblclick` and both have already left the page — holding one back is exactly what must not happen — so they are withdrawn at the ingest by **`main/services/dblclick-supersede.ts`** (pure, the `recorder-navigation.ts` argument), which matches on locator identity *including* `nth` (`healKeyFor` drops it so an indexed step can heal; here it is what tells row 1 from row 2) and removes from the INSERTION POINT rather than the list end. **A right-click cannot be captured from the page at all** — that gesture already opens the trainer's own tools menu — so the action lives IN that menu as "Record a right-click here", which inserts the step directly; mabl solves the same conflict with a Trainer preference. Covered by `recorded-actions.test.ts` (emission, both-ends round-trip, the heal-map consequence, the refused `button: "middle"`), `dblclick-supersede.test.ts`, `main/recorder/recorded-actions.dom.test.ts` (thresholds revert-verified), real-gesture tests in `e2e/retrain-capture.spec.ts`, and `e2e/recorded-actions.spec.ts` — whose fixture counts each interaction separately, so a single-click control stays green while the double-click claim goes red without the fix.
- **`main/recorder/capture-harness.ts` (2026-08-21)** — the page-with-the-real-capture-script-installed harness, extracted from `capture-egress.dom.test.ts` when `typing-mode.dom.test.ts` needed the same thing. Not named `*.test.ts`, so neither Vitest project collects it. An iframe per test, because the script's window/document listeners are not removed by resetting `body.innerHTML` and would otherwise accumulate across tests in a file.
- `main/services/upload-store.ts` — staging for `upload` steps: "Choose file…" copies the pick into scripts/uploads/<testId>/ (basename flattened to a safe alphabet, id flattened stricter — no dots — so the result always passes the emission guard; 25MB cap; dedupe by suffix), and the step stores the scripts-relative path. `isSafeUploadRelPath` lives in `main/recorder/types.ts` (pure) and is enforced at EMISSION and at PARSE — the boundary passes the value through as an ordinary string, deliberately, so the pair is pinned in `check:step-ingest` against being "simplified" away. Test deletion removes the uploads dir (inline in test-store's remove, bounded by the scripts root — upload-store imports test-store, so calling back the other way would be a cycle). Session-scoped staging IPC (`recorder:stageUpload`); preview stage is canned. Covered by `upload-store.test.ts` (hostile basename/id stay emittable, dedupe, cancel) and `upload-emission.test.ts` (guard battery, forged-path refusal revert-verified, round-trips incl. foreign-call skip).
- **API request steps (2026-08-19)** — `api` step type: one HTTP request through Playwright's request context, emitted as ONE awaited `glazeApiRequest(page, V, {...})` line (helper in `glaze-runtime-source.ts`) with a fixed key order the parser reads back key by key (`parseApiOptions` + `scanOptionValue`; any unknown key makes the whole call skipped). No expectStatus still FAILS on 4xx/5xx — silence would hide the point. Header values refuse CR/LF at boundary AND emission (request-splitting); method/status/capture-name/capture-path all double-guarded; url/body/header values go through valueExpr so ${variable} references (secrets included) work. captureVar stores the response (capturePath walks the parsed JSON — data, never evaluated) into V. Composer kind "API request" (method Select, URL, headers-as-lines, body, expected status, capture var+path — a malformed piece refuses the WHOLE submit). Preview narrates. Covered by `api-emission.test.ts`: emission/interpolation/forged-field guards (method revert-verified), boundary rows, round-trips + fixed point + foreign-key skip, and the RUNTIME imported as real JS driven against a fake page (status semantics, captures, JSON errors).
- `shared/totp.mjs` — RFC 6238, ONE implementation for the two code-deriving sides: the generated runtime (glaze-runtime.mjs embeds `totpCode` via toString and wraps node's createHmac) and the trainer's replay (`deriveTotpCode` in recorder-service). Pure per the shared/ rules, so the HMAC comes IN as a bytes-in/bytes-out function. `totp: true` on a SECRET variable makes the V header emit a getter — fresh code at every read, sitting after the GLAZE_VARS spread so a dataset row can't shadow it; the setup key itself lives in the encrypted store like any secret. Pinned against RFC 6238 Appendix B vectors (`totp.test.ts`, incl. the 64-bit counter), with emission/resolver/runtime rows in `totp-emission.test.ts` (resolver branch revert-verified; the embedded copy is asserted verbatim and executed).
- `main/services/session-state-store.ts` — saved login sessions (userData/recorder/sessions/<id>.json): a `saveSession` test's PASSING run writes storageState there (capture fixture teardown, both paths, gated on testInfo.status; env GLAZE_SAVE_STATE), and a `useSessionFrom` test's runs start from it (config template's `storageState: GLAZE_STORAGE_STATE || undefined`). Freshness cap 24h — stale is refused OUT LOUD in run output, never used quietly, and nothing auto-runs the source test on a miss. Paths bounded against hostile ids (uploads-dir drill); deleting a test clears its state. Plaintext deliberately — parity with run artifacts (network.json already holds the same cookies), not the secrets store. UI: Variables tab's "Login session" section (save Switch, source Select over session-saving tests, fresh/stale/never status, Clear). Covered by `login-session.test.ts` (store rules with freshness revert-verified + source pins for the fixture/config/runner wiring a unit test cannot reach).
- **Configurable test-id attributes (2026-08-20)** — Settings → Recording's "Extra test-id attributes" (`RecorderSettings.extraTestIdAttributes`, normalized by `normalizeTestIdAttributes` in `shared/testid-attr.mjs`): the capture and count scripts get the probe list injected as JSON (`TID_ATTRS`, reassigned over the helpers' baked-in default), and `testIdOverride` widened from the fixed pair to the shared lowercase-data-* GRAMMAR — one rule for ingest, emission, heal key, the parser's inverse, and the injected oracle (embedded via toString; the dom test pins the embedded copy against `isTestIdAttributeName`). The DEFAULT stays immutable: absent `attr` means data-testid forever, so existing locators and heal keys never re-spell. Covered by `main/recorder/testid-attributes.dom.test.ts` — grammar battery (revert-verified), agreement pin, round-trip with `data-cy`, and the REAL injected helpers evaluated in jsdom (probe order, default-wins, the recorded-attribute-only oracle rule, forged-extras never reaching a script).
- `main/services/ai-check.ts` — post-run evaluation for `aiCheck` steps (AI visual checks): pairs each `glazeAiCheck` screenshot with its claim by the generator's own numbering rule (non-disabled checks 1..N in expanded order — two counters over one list, pinned against each other), asks `visionVerdict` in llm-service (one-shot vision per provider: Anthropic image blocks, Ollama native `images`, LM Studio data-URI), and records pass/fail/unevaluated per check — unevaluated carries the provider's own sentence, with a two-consecutive-failures circuit breaker. Results: ai-checks.json in the run's artifact dir, counts on the RunRecord, lines in the output panel, an "AI checks" row on the run summary strip. THE RUN'S VERDICT IS SEALED — evaluation happens after exit and never touches runStatus (see DECISIONS for why in-spec assertion was rejected). Covered by `ai-check-emission.test.ts` (numbering incl. disabled-skip revert-verified, q()'d claims, reparseable disabled markers, round-trips, evaluator behaviors against a mocked verdict).
- **Step groups (2026-08-20)** — `group`(label)/`endGroup` pair: named sections in the step list, emitted as `// ── group: <name> ──` / `// ── end group ──` comment markers (commentSafe; a hostile label stays inside the comment), NOTHING executes and generated indentation is untouched — nesting is the list's rendering. The parser's comment stripper preserves exactly these markers (alongside the disabled/wait-until ones) and they are the only comments that round-trip into steps; ordinary comments are still eaten (pinned). Composer kind "Group steps" inserts the pair; group rows rename inline (`label` joined the editable-field keys); halves excluded from disable/continue/lone-replay like the other block halves. Covered by `group-emission.test.ts` (markers-can't-break-spec, stray-half-is-fine, preprocessor survival revert-verified, separator-in-label, nesting, fixed point) plus depth/parity/replayer/composer rows.
- **Dialog steps (2026-08-20)** — `dialog` step type: arm the NEXT JS dialog (accept + optional variable-aware prompt text, or dismiss), emitted as `await glazeArmDialog(page, "<action>"[, text])` — action allowlisted at boundary AND emission, one-shot `page.once` in the runtime (armed before the trigger, the download-arming argument; a lost race is swallowed, driven in the runtime test against a fake page). Composer kind "Handle next dialog" (Accept/Dismiss segments, prompt field only for accept); preview narrates ("answer the dialog yourself"). Recording stays manual — auto-capture needs a page-world wrap of confirm/prompt, deliberately a separate design. Covered by `dialog-emission.test.ts` (forms, ${var} prompts, forged-action revert-verified, round-trips incl. disabled/wrapped, the one-shot/race behaviors).
- `main/services/import-service.ts` — Imports existing Playwright tests. `importFromFiles()` opens a native directory picker (`dialog.showOpenDialog`), scans for `*.spec.ts`/`*.test.ts` (skips node_modules/.git/etc, depth+file caps). `importFromGit(url, ref?)` shallow-clones (`git clone --depth 1 [--branch <ref>] -- <url>` via `execFile`, validates URL scheme, ENOENT→friendly "git not installed") to an `os.tmpdir()` temp dir, scans, cleans up. **`ref` is validated, not escaped** — `execFile` spawns no shell, so the risk is not injection but a ref like `--upload-pack=…` that git honours as an OPTION; it goes through `branchNameProblem` from `shared/branch-paths.mjs` (the module that already spells this rule) and `--` separates the arguments from the URL, both belts because only one can be forgotten. **`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`** is set on the clone: a private HTTPS repo otherwise sits on git's invisible credential prompt for the whole 120s timeout and then reports as a network problem; it now fails in seconds with an authentication message that says this app clones anonymously or with ambient credentials. It refuses to ASK, not to use. Each file → a `TestRecord` (file content is the script, `steps` parsed via `spec-parser.ts`, `scriptEdited: true`, name from first `test("…")` or filename, url from first `page.goto("…")`, `sourceDir` = the spec's original folder). `copyRelativeImports()` copies the spec's `./`/`../` sibling modules (recursively, ext/index probing, 5MB cap) into the scripts dir at import time so the spec runs standalone; `repairImports(id)` re-copies from `sourceDir` (exposed as `tests:repairImports`). `backfillBaseUrl(id)` re-reads the source project's config for a record that has no `baseUrl` — called by the runner at the moment it would otherwise refuse a run, which is what makes tests imported before this existed work without being edited one by one; it never replaces a value already on the record. **The spec is not the whole test.** Each import also reads the source project's own `playwright.config` through `imported-config.ts` (cached per spec dir) and carries two fields off it: `baseUrl`, without which the suite cannot run at all, and `testTimeoutMs`, without which a suite written against a 120s limit fails at this app's default for reasons unrelated to the test. `url` is now the first navigation resolved to an ABSOLUTE address (it used to be whatever `.goto("…")` matched — `""` for every suite that wraps navigation in a helper, and `"/"` for one that doesn't). `ImportResult` gained `needsBaseUrl` (tests that navigate relatively with nothing to resolve against) and `unsupported` (config features this app does not reproduce), both surfaced as toasts by `renderer/lib/import-warnings.ts` from BOTH import entry points. **`renderer/lib/import-from-files.ts`** is the folder import's whole ACTION — picker, toasts, navigation — extracted from `library-sidebar.tsx`, which is why that importer was reachable from the rail's `+` and nowhere else; Home and the ⌘K palette now call the same function, and it takes `invalidateTests`/`goToTest` as arguments rather than calling hooks so an event handler can use it.
- `main/services/imported-config.ts` — what an imported spec inherited from its own project and cannot run without. `readProjectConfig(specDir, scanRoot)` finds the nearest `playwright.config.{ts,js,mts,mjs,cts,cjs}` by walking UP from the spec — through the scan root and up to 3 levels beyond it, stopping after a directory holding `package.json`/`.git` — because the folder a user picks is routinely `tests/` while the config sits beside `package.json` one level up. Both paths are realpath'd before comparison (the `/var` → `/private/var` trap that `copyRelativeImports` documents). **The config is read as TEXT and never evaluated**: `import()`ing a stranger's config would execute it at IMPORT time, before a single test has run, which is exactly when the user believes they are only looking. So `extractBaseUrl` lifts string literals only (`baseURL: "…"`, and the `process.env.X || "…"` / `?? "…"` fallback most checked-in configs use), `extractTestTimeoutMs` reads a top-level numeric `timeout`, and `findUnsupported` names features this app does not reproduce (`webServer`, `globalSetup`, `globalTeardown`, `storageState`). A computed base URL yields nothing here — answered by the user typing one in, not by a cleverer parser. `normalizeBaseUrl` is the ONE gate every base URL passes, parsed or typed, and admits only http(s). `navigationTargets`/`usesRelativeNavigation`/`firstNavigationUrl` share one definition of what counts as a navigation, matching any `goto`-ish call rather than `page.goto(` alone — a real suite wraps navigation in a helper, so the narrow pattern reports a suite of entirely relative navigations as having none. `treeNeedsBaseUrl(spec, sandbox)` asks the whole sandbox for the same reason, and `shouldRefuseForMissingBaseUrl` is the runner's pre-flight refusal.
- `main/services/spec-parser.ts` — `parseSpec(source) → Step[]`: best-effort reverse of `script-generator.ts`, now round-tripping its **full** vocabulary (not just a subset): `page.goto`/`page.waitForTimeout`/`page.setViewportSize`/`page.keyboard.press`, `<locator>.click|.fill|.selectOption|.check|.uncheck|.press|.waitFor` with `getByTestId/getByRole/getByLabel/getByPlaceholder/getByText/locator(...)` locators, and `expect(...)`/`expect.soft(...)` for all 18 assert kinds (incl. page-level `toHaveURL`/`toHaveTitle`, `.not.toBeChecked`, and `toHaveCSS` — whose second argument selects the match mode, with the `new RegExp(...)` arm checked FIRST so a `contains` assert isn't downgraded to an `is` against a regex source). **The page-level kinds are told apart by their ANCHORS** (`^…$` → `urlIs`/`title`, `…$` → `urlEndsWith`, bare → `url`/`titleContains`) — except `urlPathIs`, whose pattern is STRUCTURAL (`urlPathPattern`'s literal prefix and suffix) and is checked BEFORE the anchor classification, because it starts with `^` and ends with an alternation containing `$`, so the anchor rule would file it as `urlIs` carrying the raw pattern as its value and the next regeneration would escape the escapes; an empty middle reads back as `"/"`, since the pattern spells the site root as nothing, and a bare STRING argument now reads back as the exact kind (`urlIs`/`title`) rather than as `url` — reading it as `url` was the parser agreeing with the generator's old bug, which is why the round trip stayed green over an assertion that could not pass. **`reUnescape` is applied on every regex path, not only the conditional-wait one**: the step stores the user's literal substring and the generator escapes it, so reading a pattern back without undoing that escape stored `example\.com`, and the next generation escaped the backslash too — `urlEndsWith`/`urlIs` had been drifting one escape further from the user's value on every hand edit and every applied AI fix since they were written. `.hover()`/`.focus()` parse to `state` steps (they used to count as skips, permanently flagging any imported test that hovered) and `page.mouse.down()`/`.up()` to `press`/`release`. `parseSpecDetailed(source) → {steps, skipped}` (which `parseSpec` wraps) also counts statements that look like actions/assertions but can't be classified — used by the `tests:updateScript` handler to set `TestRecord.stepsDiverged` instead of silently undercounting. Also round-trips conditional blocks: `parseCondition(raw)` reverses `conditionExpr` (element + page conditions, incl. negated `unchecked` and count-based `exists`), a recognized `if (…) {` becomes an `if` step (`ifDepth` counter) and its matching `}` an `endif`; an unrecognized/foreign `if` skips the whole `{…}` block (via `matchBrace`) so no half-parsed `if` is emitted without its `endif`. Conditional waits round-trip too: `stripComments` PRESERVES the generator's trailing `// wait until` marker (the second such exception, alongside `// disabled — skipped:`), and a marked `expect` becomes a `wait` step instead of an `assert` — without it every conditional wait would silently come back as an assertion, since the two compile to the same call. `.waitFor({ state })` now carries its state back as a `waitUntil` (a wait-for-hidden used to parse as a bare wait and regenerate as wait-for-VISIBLE); `state: "detached"` has no counterpart in the model and is counted as unclassified rather than inverted. **Also reads the shapes a MODEL writes, which the generator never emits:** a locator bound to a `const` and used later (`const submit = page.getByRole(…)` … `await submit.click()`) resolves through a per-body variable map, and `expect(submit)` resolves the same way — both halves used to disappear, the declaration as a skip and the *use* as nothing at all, which is how a prompt-generated spec arrived with its `goto` and no actions. A REFINED chain (`.first()`, `.filter()`, `.or()`) is deliberately left unregistered and counted, because storing the base locator would regenerate a selector matching the wrong element; likewise `expect(<jsValue>)` has no step and is now counted rather than ignored. `extractTestBodies` skips `test.step` alongside `test.describe`/`test.beforeEach`: its body runs steps, but it sits INSIDE an already-extracted `test(...)` body that the scan walks through anyway, so matching it emitted every statement twice. `locatorActionStep()` is the single place a `<locator>.<action>(…)` becomes a step, shared by the inline and variable branches so a method added to one can't be missing from the other. Regression coverage: `main/services/__tests__/spec-parser.check.ts` (sections 5–6 cover if/endif, 7–9 cover conditional waits, 18–20 cover `test.step`, locator variables, and what is counted rather than swallowed). **`.nth(k)` is part of the LOCATOR here, not a refinement.** The generator emits it and this parser had no case for it, so `page.getByText("Save").nth(1).click()` matched no action shape and the WHOLE STEP was dropped on every re-parse — which is every hand edit and every applied AI fix, for exactly the steps that needed an index. Deliberately the opposite decision from `.first()`/`.filter()`/`.or()`, which stay counted-as-unclassified because the model has no field for them; `nth` has one with the same meaning. Section 21 of `spec-parser.check.ts` pins all four step shapes plus byte-identical regeneration. **Sandbox note:** `npm run check:spec-parser` (tsx) fails under the agent sandbox with `listen EPERM` (unix socket); run it via `npx esbuild main/services/__tests__/spec-parser.check.ts --bundle --platform=node --format=esm --outfile=$TMPDIR/check.mjs && node $TMPDIR/check.mjs` instead. **Reads element-context CHAINS back (2026-08-14), which is a precondition of emitting them** — see the `.nth(k)` note above for what happens when the generator writes something this file cannot read. `parseBuilderAt(s, idx)` parses exactly ONE builder call in isolation, because a context-carrying locator contains up to three and `parseRoleOptions` anchors on the first `getByRole(` in whatever string it is handed — passing it the whole expression reads the CONTAINER's role and name onto the target and yields a well-formed step naming the wrong element. `parseLocator` then reads `.filter({hasText})` (held until a chained builder proves there is a container), a chained builder (which makes the first locator a `within`), and repeated `.and(page.…)`. A `.filter()` on the TARGET has no field in the model and is still REFUSED — and the refusal now counts a skip, which it did not while this was reached through the old pre-sliced path. **Three pre-existing bugs the round-trip check surfaced:** the action branch sliced the source at the first builder's close paren and allowed exactly one continuation (`.nth`), so any other chain matched no action shape and the step was dropped — it now asks `parseLocator` how much it consumed; `firstStringLiteral` stopped at the first quote of any kind, so `"a\"b"` came back as `a\` and regeneration wrote a DIFFERENT literal (worse than dropping it: the step still runs and points at nothing); and `locator("xpath=…")` read back as a CSS locator whose selector began with `xpath=`, which round-tripped by luck while every consumer branching on `k` saw the wrong kind. **Exact text (2026-08-22):** `parseBuilderAt` reads `{ exact: true }` off a `getByText` call back onto the locator, so a Script-tab edit or an imported spec keeps the whole-string match rather than loosening it into the substring it was chosen to avoid; pinned by `check:locator-roundtrip` and `check:spec-parser`.
- `main/services/run-history-store.ts` — Log database for completed runs. `append(run, logText)` writes `userData/recorder/logs/<uuid>.log` + appends a `RunRecord` to `userData/recorder/run-history.json` (capped at **50,000** records, oldest pruned). `list()` (newest first), `readLog(id)`, `searchLogs(query)` (case-insensitive across all `.log` files → `{runId,testName,status,startedAt,matchCount,snippet}`, cap 200), `resetStats()` (clear index, KEEP `.log` files), `deleteAll()` (clear index + delete all `.log` files, incl. orphans), `deleteRange(fromMs,toMs)` (remove records + logs started in inclusive range), `logsDirPath()`. **`totals()` → `RunTotals` is the one answer this file cannot derive from its own index**: the index is CAPPED, so its length is the size of a cache and not the size of a history. Pruning counts what it drops into `userData/recorder/run-history-pruned.json` (executions only; baseline updates are events), and `totals()` adds that tally to the retained counts → `{runs, passed, failed, retained, pruned}`. Both prune call sites go through one `pruneToCap` helper so the tally cannot be kept on one path and not the other. A corrupt tally is discarded WHOLE — all three fields or none, and rejected if `passed + failed !== runs` — because a partially-sanitised counter can render three numbers on one row that cannot all be true. `resetStats`/`deleteAll` zero it; `deleteRange` deliberately does not (everything it counts was pruned for being older than every surviving record). **And a per-day breakdown beside it** (`days`, most recent 60 local days), because a lifetime total cannot repair a figure that counts a WINDOW: pruning takes the OLDEST records, so a suite that fits a thousand runs inside a week loses that week's own early days — the weekly digest read "1000 runs this week" on a week that had 1019. `RunTotals.prunedDays` feeds `weeklyDigest` and `buildDailyBuckets`; those count records PLUS pruned days, while offenders and flake stay record-only (both need per-run identity, which a pruned run no longer has). The breakdown is all-or-none on read like the totals, and its `dayStart` is validated as a SIGNED integer — validating a timestamp with the counter's non-negative rule discarded the whole breakdown for any pre-1970 clock. **Records and raw logs are pruned on SEPARATE dials.** They were one: pruning a record deleted its .log with it, so a ~700-byte record was rationed at the rate of the tens of KB beside it, and every count on the Stats screen inherited a ceiling that existed to bound disk. `MAX_RECORDS` is now 50,000 (bounded by what `writeAll` costs per run — measured: 1k ≈ 7 ms, 10k ≈ 55 ms, 50k ≈ 360 ms), and `pruneLogsToBudget` deletes the .log of every run past `runLogRetainedRuns` (a user setting, default 1000, 0–50000) while KEEPING the record: `logFile` is cleared and `logBytes` kept, the same shape `markTestDeleted` produces. **`adoptLifetimeFloor(lifetime)` is the one-time recovery**: the counter can only count prunes that happened while it existed, so on an app already past the old cap it starts life understating the history. `metricsStore.seedRunHistoryFloor()` calls it at startup with `lifetimeRunCounts(db)` — the metrics DB holds a row per run and nothing deletes rows, so it is the only surviving evidence of runs pruned before the counter shipped. It raises a FLOOR (a smaller DB changes nothing) and records `adopted: true`, because a seed that ran on every launch would resurrect a history the user cleared, out of a file that is not the store of record for it. Guarded by `check:run-totals` and `check:metrics-db`. **`setFailureReason(runId, reasonId, by, signal?)`** labels WHY a failed run failed — failed real runs only (labelling a pass would invent a failure, and a baseline update is an event), stored as a reason ID resolved against the vocabulary at display time so a rename reaches history. MANUAL WINS: an `"auto"` assignment refuses to touch a record carrying any reason and never clears; a `"user"` one overwrites anything and `null` clears. The id's existence is validated by the caller (the `runs:setFailureReason` handler; the auto path maps only onto built-ins) — this store deliberately does not import the definitions. Covered by `run-history-failure-reason.test.ts`.
- `main/services/failure-reason-store.ts` — the CUSTOM half of the failure-reason vocabulary, a flat JSON index (`userData/recorder/failure-reasons.json`, annotation-store's pattern) of `CustomFailureReason` (`{id,name,description,disabled?,createdAt,updatedAt}`). `list/create/update`; there is deliberately no delete — disabling hides a reason from the picker and stops new assignments (manual and automatic alike) while every run already labelled keeps resolving, and renaming updates the record in place so the ID on historical runs picks up the new name. Validation throws user-readable messages (the handler forwards them): names trimmed, ≤100 chars, unique case-insensitively across built-ins AND all customs (disabled included, or re-enabling surfaces a collision); descriptions ≤200; at most 50 ACTIVE customs, enforced on create and on re-enable. Read directly off disk by the MCP for display resolution. Covered by `failure-reason-store.test.ts` and the failure-reason block in `handlers.test.ts`.
- `main/services/playwright-runner.ts` — Resolves `@playwright/test` CLI by scanning candidate node_modules roots; spawns `cli.js test <spec> --config <playwright.config.ts> --headed --browser=<engine> --reporter=line`; installs the chosen engine first if missing; streams output. **Browser selection:** `start({browser?})` resolves explicit → `TestRecord.runBrowser` → `RecorderSettings.defaultRunBrowser`. `isBrowserInstalled(engine)` matches directories by the `<engine>-` prefix, NOT a bare `startsWith` — Playwright also unpacks `chromium_headless_shell-*`, which is not a usable headed browser, so the old `startsWith("chromium")` reported a shell-only install as complete and the run failed at launch. **Awaiting a run:** `start` is fire-and-forget, and the `runs` map can't be used to await one (it holds an entry only while the child process is alive — empty during browser install and after exit but before the `RunRecord` is written), so a separate `inFlight` map holds a per-run promise resolving to the exit code, exposed as `waitFor(runId)` (null when not in flight). This is what batch runs sequence on. `ensureConfig()` writes `playwright.config.ts` next to the specs from `playwright-config-source.ts` since the CLI has no `--slow-mo` flag; **every `ensure*` writer now goes through `writeIfChanged` (compare, then temp-file + atomic rename)** — these are files SHARED by every run, rewritten on every run, while other runs' Playwright processes may be reading them, and truncate-then-write is not atomic. Content is fixed per app build, so after the first run of a session they write nothing at all. **Concurrent browser install** is serialized through an `installs` map keyed by engine: several parallel runs can discover the same missing engine at once, and N `playwright install` processes unpacking into one directory produces a half-written browser that then fails to launch for every later run; the first caller installs and the rest await the same promise (and are told so, since the install's output streams to a different run's Output panel). **`PW_OUTPUT_DIR`** gives each run its own Playwright scratch dir (`<scriptsDir>/test-results/<recordId>`, removed in the `finally`) — the default is derived from the SPEC's path, so two concurrent runs of one spec would share and clean the same folder; the speed→delay table and the per-test timeout rule now live in `run-pacing.ts` and are passed via the `PW_SLOWMO_MS` env var and `--timeout` per run. **Shopify crawler signatures:** a run whose test's OWN origin has a registered, readable, unexpired signature joins the redirect gate too, writes `glaze-signature.mjs`, and passes `GLAZE_SIG_COUNT` plus one env var per value (never a JSON blob — the rule `variableEnv` states for secrets, because a blob shows up whole in a crash dump). Two different rules deliberately: what ARMS the run is the test's own origin, because routing disables the context's HTTP cache and a machine with a signature registered must not pay that on every unrelated test; what is INSTALLED once armed is a route for every registered host, so a test that navigates between two registered stores signs both. Like settling, signing is NOT part of `artifactRun`. `announceSignatureState` emits a line for each of the six outcomes — sent, expired, unreadable, wrong-domain, imported spec, redirect failed — because an unsigned run against a store with bot protection does not fail at a login form where the cause is obvious; it fails further down as a timeout and reads as a flaky test. **Crawl speed:** a `crawl` run of an app-generated test also joins `captureArtifacts || healing || a11y || recordLogs` as a reason to redirect the spec's `@playwright/test` import to the capture fixture, writes `glaze-settle.mjs`, and sets `GLAZE_SETTLE=1` (see `settle-fixture-source.ts`). Settling alone does NOT make the run an artifact run — `artifactRun` gates the prune/`ensureRunDir`/`writeSteps` bookkeeping separately, so a crawl run with healing off doesn't prune the artifact history to create an empty dir. An imported test can't be redirected at all, so it gets the step delay and an explicit "page-settling is skipped for imported tests" line rather than silently slower-but-identical behaviour. **Run persistence:** accumulates visible output per run in `logBuffers` (via `emitOutput`) and on `finally` calls `runHistoryStore.append(...)` (status = exitCode===0?passed:failed) then pushes `runs:changed` before `runner:done`. **Visual-testing capture (Phase 1):** mints a fresh `recordId` uuid per run (used for BOTH the artifacts dir and the `RunRecord` id, so a test's runs stop overwriting each other); when `captureArtifacts && !rec.sourceDir`, calls `ensureCaptureFixture` + `prepareCaptureSpec` (temp `<recordId>.capture.spec.ts` with line-1 `@playwright/test`→`./glaze-capture.mjs`), prunes old run dirs, creates the run dir, and runs the temp spec with env `GLAZE_ARTIFACT_DIR`/`GLAZE_TEST_ID`/`GLAZE_RUN_ID` (`GLAZE_CAPTURE_ARTIFACTS`=1); deletes the temp spec in `finally`; step line map built from the spec actually run. `buildStepLineMap` unchanged (line-preserving redirect). **Phase 2:** accumulates per-step pass/fail in `stepStatusMaps` (via `emitStep`, keyed by **attempt** and then by the reporter's step index — R24a; flat, a retry's outcomes overwrote the failing attempt's step for step and `buildReplay` computed `failedIndex === null`) and, in `finally` when capturing, writes `replay.json` via `buildReplay(...)` from attempt 0's map — the single place that correlates the reporter statuses + the capture manifest against `Step[]`. **Reporter-attribution gotcha:** the capture fixture WRAPS action methods, so Playwright attributes wrapped actions' step location to `glaze-capture.mjs` and the step-reporter's file guard drops them. Since 2026-08-15 the wrapper announces those steps itself (see `capture-fixture-source.ts`), so a capture run reports every step rather than none; `buildReplay` still combines two signals, but reported statuses now cover the whole spec and the screenshot-presence rule is the fallback rather than the load-bearing half.
