The failing step is the first reported failure, else (for a failed run) the first uncaptured non-control step after the last screenshot. **Trace salvage is gated on `anyAttemptFailed`, not the exit code (R24a):** `trace: "retain-on-failure"` keeps a trace for each FAILED attempt, and a run that passes on retry exits 0 — so the old gate deleted the failing attempt's trace with the scratch dir, which is the only trace worth opening. `findTraceZips` returns every trace with the attempt it belongs to (from `attemptFromPlaywrightOutputDir`), each copied into its own attempt directory; only attempt 0's sets `hasTrace`, because Open Trace reads the run directory's own. **`stepLineMaps` is dropped in the run's `finally`, not when the child process closes** — `runCli` is also how a missing browser is installed, under the same runId, and deleting it there threw the map away before the first run on a new engine ever started. `captureMethod(step)` mirrors the fixture's PAGE_ACTIONS/LOCATOR_ACTIONS to match manifest entries to steps by method (so a misclassification can't cascade-desync the timeline).
- `shared/capture-fixture-source.mjs` — `captureFixtureSource`: the `glaze-capture.mjs` fixture as a raw JS string (bare `"` inside the template literal — escaped quotes trip `no-useless-escape`). Extends Playwright's `test` so its `page` fixture patches page-level (`goto`/`reload`/`setViewportSize`/…) and Locator-prototype (`click`/`fill`/`check`/`selectOption`/…) action methods to screenshot AFTER each action into `GLAZE_ARTIFACT_DIR` as `<index>.png` (index = 0-based action order), then writes `manifest.json` on teardown. Env-gated: exports plain `base` when nothing that needs it is on (zero overhead, no patching). Every screenshot is `try/catch`-wrapped + `screenshot({timeout:5000})` so a failure is logged to stderr and skipped, never failing/altering the test. **It is also where per-step progress is announced (2026-08-15).** `wrap` emits a `__GLAZE_STEP__:` begin before the call and an end after it — `ok:false` from the catch, before rethrowing, because the step that threw is the one the user has to be shown. It has to be here: Playwright takes a step's location from the first stack frame outside its own library, which for a wrapped action is this file, so the reporter's file guard drops it and there is nowhere else the spec's line is still visible. `specCallerLine()` reads it by searching the stack for `testInfo.file` (the path Playwright resolved, so a capture run's redirected temp copy needs no special case) — the whole stack, not one frame, so a future patch nesting on either side does not silently stop progress. `patchOnce` is therefore **unconditional** for any run that loads the fixture, not gated on screenshots: heal-only and crawl-only runs wrap the same methods and need the announcement just as much. **Attempt-aware since R24a**: `attemptNo` is read from `testInfo.retry` per test (a `page` fixture is re-entered for every attempt), every marker carries it, and the capture context is rooted at `attemptArtifactDir(attempt)` rather than at `GLAZE_ARTIFACT_DIR` — so a retry writes to `attempt-1/` instead of over the failing attempt's files. The directory rule is interpolated from `shared/attempt-artifacts.mjs` as `ATTEMPT_HELPERS`, not hand-copied: this file WRITES those directories and the app READS them.
- `shared/page-actions.mjs` — `PAGE_ACTIONS` (`goto`/`goBack`/`goForward`/`reload`/`setViewportSize`/`setContent`) and `LOCATOR_ACTIONS` (`click`/`fill`/`check`/`selectOption`/…): the canonical set of page-mutating Playwright methods, plus `actionsLiteral()` to embed them in a fixture source string. **Two** fixtures patch these now (capture screenshots after each; settle waits after each), and they are separate source strings — a second hand-maintained copy would drift the first time an action was added to one, and the failure is silent (the step still runs and still screenshots, it just never settles). One list, interpolated into both. Pinned by `check:crawl-speed`.
- `shared/settle-fixture-source.mjs` — `settleFixtureSource`: the `glaze-settle.mjs` fixture as a raw JS string, written next to the specs like the capture and heal fixtures. `installSettle(page)` patches the `page-actions.ts` methods so that after each action resolves — and **before control returns to the spec** — it awaits `waitForLoadState("load")` (15s cap), `waitForLoadState("networkidle")` (5s cap, expected to time out on a page with an open connection; reported once per run, not per step), then `document.fonts.ready` + a double `requestAnimationFrame` (2s cap). Env-gated on `GLAZE_SETTLE`, set by the runner only for a `crawl`-speed run of an app-generated test. **Never fails a test:** every wait is individually bounded and swallowed, and `withTimeout` attaches a terminal `.catch` — an abandoned wait whose page later navigates would otherwise become an unhandled rejection and fail an otherwise-fine run. The paint timer is deliberately NOT `unref()`d: an unref'd timer doesn't hold the event loop open, so when it is the only pending work the process exits instead of the fallback firing. `onSettled(fn)` is the extension seam page indexing will hang off — post-settle is the one moment the DOM is known stable. Why a fixture and not generated code: a spec is generated once and lives on disk while speed is changed afterwards from the sidebar slider, so emitted `waitForLoadState` calls would silently disagree with the setting.
- `renderer/main/test-detail-view.tsx` also carries the **Shopify signature chip**: an error `Status` beside the toolbar's Base URL control, shown only when this test's origin has a registered signature that is expired, unreadable, or unusable because the test is imported. Never shown for a working one — that would put it on screen for every run of every test against a registered store. Refreshed off `settings:changed` (see `useSettingsFreshness`), since signatures are edited in Settings → Integrations.
- `main/services/shopify-signature-store.ts` — the two files above, behind an API the renderer can only read STATUS from: `list()` (host/expiry/state, never a value), `upsert()` (by HOST — appending would leave two candidates for one request and a silent first-wins), `remove()`, `entries()`/`signatureFor(url)` for the trainer and runner, and `headerValuesForRedaction()`. That last one returns **two** values, never `Signature-Agent`: it is the public literal `"https://shopify.com"`, and redacting it would replace that string everywhere it legitimately appears, including in this app's own documentation. It DOES include expired entries — an expired signature is no longer sent, but one sent an hour ago is still in yesterday's run log.
- **The live page's half** (`main/services/live-page-service.ts`, 2026-08-24) — the app's THIRD surface that loads a customer's site, and the one that had neither credential: it launched Playwright and `goto`'d the test's address with no signature and no basic-auth answer, so a protected storefront served the Script IDE the password page while the same test's runs sailed through. Now both. **Basic auth** is `httpCredentials` on the context, scoped through the SHARED `credentialOrigin` — the same function the generated `test.use` and the trainer's `login` handler derive their scope from, because an unscoped credential answers any server's 401. It needs a context rather than `browser.newPage()`, which is why `LiveBrowserLike` grew `newContext` and the seam gained `LiveContextLike`/`LiveRouteLike`; `livePage:open` gained a `testId` (narrowed to a string in the handler, looked up in the store, an id naming no test yielding no credentials rather than an error). **The signature** is a `route` with a host predicate, never `extraHTTPHeaders`, for the reason `signature-fixture-source.ts` gives — and the run's TWO rules are copied deliberately: ARM only when the address being opened at is itself registered (routing disables the context's HTTP cache, so an unrelated live page must not pay it), then INSTALL for every registered host so a second registered store typed into the address bar signs too. Unlike the run it is NOT gated on an imported test — the live page is a browser the editor drives, not a spec the app rewrote — and the toolbar's signature chip says "runs of this test — and its live page" for the expired/unreadable cases it now shares. The same armed-against-signed tally the trainer keeps is logged when the page closes. **One limit it shares with the run, stated because it is Playwright's and not ours:** a route's predicate is consulted only for the ORIGINAL request — a redirect hop is never re-routed — and `continue({headers})` carries its headers through redirects, so a registered storefront that redirects to an unregistered authority hands that authority the signature. Measured on 1.62.1; the trainer does not have it, because Electron's `onBeforeSendHeaders` fires per hop. See DECISIONS 2026-08-24 for why the obvious fix is worse. Proven by `check:live-page`, which serves a wall that answers only a signed request (control first, so a green signed row cannot be a server that always says yes), and by the arm-rule and scope rows in `live-page-service.test.ts`.
- **The trainer's half of the crawler signature** (`main/services/recorder-service.ts`) — a single `onBeforeSendHeaders` on the recording's own `recorder-incognito-<uuid>` session, armed when any READABLE, UNEXPIRED signature is registered (`entries()`, never the plaintext register) and deciding per REQUEST through `signatureForUrl`, so every hop of a redirect chain and every subresource is judged on its own authority and a storefront's CDN, analytics and chat widgets are never offered the credential. **Proven end to end by `e2e/shopify-signature.spec.ts` as of 2026-08-24** — DECISIONS 2026-08-18 left this to a manual pass, and the source-level assertions in `check:shopify-signature` pin the hook's shape but cannot say whether the header reached the wire. Four rows against a server that serves the store to a signed request and "Enter password" to an unsigned one: every hop of a session (initial navigation, redirect hop, subresource, a click that navigates), the negative control (nothing registered → the wall holds), a third-party host loaded by the same page (never signed — two ports are two authorities), and a REOPENED test, which is the `editing: true` branch a user is on when they say "I open the browser for training". **Two diagnostics beside it, both answering "can anyone tell?":** the session's `signedRequests` tally, logged against `signatureArmed` when the session ends (`stopPolling`), because armed-and-signed-nothing is this feature's likeliest silent failure and reads identically to armed-and-signed-everything — the run fixture has counted this since the feature landed and the trainer had no counterpart; and a third `recorder:signatureNotSent` reason, `other-host`, which names what IS registered when the host being trained against has no signature. That case was pure silence in the trainer while the run has named it from the start (`announceSignatureState`), which left "I registered one and the trainer still shows the password page" with no answer in the app. Raised only when something is registered — a machine with no signatures has not asked for this feature.
- `shared/signature-fixture-source.mjs` — `signatureFixtureSource`: the `glaze-signature.mjs` fixture as a raw JS string, plus `signatureEnvNames(index)` so the runner's writer and the fixture's reader cannot disagree about an env var name. `installSignatureHeaders(page)` registers ONE `context.route` with a **predicate** (`URL.host`, exact, port included) rather than a glob, and `route.continue({headers})` merges over `allHeaders()` — `continue` REPLACES the header set, so anything omitted is dropped from the request. **Why a route and not `use.extraHTTPHeaders`:** that option is context-wide, so it would hand the credential to `cdn.shopify.com`, the storefront's analytics, its chat widget and every installed app's host. It is also technically wrong — the signature covers `@authority`, so presenting it elsewhere is an INVALID signature rather than a missing one. Installed FIRST in the capture fixture's `page` fixture, ahead of the three action-method patches, so a heal retry cannot re-enter it. `reportSignedRequests()` writes a per-host count to **stderr** (stdout carries the step reporter's markers): a run that arms a signature and signs zero requests is this feature's likeliest silent failure and is otherwise indistinguishable from success.
- `main/services/run-pacing.ts` — pure run-pacing policy, split out of `playwright-runner.ts` so a timeout rule can be tested without bundling the visual pipeline. `SLOW_MO_MS` maps `TestRecord.speed` (`crawl`/`slow`/`medium`/`fast`) → `2500`/`1200`/`400`/`0` ms. `resolveTestTimeoutMs(perTest, settingsDefault, speed)` resolves explicit per-test → Settings default → 60s, then raises a `crawl` run to a `CRAWL_MIN_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 min) **floor**, returning `{timeoutMs, raised}`. The floor exists because crawl multiplies run time: against the 60s default, choosing the resilient speed would make tests fail. It is a floor, not an override — a longer timeout is left alone — and `raised` is what lets the runner say so in the output rather than changing a timeout silently.
- `main/services/artifact-store.ts` — `artifactStore` owns `<userData>/recorder/artifacts/<testId>/<runId>/`. `runDir`/`ensureRunDir`, `pruneRuns(testId, keep=DEFAULT_RETAINED_RUNS=10)` (delete all but newest `keep` run dirs by mtime), `listRuns`/`readManifest`, `deleteTest` (called from `tests:delete`). **Retention default kept at 10** (Phase 5 revisit): real usage measured ~0.6 MB per captured run for a small test (page PNGs ~0.1–0.4 MB each), so 10 runs ≈ 6 MB small / tens of MB large — recent history without waste. **`RESERVED_DIRS = {"baseline"}`:** both `pruneRuns` and `listRuns` now exclude the sibling `baseline/` dir — a Phase-5 bug fix, since it previously counted as a prunable "run" and retention could silently DELETE the pinned baseline (breaking the Phase 3/4 "baseline survives pruning" premise). The e2e regression check locks this. `ArtifactManifest`/`ArtifactStepEntry` = the per-step capture model (screenshot success). **Phase 2 replay model:** `RunReplay`/`ReplayStep`/`ReplayStepStatus` (`passed|failed|skipped|unknown`) + `RunReplaySummary`; `writeReplay`/`readReplay` (per-run `replay.json`), `listReplays()` (scans the artifacts tree for all `replay.json`, newest first — naturally reflects retention), `readShot(testId,runId,file)` (returns PNG bytes; `file` validated to `^\d+\.png$` so it can't escape the run dir). `writeStepMatches`/`readStepMatches` hold what each failing locator actually resolved to (`step-matches.json`), returned RAW because the rebuild belongs at the IPC edge with the other boundary normalizers. `hasHealFailures` answers whether a run left EITHER file behind, so the AI panel can decide whether to offer the structure request without reading and normalizing to find it empty. **Who calls the sweep changed on 2026-08-25 (R17):** `pruneAllTests` brackets itself with two `usage()` walks — each a recursive `statSync` over the whole tree — so it is now reached only from startup, the Settings button, and `sweepRetentionIfDue()`'s half-hour throttle in `retention.ts`. A RUN prunes just the test it ran, through `applyRetentionForTest`. It previously sat in every run's `finally`, which cost a hundred-test batch two hundred full-tree walks on the thread streaming its own output. `check:retention` pins the call sites too, since restoring the expensive one changes no answer anywhere.
- `shared/step-marker.mjs` — the per-step progress marker: `STEP_MARKER` (the one spelling of the `__GLAZE_STEP__:` prefix, imported by all three writers rather than transcribed) and `splitStepMarkers(buffered, chunk)`, the runner's stdout splitter. Pure, so the two rules that are not obvious are unit-testable without a child process: a marker can straddle chunk boundaries, and a marker does **not** always start its line — Playwright's `line` reporter writes its cursor-control prefix with no trailing newline, so a marker written from the WORKER (where the capture fixture runs) lands directly after it, and a `startsWith` check drops it and prints it at the user instead. Rejects a payload that isn't exactly a step transition: the same stdout carries Playwright's own output, which quotes page-controlled text. **Carries `attempt` since R24a** — normalized rather than validated away, because a marker from a writer that predates the field is still a real step transition and dropping it costs the run its progress, while a wrong attempt costs it only one attempt's separation.
- `shared/attempt-artifacts.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **where one attempt's evidence goes (R24a).** Playwright re-runs a failed test from the top, and the capture fixture is a `page` fixture, so every attempt re-enters it with the step counter back at 0. Into one fixed directory that meant attempt 2 wrote its screenshots, `manifest.json`, `console.json` and `network.json` straight over attempt 1's — and a retry only ever follows a failure, so the attempt destroyed was always the one worth reading. Nothing is recoverable afterwards: the scratch dir is deleted at run end, so an attempt's evidence is written once or never. The rule: **attempt 0 keeps the run directory; every later attempt gets `attempt-<n>/` beneath it.** The asymmetry is not just compatibility — attempt 0 is the attempt that failed, and it is where `readManifest`, `readShot`, the replay, the visual diff and Open Trace already point. `attemptDirName`/`attemptArtifactDir`/`parseAttemptDirName`/`normalizeAttempt`, plus `attemptFromPlaywrightOutputDir` (`<slug>-retry2` ⇒ 2), read out of the installed `playwright/lib/worker/workerProcessEntry.js` rather than assumed — this repo's own note claimed a nested `retryN/` directory Playwright does not produce. `ATTEMPT_HELPERS` is the module's own functions stringified for the capture fixture, which writes these directories from inside a worker and cannot import anything. Guarded by `check:retry-evidence` and `e2e/retry-evidence.spec.ts`.
- `shared/run-attempts.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **what a retry MEANS (R24)**, for the record and for the maths. `docs/ROUTINES.md` refused a retry policy in v1 because "a routine-level retry stacked on top makes a flaky test look stable — which is precisely the signal the Stability panel exists to give", and named its condition: a retry must mark the `RunRecord`. This is that marking plus the counting rule without which it is decoration. **A retried pass is PASSED as an outcome and FAILED as a signal** — the pass rate says yes, the flake transition series says it went red — and collapsing the two either way is the bug. `retryFields({status, maxAttempt})` returns FIELDS to spread, empty when nothing retried (an `attempt: 0` on every row is indistinguishable from a row predating the field); `flakeSignal` is the transition-series entry; `everFailed` answers "did this go red at any point"; `normalizeRetries` answers null rather than 0 for an unusable value, so `--retries eight` is refused by name rather than quietly running once. Four callers across three processes — both runners WRITE, `flake-analysis.mjs` and `period-digest.mjs` READ, through the MCP as well as the app.
- `main/services/replay-builder.ts` — the runner's replay-correlation brain, **extracted from `playwright-runner.ts` in Phase 5** so it's regression-testable without spawning Playwright: `captureMethod(step)`, `buildReplay(...)` (correlates reporter statuses + capture manifest → `RunReplay`), `enrichWithVisualDiffs(replay, threshold)` (Phase 3 diff pass). Depends only on artifact/baseline stores + `diffPngBuffers` + `describeStep` — no `child_process`/IPC — so a standalone check can drive it. `playwright-runner.ts` now imports these two functions instead of defining them (behavior identical).
- **`runStep` is the single replay dispatch point** (`recorder-service.ts`). All four replay paths route through it. `cookie` steps CANNOT go through `buildReplayScript` — an httpOnly cookie is invisible to `document.cookie` — so they branch to `applyCookieStep`. Added deliberately as one dispatch point after the Auto-Heal bug, where a capability was wired into one replay path and silently missing from the other three: a new step kind now cannot be handled in some paths and missed in others. `withCaptureSuspended` is the same pattern one level up — one place that decides what a replay does to the SESSION, for the same reason.
- **Testing (two systems, one command).** `npm run test:all` = the 15 standalone `check:*` scripts, then Vitest. `npm test` runs Vitest alone; `npm run test:watch` for watch mode.
  - **Vitest** was added because Vite 8 was already a devDependency, so it reuses the toolchain rather than adding a second bundler. Config in `vitest.config.ts`, two projects: **`node`** (backend + pure renderer logic; `main/**/*.test.ts`, `mcp/**/*.test.ts`, `renderer/lib/**/*.test.ts`) and **`dom`** (jsdom; `renderer/**/*.test.tsx` + `renderer/dev/**/*.test.ts` + `main/**/*.dom.test.ts`). `renderer/dev/**/*.test.ts` is listed explicitly because the preview bridge is a `.ts` that needs a `window` but renders nothing — without it the file matches NEITHER project and is silently never run.
  - **The hard part is `@glaze/core/*`**, which resolves through the Glaze runtime's ESM loader hooks + tsconfig paths, neither of which exists under a runner. `backend` → the test stub (NEVER the real module — it talks to the native host and would touch real userData); `components`/`hooks`/`ipc` → the SDK's REAL prebuilt ESM bundles, so component tests exercise the actual design system. Those bundles sit outside the project root, so their bare imports (`react`, `react-dom`, `@tanstack/react-query`) are pinned to this project's copies — which also guarantees ONE React instance, since two produce "invalid hook call" errors that look like component bugs. To find the set if the SDK is upgraded: `grep -ohE 'from"[^".][^"]*"' <sdk>/components.js | sort -u`. `sonner` is STUBBED, not installed (it's the SDK's dependency, not this app's); the stub also makes toasts assertable.
  - **`.dom.test.ts` vs `.test.ts` matters:** the node project explicitly excludes `**/*.dom.test.ts`, or DOM tests match both globs and run a second time with no document, failing for reasons unrelated to the code.
  - **jsdom has no layout engine** — `getBoundingClientRect()` returns zeros, so the replayer's `visible()` (correct for a real browser) treats everything as hidden. `step-replayer.dom.test.ts` installs a nominal box; without it, visibility tests fail AND "hidden → passes" silently becomes a test that proves nothing.
  - **Lint note:** `main/**/*.dom.test.ts` runs in jsdom but lives under `main/`, which the SDK's shared lint config treats as Node-only. Browser globals need a `/* global ... */` directive (flat config doesn't support `eslint-env`).
- **Cross-copy parity checks are a house pattern.** Five places duplicate logic that can't be shared, each pinned by a test: app↔MCP batch summary and app↔MCP concurrency clamp (`check:batch-runner`), app↔MCP `playwright.config.ts` (`check:runner-config`), backend↔renderer `describeStep` (`describe-step-parity.test.ts`), and the capture-script↔replayer locator contract (`step-replayer.dom.test.ts`). Two of them caught real drift.

- **The trainer↔runner parity harness is the biggest of those, and it works in two layers.** The subject is not a duplicated constant but a duplicated *engine*: what a step means to the injected replayer versus to the generated Playwright spec. `main/services/assert-emission.test.ts` is the fast layer — it evaluates the emitted matcher ARGUMENT and applies Playwright's own rule to it (a RegExp is `.test()`, a string is equality), catching escaping, anchoring and case bugs in milliseconds with no browser, and `main/services/step-replayer-parity.dom.test.ts` covers the replayer side in jsdom. `e2e/assert-parity.spec.ts` is the authority those depend on: one HTTP-served fixture page, every row run through the real injected replayer AND the **real emitted source executed by real Playwright**, asserting the two verdicts are equal *and* that both equal what the fixture independently says — because two engines can agree and both be wrong. It is deliberately not an Electron test; its subject is matcher semantics, not a window, so a plain browser page is sufficient and far faster. Reverting the `url` fix turns it red with `"the run says false, the trainer says true"`, which is the original bug report restated as an assertion.
- **`e2e/step-progress.spec.ts` is the third non-window spec, and it exists for the same reason: only real Playwright can answer its question.** Which steps a run REPORTS decides which step the app can highlight, and both writers are unreachable from a unit test — the reporter only exists as a string written next to the specs and loaded by the Playwright CLI, and the capture fixture's wrapper only exists inside a Playwright worker. Nothing here had ever asked real Playwright which category it files an assertion under; the answer (`expect`, not `pw:api`) was the whole bug, and it left a failing assertion reported by nothing while a capture run reported no step at all. The spec runs the real generated spec through the real CLI in both plain and capture modes and reads the stream back through the runner's own `splitStepMarkers`. `check:step-progress` is the laptop-speed half, pinning the same properties at source. **Changing what reports a step — the reporter's categories, the fixture's wrapper, the marker format? Add a row.**
- **Regression checks** (`main/services/__tests__/*.check.ts`, run via `npm run check:*`). There is **no test runner** in this project — each check is a standalone script using plain assertions + a non-zero exit code, and adding one alongside a behavioral change is the house convention. Eighteen exist: `spec-parser`, `visual-pipeline`, `ai-debug-scroll`, `capture-overhead`, `retention`, `auto-heal-wiring`, `run-filters`, `batch-runner`, `runner-config`, `batch-history`, `test-tags`, `mcp-select`, `alerts`, `cookie-steps`, `batch-order`, `paginate`. `glaze-backend-stub.ts` now also stubs `safeStorage` — it reports encryption as UNAVAILABLE so a check can never write a real secret to disk. **The esbuild-bundled checks do NOT type-check** — `npm run type-check` is the real gate for them (a spread overwriting an explicit property slipped past a green `check:batch-history` once). Three styles: pure-logic checks run under `tsx` directly; the MCP checks are plain `.mjs` run by `node`; anything importing `@glaze/core/backend` must be bundled with esbuild + `--alias:@glaze/core/backend=./main/services/__tests__/glaze-backend-stub.ts` (plain tsx/node can't resolve that specifier). `check:auto-heal-wiring` is a *source-level* check — it slices each replay method out of `recorder-service.ts` and asserts it calls `healAndRetry`, guarding against a path silently losing Auto-Heal again. `check:replay-suspend` is its sibling and works the same way: it slices out each replay method and asserts it runs inside `withCaptureSuspended` and never touches `session.paused` directly — a replay path that skips it RECORDS WHAT IT REPLAYS, appending a duplicate step every time the user previews one, which is silent and looks like a test that grew on its own. `check:push-consumers` is a third source-level one: it harvests every channel `sendToMain` can push (following a same-file `const`, and a forwarder's own `emit("…")` literals) and asserts each has an `api.on` in `renderer/`. **No allowlist by design** — an "expected orphans" list is what would have hidden the two bugs that motivated it (`trainerPanel:viewportNarrowed`, `recorder:loadFailed`), since the fix for a red check becomes "add the channel to the list". Its own two failure modes are pinned: a scanner that goes blind (harvest floors + one named channel per parsing path) and a send it cannot resolve (which fails rather than being skipped). It skips a balanced `<…>` before the call parens because `api.on<{ status: "begin" | "end" }>("recorder:replayStep", …)` otherwise parses `begin` as the channel. **`check:derived-cache` is its complement, and exists because push-consumers cannot ask the two questions that decide whether the screen actually updates**: is the listener mounted when the event fires (a `runs:changed` subscription in a route component satisfies push-consumers while being dead on every other route), and does a cache a run writes have any refresh path at all (`["flake"]` and `["heals"]` had none — neither is a push problem, so no push-side check could ever see them). It asserts that no `runs:changed` handler outside `recorder-store.tsx` invalidates, that the store's does call `invalidateRunDerived`, that every key in the list is read by a real `useQuery`, and that `["script-changes"]` stays out. Its own blindness is guarded by a positive control: the detector is run against a synthetic violating fixture and must flag it.
- `main/services/__tests__/visual-pipeline.check.ts` — **Phase 5 end-to-end regression check** covering the whole visual pipeline in one path: seed artifacts → `buildReplay` correlates → seed baselines → intentional change flagged `changed` (+ diff overlay) → `acceptStep`/`acceptRunBaseline` clears the flag → annotation upsert/list round-trip → `pruneRuns` retention (baseline survives). Drives the REAL stores against a temp `userData` dir; `@glaze/core/backend` is aliased to `__tests__/glaze-backend-stub.ts` (lazy `app.getPath`→`GLAZE_TEST_USERDATA`, no-op `logger`). Run via `npm run check:visual-pipeline` (esbuild bundle `--format=cjs` with the alias, then `node` — plain tsx/node can't resolve `@glaze/core/backend`, and pngjs is CJS so esm output breaks on dynamic `require`; sandbox-safe, no listen socket).
- `main/services/app-window.ts` — Holds main-window ref; `sendToMain(channel, payload)` for backend→renderer push. **Also fans out to registered auxiliary windows** (`registerAuxWindow`/`unregisterAuxWindow`), which is how the trainer panel receives every existing channel without touching the ~50 call sites. The fan-out lives INSIDE `sendToMain` on purpose: a panel that receives most pushes but not all would leave two trainers disagreeing about the step list, which reads as a step-ordering bug in the recorder rather than a missing subscription.
- `main/services/ui-scale.ts` — the "Font size" setting, implemented as **proportional zoom** rather than a type scale. `attachUiScale(win)` is called once per APP window at creation (`main/index.ts`, `windows/trainer-panel-window.ts`): it sets `webContents.setZoomFactor` from `RecorderSettings.uiScale` and re-applies on `did-finish-load`, because Chromium resets the factor across a navigation and a window that reloads would otherwise snap back to 100% silently. `applyUiScaleToAllWindows()` is called from the `recorder:setSettings` handler so the change lands while the user is looking at it; it names the app's own windows rather than enumerating them. The settings WINDOW used to be a third entry in that list because it was not an aux window and was the one window the setting is changed in — Settings is a route in the main window since 2026-08-24, so the special case is gone. **Zoom and not a `--gl-font-scale` multiplier** because the theme is tuned in whole pixels (108 `font-size` declarations, 9.5px labels inside 24px controls inside a 34px strip) — growing the text inside fixed-height chrome clips rather than helps, and zoom re-lays the page out so text and boxes grow together. **The training PAGE is deliberately excluded**, which is the load-bearing property (the training browser's own URL strip IS scaled — it is app chrome, and `recorder-service.ts` is a fourth caller applying `uiScale()` to that view and nothing else): it renders the arbitrary site under test, so scaling it would change what a responsive site serves, what a click lands on and what a visual baseline captures. The module never imports `BrowserWindow`, so there is no `getAllWindows` to reach for; `ui-scale.test.ts` pins that structurally alongside the reload and destroyed-window behaviour. Validation lives in the store, not here — `uiScale` is membership in `UI_SCALES`, never a clamp, because a `0` or `1e9` would draw the Settings screen (the only way back) unreadable. **It also owns window SIZING**: `scaled(cssPixels)` converts a layout measurement into points, and `attachUiScale(win, {width, height})` records a window's floor in CSS pixels so it can be re-applied on every change — a floor left in points is a smaller viewport than it was measured for (960 points is 768 CSS px at 125%, under the ~928 the widest toolbar needs). `setMinimumSize` does not resize an already-smaller window on macOS, so a window found under its new floor is grown; one above it is never shrunk. The trainer panel deliberately passes NO floor: its minimum is its docked width, latched for the session in `trainer-panel-window.ts`, because the dock, the follower and `computeUndock` must all agree on one number. Covered by `ui-scale.test.ts` and `e2e/ui-scale.spec.ts` — and the e2e asserts on the renderer's `clientWidth`/`scrollWidth`, never a screenshot, because Playwright captures a zoomed Electron window as a crop at the pre-zoom device scale and a correct viewport photographs as a clipped one.
- `main/services/panel-dock.ts` — **Pure** dock geometry for the trainer panel. `computeDock` (dock-time: splits the browser's footprint into browser + panel, total-preserving, `null` when the display can't hold both at their minimums — unless `DockOptions.preserveBrowserWidth`, for a session recording at a **window-size preset**, where the browser's width is the size the test replays at rather than a footprint the user dragged: the pair's total grows instead of splitting, `BROWSER_MIN_WIDTH` does not apply because a 390-wide mobile preset is deliberately below it, and not fitting the display returns `null` so the panel opens undocked rather than resizing a browser it was told not to), `computePanelFollow` (every move/resize thereafter: places the panel against the browser's edge and does NOT touch the browser — flips to the other side when the preferred one runs out of display, `null` when neither fits so the caller undocks), `computeParkedPanel` (where the panel opens when `computeDock` said no: flush beside the browser if a whole panel fits there, else against the work-area edge that covers LESS of the browser — because "undocked" still has to be a rectangle somebody chose, and a window created with no coordinates is centred on the display, i.e. over the page under test), `computeUndock`, `boundsEqual`, `isDuplicateApply`. The two operations must stay separate: one function serving both re-splits an already-split footprint on every drag, walking the browser a panel-width narrower each time. Pure for the same reason as `recorder-navigation.ts` — display edges, negative-origin monitors and too-narrow screens are all one call here and unreachable by hand.
- `main/services/window-fill.ts` — **Pure**. `fillWorkArea(workArea, min)` is the size and position the main window OPENS at: the work area of the display it opens on, never smaller than the window's own layout floor. The app opens filling the screen because nothing restores a size the user chose — Glaze's `windowKey` frame persistence has no Electron equivalent and `@shell/backend` strips the option — so the opening size is the size the window has on every launch, and every screen here is a list that grows. The work area rather than the display bounds, because a window under the menu bar or behind the Dock has put its own chrome where it cannot be reached. Two things the arithmetic settles that a display cannot be arranged to test by hand: a **work area whose origin is negative** (a second display placed left of the built-in one), and a **display smaller than the 960×456 floor**, where the floor wins — it is the measured requirement of the widest toolbar, and Electron would clamp the size up regardless, so doing it here is what keeps the position coherent with the size instead of leaving a window centred half off two edges. The overflow then goes off the right and bottom, so the title bar and the sidebar stay reachable. Its arguments are both in POINTS: the floor arrives already through `scaled()`, and a work area is measured in points to begin with, so nothing is scaled here. Covered by `window-fill.test.ts` and — for the property a unit test cannot reach, that a REAL window is created against a REAL display — `e2e/app-launch.spec.ts`.
- `renderer/recorder-chrome/` — the training browser's **URL bar** (`index.tsx` → `recorder-chrome.html`, the fourth Vite entry; `url-bar.tsx` + tests). Renders into `chromeView`, a `WebContentsView` pinned to the top 36pt of the recorder window, above the untrusted page. Shows the live URL with the origin dimmed and the path bright, a copy button, and an **Assert URL** menu (native `Menu.popup` — a React popover would be clipped to the strip's height) that opens a prefilled URL assertion through `recorder:assertUrl`. Subscribes to `recorder:trainingUrl` and seeds itself from `recorder:getTrainingUrl` (subscribe first, then fetch — the other order loses a navigation that commits in between). **Deliberately not editable and deliberately not `RecorderProvider`**: a navigation the user types is one the recorder does not record, and the strip needs one string rather than the whole session's pushes delivered inside the window whose responsiveness the user is judging the page by. NOT named `*-window.html`, because it is not a window. Drawn in the `--gl-*` theme layer since 2026-08-12 (`.gl-chrome-strip*` in `screens.css`); reviewable at `?view=chrome` in the browser preview. Its CSP is tighter than the three windows' — this is the app's only owned pixel inside a window whose other occupant is an arbitrary site, and it loads no remote anything.
- `shared/url-assert.mjs` — `urlAssertPrefill(kind, url)`: what a URL assertion's "Expected" field is seeded with. `urlIs` gets the absolute URL (it generates an exact whole-URL match, so anything shorter can never pass); `url`/`urlEndsWith` get the path + query + fragment, because the origin is what differs between environments and asserting it is why those kinds exist; `urlPathIs` gets the pathname ALONE — the query and fragment are what the kind exists to not assert — and at a site root it suggests `/`, which is safe for an exact path match where it would be vacuous for a contains one. A site root falls back to the HOST rather than suggesting `/`, which as a contains-assertion passes against every URL on every host — a green assertion that tests nothing. Returns `""` for unparseable or non-http URLs rather than something plausible (including for the two `title` kinds, which this helper does not seed — the training browser's right-click menu prefills those from the live `document.title` via `currentPageTitle()`, and the two renderer menus deliberately leave them empty because neither window tracks the live title). In `shared/` because four callers on both sides of the IPC boundary need the identical answer: the training browser's URL strip and its right-click menu (backend), and the assert menus in `recording-view.tsx` and `trainer-panel-view.tsx` (renderer). **The path prefill is only correct because `url` now generates a SUBSTRING match** — this file's doc comment used to claim `url` emitted "a substring/contains match" while the generator emitted a bare string, i.e. an exact whole-URL check, which a path can never satisfy. See DECISIONS 2026-08-13.
- `shared/shopify-signature.mjs` — what a Shopify crawler signature IS: `SIGNATURE_HEADER_NAMES`, `SIGNATURE_AGENT_VALUE` (quotes included — the header is a structured-field String, and a bare `https://shopify.com` is a different, invalid value), `parseSignatureInput`, `headerValueProblem`/`validateHeaderValue`, `normalizeSignatureHost`, `signatureState`, `signatureForUrl`, `EXPIRY_WARN_DAYS`. In `shared/` because three processes need identical answers and cannot share a `.ts`: the main process attaches the headers, the renderer labels a settings row, and the standalone MCP explains why a run went unsigned. **The parser is a scanner, not a regex** — `keyid` is a quoted base64 blob that can contain the literal `expires=999`, so a `/expires=(\d+)/` scan reads an expiry date out of attacker-adjacent base64; `topLevelSegments` tracks quote and paren state and reads parameters only at parameter position. **A missing `expires` returns null and surfaces as `unknown`**, never a guessed ninety days — it is optional in RFC 9421. `headerValueProblem` rejects anything outside printable ASCII, which is header injection: the value is concatenated into a request by the trainer and travels to Playwright as CDP JSON, which is a far weaker guarantee than Chromium's own header parser. `signatureForUrl` matches the host EXACTLY (no subdomain or apex fallback) and refuses an expired entry, so the trainer, the runner and the fixture cannot disagree — see DECISIONS 2026-08-18.
- `shared/email-code.mjs` — what "sign in with the code they emailed you" IS: `endpointProblem`/`tokenProblem`/`addressProblem` (the same problem-STRING shape `headerValueProblem` uses, because "that is not a URL" and "that is not https" send the user to different fixes), `normalizeAddress`, `messagesUrl`, `codeWatermark`, `codeFromMessage`, `pickCode`, `parseMailMessage`, and the polling budget. In `shared/` because three processes need identical answers: the trainer resolves a code to preview a step, the generated runtime resolves one so the RUN can type it, and the renderer labels the step and the Settings row. **The watermark is the load-bearing rule** — a message counts only when it arrived after `max(runStartedAt, lastConsumed[address])`. Without the first half a run types a code minted before it existed; without the second a spec that signs in twice reuses the first. Both fail SILENTLY, as six plausible digits Shopify rejects — and five rejections locks the customer out for thirty minutes, which is why this is a correctness rule and not an optimisation. `codeFromMessage` and `codeWatermark` are SELF-CONTAINED, because the runtime embeds them via `toString()`; `codeFromMessage` reads the SUBJECT before the body (a subject holds one number, a body holds an order number, a year and a tracking id) and matches only a digit run bounded by non-digits, so a six-digit scan can never return the prefix of an eight-digit order number. `parseMailMessage` runs in the WORKER rather than the app, and is here anyway: it decides what `subject` and `body` are while `codeFromMessage` decides where to look, and `workers/` matches neither vitest project so a test beside the Worker would be silently never run. See DECISIONS 2026-08-26 and docs/plans/shopify-account-auth.md.

- `shared/step-semantics.mjs` — **the one definition of what a step's predicate MEANS**, and the answer to the class of bug where the trainer and the generated spec disagreed. Declares per predicate the three things Playwright itself varies: `match` (`exact`/`substring`/`endsWith`), `caseSensitive`, `normalizeWhitespace` — values taken from what Playwright's matchers actually do (`toHaveTitle`/`toContainText`/`toHaveText` normalize whitespace; `toHaveURL`/`toHaveValue`/`toHaveAttribute` compare raw). Two tables (`ASSERT_SEMANTICS`, `WAIT_SEMANTICS`) plus `WAIT_TO_ASSERT_KIND` mapping the overlap, so "wait until the URL contains X" and "assert the URL contains X" cannot diverge. `textMatchExpr(value, semantics)` builds the `new RegExp(…)` source THREE places need (the generator, `describe-step.ts`, the parity harness). `matchesValue(actual, expected, semantics)` is the comparator; `matchSource()`/`visibilitySource()` return it and `isVisibleByRect` **as source text** via `Function.prototype.toString`, for embedding in the injected replayer — which is why both must be self-contained, a rule `check:step-semantics` enforces by evaluating them in an isolated scope. **URL predicates ignore case, content predicates do not**: a URL's host is case-insensitive by RFC 3986 and the prefill hands back a bare host, while page text changing case is a real change and case-sensitivity is Playwright's own default. **`urlPathIs` ("URL path is") compares the URL's PATH alone** — query string and fragment ignored, one trailing slash tolerated, a missing leading slash supplied — via `urlPathPattern(value)` (RegExp source), `urlPathExpr(value)` (the `toHaveURL` argument as JS source) and `urlPathSource()` (the pattern builder as injectable text, self-contained like `matchesValue`, which is why it INLINES the escape rule instead of calling `reEscape` — `assert-emission.test.ts` pins the two spellings together). The pattern anchors on the URL's structure (`^scheme://host` + path + `/?(?:[?#]|$)`), so the generator, the replayer and `describe-step` apply ONE pattern to ONE string and cannot disagree about how a path is carved out of a URL. It exists because the other three URL kinds compare the FULL URL, and every URL assertion recorded in this app's own store had failed on a `?variant=`/`utm_*` that differed between the recording and the run — see DECISIONS 2026-08-19. 
- `shared/heal-key.mjs` — **how a locator's heal-map key is SPELLED**, and the second file admitted to `shared/` for the same reason as the first. Run-time Auto-Heal tags every Playwright locator with the app's canonical key as it is created (`__glazeKey`) and looks the failing step up by it; two pieces of code build those keys, in different worlds — `healKeyFor` in `playwright-runner.ts` (compiled TypeScript, from the app's `Locator` MODEL, when the map is written) and the factory patches in `heal-fixture-source.ts` (a raw JS string loaded by Playwright's Babel transform, from the ARGUMENTS a factory was called with, at run time). Until element context a key came from ONE factory call, so both sides were a flat switch and drift meant a misspelling. A context-carrying locator is a CHAIN, so the key is COMPOSED and has an order and separators — a grammar, and a transcribed grammar is right the day it is written and silently divergent afterwards. `healKeyWithin`/`healKeyHasText`/`healKeyAnd` are the three operators; `healKeyOperatorSource()` returns them as source text for embedding in the fixture, the same technique as `matchSource()`. The failure they prevent is the quietest in this codebase: nothing throws, no run changes outcome, healing is simply off.- `main/services/panel-dock.ts` — pure dock geometry. `computePanelSpan(browser, workArea)` decides the panel's vertical placement for both `computePanelFollow` and `computeParkedPanel`: the panel is pinned to the browser's TOP edge and shrinks to whatever is left above the work area's bottom, down to `PANEL_MIN_HEIGHT`. It replaced "full browser height, then clamp `y`", which was safe and wrong — a default 820pt browser on an ~870pt work area gave the panel about fifty points of vertical travel, after which it stopped following while the browser kept going. Being flush is what *docked* means, so height is the negotiable part. `computeDock` still clamps, correctly: it MOVES the browser too, so it places the pair rather than chasing it. **`healKeyText(value, exact)` (2026-08-22)** spells the text key — `text|…` for the substring form, `text!|…` for `{ exact: true }` — because they resolve different sets and a heal recorded for one must not apply to the other; embedded into the fixture with the other operators by `healKeyOperatorSource`.
- `shared/overlay-rules.mjs` — **what an overlay dismissal rule IS, and the watcher that enforces one.** In `shared/` for the process boundary the feature is built across: the trainer resolves a rule inside a live Electron page and the run's fixture resolves one inside a Playwright worker, and neither can import the other's module. Carries `OVERLAY_LOCATOR_KINDS` (css/testid/text/role — **no xpath**, which encodes a DOM snapshot rather than an element), `hostMatches` (suffix matching on LABEL boundaries, so a rule covers `www.` but never `evil-ritual.com` — a rule CLICKS THINGS, so leaking one onto a lookalike domain is a liability), `armedRulesFor`, and `watcherSource()`. The watcher is deliberately NOT self-contained: it calls `matchesFor`, which whichever host interpolates it must already define — that is what makes one resolver serve both sides, shadow piercing included. It clicks rather than hides (a hidden banner is still in the a11y tree), never throws, and clicks each ELEMENT at most once via a WeakSet keyed on node identity. `check:overlay-rules` fails if a second copy of the watcher or the resolver appears. See DECISIONS 2026-08-22.
- `shared/popup-presets.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **the Handle pop-ups switch, and the BUILT-IN handlers it arms beside the rules a user taught** (2026-09-01). `DEFAULT_HANDLE_POPUPS` (true — taught rules were always on before the switch existed, and a default of off would turn every one of them silently off), `POPUP_PRESETS` (two entries: `klaviyo-form-close`, `datagrail-consent-close`, each a CSS target scoped to markup only that vendor renders and a label under `MAX_OVERLAY_LABEL`), `normalizeDisabledPresets` (known ids only, deduped, in preset order — an unknown id is dropped rather than kept), `presetRules(disabledIds)` (the presets as armed rules, `host: ""` and `builtIn: true`, ids prefixed `preset:` so a run report can tell them from a stored rule's UUID), `resolveHandlePopups(testValue, settingValue)` (the test's field, else the global default, else the shipped default — the three-layer rule every other per-test run option resolves by) and **`armedPopupRulesFor({ rules, url, handlePopups, disabledPresets })`**, the one function every arming site calls: the app's runner, the trainer's injection, the MCP/CLI runner and `describeRun`. Off returns `[]` — the taught rules included — because a test whose subject is the pop-up has to be able to keep it. **Pure**, for the reason `shared/` exists: three processes arm these, and a transcribed "is handling on" is how a test that turned pop-ups off in the app would still have them clicked away in CI. **The presets do not violate the store's no-typed-targets rule.** That rule refuses a selector nobody saw match; each preset is scoped to its vendor's own container (so it resolves to nothing everywhere else and never touches a site's own dialogs), clicks the vendor's own close control and nothing else (DataGrail's never Accept or Reject — a preset must not make a consent decision on the user's behalf), and is exercised by a real Playwright run against that markup in `e2e/popup-dismissal.spec.ts`, by the capture harness in `main/recorder/popup-presets.dom.test.ts`, and against ritual.com itself by `scripts/probe-popups.mjs` and the `GL_LIVE_SITE`-gated live row. The settings pane, the env transport (`dismissEnv`) and the watcher all read this list, so a new vendor is one entry here plus its fixtures. The switch's storage is `TestRecord.handlePopups` (absent = inherit, in `EXPORTED_FIELDS` and `DUPLICATED_FIELDS`) and `RecorderSettings.defaultHandlePopups` + `disabledPopupPresets`. See DECISIONS 2026-09-01.
- `shared/heal-artifacts.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **what a run's heal map and heal evidence are CALLED**, and the third of the heal trio in `shared/` (`heal-key.mjs` spells the key, `testid-attr.mjs` spells one kind of selector inside it, this spells the file the whole map arrives in). `healMapFileName(runId)` and `healDirName(runId)`. Admitted after R49, which is the same failure the other two exist to prevent, one level up: the runner and the fixture are joined by a FILENAME and by nothing else, so when the MCP/CLI path pointed `GLAZE_HEAL_MAP` at `<testId>.heal.json` — a name no code in this repository has ever written — the fixture installed itself, patched every locator factory and took the `!entry` arm on every action. No error, no heal, no evidence of having tried, and `ran.autoHeal` reporting the capability as present. Keyed by RUN and not by test because two runs of one test can be in flight at once (`run_batch`), and a per-test name means the second overwrites the first's map mid-flight. `check:ci-fixtures` asserts these are the only heal-artifact names outside tests, and — the assertion that would have caught R49 — that a `GLAZE_HEAL` set to anything but `"0"` implies both a map named through here AND a writer for it.
- `shared/dismiss-fixture-source.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — the overlay-dismissal fixture, `glaze-dismiss.mjs`, as source text written beside the specs. It was `main/services/dismiss-fixture-source.ts` until 2026-08-25 and moved the moment `locator-engine.mjs` made it possible, because it had to: **the capture fixture imports it unconditionally**, so from the loader's point of view it is a DEPENDENCY and not a capability — exactly like `glaze-runtime.mjs` is a dependency of a spec that uses a helper. While only the app could write it, every MCP or CLI run with any capability on wrote a `glaze-capture.mjs` whose first imports could not resolve; the spec did not load and Playwright reported "no tests found". It worked on a machine where the app had run, and failed on every fresh CI container. `check:ci-fixtures` now DERIVES the written set from the capture fixture's own import statements rather than transcribing it, which is what missed this: the transcription named four siblings where the source imports five. The watcher it installs (through `context.addInitScript`, so a rule is enforced on every document a navigation produces, not at step boundaries) carries the shared locator engine, so a rule taught in the trainer and a rule enforced in a run resolve through ONE `matchesFor`. `dismissEnv` moved to `dismiss-fixture-names.mjs` with it — three processes write that environment now (the app's runner, the MCP/CLI runner, and the fixture reads it back inside a Playwright worker), and a rule that arms in one and not the other looks exactly like a page with no banner.
- `shared/heal-probe.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **what the page is asked when a step's locator stops resolving.** `buildHealProbeScript(step, pastHints)`: one injected script that walks the DOM with the locator engine, builds a candidate locator for every element that could plausibly be the one the step meant, scores them against the original locator and against what it resolved to in past runs, and returns the best few. It was in `main/services/auto-heal.ts` until 2026-08-25 and came here the moment R51 made it possible: it interpolates the engine and `JSON.stringify(step)` and nothing else. **Candidate ranking is where every Auto-Heal bug so far has lived**, so a second implementation would not fail — it would rank differently, and the symptom of that is a run clicking a plausible wrong element and passing. The trainer, an app run and an unattended run all evaluate this exact string.
- `shared/heal-map.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **the map that makes run-time Auto-Heal do anything at all.** Canonical locator key → the step it belongs to, plus a pre-built probe. The fixture's first line on a failed action is `if (!entry || !entry.probe || …) throw err`, so an absent or mis-keyed map is not a degraded heal, it is no heal — which is exactly R49, where the switch was on and the map named a file nothing wrote. Two processes build it now (the app from a `TestRecord`, the MCP/CLI from the same steps on disk) and one builder is the point: the KEY has to match what the fixture derives from Playwright's factory arguments and the PROBE has to rank the way the trainer does. **`stepLabel` is injected rather than computed** — `describeStep` still lives in `script-generator.ts` beside its own renderer mirror, so the app passes it and an unattended run passes nothing; entries carry an empty label and the fixture falls back to the step id, which puts the gap in the artifact where it is visible instead of in the ranking where it would not be. `main/services/heal-map.test.ts` holds the whole contract, including that the map provides every field the fixture SOURCE is read to consume. **Since PR 3 of preemptive-updates the map can carry `seeds`** — pending cross-test proposals' locators for a key, capped at `MAX_MAP_SEEDS`, which the fixture tries BEFORE spending a probe once the recorded locator has actually failed; a working seed records an ordinary healed event flagged `seeded`, so the journal still shows the page changed and the stored test is untouched. Both map writers pass them (`propagationService.seedsFor` in the app, `seedsForTest` over `propagations.json` in `mcp/run-tests.mjs` — `check:ci-fixtures` pins the CALL, not the import).
- `shared/origin.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **what ORIGIN a URL is on** — `originOf` (URL.origin, so case and default port normalise) and `isOnOrigin` (startsWith plus a boundary check, so `shop.example.commerce.test` never matches `shop.example.com`). Moved out of `main/services/origin-variable.ts` (which re-exports both, so its callers never noticed) because cross-test propagation groups donors and targets by origin and the MCP/CLI runner has to derive the SAME grouping when it seeds a heal map — plain `.mjs`, so the rule could not stay in compiled TypeScript. `originsIn` stayed behind: it walks `mapInterpolatable`, an app-side model.
- `shared/propagation.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **cross-test heal propagation, the pure core** (PR 2 of `docs/plans/preemptive-updates.md`): a locator fix confirmed on one test → which steps in which other tests on the same origin should get it, at what confidence, and which runs to protect meanwhile. `donorsFromJournal` gates the evidence (a REVERTED heal is anti-evidence; a pending run heal counts only when its run PASSED — the same mis-heals-usually-succeed rule `collectRunHeals` applies before writing, read from the other side); `donorFromEdit` shapes a hand-made fix (the strongest kind; hooked by the update handlers and never by the propagation apply path, which is what makes feedback loops impossible). `proposalsFor` joins on `healKeyFor` (nth-insensitive, so an indexed sibling matches), groups by origin preferring the heal's recorded `pageUrl` over the start URL, REFUSES a donor group that disagrees on the fix, respects settled decisions (a dismissed proposal for the same step + from + to is never re-asked), and returns a write plan — create / refresh / supersede / stale / conflicts — the store applies idempotently. Confidence is a sum of NAMED contributions (codes in `REASON_CODES`; the renderer owns the copy, the insights-view rule), with two floors: `PROPOSE_MIN`, and `AUTO_APPLY_MIN` which additionally demands the strong corroboration (`toLocator` among the target fingerprint's own recorded candidates) or ≥2 agreeing donors — and refuses `PROPOSE_ONLY_TYPES` (`assert`, `if`, `capture`: steps that consume the element SILENTLY, where success never proves the element was right). `seedsForTest` hands a run's heal map the pending fixes for keys the test still uses, best-first, capped at `MAX_SEEDS_PER_KEY`. Pure — `now` is an input — and its contract lives in `main/services/propagation.test.ts`, whose rules were each verified by mutation (eleven single-rule deletions, eleven red suites).
- `shared/locator-engine.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **the DOM walk every locator question in this app is answered by**, and the largest thing in `shared/` by some margin. `DOM_HELPERS` (what an element IS: `roleOf`, `accName`, `txt`, `cssEscape`, the shadow walk), `CONTEXT_HELPERS` (`ctxFilter` — narrow a match set by the user's pinned element context), `UNIQUENESS_HELPERS` (`matchesFor` — the elements a recorded locator would actually resolve to under strict mode) and the three scan caps. All source TEXT, ES5-flavoured and closing over nothing, because none of it runs in the process that owns it: each is interpolated into a script some other runtime evaluates — the recorder's isolated world, the injected replayer, the heal probe's `page.evaluate`, the dismissal watcher's init script. It lived in `main/recorder/capture-script.ts` until 2026-08-25, which made it reachable only from the compiled app, and **two features were blocked on precisely that**: standing overlay rules did not run on an MCP or CLI run (a banner the app clicks away stayed up, so a step behind it failed in CI and passed in the app), and run-time Auto-Heal was worse — switched ON for those runs and healing nothing, because the heal map holds a probe script per step built from these strings and nothing could build one (R49). `PICKED_HELPERS` and `CSS_PROPS_HELPER` deliberately did NOT come: they describe a PICKED element for the refine dialog, and the second interpolates `CSS_ASSERT_PROPS` from `main/recorder/types.ts` — moving them would drag an app-owned model across the boundary to serve a caller that does not exist. The extraction changed no emitted byte (capture script, count script, heal probe, replayer and dismiss fixture all hash identically across it); `main/recorder/locator-engine-single-source.test.ts` holds the two properties that matter afterwards — each name is declared in exactly one file and every consumer imports it from here, and the module LOADS under a bare `node` with no loader, alias table or bundler, which is the whole point and the one thing reading its import list cannot prove.
- `shared/script-path.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **where a test's spec is, on the machine asking.** `scriptPath` is stored ABSOLUTE from the machine that recorded the test, and every reader trusted it — so a library copied anywhere else (a CI runner, a restored backup, a `userData` move) resolved through `path.relative` to a `../../../..`-prefixed path back up to the author's home directory. Playwright found no tests and the run reported that as a test failure rather than as a library that did not arrive; **R14's GitHub Action would have shipped a workflow template that cannot work**, which is why R10 blocks it. The rule is the one `testStore.writeScript` already applied before WRITING — only a path inside this scripts dir is honoured — moved here and applied on READ, where all three processes need it. `resolveScriptPath(scriptsDir, record)` returns the stored path when it is usable here (so nothing changes on the authoring machine, and no migration is needed) and the DERIVED position otherwise: `<id>.spec.ts`, or `imported/<id>/…` for an imported test, whose spec relative-imports its siblings and so must keep its position. **A copied bundle's `tests.json` is untrusted input** — it is a file somebody else wrote, exactly like an imported project, and the derived path is joined onto the local scripts dir and then read and EXECUTED as a spec. So `isSafeId` refuses an id that is a path, every derived segment is validated, and the result is re-checked for containment; with both defences removed a `..` tail escapes the scripts directory entirely, which is what `script-path.test.ts` measures. `scriptsDirFor` is there for the same reason one directory up: the app, the MCP server and the CLI each joined `recorder` + `scripts` themselves, and a run resolving a spec against a different directory from the one that wrote it is this same failure one level out.
- `shared/step-line-map.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **which step a spec LINE is.** The step reporter emits `{ event, line, ok }` and deliberately carries no title (it is the only field that could hold page-derived text), so a line number is all a runner gets and turning it into a step index is what makes "the run failed HERE" sayable. The app has a better source for a spec it generated — it knows which line it wrote each step onto — so this is the FALLBACK, counting `await` lines inside the test body. It moved out of `playwright-runner.ts` on 2026-08-26 because the unattended runner needs it too: until then that path wrote `step-reporter.mjs` beside every spec and passed no `--reporter`, so **no marker was ever emitted**, the run record carried neither a label nor an index, and a JUnit report built from it said `exit 1` and nothing else — the thing the emitters were written to replace. Written-but-unwired is R49's shape with the switch removed, and `check:ci-fixtures` now asserts a fixture this path writes is a fixture it loads.
- `shared/testid-attr.mjs` — **how a testid locator's ATTRIBUTE is spelled**, admitted for the same reason as `heal-key.mjs`. The recorder accepts three test-id attributes (`data-testid`, `data-test-id`, `data-test`) as one "testid" kind, but `getByTestId` resolves only `data-testid` — nothing here configures Playwright's `testIdAttribute` — so a locator recorded off either other attribute carries `Locator.attr` and is emitted as `locator('[data-test-id="v"]')`. That selector string is then load-bearing in four worlds: the generator writes it into the spec, `healKeyBase` keys the heal map with `css|<selector>` (which must equal the key the run-time fixture derives from the `locator()` factory's argument — this very string), the heal fixture's `baseFromModel` rebuilds a live locator from it via `testIdSelectorSource()` (the `toString` embedding technique again), and the renderer's three locator renderings (step list, refine dialog, LLM prompts) promise the call the script contains. `testIdOverride()` is the allowlist every emitter guards with — the field reaches the generator from stored JSON and reaches the fixture from a page-controlled probe, so the type is not a check; `parseTestIdSelector()` is the deliberately narrow inverse the spec parser reads the emitted selector back with (a hand-written `[data-testid="v"]` stays css — the relabel would change nothing). The default attribute normalizes to ABSENT in `normalizeLocator`, so one locator never has two spellings and therefore two heal-map keys. The oracle side lives in `matchesForBase` (capture-script.ts): it scans ONLY the recorded attribute, where it used to scan all three for every testid locator — which is how a step recorded off `data-test` was "unique" in the trainer and matched nothing on every run, and how a unique `data-testid` was rejected because another attribute held the same value. Guarded by `check:locator-roundtrip`, `heal-fixture.test.ts`'s key-agreement rows, `locator-uniqueness.dom.test.ts`, and three `e2e/context-parity.spec.ts` rows.
- `shared/frame-ref.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **how an iframe in a locator's frame path is spelled**, the same shape as `testid-attr.mjs` and for the same reason: the generator writes `frameLocator(<selector>)` (via `root()` in `script-generator.ts`), the spec parser reads it back (`parseBuilderAt`), and the renderer's step list renders it (`describe-step.ts`), so two spellings would mean the parser cannot recognise what the generator wrote and a hand edit of a framed step drops it. `frameSelector(ref)` maps a `FrameRef` (`name`/`url`/`testid` → an `iframe[…]` attribute selector; `css` verbatim) and `parseFrameSelector` is its inverse, a fixed point so `check:locator-roundtrip`'s frame rows hold. Pure; the value is quoted by `q()` at emission, never here. See DECISIONS 2026-08-22.
- `shared/basic-auth.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — the ONE rule for which origin a test's basic-auth credential is scoped to. `credentialOrigin(url)` and `answersLoginFor(testUrl, baseUrl, requestUrl)`: an unscoped credential leaks (Playwright's `httpCredentials` without `origin` answers any 401; Electron's `login` event fires for every authenticating request), so both the generated `test.use({ httpCredentials, origin })` and the trainer's `login` handler derive the scope from this one function — they cannot drift into disagreeing about where the password may go. Pure. Guarded by `check:basic-auth`.
- `shared/start-url.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — what a TYPED SITE resolves to. `hasScheme(input)` and `normalizeStartUrl(input)`: the input as typed when it already names `http://`/`https://`, `https://` + input when it does not. Was a private `normalizeUrl` in `recorder-service.ts` until the New recording dialog needed to SHOW the answer as well as obey it (#134) — two copies of "what counts as already having a scheme" would have let the note claim one address while the recorder opened another. **For a recording's START URL only**, which is what the name is for: a `goto` step's URL may legitimately be `baseUrl`-relative, and normalizing `page.goto("/checkout")` would break the test in the one place the failure looks like a product bug. Pure; covered by `main/services/start-url.test.ts` (there, not beside the module, for the reason `branch-paths.test.ts` gives).
- `renderer/lib/start-url-hint.ts` — the DISPLAY half, on the renderer side of that rule. `startUrlHint(typed)` answers with the resolved address, or null when there is nothing worth saying — an empty field, or one that already names its own scheme. Both dialogs that take a typed site read it, so the two cannot disagree about when to speak; and the silence is load-bearing, since no note under `http://localhost:3000` is what tells the user their scheme was not upgraded.
- `main/windows/trainer-panel-window.ts` — Panel lifecycle (modeled on the settings window, which is gone — see docs/plans/settings-view.md) + the follower. `openTrainerPanel`/`closeTrainerPanel`/`dock`/`undock`/`isTrainerPanelOpen`/`isTrainerPanelDocked`/`getTrainerPanelDockState`, and `FOLLOW_EVENTS`. `openTrainerPanel(recWindow, {fixedBrowserWidth})` latches a session-long `preserveBrowserWidth` (set by `recorder-service` when the session has a window-size preset) that EVERY later dock reads — the user can undock and re-dock, and a re-dock that forgot it would shrink the browser the first dock left alone. It also suppresses two things that would then be wrong: `undock` skips giving back width the dock never took (that would push the browser past its preset), and `viewportNarrowed` isn't sent, because nothing narrowed (`move`/`moved`/`resize`/`resized`, attached by iteration). **Reentrancy is the whole risk**: docking resizes the browser → `resize` fires → the panel moves → `move` fires. Three defences, all required — every bounds write goes through `applyBounds` (raises an `applying` flag, records the rectangle), `isDuplicateApply` rejects an event echoing a write we just made (the flag alone loses to an event delivered a tick late), and the follower listens to the BROWSER only, never the panel. mabl shipped this loop; it is silent (nothing throws, the app wedges). Degraded paths: full-screen → auto-undock (re-dock on leave), minimize → hide, no room on the display → the panel opens at `computeParkedPanel`'s rectangle + `trainerPanel:undocked` push. The panel's opening x/y are never left off: an omitted coordinate is centred by the window layer, which is on top of the page. That first state can only be FETCHED (`trainerPanel:getState`) — the push announcing it is emitted while the panel is still loading its page, so a panel that opened undocked never hears it.
- `main/index.ts` — the main window opens **filling the display's work area** (`fillWorkArea`, above); `windowWidth`/`windowHeight` remain only as the fallback for a display query that throws, which is why `check:narrow-layout` still reads them. It is also created **untitled**, and refuses `page-title-updated`. Both halves are needed and neither is obvious: Chromium pushes a document's title up to the window whenever it changes, so `title: ""` survives only until something in the renderer assigns `document.title` (which the entry point did, from the build-time `__APP_DISPLAY_NAME__`), and an empty `<title>` in `main-window.html` survives only until a library sets one. The app's name is already in the menu bar and the Dock; a title bar repeating it above the app's own chrome is noise. Pinned at the source by `check:app-identity` and behaviourally by `e2e/window-title.spec.ts` — jsdom has no window title, so the e2e run is the only place the refusal can be exercised.
- `main/handlers/index.ts` — Registers all `recorder:*`, `tests:*`, `runner:*`, `llm:*` IPC handlers. **No `app:getInfo`**, and the absence is deliberate enough to be worth an entry: the Glaze template shipped one, answering with three literals (`name: "My Glaze App"`, `version: "1.0.0"`, `environment` off `NODE_ENV`, which nothing sets in a packaged Electron build), and it survived the SDK port because a handler with no caller has no symptom. It was deleted on 2026-09-03 together with `main/handlers/app.ts`, the template's whole app-handlers module. Correcting it was the other option and was rejected: the preload exposes a GENERIC `glaze.ipc.invoke(channel, …)` rather than a per-channel surface, so an unused channel has nothing declaring it and nothing calling it — the exact shape that let the first one live — and `renderer/preload.ts`'s own porting rule is that only APIs with real renderer call sites are exposed. Two guards, neither subsuming the other: `main/handlers/handlers.test.ts` asserts the channel is not registered at RUNTIME (catching a re-add however it is spelled), and `check:app-identity` scans SOURCE for the template's name, the channel, and any stated app version. See DECISIONS 2026-09-03.
- `main/services/llm/types.ts` — LLM types: `LlmProvider` (`"ollama"|"lmstudio"`), `LlmModel`, `LlmMessage`, `LlmProviderStatus`, `LlmConfig`, `LlmChatParams`. `LlmModel.loaded` is **three-state**: `true` (in the provider's memory), `false` (downloaded, not loaded), `undefined` (the provider doesn't report it — Ollama, Claude, LM Studio before 0.3.6). `undefined` must never render as "not loaded"; mirrored in `renderer/lib/llm-types.ts`.
- `main/services/llm-config-store.ts` — persists provider/model/baseUrl overrides to `userData/recorder/llm-config.json` (default provider `ollama`, model `null`). **Three role slots since Phase 3 (2026-08-23):** `roles.chat` / `roles.instant` / `roles.autocomplete`, each `{ provider, model }`, merged PER KEY by `set()` (a patch naming one role keeps the others; `null` clears one — `LlmConfigPatch`). The chat slot and the flat pair are kept in step in both directions, so older readers and the MCP see one answer. Only chat is seeded: an absent instant slot FOLLOWS chat (`resolveSlot` in `llm-service.ts` falls back to the flat pair), which the pane spells as "Same as chat"; an absent autocomplete slot is off. A hosted autocomplete slot is dropped on read and on write (`normalizeRoles`) — see `check:editor-egress`.
- `main/services/ts-service/` — **the TypeScript service behind the Script IDE (Phase 4, 2026-08-23).** `core.ts` — the language service and the app's inspections; imports `typescript` and `node:path` only, so it runs the same in the child, under Vitest and in `check:ts-service`. The document is a virtual file beside the runner's `node_modules` (so `@playwright/test` resolves to the bundled CLI's `.d.ts`), the generated runtime helper a second virtual file handed in as text; `compilerOptions` are strict ES2022/ESNext/Bundler with `allowJs` for the helper. `diagnostics`/`completions` (capped at 200)/`hover`/`inspections` answer in character offsets. The inspections walk the AST with the checker: `missing-await` (a call statement whose type is a Promise; fix inserts `await`), `no-wait-for-timeout` (fix removes the statement), `no-force-without-reason` (quiet with a comment on the line or the one above; fix removes the property and its comma), `unwrapped-statement` (a `page.`/`expect(` statement directly in the `test` body and not inside `test.step`; fix wraps it with a title from `stepTitleFor`), `raw-css-locator` and `index-pinned-locator` (hints, no fix). `child.ts` — the `utilityProcess` entry: `process.parentPort` under Electron, Node IPC when forked by plain Node; `typescript` resolved at runtime from `--node-modules=`. `client.ts` — forks `build/main/ts-service.js` (path from the main bundle's `import.meta.url`), matches replies by id with a 10 s timeout, replays open documents into a restarted child, gives up after three exits, and reports `status()` — unavailable is a state the bar shows, never an error. `protocol.ts` — the request/response types. `scripts/build-main.mjs` has a third entry for the child with `typescript` external; `typescript` moved from devDependencies to dependencies for it (verify-package's closure picks it up).
- `shared/inspections.mjs` — the inspection catalogue: ids, labels, summaries, severities, whether a fix exists, `defaultInspections()` (all on) and `normalizeInspections()`. Read by `core.ts` (the rule ids), the settings store (`RecorderSettings.inspections`, merged per rule on `set()`), `settings-schema.ts` (one row per rule, all keyed `inspections`) and the Inspections pane.
- `main/services/llm/fim.ts` — fill-in-the-middle request shapes for ghost text: Ollama's `/api/generate` with `prompt` + `suffix` (the server applies the model's own template), LM Studio's `/v1/completions` with the template chosen by `fimTemplateFor(model)` (starcoder, deepseek, codellama, then the qwen/coder family — order matters, a starcoder id contains "coder"), and `trimCompletion`. `llmService.fim` resolves the autocomplete slot, refuses when none is set or when it is hosted, and goes through the same egress redaction as chat.
- `main/services/llm-service.ts` — provider client for Ollama / LM Studio / Anthropic. `status(provider)` probes each (local: `GET /api/tags` or `/v1/models`; Anthropic: `hasKey` then `GET /v1/models`); `detect()` probes the local pair only; never throws — returns `{reachable,models,error?,hasKey?}`. `chat(params)` branches by provider: local → OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions`; Anthropic → `/v1/messages` (see Integrations). Both stream SSE `data:` lines and push `llm:chunk`/`llm:done`/`llm:error` via `sendToMain`; returns a `requestId`; `cancel(requestId)` aborts via a per-request `AbortController`. Local base URLs are loopback to avoid the macOS local-network prompt. For LM Studio, `fetchLmStudioLoadState` additionally probes **LM Studio's own REST API** (`GET /api/v0/models`, 0.3.6+) for each model's `state` and stamps `LlmModel.loaded` — a separate, failure-tolerant call on purpose: `/v1/models` remains the source of truth for which models exist and whether the provider is reachable, so an older LM Studio (404) or a different OpenAI-compatible server on the port costs a missing badge rather than an empty list or a false "not connected". Any `state` other than `loaded`/`not-loaded` leaves `loaded` unset rather than guessing. **Authentication:** LM Studio's server can be configured to require a bearer token, and when it is, EVERY route 401s — so `localAuthHeaders(provider)` stamps `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on all three LM Studio calls (`/v1/models`, `/api/v0/models`, `/v1/chat/completions`); no stored token means no header, which is the correct request for the default unauthenticated setup.
