A 401/403 from `/v1/models` is thrown as a `ProviderError` so `status()` keeps the decoded sentence instead of overwriting it with "make sure it is running" — the server *is* running. `status()` also returns `hasToken` for LM Studio (`undefined` elsewhere). **Since 2026-08-18 the provider round trip is extracted** (`streamChatOnce`, sink-based) so two consumers share one parser: `chat()` keeps the exact streaming event semantics (its synchronous prefix is load-bearing — the no-model `llm:error` and the `activeRequests` registration both land before `chat()` returns), and **`complete(params, {timeoutMs, signal?})`** collects the same stream in-process for MAIN-side callers (the insights report) — `sendToMain` drops every event silently when no window exists, which is exactly the situation an unattended job runs in. `complete()` never enters `activeRequests` (so `llm:cancel` can't reach it and **`activeCount()`** stays an interactive-only count for the insights busy-guard), enforces a real timeout via its own AbortController (chat has never had one), inherits the empty-stream-is-an-error rule, excludes reasoning deltas from `text`, and rejects with an `LlmCompletionError` carrying the same `LlmErrorKind` vocabulary as `llm:error`. The extraction also fixed a real bug the refactor's tests caught: the Anthropic branch never fed `sawContent`, so every successful Claude stream delivered its chunks and then ended in `empty-response` instead of `done`.
- `main/services/encrypted-secret-store.ts` — `createEncryptedSecretStore({fileName, label})`: one safeStorage-encrypted secret under `userData/recorder/`, with the atomic tmp+rename write and the in-process decrypted cache. The ENOENT-vs-other split in `get()` is load-bearing — a decryption failure is not cached, because caching a recoverable keychain state makes it permanent for the session. Backs both credential stores below.
- `main/services/mailbox-store.ts` — the test mailbox behind the `emailCode` step: one endpoint and one bearer token for the whole library, split across `mailbox.json` (PLAINTEXT register: endpoint, host, savedAt — **never the token**) and `mailbox-token.bin` (encrypted). The split is the Shopify signature store's, for the same reason: without the register the standalone MCP cannot tell "no mailbox configured" from "one is, and I cannot read it". Three states, and `unreadable` is NEVER collapsed into `none` — a run that goes without a code because the keychain was locked fails as a login timing out, which reads as a flaky test. Write order is asymmetric (register first when adding, blob first when removing) so a partial failure always leaves the VISIBLE state, and `credentials()` requires BOTH halves — half a configuration would send an empty Authorization header, which reads as a rejected token rather than a missing one. The endpoint is deliberately NOT treated as a credential: it is guarded by the token, the Worker fails closed without one, and a run that cannot say which host it polled is one nobody can debug. Re-validated on READ, so a hand-edited register cannot put an endpoint the app would refuse into a run's environment.
- `main/services/mailbox-service.ts` — `probeMailbox()`, behind the Test button in Settings → Integrations. It asks the endpoint for `probe@example.com` (RFC 2606, so it cannot collide with a real mailbox) and maps each answer to something actionable: 401/403 is the token, 503 is a Worker with no secret or KV binding, 404 is a URL missing its `/messages`, a 200 that is not a message list is a URL pointing at something else. It proves reachability, the token and the shape — **never mail routing**, since no mail was sent, and the UI says so. It exists because every other way to find out is "record a test, run it, watch a sign-in time out sixty seconds later", at which point three different causes look identical.
- `main/services/anthropic-key-store.ts` — the Anthropic API key (`getKey`/`setKey`/`clear`/`hasKey`) over `encrypted-secret-store`. Key never leaves the backend.
- `main/services/lm-studio-token-store.ts` — LM Studio's API token (`getToken`/`setToken`/`clear`/`hasToken`), same mechanism and same write-only contract. Needed because LM Studio can require a bearer token, and without one the provider is unusable rather than degraded.
- `main/services/github-token-store.ts` — the GitHub token the branch switcher lists pull requests with, same mechanism and same write-only contract as the two above.
- `main/services/proxy-password-store.ts` — the manual proxy's password (`get`/`set`/`clear`/`has`) over `encrypted-secret-store`, same write-only contract as the key stores above. It is the ONE half of the proxy configuration that is a secret: everything else lives in plain `recorder-settings.json` where the MCP server can read it, and `normalizeProxyUrl` refuses credentials inside the URL precisely so this split cannot be quietly undone by pasting `user:pass@host`.
- `main/services/proxy-service.ts` — applies Settings → Proxy (see the pane, and `shared/proxy-config.mjs` for the rules) to every place the app touches the network, and validates it on demand. Three surfaces: the app's own Node-side requests go through **`appFetch`** — plain global fetch until the settings say otherwise, then undici's fetch with a `ProxyAgent`/`Socks5ProxyAgent` dispatcher (cached per configuration, closed on change; **undici is imported dynamically**, same shape as metrics-store's `node:sqlite` and for the bundling half of the same reason — it is CJS, and a static import in an esbuild ESM bundle throws `Dynamic require of "node:assert"` at module load in every `check:*` bundle that lacks the app build's `createRequire` banner); Chromium sessions (the training browser's per-recording partition via `applyTestSessionProxy`, the default session — which carries the renderer's site-icon fetches — at boot and on every settings change) get `setProxy` plus a certificate proc that, with SSL Verify off, trusts everything EXCEPT loopback; and test runs get `runProxyEnv()` → the shared `playwrightProxyEnv` rule with the decrypted password added, which only this process can do. Automatic mode resolves the OS proxy **per URL** through `session.resolveProxy` (PAC files included) and drives undici with the answer — `parsePacProxies` maps Chromium's resolver grammar, dropping entry types undici cannot drive rather than guessing. **Loopback never proxies** (`isLoopbackHost`): Chromium bypasses it implicitly, undici has no implicit rules, and a manual proxy handed the local Ollama would refuse or misroute it. Proxy auth is answered in two places, both here: `app.on("login")` for webContents traffic, and the per-request `login` event on the `net.request` the test-traffic validator makes — credentials exist in manual mode only. The two validators (`verifyAppConnectivity`, `verifyTestConnectivity`) never throw: a failure is the answer, returned as `{ok, url, via, detail}` where `via` names how the connection was actually made, so a green check can't silently mean the proxy was never in the path. The decision core (`decideAppProxy`) takes the settings, the resolver and the password reader as arguments, which is what makes the rule testable without Electron (`proxy-service.test.ts`). The browser installer's download is APP traffic (the app fetching its own tooling), so `downloadProxyEnv()` hands it the conventional `HTTPS_PROXY` variables in manual mode. **Optional**: a public repository lists open PRs unauthenticated. A private one does not, and GitHub answers **404 rather than 403** for a repository a request can't see — so without a token the failure reads as "no such repository", which is why the error text names the token explicitly.
- **Issue tracker** (`main/services/issue-tracker/`) — sending a defect to Linear **or GitHub Issues**. Which one is a user setting (Settings → Integrations), stored in `issue-config-store` and read per call; the compose dialog deliberately has **no picker**, because filing a defect is a moment someone is looking at a failure and a destination question there is one more thing to get wrong on the way.
  - `renderer/components/issue-compose-dialog.tsx` — the compose form. Its load effect depends on **`defectSourceKey(source)`, never on `source`** (2026-08-25, #121): all three callers build that object inline during render and all three re-render when a run finishes, and the effect's first act is to BLANK THE FORM — so a Routine running in the background used to discard a half-typed report every few seconds. The key is an exhaustive switch over `DefectSource` (including `scope`, since a widened accessibility send builds a different draft from the same anchor), so a new kind that went unspelled is a type error rather than two defects sharing a draft. Fixed here rather than by memoizing the callers: a caller that forgets is invisible, and the next one added would forget again.
  - `types.ts` — the `IssueProvider` contract and the `IssueProviderError` class; re-exports the wire shapes from `renderer/lib/issue-types.ts`. The interface is designed against **two** APIs rather than one, because an interface derived from a single example becomes that example renamed: every decision is one that survives Linear (GraphQL, UUID + human identifier, labels as node ids, team → project, upload-then-reference) **and** GitHub Issues (REST, repo-scoped integers, labels as strings, repo → milestone, markdown-embedded assets). Hence: methods express intent never a route; containers are named by the provider (`ProviderVocabulary`) not by this file; containers nest exactly two levels; and providers are **stateless with respect to the credential** — the key is a parameter, so no provider instance holds a decrypted key for the life of the process. **Both are now implemented, and the bet mostly paid**: `createIssue`, `addComment` and `verify` took GitHub with no change at all. Two places it did not, which is what the second provider actually cost — `listSubContainers`/`listLabels` gained a `containerId` (Linear scopes neither, GitHub scopes both, and no interface taste removes that disagreement), and the vocabulary gained `supportsImageUpload` and `containerPlural`. Neither is an escape hatch: the first is a narrowing hint both providers answer honestly, one by using it and one by ignoring it; the second two are facts about a provider that the UI has to state before a send, not behaviour it has to branch on.
  - `linear-provider.ts` — `verify` / `listContainers` / `listSubContainers` over Linear's GraphQL API, with an injectable `fetch` for tests. **No error may carry the key**: a raw fetch rejection can carry the request and a raw body is written by a remote server, so every throw is an `IssueProviderError` built from a status code plus at most a scrubbed, flattened, 300-char GraphQL message. Personal API keys go in `Authorization` **raw, with no `Bearer` prefix** (OAuth tokens use it) — getting that wrong produces a 400 that reads as "your key is bad" and sends people off to regenerate a working key. Linear reports a bad key as a **200 with an `errors` array**, so a status-code check alone misreports it as a parse failure. Remote lists are rebuilt, not trusted; a project is attributed to a team only when it belongs to exactly one, since a Linear project may span teams.
  - `issue-tracker-service.ts` — what the IPC handlers call. The judgement it encodes: **"a key is saved" and "the key works" are different claims and the UI must make both**. `status()` is local and never touches the network; `verify()` is the only thing that does; the verified account is cached **in process and never persisted**, because a claim about right now read off disk at launch is how a revoked key keeps saying Connected. `connect()` **saves before it verifies and does not roll back on failure** — a good key pasted on a dead network should not have to be pasted again. `disconnect()` clears the defaults with the key, since a team id is only meaningful inside the workspace that key opened.
  - `provider-registry.ts` — provider → `{provider, key store}`. A table rather than a direct import so callers already ask "which provider?".
  - `linear-token-store.ts` — the Linear key over `encrypted-secret-store`, same write-only contract as the three above. One key means one workspace, by decision; the filename is provider-specific, so a second provider is a second store rather than a migration — which is exactly what `github-issue-token-store.ts` turned out to be.
  - `provider-registry.ts` — the table of providers and where each keeps its key, plus `PROVIDER_IDS` and `isProviderId`. **Owns the runtime list**: `renderer/lib/issue-types.ts` declares the union type but holds no array, because it is imported type-only by the main process and a `const` there would be the first runtime import to cross that boundary. `isProviderId` guards both untrusted entrances — the IPC boundary and the JSON file on disk — since the value indexes the registry directly.
  - `payload.ts` — **PURE, and the single place the outgoing body is decided.** Pure for the reason `buildAlertPayload` is: it makes the guarantee checkable, and `check:issue-payload` constructs hostile inputs by hand and asserts against the returned object. Four properties are pinned independently: the raw Playwright log NEVER appears (only `errorSignature`'s reduction of its first line); console lines appear for a FAILURE only and only the errors the loader already narrowed to; network entries appear for a failure only and **only when the run had its headers filtered**; header values never appear at all, filtered or not — `FailureRequest` has nowhere to put them, which is stronger than remembering to strip them. `line()` also neutralises backticks, which covers two escapes at once: closing a code span to open a link, and ending the fence the error signature is rendered in.
  - `defect-loader.ts` — the impure half: reads the evidence off disk and hands the builder plain data. Owns two rules that fail silently if forgotten. The RAW RUN LOG never leaves this file — `readLog` is called for exactly one purpose and only the failure LINE continues onward. And network entries are dropped **whole** when `RunLogs.headersFiltered` is false, rather than narrowed: a run captured with `GLAZE_RECORD_ALL_HEADERS=1` holds real Authorization and Cookie values, and not reading them is stronger than filtering them. `readImages` re-reads approved images by filename at send time, bounded twice — `readShot` refuses a traversing name, and this refuses a name the draft never offered.
  - `issue-link-store.ts` — which defects already have issues, keyed **without the run id**. That is the design: a visual difference or an a11y violation reappears every run, so a run-keyed link would report "not filed" every time and produce one duplicate per run. `stepId` is stable across runs. `kind` and `ruleId` are in the key because one step can carry a visual change AND several a11y violations at once. **One derivation (`keyFor`) serves both the writer and the reader** — it had two, they used separators that rendered identically and were not equal, and the symptom was a link that saved fine and could never be found again.
  - `github-provider.ts` — the same contract over GitHub's REST API. Inherits the key-never-in-an-error rule and deliberately goes further: **no remote text is displayed at all**, because GitHub's status codes say what happened where Linear's 200-with-`errors` does not. Three things are specific to it. The container id is `owner/repo` and therefore **a URL path segment** — the one id in this integration that is also a route, arriving from a remote list, through disk as a default, back over IPC — so `repoPath` re-validates it at the point of use and refuses before any request is made. A **403 is two different answers** (throttled vs. forbidden) and only `x-ratelimit-remaining` separates them; reporting the wrong one sends someone to regenerate a working token. And **there is no image upload**: GitHub's attachment endpoint is browser-only, every documented alternative means publishing the screenshots somewhere public, so the provider declares `supportsImageUpload: false`, the dialog warns before the send, and the issue body names what stayed behind. A request also **must** carry a User-Agent or GitHub answers 403 to everything, which maps onto "your token is bad".
  - `github-issue-token-store.ts` — the GitHub token issues are filed with. **Separate from `main/services/github-token-store.ts`, which is the branch switcher's**, and the separation is load-bearing: `disconnect()` clears the provider's key, so sharing one would silently stop the branch switcher listing pull requests in another window; and the scopes differ (listing PRs is read-only and works unauthenticated on a public repo, filing an issue needs write). The cost is pasting two tokens, which is visible and explainable; the alternative failure is neither.
  - `issue-config-store.ts` — **which** tracker is active, plus default container/sub-container **per provider** (`userData/recorder/issue-tracker-config.json`), mirroring `llm-config-store`. Keyed by provider because a Linear team id means nothing to GitHub. NOT in `RecorderSettings`: these are opaque ids minted by a remote API that stop meaning anything when the key is removed, and a settings key that silently means nothing lies in the "differs from default" count. Values are **rebuilt** on read (bounded, string-only) for the same reason `normalizeRawStep` is — this is JSON on disk that a later feature sends back out in a create request. `undefined` leaves a field alone, `null` clears it; collapsing the two makes clearing impossible to express. The **active provider lives here rather than in `RecorderSettings`** even though it is exactly the kind of value that pane holds, because it is meaningless apart from the per-provider ids beside it — choosing GitHub and reading a Linear team id is the failure this file exists to prevent, and splitting them puts them in two files that can disagree. Only an EXPLICIT choice is written, so `DEFAULT_PROVIDER` can change later without overriding somebody's decision, and the choice **survives a disconnect**: removing a key does not mean "go back to the other tracker".
- `shared/deep-link.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — `goodlooks://` parsing, and **the whole validation surface**. In `shared/` because the Electron shell receives the URL and the renderer routes it, and a transcribed copy of the rule is right the day it is written. A deep link is UNTRUSTED INPUT in the same sense as a branch name from a PR head ref: it sits in a Linear issue anyone can edit and is clicked by whoever opens it. It SELECTS A VIEW and nothing else — never runs, writes or deletes. **Dot segments are rejected on the RAW STRING, before `new URL()`**, because the URL parser RESOLVES `..` rather than preserving it (`goodlooks://test/t/run/..` parses to `/t/`): a validator inspecting parsed segments can never see a traversal, because by then it has been applied and silently changed which target the link resolves to. Guarded by `check:deep-link`.
- `main/shell/deep-link.ts` — protocol registration and `open-url`. In `main/shell/` because it imports `electron`. Registered at MODULE SCOPE: on macOS the `open-url` that launched the app fires before `whenReady`, so a listener attached later misses the click that started everything and a cold start opens on the home screen. The target is held and replayed to the first window, for the same reason.
- **Branch switcher** (a testing tool for whoever is building this app, not a product feature — Electron only):
  - `shared/branch-paths.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — **the whole security surface**, and in `shared/` because both the app and `scripts/switch-branch.mjs` need it and cannot share a `.ts`. A branch name is untrusted input in the same sense as a recorded step: it arrives as a PR's head ref, which anyone who can open a PR chooses, and it becomes both a **git argument** and a **directory name**. `branchNameProblem` refuses a leading `-` (execFile spawns no shell, so this is not injection — but `git fetch --upload-pack=…` runs what it names, and quoting cannot help), anything outside `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`, `..`, empty segments, and the names git itself rejects. `worktreeDirFor` slugs the name **plus a hash of the full name** (so `feat/login` and `feat-login` cannot collide into one build) and **throws** rather than falling back if the result escapes the builds root. `branchFromArgv` re-validates on the way back IN, because a command line is not a trusted channel just because we usually write it.
  - `main/services/branch-repo.ts` — reads the repository: the **primary worktree** (`git worktree list` reports it first from anywhere, which is how "get me home" is answerable from disk instead of from remembered state), `origin` as owner/repo, HEAD, and `origin`'s branches from local remote-tracking refs.
  - `main/services/github-prs.ts` — open PRs from api.github.com. **The app's second outbound host** and the first it reaches without being told a URL; see DECISIONS 2026-08-09. A PR whose head ref the validator refuses is dropped and logged, never offered.
  - `main/services/branch-switcher.ts` — the gate (`status()` never throws: an unavailable feature has to be able to say *why*, and a rejected status call renders as a broken view), the spawn of `scripts/switch-branch.mjs` with **`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`**, progress streaming on `branches:progress`, and the relaunch. `app.relaunch` rather than spawning a replacement, because Electron starts the new instance only after this one exits — two live copies would share one data directory, and the stores are read-modify-write JSON with no locking. The consequence is that the new instance inherits this one's ENVIRONMENT, which is why the branch travels in **argv** (`--gl-branch=`) and why every relaunch carries `--gl-no-dev-url`.
  - `renderer/main/branches-view.tsx` (route `/branches`) + `renderer/lib/branch-types.ts` — the wire types, declared once and imported **type-only** by the main side rather than mirrored the way `recorder-types.ts` is; six interfaces are small enough to share outright and `import type` erases at build time.
  - Guarded by `check:branch-switch` (which runs the real script against hostile branch names), `branch-paths.test.ts`, `branch-switcher.test.ts` and `branches-view.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/lib/api.ts` — Typed `window.glazeAPI.glaze.ipc` wrappers + `api.on(channel, cb)` for push events.
- `renderer/theme/shell/rail-flyout.tsx` — `RailFlyout`, the menu that flies out sideways from a rail row. **Portalled to `document.body` and positioned `fixed` from the trigger's rect**, because `SplitView` renders the sidebar inside `overflow-hidden`: a panel absolutely positioned in the rail renders, has a size, sits in the accessibility tree, and is almost entirely off screen — the exact class of bug jsdom cannot see. A portal also sidesteps the `transform`/`filter`-creates-a-containing-block version of the same trap, which matters in an app that paints atmosphere overlays. `rail-flyout.test.tsx` pins the part jsdom CAN observe — the panel is not a descendant of the rail — and placement is checked by looking at the running app. Hover carries an intent delay (so dragging past the row doesn't flash a menu over what you were reaching for) and a close grace period; ArrowRight/ArrowDown open it and move into the items, ArrowLeft/Escape close and return focus to the row, and Enter is deliberately left to the row's own click. The panel repeats the wrapper's pointer handlers as belt-and-braces only: React propagates portal events along the React tree, so the wrapper already sees them — deleting the panel's copies leaves every test green, which is how that was established.
- `renderer/main/branch-menu.tsx` — the rows inside that flyout, purely presentational over a `BranchMenuModel`. A row is **two sibling buttons**, the branch and its pull request, never a button nested in a button — nesting is invalid HTML and browsers resolve it by dropping the inner control, so the icon would render and do nothing on a surface where "nothing happened" is the expected result of hovering. The PR icon calls `nativeShell().openExternal` with the API's own `html_url`; it constructs no URL, which is why no github.com string exists in the renderer for `check:renderer-egress` to weigh in on. Covered by `branch-menu.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/lib/branch-menu.ts` — pure rules for the sidebar's branch hover menu (`buildBranchMenu`, `BRANCH_MENU_LIMIT`), covered by `branch-menu.test.ts` in the **node** project. Here rather than in the flyout because a hover surface is the worst place to debug an ordering rule. **"Most recently created" is really most recently updated**: git records no branch creation time — a ref is a file holding a sha, and nothing survives a rebase, force-push or fresh clone that could reconstruct one — so the tip's committer date is the signal, which is what `listOriginBranches` already sorts by. Encodes: the pinned row is labelled from `status.checkoutBranch` (never guessed as "main") and is excluded from the five so one destination never gets two rows; **the running branch is guaranteed a row**, displacing the oldest of the five rather than being appended, because "what am I running?" is the first question the menu answers and the count is the design's; hidden branches are counted **by name**, since a `current` row that was never a candidate makes a length subtraction understate the remainder; PRs match head refs exactly (refs are case-sensitive) and `pulls: undefined` — loading, failed, rate-limited, or a non-GitHub remote — uniformly means no icons.
- `renderer/lib/native-shell.ts` — the one typed accessor for `glazeAPI.shell` (`showItemInFolder`, `openExternal`). Separate from `api.ts`, which is this app's own IPC surface, where these are host capabilities underneath it. It existed twice before — a local `interface NativeShell` in `library-sidebar.tsx` and a differently-shaped inline type in `stats-view.tsx`, each describing whatever its own file called — and `openExternal` is the method where guessing the contract costs something: the main side refuses a URL it dislikes and the preload swallows the rejection, so a caller working from a wrong type sees nothing happen with nowhere to look.
- `renderer/lib/use-llm-chat.ts` — `useLlmChat()` hook: calls `api.llm.chat`, subscribes to `llm:chunk/done/error` filtered by the returned `requestId` (a ref, so late/stale events from a previous request are ignored), accumulates streamed text. Exposes `{content, status, error, start, stop}`.
- `renderer/lib/llm-prompts.ts` — `buildDebugMessages(ctx)`: the shared persona/prompt-construction pipeline for LLM features. System prompt hard-codes this app's actual spec conventions (prefer `getByRole/getByTestId/getByLabel/getByPlaceholder/getByText` over raw `locator()`, no `waitForTimeout`, web-first `expect(...).toBeVisible/toContainText` — mirrors `script-generator.ts`), a bad/good locator example, and an output-format rule (plain text, no markdown — the response renders in a `<pre>`, not a markdown viewer). User message adds dynamic context: `imported` (`test.sourceDir` set → hand-authored script, locator conventions may not apply) and `speed` (maps to the run's `slowMo` delay via a local `SLOW_MO_MS` mirror of `playwright-runner.ts` — flags whether timing/race causes are more or less likely). Keeps the existing head-truncate script (6000 chars) / tail-truncate output (8000 chars, errors are usually at the end). Also exports `buildGenerateMessages(ctx)` for whole-spec generation by prompt (single fenced ```ts block, whose system prompt additionally pins the vocabulary `spec-parser.ts` can read BACK — one self-contained `await page.<builder>(…).<action>(…)` per statement, no locator variables, no refinement chains, no `test.step` wrappers, no data extraction or branching, every `expect()` subject a locator or `page` — while explicitly keeping comments and `console.log` welcome, since the parser consumes both without counting a skip; guarded by `llm-prompts.test.ts`, which parses a spec written to those rules and asserts every action becomes a step with nothing skipped), `buildGenerateStepsMessages(ctx)` for the trainer's AI-steps flow — a `GENERATE_STEPS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` documenting the exact `Step[]`/locator/assert JSON schema so the model emits a fenced ```json array of editable steps, including the locator's `ctx` context object (`within`/`withinHasText`/`and`, with the same rules the validator enforces: `withinHasText` needs `within`, context locators carry no `ctx` of their own, and it is only for containers the description itself names) — and `buildStepDebugMessages(ctx)` for per-step trainer Console debugging (focused single-step diagnosis, asks for prose + an optional corrected-expression ```ts snippet, not a whole-file replacement). Also exports `locatorToPrompt(l)` (Playwright-style expression from a `Locator`, **including `.nth(k)` and the pinned `ctx` chain** — dropping either showed the model a locator that could not produce the failure being diagnosed: the missing index made a refined locator's zero match count read as "the element is gone" rather than "the index is past the end", and the missing context chain hid the container/hasText clauses whose failure is the commonest reason a chained step fails, while the bare target's match counts contradicted the error. Rendering is delegated to `locatorExpr` in `renderer/lib/describe-step.ts` — the step list's mirror of the generator's `locatorExpr`, so the renderer holds ONE copy of the chain rule — and is pinned twice: `describe-mirror.test.ts` diffs the two functions, and `main/services/__tests__/locator-prompt-parity.test.ts` pins the prompt rendering against the line `generateSpec` actually emits — see DECISIONS 2026-08-21). **The assert-kind schema is GENERATED from `ASSERT_KINDS` + `ASSERT_SEMANTICS`, not transcribed** — the prose copy had drifted to 15 of 17 kinds while `parse-llm-response.ts` kept a third copy that omitted three more and SILENTLY DROPPED any step using one, so the prompt told the model to emit `urlEndsWith` and the validator deleted it. Both now derive from the runtime lists in `renderer/lib/recorder-types.ts`, and `renderer/lib/llm-knowledge.test.ts` asserts the property that matters: every kind the prompt offers survives the validator. The step-debug prompt also carries the trainer's OWN failure modes (strict-mode ambiguity, occlusion, missing select option, absent attribute) written for the trainer's error text rather than Playwright's — its strict-mode error prints no `aka` alternatives, so the run-level guidance could not be copied across.
- `renderer/lib/parse-llm-response.ts` — `parseResponse(text)` tokenizes a (possibly still-streaming) markdown-ish response into `text`/`code` segments (unterminated trailing fence → `closed:false` so partial streamed code still renders); `extractCorrectedScript(text)` returns the largest *closed* code block containing both `import` and `test(` (a plausibly-complete applyable spec) or `null`. `extractStepsJson(text)` pulls a JSON array (prefers a fenced ```json block, else the widest `[...]` span), parses it, and validates each entry against the `StepType`/`AssertKind`/`LocatorKind` enums (dropping invalid), returning `RawStep[]` or `null` — used by the trainer's AI-steps flow. Locators are rebuilt field-by-field by `validateLocator`/`validateLocatorContext`, mirroring `normalizeLocator`'s rules (never a spread; the testid `attr` override validated by `shared/testid-attr.mjs`'s `testIdOverride`, default-attribute spellings dropped to absent; one level of `ctx` — a context locator's own `ctx` is dropped, not recursed into; `withinHasText` dropped without `within`; an empty context parsed as absent) so an AI-proposed step can carry element context ("the Save button inside the Billing dialog") without loosening the shape discipline. Pure, no React.
- `renderer/main/ai-debug-panel.tsx` — `AiDebugDialog`: calls `buildDebugMessages` with the test name/url/script/output plus `imported`/`speed` (passed down from `test-detail-view.tsx`), auto-sends on open (keyed by `testName:output.length` so it won't re-fire on every render), renders the streamed response as parsed segments (prose + `CodeBlock` cards) in a `Dialog size="xl"` with Stop/Regenerate/Copy controls. On `status==="done"`, if `extractCorrectedScript(content)` is non-null and an `onApplyScript` prop is supplied, shows an "Apply to script" button (`AlertDialog` confirm) that calls `onApplyScript` → `test-detail-view.tsx` runs `tests:updateScript` + invalidates `["script",id]`/`["test",id]`; toasts success/failure, disables after apply. Errors from the backend (e.g. "No model selected.", unreachable server) get an appended hint to open Settings (⌘,). **Neither dialog renders a close "X"** (2026-08-17): dismissal routes to `minimize`, so the corner holds one control — minimize, via the `headerActions` slot — instead of two buttons doing the same thing under different names. Discard stays in the description row, where an accidental click is unlikely. **Also exports `StepAiDebugDialog`** — a leaner sibling for per-step trainer Console debugging (no "Apply to script", just diagnosis + copyable snippet), driven by `buildStepDebugMessages`; `CodeBlock`, `ModelPicker`, `friendlyError` are exported for its use.
- `renderer/main/generate-test-dialog.tsx` — `GenerateTestDialog`: opens from the sidebar + menu ("Generate from prompt"). Pre-selectable common options (test name, starting URL, execution speed via `SegmentedControl`, browser viewport via `Select`, from the shared `renderer/lib/viewport-presets.ts` list, and the **model**). The model picker is a **per-generation override**, seeded from `llm:getConfig` and passed explicitly on `llm:chat` — never written back to the config, since persisting it would silently retarget the AI debug panel and step generation too. A configured model missing from the provider's list is replaced (preferring one already loaded) rather than sent to fail mid-generation. When the provider reports load state (LM Studio), the native menu groups by it — "Loaded" / "Not loaded" / "Load state unknown", with SF Symbol icons and `sublabel`s — and a `Badge` beside the trigger carries the selected model's state in the DOM, which is also the only part assertable in jsdom (native-menu options never render). A cold selection gets an explicit warning that the first request stalls while the model loads, because from this dialog that is indistinguishable from a hang. Covered by `generate-test-dialog.test.tsx`. Shows prompt-formatting tips in a bordered callout, a `Textarea` for the prompt, a Generate button that calls `buildGenerateMessages` + `useLlmChat.start`, streams the response with the same `parseResponse`/`CodeBlock` rendering as the AI debug panel, and on `status==="done"` if `extractCorrectedScript(content)` is non-null shows a "Create test" button that calls `api.tests.createFromPrompt` (persists a new `TestRecord` with empty steps + the generated script), invalidates `["tests"]`, closes the dialog, and navigates to the new test. The dialog stays mounted in `LibrarySidebar` (controlled by `open`); generation is explicit (no auto-start) so the user can edit options and re-generate. **Re-themed in B10 (2026-08-18)**: the form is `.gl-create*` + `.gl-input`/`.gl-textarea`/`.gl-section-title`, the speed row is the theme's `Segmented`, the buttons are `Btn` (Generate takes the `ai` holo treatment, "Create test" is the screen's one `go` — they do not compete because `ai` is a treatment rather than a hue), and the load-state badges are NEUTRAL `StatusChip`s, since "Loaded" is a fact about the provider's memory and not an outcome. `Select` and `ScrollArea` stay (native menu; follow-the-bottom scrolling). It no longer hand-copies `CodeBlock` — it imports the AI debug panel's, which is why B9's re-theme had left the same block looking like two different components.
- `renderer/lib/recorder-types.ts` / `describe-step.ts` — Renderer mirrors of the backend types + step formatter. **The locator mirror renders the full chain as of 2026-08-19** — context clauses and `.nth()`, not just the base builder, which it had silently stopped at since context shipped: a step pinned "inside billing-card" with an index displayed as a bare `getByRole(...)`, hiding exactly the disambiguation the run enforces. The sync is now mechanical, not a comment: `describe-mirror.test.ts` runs the renderer's `locatorExpr` and the generator's (exported for exactly this) over a battery of every kind × every context shape × every legal index and diffs the strings.
- `renderer/lib/diff-steps.ts` — `stepSignature(step)` / `diffSteps(before, after)` / `newStepIds(before, after)`: an LCS diff over step lists **by content**, behind the newly-added-step highlight. Content, not id, because `tests:updateScript` re-parses the whole spec and `spec-parser.ts`'s `makeStep` mints a fresh `randomUUID()` for every step on every parse — after an apply, every id differs, so an id-based diff marks the entire list as new. The signature is an explicit field list (id and timestamp excluded); a step field added later is inert until deliberately added here, which is a missed highlight rather than a false one. An LCS "add" whose signature also appears among the removals is cancelled as a MOVE, one removal per addition, so reordering does not read as an addition while a genuinely-new duplicate still does. Companion to `line-diff.ts` (same LCS, over the spec's text, for the pre-apply confirm). Guarded by `diff-steps.test.ts` and `check:step-glow`.
- `renderer/lib/viewport-presets.ts` — the one list of window/viewport sizes offered to the user (Default / Desktop 1280×800 / Laptop 1440×900 / Tablet 768×1024 / Mobile 390×844), shared by the New Recording dialog's "Window size", the Generate-from-prompt dialog's viewport, Settings' "Default window size", and both "+ Add step → Set viewport" pickers — they all ask the same question, so a size missing from one would read as a bug (the two Add-step dialogs each carried their own copy until 2026-08-06). `RESIZE_PRESETS` is the list minus "Default", for the step pickers: "don't choose a size" is a coherent answer for a window-opening dialog and a 0×0 step everywhere else. `clampViewportAxis(raw, fallback)` bounds a hand-typed custom size (200–4000, mirroring `MIN_VIEWPORT`/`MAX_VIEWPORT` in window-size.ts, which stays the authority). `viewportForPresetId(id)` → `{width,height} | null` ("Default" and unknown ids are null); `presetIdForViewport(size)` maps a persisted size back to its picker value, falling back to "Default" for a size matching no preset so the trigger is never blank.
- `renderer/lib/typeface.ts` — applies `RecorderSettings.uiTypeface` to the document. `startTypeface()` is called once per window from each renderer entry point (each window is its own document); it reads the setting and subscribes to the `settings:appearanceChanged` push so an open window follows a change made in another. `applyTypeface(t)` writes `data-gl-typeface` on `<html>`, which `renderer/theme/tokens.css` selects on to swap `--gl-mono`, `--gl-sans` and `--gl-track-label`; it **removes** the attribute for the default rather than writing `"space"`, so the untouched app carries no attribute and there is no boot instant waiting for one. **Not a hook**, because the typeface has to be right for the first paint of the whole document and the entry points are plain module scope — a hook would need a wrapper component in each entry file, which is one chance per window to reskin all but one of them. `asTypeface` narrows an unknown to the three names: the backend validates too, but a bad value here fails *silently* (an unmatched selector styles nothing), which reads as a broken setting rather than a refused one. The Appearance pane calls `applyTypeface` directly on save. That was load-bearing while Settings was its own window — it was not registered as an aux window, so it never received the push, and the one window the user was looking at while changing the setting was the one that did not change. Settings is a route in the main window now, which DOES receive it; the direct call is kept because it is what makes the change land in the same frame as the click rather than after an IPC round trip. Tested in `typeface.test.tsx` — `.tsx` on purpose, since `renderer/lib/**/*.test.ts` matches the **node** project and would run without a `document`.
- `renderer/lib/use-disabled-enhancements.ts` — `useDisabledEnhancements()` hook: loads `RecorderSettings.disabledAestheticEnhancements` via `api.recorder.getSettings()`, returns a `Set<string>` of disabled feature IDs (empty = all enabled). Extracted from `ai-debug-panel.tsx` (its "AI thinking gif" toggle, ID `aiThinkingGif`) so `home-view.tsx` could reuse it for the new `homeBlackHole` feature ID rather than duplicating the fetch logic.
- `renderer/lib/use-play-step-shortcut.ts` — `isPlayStepChord(e)` + `usePlayStepShortcut(onPlay)`: ⌘P / Ctrl+P plays the currently selected step, bound by both trainer views against their OWN selection. Renderer-side deliberately, unlike its sibling ⌘R: ⌘P collides with nothing in the menus, and which step is selected is per-window renderer state the main process never sees. `onPlay: null` (no selection, controls disabled) still consumes the chord. The matcher is tested in the node project; the binding through the views' dom suites.
- `renderer/lib/next-action.ts` — the trainer's MECHANICAL next-action rules (2026-09-01, PR 2 of the bar plan): `suggestNextAction` computes the ONE suggestion the context band's idle slot may offer — after a `fill`, assert that field's value (prefill EMPTY for a `sequential` fill, whose recorded value is not what the field holds; SILENT for a password field or a value interpolating a variable, because a secret's value must not be transcribed into an assertion); after a navigation, assert the URL path via `urlAssertPrefill` (an observed navigation BEATS the fill rule on purpose — the filled field is no longer on the page; a `goto` anchor uses its OWN url, since an inserted goto has not executed). `pickedFromStep` rebuilds a composer-shaped `PickedElement` from a recorded step with the RECORDED locator first among the candidates, and `suggestFlowName` names a selection's flow after its last named click ("Sign in" for a password fill with no nameable click), prefilled into `CreateFlowDialog`'s field. NO LLM, deliberately: every rule reads only the step list and the URL the trainer already displays, so nothing leaves the app and there is nothing to gate — the AI suggestion strip is a later phase with a settings flag and its own egress check. Pure, node-project tested; the one fact a pure function cannot compute (did the page move AFTER the anchor step landed) is tracked by `useNextAction` in `trainer-bar-controls.tsx` and handed in. Suggestions carry a stable `id` (the fill step's id; the anchor + path for URL offers) so a dismissal hides THIS offer without silencing the next.
- `renderer/lib/agent-run.ts` — the renderer's model of a trainer-agent run: hand-kept mirrors of the `agent:event` shapes plus `applyAgentEvent`/`reduceAgentEvents`, a REDUCER for the late-window problem the step list already solved — a window that opened mid-run seeds from the `agent:getRun` snapshot and applies live pushes on top, with `seq` making the overlap harmless and a record for a different run replacing the view (a new start is a new conversation). Proposal cards resolve IN PLACE in the feed rather than moving. `agentStateLabel` is the one spelling of the drawer's state line. Node-tested.
- `renderer/lib/command-parse.ts` — the command box's MECHANICAL first pass: phrases with one honest local meaning route to the trainer's own controls (an anchored assert verb + the state vocabulary arms the picker, negations checked before their positives so "not visible" cannot arm "visible"; URL/title asks open the composer prefilled the way the assert menu's page group does; "wait 2 seconds" opens the wait form holding the duration), and null — a goal, a value claim, a mid-sentence verb — goes to the agent verbatim. A parse that guessed at goals would insert steps nobody stood behind. Node-tested; the element-state table runs before the title branch because "the exact text of the title" is an element ask that happens to contain the word title.
- `renderer/main/agent-panel.tsx` — the command drawer, the AI tile's surface on both trainers: one implementation with a per-surface frame class (`.gl-trainer-ai` / `.gl-panelwin-ai`, the BarContextZone rule) mounting between the context band and the step list, so opening it shifts the list the way the composer does and the bar above never moves. Holds the transcript feed (say/step/proposal rows; the verify gate's three verdicts as phosphor/red/tertiary glyphs), the state line from `agentStateLabel`, Stop while running, a "Generate steps…" route to the classic dialog, and the input — which stays LIVE during a run, because a mid-run message is the mailbox working as designed. The VIEW owns what a submitted line means (command-parse first, then say-or-start); the drawer only collects it. `AGENT_PLACEHOLDER` is exported for the tests, the TILE_COPY argument.
- `renderer/main/recorder-store.tsx` — `RecorderProvider`/`useRecorder`: subscribes to push events, holds live steps + run output, exposes actions. **`settleStepStatus` (2026-08-22)** is applied to `RunInfo.stepStatus` on `runner:done`: a step's `end` marker is not guaranteed (Stop is a SIGKILL; a crash ends the same way), and a step still `running` when the run has finished kept its spinner and never went red — the exit code decides what it settles to. Now subscribes to `recorder:steps` (replaces the whole list — the backend owns ordering) and exposes `insertStep`/`reorderStep`/`updateStep`/`setCursor`/`replayStep` + `setAssert(mode, soft)`. Refine Selector: subscribes to `recorder:picked` (holds `picked`), tracks `refiningStepId` (set by `startRefine(stepId)`, cleared by `endRefine`), and exposes `startRefine`/`endRefine`/`clearPicked`. The stepId association lives in the renderer store (the backend only tracks `refineMode`); the dialog applies the chosen locator via `updateStep(refiningStepId, {locator})`. **`addVariable(v)`** (2026-08-17) declares a variable on the live session and applies the reply as the new state rather than waiting for the broadcast — the caller selects what it just created the moment this resolves, and a picker that did not yet list it would drop the selection on the next render. It rejects with a message meant to be SHOWN (invalid name, duplicate, empty secret): the backend owns those rules and the form prints what it says. Step debug panel: `replayStep`/`replayFromStart`/`replayFromCurrent` wrappers update `debugEntries` (`DebugEntry[]`, latest attempt per step); loads persisted entries via `getDebugLogs(testId)` on session open and on `recorder:debugLogs` push; exposes `debugEntries` + `clearDebugEntry(id)` (persists via backend). Live replay: subscribes to `recorder:replayLog` and builds a `ReplayRun` model (`replayRun`: running/total/ran/passed/failedAtIndex/steps[]) consumed by the Console tab; exposes `replayFromCurrent(startIndex)`. Exposes an `executing` flag (set around every replay action's await — replayStep/replayFromStart/replayFromCurrent) that the trainer uses to show "Running" and lock editing. Newly-added steps: exposes `newStepIds: Set<string>` and `verifyGeneratedSteps(steps, label)` — the Generate Steps dialog's ONLY insert path (2026-08-22; it replaced `insertGeneratedSteps`), which hands the whole list to `recorder:verifySteps` in one call (the backend runs each step against the live page and inserts it as it works, so ordering is its problem rather than a race between invokes), then re-reads `getSteps()` rather than waiting on the `recorder:steps` push (invoke reply and push are different channels with no ordering guarantee) and diffs to find what actually landed — a failed step and everything after it did not, and backend normalization can drop a model-produced step. Resolves with the `VerifiedStepsResult` the dialog renders as its activity log. Every hand mutation (`insertStep`/`deleteStep`/`reorderStep`/`updateStep`) and `start()` clear the set: once the user is editing, "the AI added these" is no longer a claim the store can make. **The AI suggestion strip (2026-09-01, PR 4):** `aiSuggestions` mirrors the `suggest:changed` push WHOLESALE (the main-process service owns membership and staleness), seeds from `suggest:get` through the same resolved-promise hardening as the agent seed, and clears itself when `state.recording` flips false — the page the chips describe is gone, and the service only clears its own copy on the next capture. `acceptSuggestion(id)` awaits `suggest:accept` and toasts only the failure (success shows itself as the inserted step); `dismissSuggestion(id)` removes the chip optimistically before the round-trip settles.
- `renderer/main/step-row.tsx` — Shared step row. Read-only in the detail view (index + description). In the trainer, optional props enable a drag handle (reorder), inline value/text edit (pencil) — which since 2026-08-17 carries an **Insert a variable** button whenever the host passes `variables` and the field is one the generator interpolates (`value`/`text`/`url`; never the numeric ones, which are emitted as bare numerals). It opens a native menu of `${name}` references and splices the pick in at the CARET, which is what makes `${user}@example.com` a one-step edit. Two things keep the editor alive under it: `preventDefault` on mousedown, so focus never leaves the input, and a `menuOpen` latch, because `Menu.popup` blurs the page anyway and a blur that committed would unmount the field mid-pick. This is the path for a step recorded BEFORE the variable existed — a typed password already sitting in the list — a ▶ replay button with pass/fail flash, an `onRefine` crosshair "Refine selector" icon (rendered only when `onRefine` is set AND `step.locator` exists) placed between the ▶ replay and ✕ delete icons, and `selected`/`onSelect` (clicking the row selects it — accent ring; clicks on buttons/inputs are ignored). Shows a `soft` badge for soft assertions. Handles `if`/`endif` rows: purple badge (`endif` labeled "end if"), an `indent` prop (`marginLeft = indent*20`) for block nesting, inline edit of a page-condition substring, replay suppressed on `endif`. Both the trainer (`recording-view.tsx`) and read-only detail view (`test-detail-view.tsx`) compute per-row depth via `computeStepDepths` and pass `indent`. **Run status is an inset RAIL since B5a**, not a tinted background plus a ring. Three problems went with the fill: in this palette a filled row is the largest coloured surface on screen, so a list with four failures read as mostly-red before a word of it was scanned; a ring draws on all four sides, so a column of rows became a stack of boxes rather than a list; and the fill sat under the description, which is the text the colour is actually about. An inset shadow rather than a border, because a border participates in layout and the list would jump by 2px per row as statuses land live during a run. The REPLAY flash keeps its fill deliberately — it is a 2.5s answer to something the user just clicked, not a persistent property of the row. **Selection went neutral at the same time** (`SEL_BG` + `SEL_RING`, no hue): a selected row in the accent competes with what the rail beside it is reporting, and `check:selection-neutral` pins it. Both are box-shadows, so they compose in one declaration; the rail is listed first so it paints over the selection fill's edge. An `isNew` prop marks a step that arrived without the user writing it (an applied AI-debug fix, an inserted AI-generated flow): it adds `data-new-step` plus the `.step-new` class from `renderer/styles.css` — a pulsing green **inset outline**. Outline, not border, so the row's height does not change when the highlight clears and so it does not collide with the drag-over `border-t-2`; the keyframes animate **only `outline-color`**, because the row's selection and run-status highlights are `ring-*` box-shadows and an animated `box-shadow` would silently replace them. The highlight is therefore additive: a step can be new AND failing at once, and neither claim hides the other. Removed steps get no styling — the step count carries that. Stops pulsing (keeping the outline) under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- `renderer/main/recording-view.tsx` — **the trainer, reskinned in B6.** The session state is a `StatusChip`, and NONE of its states takes a status hue — a real change rather than a restyle, because `Recording` was the SDK's `error` variant, i.e. RED, on the one screen where nothing has run yet. Recording, Editing, Replaying and Running are all IN FLIGHT and take the holo `running` treatment ("a treatment says this is not a result"); Paused and the two loading states are neutral. The word separates them, which is the split the palette explicitly asks for. **The Recording/Paused chip is also the pause TOGGLE (2026-08-28):** it replaced the Pause/Resume button at the status row's right edge — users kept clicking the chip anyway, and the freed space was claimed by the 2026-09-01 reorganisation below. Safe by branch order: either word only renders when the page is ready, the steps are loaded and nothing is replaying, exactly when the old button was enabled; the other three states stay passive spans. ⌘R toggles the same state from the keyboard (main-process `before-input-event` — see main/recorder/recording-shortcut.ts) and ⌘P plays the selected step (renderer/lib/use-play-step-shortcut.ts). "Generate Test" is `Btn tone="go"` (it ends the recording, but what it DOES is produce the test). The three-tab console keeps its shape and takes `.gl-tabs`. **The tool row became the TILE STRIP + CONTEXT BAND on 2026-09-01** (Direction A, picked from the `?view=bar-lab` mocks): four `ToolTile`s — Assert, Add step, Replay, AI (`tone="ai"`, the holo border) — whose membership, order and geometry never change (disabled-gated, never render-gated; folded by default per REDESIGN §B6, a chevron unfolds the one-line descriptions), over `BarContextZone` (`.gl-trainer-context`, reserved `min-height`), where EVERY transient now lives: the armed-assert kind + Hard/Soft + cancel, the refine notice (its own banner retired), the replay result, and an always-mounted Create flow whose disabled title carries `extractableRange`'s live verdict. The old row's defining defect was transients injecting themselves INLINE and reflowing the row mid-reach — the failure DECISIONS 2026-08-17 banned on Visual, unguarded here until `check:narrow-layout` §5. Hard/Soft mounts ONLY inside the armed context now (it held permanent width for a choice that only matters while arming), still the theme's `Segmented`, still click-drivable. Menus, vocabularies and the create-flow gate live in `trainer-actions.ts`; the band in `trainer-bar-controls.tsx` — both shared with the panel.
- `renderer/main/trainer-actions.ts` — the ONE copy of what the trainer's action bar OFFERS: the assert vocabularies (`ASSERT_PICKABLE` / `ASSERT_PAGE` / `ASSERT_LABEL`), `ADD_STEP_KINDS`, the native menus (`pickAssertFromMenu` / `pickAddStepFromMenu` — the URL group's `commandId + 100` encoding exists only here now, where two hand-synced copies used to ask the next person to keep them agreeing), and `createFlowGate`, the disabled-gated-never-render-gated verdict (count, disabled, title) both bars spread onto their Create flow button. Covered by `trainer-actions.test.tsx` — a `.tsx` although nothing renders, because a `.ts` test under renderer/main matches neither vitest project.
- `renderer/main/trainer-bar-controls.tsx` — `BarContextZone` (the reserved transient band both trainers mount under their tile strips; the frame class stays per-surface — `.gl-trainer-context` / `.gl-panelwin-context` — per DECISIONS 2026-08-12, ONE implementation so the two cannot describe one session differently), `StrictnessControl` (Hard/Soft, mounted only inside the armed-assert context), `TILE_COPY` — the tiles' names and descriptions, exported because a folded tile's description is only reachable as a hover title, and a string only reachable by hover is one no jsdom test can assert on — and `useNextAction` (2026-09-01), the view-side half of `lib/next-action.ts`: tracks the page URL at the moment the anchor step landed (the memory the pure module cannot hold) plus per-window dismissal, and returns the suggestion the band's idle slot renders as the `.gl-context-chip` — a band OCCUPANT below armed/refine/note in the branch order, never a strip member (`check:narrow-layout` §5 pins that), withheld by the views while the composer is open or the controls are disabled.
- `renderer/main/refine-selector-dialog.tsx` — `RefineSelectorDialog`: shows the `PickedElement` (optional `stepLabel` of the step being refined, description, candidate locators as a radio list via a local `formatLocator`, computed CSS grid). `onApply(locator)` hands back the chosen locator (Update selector); closing either way calls back to resume the session. Owned by `recording-view.tsx` (rendered when `picked !== null`), which applies it via `updateStep(refiningStepId, { locator })`. Since 2026-08-14 it also renders `ElementContextPicker`, and applies the chosen context ON the locator rather than as a second patch — `updateStep` drops a step's fingerprint when its locator changes, and two writes would fire that twice for one edit. **Since 2026-08-19 the candidate list ends in a "Custom" row** — `CustomLocatorField`, the hand-typed CSS/XPath escape hatch — with the confirm gated on the draft being a valid locator, so an invalid draft can never silently fall back to a candidate. Covered by `refine-selector-dialog.test.tsx`. **And a "Position among matches" row (2026-08-19)** — `PositionField`, shared with the composer's target picker: Auto / First / Last / Nth, writing `Locator.nth` (Last is `-1`, the one negative the model admits; Playwright's "last match" and the stable ordinal for a set whose size changes between runs). Composed after context, matching the emitted chain's order. **The same match-count gate as the composer (2026-08-22):** `compose()` is the locator Update would write, counted on the page whenever it changes and without a position, and the confirm is disabled with `ambiguousTargetHint` while it matches more than one element — this dialog being the other way an ambiguous locator gets written.
- `renderer/main/position-field.tsx` — the ordinal control both pick surfaces share. People count matches from one, the model from zero, "Last" is -1: that conversion is this component's whole job, plus the strict-mode hint while on Auto. "Nth…" is local mode state entered before it has a number — a fully value-controlled segment could not show its input until the parent echoed a value back. Covered by `position-field.test.tsx`; the model/generator/parser/replayer sides of `-1` are pinned in `check:step-ingest`, `check:locator-roundtrip` and `step-replayer-parity.dom.test.ts`.
- `renderer/lib/custom-locator.ts` — the custom-locator GATE: `classifyCustomLocator` (plain CSS, `xpath=`/`//…` XPath; REFUSES `>>` chaining, Playwright engine prefixes and Playwright-only pseudo-classes with directions, because the in-page oracle can't count any of them and an honest refusal beats a confident wrong number) and `lintCustomLocator` (advisory warnings for positional paths, build-hashed class names, deep descendant chains — the copied-from-DevTools shapes). Pure string logic so it runs under the node project; `custom-locator.test.ts` pins the refusal set as hard as the acceptance set.
- `renderer/main/custom-locator-field.tsx` — the field both surfaces share (Refine dialog, composer target picker): input + kind badge, local syntax validation against this document (an unparseable draft never reaches the count channel, where the in-page try/catch would read as "0 matches"), a debounced live count via the same `recorder:countMatches` the picker uses — composed WITH the pending context so the number shown is what the finished step resolves — and the lint warnings, advisory only. Emits `Locator | null`, and the null matters: without it a parent keeps the last valid locator while the field shows an error. Covered by `custom-locator-field.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/element-context-picker.tsx` — **"Which one did you mean?"** (2026-08-14). The answer to a step whose target is one of many similar elements. Ticking a row pins a property of the picked element — most usefully the container it lives in — onto the step's locator, where it scopes the emitted Playwright chain AND hard-filters Auto-Heal's candidates. Rendered by both consumers of a `PickedElement`: the composer's `TargetElementPicker` and `RefineSelectorDialog`. **Two interaction rules fall out of the data model**: a container is a CHOICE (`LocatorContext.within` is one field, so those rows replace each other), attributes and classes ACCUMULATE (`and` is a list) — rendering both as checkboxes would let a user tick two containers and silently drop one. `KIND_RANK` is the durability ranking, most durable first; it is a RANKING and not a filter because durability is a property of the site, not of the property kind, so brittle rows are ranked last and LABELLED rather than hidden. **Every row is priced**, and the numbers are the feature: without them the user is guessing whether "inside .card" narrows nine matches to one or to four, and because context is a real constraint at run time a signal that narrows nothing is a pure loss. The per-row counts come from the pick; the COMBINED count cannot be derived from them (two signals that each leave three matches might leave three between them or none), so it is asked of the live page via `api.recorder.countMatches` → `recorder:countMatches` → `recorderService.countMatches` → `buildCountScript`. **-1, never 0, for a failed count**: "nothing on this page matches" is a different claim and one the user would act on. **Opens expanded only when `picked.ambiguous`** — the app's own admission that no locator NAMES the element, i.e. exactly the "one of many similar selectors" case; collapsed otherwise, because each pinned property is load-bearing at run time and prompting where the locator is already unique invites pinning that costs stability and buys nothing. Copy constants are exported for the same reason the trainer's are: a string only reachable by hover is one no jsdom test can assert on. Covered by `element-context-picker.test.tsx`.- `renderer/main/add-step-dialog.tsx` — `AddStepDialog`: one form dialog for all manual step kinds (assertion/**condition**/wait/goto/press/find/viewport). The `condition` kind picks a `ConditionKind` (element conditions use the `TargetElementPicker`; page conditions show a substring `Input`) and builds an empty `if`/`endif` pair. `build()` returns `RawStep[]` and `onAdd(steps)` inserts each (so one submit can add multiple steps); other kinds return a single-element array. The `wait` kind is the only one whose form is multi-select: three checkboxes (element / Wait until / duration) each contribute their own `wait` step to the returned array, and a ticked box with no target refuses the WHOLE submit rather than quietly emitting the others. Both element-scoped wait boxes share the single `TargetElementPicker`, because the dialog holds one pick. `AddStepKind` order = native menu commandId; `ADD_STEP_KINDS` in `recording-view.tsx` must match. Covered by `add-step-dialog.test.tsx` — note the SDK `Select` can't be driven in jsdom, so the predicate dropdown itself is covered at the generator/parser layer instead. Also exports `ambiguousTargetHint(count)`, the one sentence the composer and the Refine dialog both show when the chosen locator matches several elements (2026-08-22) — kept here with the rest of the context copy so the two surfaces cannot drift.
- `renderer/main/generate-steps-dialog.tsx` — `GenerateStepsDialog`: AI step generation for the trainer — `buildGenerateStepsMessages` + `useLlmChat` stream, `extractStepsJson` validates the model's `Step[]`, and **"Try N steps" (2026-08-22) hands them to `onVerify`** (the store's `verifyGeneratedSteps`), which runs each against the live page and inserts it only once it has worked. The outcome renders as the **activity log** (`data-testid="ai-activity"`, one row per attempted step with `ACTIVITY_LABEL[status]` — `ran` / `unchecked` / `failed`, kept distinct on purpose) plus a sentence saying how many worked and were kept, and how many were never attempted. The dialog closes on full success and STAYS OPEN on a failure; the Try button reads "Tried" and stays down until the user regenerates, since a second try would insert the prefix again. `onVerify` is a prop, not an `api` call, so `generate-steps-dialog.test.tsx` can state the outcome; the real half is `e2e/verified-steps.spec.ts`. See DECISIONS 2026-08-22.
- `renderer/main/root-view.tsx` — `SplitView` shell (top strip + rail + primary); shows `RecordingView` while `state.recording`, else routed `Outlet`. Since A4 (2026-08-10) it mounts `<Atmosphere />` (the three global overlay layers, portalled to `document.body`, `calm` with CRT off per REDESIGN §0) and passes `<AppStrip />` as the SplitView's new `header` slot. **The pinned `SplitView.SidebarToggle` is gone from here**: the rail handle is in-flow in the strip's leading slot, so nothing floats over the pane's top-left and no `Toolbar` reserves 44px for it any more. `useTheme()` went with the light theme. **Carries no window-drag overlay.** It used to open with a `drag-region fixed left-0 right-0 top-0 h-13` div (plus a `[&:not(:has([data-toolbar]))_.drag-region]:z-50` lift on the wrapper) inherited from the frameless Glaze host window. The main window keeps Electron's default native title bar, so that div dragged nothing — but being `fixed`, it painted above the in-flow chrome and won every hit test in the top 52px, which is where the sidebar's "+" (Add test) button lives. Removed 2026-08-09; guarded by `check:clickable-chrome`. Drag regions that are still real (`Toolbar`, the `Sidebar` header, the trainer panel's `hiddenInset` header) are untouched.
- `renderer/main/run-browser-field.tsx` — **the browser picker the three form-bearing creation dialogs share** (record, generate, import-from-URL), added 2026-08-18. `useRunBrowserChoice(open)` reads `defaultRunBrowser` on each open and returns `value` (what the control shows) and **`toStore`, which is `undefined` whenever the value still equals the default** — that asymmetry is the point: `TestRecord.runBrowser` means "inherit the global default" when absent, so pinning every new test to the engine that happened to be default at creation would retire the Settings default entirely. It never writes the setting back, unlike the speed and window-size pickers beside it. The folder import has no dialog to host it, so imported-from-disk tests still inherit. Guarded per dialog (`new-recording-dialog.test.tsx`, `import-git-dialog.test.tsx`, `generate-test-dialog.test.tsx`) for what is SENT, and by `handlers.test.ts` for what the record ends up carrying — including that a bogus engine is refused, since the value picks the Playwright project the runner spawns and arrives over IPC.
- **`new-recording-dialog.tsx` is on the theme layer since B10 (2026-08-18)**: `.gl-create-field` label-above-field rows, `.gl-input`, the theme's `Segmented` for run speed (which is why `speed-picker.test.tsx` matches `aria-pressed` as well as the SDK's `aria-checked`), and a native `Select` for the window size. It also gained an inline error, because a `start()` that rejected used to escape `void onConfirm()` unhandled and leave the dialog saying nothing. It closes itself once the recording starts — see `overlays.tsx` and `check:dialog-close`. **The URL field says what it will open (2026-08-25, #134)**: a `gl-note` under the input reading "Opens https://example.com", from `startUrlHint`, shown only when the app is adding a scheme the user did not type. It is `aria-describedby`, and the field is a `div` + `htmlFor` rather than a wrapping `label`, because inside the label the sentence joins the input's accessible NAME instead of describing it. `generate-test-dialog.tsx` carries the same note, and now resolves the URL it sends to the model and stores on the record through the same rule. **Its Run speed picker now sets only the SETTING (2026-08-25, R18)** — a recording no longer stamps `speed` onto the record, so absent means inherit and changing the setting later moves every unpinned test. `test-detail-view` gained a **Pace** control beside the engine for the other half: it overrides one run and is never written back, which is why it reads "Inherit" rather than the test's own speed.
- `renderer/main/library-sidebar.tsx`, `new-recording-dialog.tsx`, `generate-test-dialog.tsx`, `recording-view.tsx`, `test-detail-view.tsx`, `run-output.tsx`, `step-row.tsx` — UI pieces. **`step-row.tsx`, `pager.tsx`, `tag-cluster.tsx`, `log-inspector.tsx` and `heals-panel.tsx` are on the theme layer since A5 (2026-08-10)** and are guarded by `check:sdk-retired`; the step row keeps its own structure (drag, inline edit, replay, the run flash) because folding that into the `StepRow` primitive is the step list's own reskin, but its badges are `TypeChip`, its buttons are `.gl-icon-btn`, and its status glyph takes the palette's tones — `running` is `--gl-cyan`, not the SDK accent. Sidebar + opens a native popup (`window.glazeAPI.Menu.popup`) anchored under the button with items: "Train manually" (→ `NewRecordingDialog`), "Generate from prompt" (→ `GenerateTestDialog`), "Select from files", "From URL". Each `SidebarListItem` row is wrapped in a `CustomContextMenu` (right-click: Reveal in Finder, Adjust Test Speed submenu with `TestSpeedSlider`). **`test-detail-view.tsx` seeds its six run controls (capture / a11y / logs / headless / browser / timeout) once per TEST, tracked in `seededFor`, not once per mount** — the router does not remount a route component when only its params change, so clicking another test in the sidebar re-renders the same instance and a boolean "already initialised" latch never fires again; every test opened after the first showed the previous one's settings, and the run used them. It also waits for the `["recorder-settings"]` query to settle, or a test that has pinned nothing latches the hard-coded fall-backs whenever that query loses the race against `["test", id]`. **The Heals tab's badge counts BOTH journals** (pending heals + pending script changes) — two routes to one hazard, and two numbers on one tab would ask the user to add them up. Its trigger is hidden for an imported test **only while it would be empty** (`imported && scriptChanges.length === 0`), which is the rule the two neighbouring exclusions state — "a tab that could only ever be empty is worse than no tab". An imported test can't be healed, but its script is exactly the kind that gets hand-edited, and hiding the tab outright put that history somewhere unreachable. **The head is ONE row since 2026-08-17** (`.gl-detail-head-row`): the name and URL on the left, every control right-aligned on the same line, clustered by question — what the test IS (Edit Test, delete) | how it RUNS (engine, timeout, base URL) | what the run RECORDS (the gang of four) | go — with a hairline between clusters and a tighter gap inside one than between them. It replaced `Toolbar`'s own stacked content-over-actions, which spent 94px on two lines that each half-filled their own; on one row the band is 54px. The row WRAPS, and that is the narrow-window contract: the controls want ~840px and this app's smallest window leaves the pane 688px, so below roughly a 1260px window they drop to a line of their own and land back on exactly the layout this replaced. `.gl-detail-ident`'s `min-width` is what decides that — a `flex-1 min-w-0` identity column shrinks to nothing rather than pushing the controls down. Three more floors exist for the same family of reason: `.gl-detail-engine` and `.gl-detail-secs` (both fields inherit `min-width: 0` from `.gl-input`, and proportional shrink took the engine trigger to 61px — "Chromium" rendered as "Chro…"), and `.gl-detail-run`, because `Stop` is 31px narrower than `Run test` and a right-aligned band moved sideways the moment a run started. All of it is pinned by `check:narrow-layout` §3; the structural half — identity and controls as siblings on one row, every control inside the group — is in `test-detail-view.test.tsx`, since jsdom has no layout engine.
- `renderer/main/heals-panel.tsx` — `HealsPanel`, the per-test Heals tab. Since 2026-08-12 it merges TWO stores into one time-ordered list — locator heals and whole-script changes — split into the existing *Needs review* / *History* sections by each entry's own `status`, not by kind: the question the user arrives with is "what has happened to this test". The amber notice above the queue covers both (`e.kind === "heal" ? applied : !reviewed`) rather than growing a second banner competing with the first, and `Clear history` clears settled entries in both stores.
- `renderer/main/load-failed-dialog.tsx` — `LoadFailedDialog` + `LOAD_FAILED_COPY`. The consumer for `recorder:loadFailed`, i.e. "Couldn't open the training browser". **Mounted from `root-view.tsx` OUTSIDE `RootShell`, and that placement is the fix, not a detail.** It lived in `RecordingView` gated on `RecorderState.loadFailed` and could never open: the service nulls the session before broadcasting (so the field is always false) *and* `RootShell` unmounts `RecordingView` once `state.recording` goes false — both true at the instant the dialog is due. It therefore holds the message from the push and reads no session state; `RecorderState.loadFailed` was removed so the old gate cannot be rebuilt by accident. "Check Stats" goes through the router via an injected `onCheckStats` (the `onFinished` pattern — the trainer panel runs the same provider with no router); it used to assign `window.location.hash`, which selects nothing under memory history. Covered by `load-failed-dialog.test.tsx`, which pins the mount position at source level as well as driving the push.
- `renderer/main/viewport-narrowed-notice.ts` — `useViewportNarrowedNotice()` + `viewportNarrowedNotice(width)`. Subscribes to the backend's `trainerPanel:viewportNarrowed` push and raises the toast that says the training browser just got narrower, and to what width. **Subscribed by BOTH trainers, and the main window is the one that matters:** `noteViewportChange` fires inside `openTrainerPanel` *before* `panelWindow.loadURL`, so on the ordinary path (a panel that opens already docked) the panel's page does not exist and the push reaches a window with no listeners — a subscription living only in `trainer-panel-view.tsx` would never fire in the app while its own component test passed. `recording-view.tsx` has been loaded since the session started and is the reliable receiver; the panel subscribes as well for the re-dock path (`dock()`, both windows alive), which is the moment the user just pressed the button that caused it. The toast does not auto-dismiss — it is raised as focus moves to the training browser, so a few seconds would expire behind another window. Copy is exported and asserted directly, as `DOCK_TOOLTIP` is, because the sonner stub records a toast as a call rather than as DOM. One-notice-per-session is the backend's `viewportHintSent` flag, not re-derived here. Covered by `recording-view.test.tsx` and `trainer-panel-view.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/import-git-dialog.tsx` — **GitHub's clone widget, in the theme layer (B10)**: a `Segmented` HTTPS/SSH row, a `.gl-input` monospace URL field, an optional Branch-or-tag field, a `.gl-note` caption. The tabs are a HINT, not a validator — the backend accepts both protocols whatever the row says, so pasting an SSH URL FLIPS the tab rather than erroring, and only when the text actually names a protocol (otherwise the row twitches on every keystroke). `Segmented` rather than Radix `Tabs` because it is plain buttons with `aria-pressed`: `fireEvent.click` drives it, and the active item stays neutral per `check:selection-neutral`. On confirm calls `tests:importGit` with the URL and the ref, invalidates `["tests"]`, toasts the result and its warnings, navigates to the first imported test, then **closes itself** (the composed `Dialog` will not — see `overlays.tsx`). On error it shows inline red text and stays open with the typed URL intact; it no longer re-throws, which never kept anything open and only produced an unhandled rejection. Mounted by the library rail, Home, AND the ⌘K palette. Guarded by `import-git-dialog.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/heals-view.tsx` — `HealsView` (route `/heals`), the cross-test Auto-Heal journal. **Reskinned in B2 (2026-08-10)**: two `Panel`s — a 330px journal and the detail — and **no toolbar above them**, because the top strip's breadcrumb already says HEALS, the count belongs to the journal it counts, and "Clear history" belongs to the list it clears. The four states are `StatusChip`s at the fixed status width, and **only two of them carry a hue**: `Accepted` is a result, `Applied` is the one that should catch the eye (the stored test has already changed and nobody has looked at it), `Suggested` takes cyan as the open item waiting on you, and `Reverted` takes none — settled, with nothing to report. Two neutral chips is deliberate: the width is fixed so they read as a column and THE WORD reports the state. The was/now block, the candidate rows and the amber notice reuse the `.gl-heal-*` classes A5 built for the per-test panel, which is `shared.css` working as designed — the second screen to want them found them there rather than growing a copy. The journal's 330px is set in CSS and NOT via `Panel`'s `flex` prop: that prop writes an inline `flex: 1 1 auto` on the section, which beats the class and silently grew the journal to half the window — a plausible-looking layout rather than an obvious bug. The amber warning renders only for a heal that is pending AND applied, because "a heal that succeeded is not the same as a heal that was right" is a sentence about something that was applied and passed; on a suggestion it would be noise, which is how a warning becomes something people click past. **Since 2026-08-12 it lists two kinds** — locator heals and whole-script changes (`ScriptChangeListEntry`, from `scriptChanges:listAll`) — merged into ONE list sorted by `at`, because the question the screen answers is "what has been changing my tests", and splitting by mechanism would make the user read two lists to answer it once.
