A script row takes `FileCode2` and a NON-violet glyph (`.gl-heals-row-icon-plain`): violet marks AI-adjacent machinery and half of these rows are the user's own hand edit, so which one it is gets said in words rather than in hue. `scriptStatusChip` reuses `Applied` deliberately — it means what it means on a heal — and has no `Suggested` counterpart, because a script change is only ever recorded once it has been written. `ScriptChangeDetail` carries the diff (`DiffView`) and offers **Revert whatever the status**, unlike a heal's: that record holds the only copy of the previous spec, so "put it back" has nowhere else to go. `Clear history` sweeps BOTH journals from one button — one that emptied half the list would read as a control that didn't work. **Since 2026-09-01 it lists a THIRD kind** — cross-test propagation proposals (`PropagationListEntry`, from `propagation:listAll`), the review home `docs/plans/preemptive-updates.md` chose: `Share2` on the plain glyph (deterministic machinery, not AI), `Proposed` in the heals' cyan, `Applied` reusing the amber word because it means exactly what it means on a heal, and the engine's housekeeping states (`Superseded`, `Stale`) toneless — nothing there to decide. `ProposalDetail` renders the was/now pair, fixed reason copy per engine code (`REASON_COPY`, the insights-view rule: codes cross the boundary, the renderer owns every sentence), donors named from the tests cache, and the **evidence figures** — two `ShotHighlight`s from one `propagation:evidence` round-trip, the donor's after-heal shot with its measured box beside the target's latest with measured-or-fingerprint box, each side simply absent when retention took it. Actions are Apply/Dismiss (pending), Keep/Revert plus the amber notice (applied-unreviewed — propagation wrote the step and nobody has looked), Revert alone (accepted), and a bulk "Apply all N on this site" behind an `AlertDialog` that names the tests it will touch — scoped to the SAME origin, applied sequentially so one stale refusal counts without failing the rest, with the honest `Applied X of N` toast. Counts ride the same one-door rule everywhere: Home's `Heals to review`, Stats' Auto-Heal tile (`summariseHeals` takes proposals and says how many arrived from siblings), the per-test Heals tab badge and `heals-panel`'s chip (which POINTS at this view rather than growing a second review surface), and the Routines outcome panel's one-line link.

- `renderer/main/script-change-row.tsx` — `ScriptChangeRow`, plus `originLabel` / `changeSummary` / `ChangeCounts`. The row for one whole-script change, shared by the per-test panel and (for the label/count helpers) the cross-test view, so the two cannot describe one event differently. It reuses the `.gl-heal-row` frame on purpose: the two kinds are the same event ("something changed this test"), and a second frame would read as two lists stacked. **One status chip, not two:** every script change was written to the test, so "applied" alone reports nothing — amber `Applied without review` is spent on the fact that matters, and a reviewed one takes the neutral `Applied to the test`. `AI Debug - <model>` takes violet (AI-adjacent, explicitly not a status), a hand edit takes the neutral chip, and a missing model degrades to a bare `AI Debug` rather than printing "undefined". The diff is computed only while expanded — O(n·m) over lines, and a list can hold fifty rows.
- `renderer/lib/ai-debug-stats.ts` — what the AI Debug feature has been WORTH, arithmetically. Pure and in `lib/` for the same reason as `stats-categories.ts`: what it can get wrong is a number. Four rules, each written against a way the screen could flatter itself. **Only a KEPT fix counts as time saved** — a diagnosis nobody applied saved nothing and one that was reverted cost time, so the figure is driven by the script-change journal's statuses joined against the run history (`confirmed` = the next run of that test passed inside `CONFIRMATION_WINDOW_MS`), never by how many sessions were run; `kept` is counted PER FIX rather than as `accepted + confirmed`, which would report one fix as two and drift the savings up by exactly the number of times the feature worked. **The wait is subtracted**, so a net saving can be NEGATIVE and is left negative — a suite where the model is asked constantly and rarely believed is genuinely costing time, and that is the most useful thing this screen can say. **Money only on request**: `netValue` is null until the user states an hourly rate (`costHourlyRate`, default 0 = not stated), which is `cost-model.ts`'s refusal to guess kept intact rather than overridden. **Tokens are admitted to be approximate** (`CHARS_PER_TOKEN`, 4 — the same approximation `provider-errors.ts` puts on screen), and a record whose provider was never stamped is counted as neither local nor hosted.
- `renderer/lib/stats-categories.ts` — the Stats category REGISTRY and the arithmetic behind each tile (`CATEGORIES`, `summariseAll`, `bandReading`, `FACET_LABELS`). Pure, and in `lib/` so the NODE test project can exercise it without React — the failure it guards against is a wrong number, which a component test is worst at noticing. Two rules live in here rather than in the UI. **Zero is not "never measured":** `display` is `null` in both non-measured states, so a caller has no number to print, and `unmeasured` (you never switched this on) is distinct from `unavailable` (the metrics DB cannot answer) because only one is the user's to fix. **`bandReading` counts CATEGORIES IN A STATE and never touches their values** — fourteen a11y steps, three flaky tests and nine healed locators are different units, and a total across them means nothing while looking authoritative.
- `renderer/main/a11y-view.tsx` — `A11yView` (route `/a11y`, sidebar "Accessibility"), the suite-wide accessibility surface, added 2026-08-19. Two tabs. **Triage** is rule-centric: a master list of rules worst-first from `["a11y-rollup"]` (impact chip, axe id, help, tests/steps/elements counts — tests counted UNIQUE, since a rule on five steps of one test is one thing to fix), and a detail pane grouping occurrences by test with an exit to each. Two verbs per rule: **Accept everywhere** (`a11y:acceptRule`, confirmed first — only that rule's keys are pinned, which is the property `a11y-baseline-ops.test.ts` guards) and **Send** — one issue per RULE, not per occurrence, via the ordinary `IssueComposeDialog` with an a11y source carrying `scope: "rule"` anchored at the first stepped occurrence; the loader widens the body with a capped "Where it occurs" list and the title names the rule's spread rather than the anchor test. "Filed as" badges come from ONE `issues:a11yLinks` read (links are keyed by anchor, so the lookup is by rule id across tests). **Baseline & trends** lists tests with accepted issues, grouped into rules with per-rule **Revoke** (`a11y:revokeRule`) beside the old all-or-nothing Reset, plus the suite posture chips and a trend strip over `["runs"]` (checked runs only, `baseline-update` excluded — the same exclusions `selectLatestA11yRuns` makes; drawn only at 2+ points, like the drift strip). The per-test Accessibility tab and the Stats severity dashboard both stay — this is the only surface where a RULE is a first-class row. **The per-test panel (`a11y-panel.tsx`) was brought in line on 2026-08-25**: it captures the anchor RUN into `sending` when Send is pressed, rather than reading `latest` live, and its replay query keeps the previous run's data (`placeholderData`) so a new run does not unmount the panel — and with it the open compose dialog — behind the `Loading…` early return. Both were needed to stop a background run discarding a half-typed report; see DECISIONS 2026-08-25.
- `renderer/main/stats/` — the board and its dashboards. `category-board.tsx` is a row of compact tiles under the verdict, **which sits in the panel's own 32px header** rather than in a full-width band above the tiles — one sentence about the board, next to the "N of N reporting" subtitle that is the same kind of thing, where it costs no vertical space on the app's landing screen (it ellipsizes there and carries the whole sentence as a `title`; the subtitle is pinned so the sentence is the only thing that ever truncates) (every tile a real `<button>`; a category whose query has not resolved renders a skeleton, NOT an "unmeasured" tile, because "loading" and "you never switched this on" are different sentences). `stats-category-view.tsx` serves both `/stats/$category` and `/stats/$category/$facet`: it owns the queries, the `BUILT` list, the Stability and Auto-Heal dashboards, and the switch that picks a body. **All eight categories open as of 2026-08-17**, one file each — `outcomes-dashboard.tsx` (the chart, the KPI cards and capture overhead, MOVED off the landing rather than copied — plus, since 2026-08-19, a **"Failures by reason"** panel that groups the retained failed runs by their failure-reason label and drills into `reason-<id>` / `reason-none` facets through the same `runsIn`/`OutcomesLeaf` pair; toneless rows because every row is already about failures, labelled reasons by count with Uncategorized last regardless of size — it is the to-do pile, not a leading cause — and names resolved from the `["failure-reasons"]` query with an unknown id shown raw), `a11y-dashboard.tsx` (severity → rules, over `a11y:rollup`), `visual-dashboard.tsx`, `speed-dashboard.tsx` and `steps-dashboard.tsx` (the last two wrapping `SuiteCostPanel` / `StepHealthPanel`, which the landing already rendered), and `ai-debug-dashboard.tsx`. **The AI Debug one is the odd tile on the board and deliberately so**: its headline is a VOLUME (diagnoses asked for) where every other category counts things that are wrong, because "how much have I leaned on this" is the question a reader arrives with. What makes it amber is separate — `aiDebugFinding` in `renderer/lib/ai-debug-stats.ts`, an ORDERED LIST of named conditions (unreviewed applied fixes, then a high failure rate) rather than a boolean, because when failing tests start diagnosing themselves this tile becomes the supervision surface and grows a third condition. Its panels are: outcomes, what the answers CHANGED (applied / reverted / confirmed by the next run — the effectiveness signal, and the model gets no vote in it), time waiting, what that saved, why attempts failed, a two-window trend, and the most-debugged tests. `rows.tsx` holds the two rows every dashboard is built from, extracted when the fourth one landed. **A dashboard never re-derives which runs to count**: the Visual screen and its tile both call `latestCaptures`, the a11y screen and its tile both go through `selectLatestA11yRuns`, and the category head prints the summariser's own `say` — a headline assembled locally is how a tile and the screen under it come to describe different suites (it also read "1 steps carry violations", which is the same bug with better manners). Step health's three findings and a11y's four severities OVERLAP by design, and both screens say so rather than implying a partition. Every leaf row EXITS to the test or the view that owns it. Route params are strings out of history and are validated against the registry; an unknown one renders an explained empty state. Guarded by `check:stats-categories`, which pins the registry-route-tile triangle, bans cross-category arithmetic, and bans any accept/revert/delete from a Stats surface — Stats answers "how much, how often, is it getting worse"; Heals and Visual answer "what do I do about this one".
- `renderer/main/stats-view.tsx` — `StatsView` (route `/stats`). **The chart, the KPI cards and `CaptureOverheadPanel` moved into `stats/outcomes-dashboard.tsx` on 2026-08-14** — they were this page's answer to "what happened", which is that category's whole question, and the same number on two screens is two places to fix it. What stays is the run EXPLORER, which answers "find me that run" and pairs with the Manage-data menu in this header. Fetches `runs:list` (React Query `["runs"]`, live-invalidated on `runs:changed`), renders a `Table` run-history list (row → `LogInspector` Dialog showing `runs:getLog` in a mono `<pre>` with copy — the dialog lives in `renderer/main/log-inspector.tsx` since 2026-08-08 so the Batch view's row badges open the same one) + a debounced raw-log search (`runs:searchLogs`). Cleanup via a native "Manage data" menu (reuses the `nativeMenu`/`nativeShell` popup pattern): reset stats / delete all (both confirmed via a `Dialog` with `confirmVariant="destructive"`) / delete-by-date (a `Dialog` with two `NativeDatePicker` From/To → `runs:deleteRange`, `dayStartMs`/`dayEndMs`) / reveal logs folder. **Reskinned onto the theme layer in B7 (2026-08-10)**: `Panel`, `Segmented`, `StatusChip`, `Btn` and the Stats section of `renderer/theme/screens.css` replace `Toolbar`/`Table`/`Badge`/`Text`/`SegmentedControl`/`EmptyState`/`Input`/`Field`; `Dialog`, `ScrollArea`, the native-menu `Select` and the `NativeDatePicker` stay, per REDESIGN §1. Two things changed beyond chrome. The status filter's items announce as `button` with `aria-pressed` rather than as Radix `radio`, so tests drive it with a plain `click` instead of the pointer-down workaround. And the run table's Status column widened to 160px: the old SDK badge sized itself to its text, while a `StatusChip` is contractually `--gl-status-w`, so a column narrower than chip + heal chip clips rather than shrinks.
- `renderer/main/router.tsx` — Routes `/` (HomeView), `/test/$id` (TestDetailView), `/stats` (StatsView), and `/visual` (VisualView).
- `renderer/main/home-view.tsx` — `/` route, **reskinned in B1 (2026-08-10)**: the plate (radial falloff + an ember wash), the 40px wordmark with the `echo` ghosting, three readouts, and two entry points. The readouts read the SAME query keys the rest of the app does (`["tests"]`, `["runs"]`, `["heals", "all"]`) so they cost nothing and cannot disagree with the screen you navigate to; a number nothing supports renders as `—`, never 0, because "0% green" with no runs in the window is a claim about a week that did not happen. `greenRate()` is exported for its test — the empty case is the whole risk and is invisible on screen. **Each readout is a `<button>` that navigates to the view explaining it** (2026-08-10): Tests → `/batch`, Green · 7d → `/stats`, Heals to review → `/heals`, from the one `STAT_DESTINATIONS` table, whose `as const` paths TanStack Router type-checks so a retired route is a `tsc` failure rather than a dead click. A real button rather than a clickable div because tab order, Enter/Space, the announced role and the focus ring come from the tag and nothing else — `home-view.test.tsx` asserts the tag alongside the route for that reason. The accessible name is `"<label>: <value>, opens the <view> view"`, because "22 Tests" read aloud is a fact rather than a control. They stay pressable while the value is `—`. The `Btn go` reads **"Record a test"** where the mockup says "Run a test": nothing is selected here, so "run" has no object. **It no longer opens with a Toolbar** either: that one held a single non-breaking space, no text and no controls, purely to reserve the 52px the pinned sidebar toggle floated over (A4 moved the toggle into the strip). The photographic `ember` plate (`acid-25.jpg`) is still not in this repo — see REDESIGN open question 6; it drops into one `background-image` on `.gl-home-plate` when it arrives. Historically: title/description (`EmptyStateTitle`/`EmptyStateDescription`) centered above `BlackHoleLoader`, absolutely centered over the pane (not the built-in `EmptyState` `media` prop, since that renders media above the title — order needed to be reversed). Animation rendered at `size={440}`, `dark` (fixed — the app is dark only, so the light ink variant has nothing to follow), and gated behind `useDisabledEnhancements().has("homeBlackHole")` (Settings toggle).
- `renderer/main/black-hole-loader.tsx` — `BlackHoleLoader({ size, dark })`: procedural looping SVG "black hole" animation (hand-drawn ink funnel), ported from user-provided OkeeDokee exports (`Black Hole Loading Animation.zip` + a re-exported `(Dark Mode).zip`, re-diffed each time the user says they tweaked it) into a plain React component (refs + `requestAnimationFrame`, no external runtime/CDN deps, unlike the original exports which fetched React/Babel from unpkg). A `VARIANTS: Record<"light"|"dark", Variant>` map holds the things that differ between the two OkeeDokee exports (stroke/gradient color, hand-ink image — `assets/hand-ink.png` / `assets/hand-ink-dark.png`, the dark one CSS-inverted — hand clip-path percentages, and the `ix/iy/iw/ih` polygon-mapping constants); `support.js` runtime and animation math are otherwise shared. `dark` prop selects the variant; effect deps include `[variant]` so it re-inits the SVG on theme change. No hole-shadow gradient/ellipse anymore (removed per the 2026-08-04 re-export); a `LINE_THICKNESS` constant now multiplies both ring and spoke stroke-widths each frame.
- `renderer/main/visual-view.tsx` — `VisualView` (route `/visual`), the **screenshot-replay hub (Phase 2)**. Two panes: a left `RunList` (w-64) of every captured run (from `api.artifacts.list()`, newest first; subscribes to `runs:changed` to refresh) and a right `ReplayViewer` (`key`ed on runId so it remounts per run). The viewer fetches the run's `RunReplay` (`api.artifacts.getReplay`), auto-jumps to `failedIndex` (or step 0), and lets the user scrub steps via prev/next buttons, ←/→ arrow keys, or a bottom **timeline** (one clickable segment per Step[] step, colored green/red/gray by status, the failed segment taller + `TriangleAlert`). The selected step's screenshot loads on demand (`StepScreenshot` → `api.artifacts.readShot`, react-query keyed by file). Failure is made obvious three ways: a red `Callout` banner ("Run failed at step N: <label>" + "Jump to failure"), auto-jump, and the timeline marker. A step that captured no frame of its own shows the last one that did, marked (see **Carried frames** below); only a gap with nothing behind it keeps the per-step empty state ("No screenshot for this step" — assertions/waits aren't captured, or the step didn't run / capture failed). Missing artifacts are still explicit per run ("Replay unavailable"). Empty state when no captured runs exist. Reuses `Step[]` + `describeStep` labels (baked into `replay.json` by the runner) — no second step representation. The sidebar nav subtitle is now "Screenshot replay". **Phase 3 (visual diffing) additions:** each step shows a `DiffBadge` ("Baseline set" / "Visual match" / "Changed X%" / "Can't compare"); a changed step also shows an "Accept as baseline" button (`api.visual.acceptStep`) and, when a baseline exists, the screenshot pane gains a `SegmentedControl` (Current / Baseline / Diff — Diff only when `diff.diffFile`) with the baseline fetched via `api.visual.baselineShot`. The run-wide accepts ("Accept visuals" → `api.visual.acceptRun`, "Accept a11y" → `api.a11y.acceptRun`) sit in the tool band as ALWAYS-RENDERED buttons, disabled when the run has nothing to accept — they were full-width banners over the stage until 2026-08-19 (see DECISIONS: two notices restating counts pushed the screenshot below the fold, and a conditionally-mounted control would make the band reflow, which `check:narrow-layout` pins against); the panel header shows amber "N visual changes" / "N a11y issues" chips beside the verdict, and the tool band a **Threshold** slider (`api.visual.get/setThreshold`, applies to future runs); the timeline marks changed steps with an orange `Eye` + orange ring; the `RunList` shows an orange `Eye` when `changedSteps>0`. The auto-jump-to-failure effect is guarded by a `jumpedRunId` ref so accepting a baseline (which mutates the cached replay) doesn't yank the user off their current step. **Phase 4 (step notes):** a `StepAnnotation` component in the current-step-detail row shows "Add note" (empty), or the note text + Edit/Clear (present) — clicking Add/Edit opens a `Textarea` with Save/Cancel; keyed by `step.stepId` so it remounts (resetting draft state) per step. Notes are fetched per-run (`api.annotations.list`, React Query keyed `["annotations", testId, runId]`) into a `Map<stepId, Annotation>`; `upsertAnnotation` patches that cache on success. The timeline scrubber shows a tertiary `MessageSquare` icon for a noted step when it isn't already showing the failed/changed icon (those take priority), and every step's title/aria-label appends "· note" when present. **The chrome above is the shape it had, not the shape it has** — the reskin (REDESIGN §B8, finished 2026-08-17) kept every one of these capabilities and re-drew all of them. What the screen IS now: `.gl-visual`, two `Panel`s — RUNS at a fixed 268px (the list narrows to protect the PICTURE, the opposite of the Heals journal's reason) and REPLAY, which carries the test name in its header's `id` slot and the step stepper in its `right` slot. REPLAY's header `right` slot carries the run's verdict `StatusChip`, the change-count chip and the step stepper; its `id` slot carries the test name AND the run's timestamp. **That split is the layout contract, not a placement preference** — `.gl-panel-right` is `flex: 0 0 auto` and the chip inside it is unshrinkable, while `.gl-panel-id` ellipses by definition, so the name is the cell that gives and the verdict never is. It is also what keeps the tool band below a CONSTANT width: while the chips lived there, a run with findings was ~110px wider than a clean one and wrapped "Masks & baselines" onto a second row. `check:narrow-layout` §3 pins both halves. Inside REPLAY: a `.gl-visual-head` tool band (threshold left, Re-run and Masks & baselines right — it wraps, and carries no run state), `.gl-visual-notices` (the failure / visual / a11y callouts as `.gl-notice` blocks with `insetRail` tones and their own dismiss), `.gl-visual-stage` on `--gl-black` with its two frame controls in one wrapping `.gl-visual-stage-chrome` row (two pinned corners overlapped by 12px at the minimum window and one ate the other's clicks), the step row, the region breakdown, drift, the note, and the frame rail in a `.gl-frame-strip` of stated height. The threshold is a styled native `<input type="range">`; the compare-mode and page/element switches are the theme's `Segmented`; the only `go` button on the screen is `Accept New Baseline`. Listed in `check:sdk-retired`.
- `main/services/visual-diff.ts` — pure-JS page-level diff (`diffPngBuffers(baseline,next,threshold,sensitivity=0.1)`): pngjs decode → pixelmatch → `{state:"match"|"changed", ratio, changedPixels, totalPixels, diffPng}` or `{state:"unable", reason}` (dimension mismatch / decode error). No native bindings.
- `main/services/baseline-store.ts` — pinned baselines keyed by `Step.id` under `artifacts/<testId>/baseline/`; `has/readShot/set/entry/manifest/readShotDataUrl/deleteTest`. Filenames sanitized defensively from stepId.
- `main/services/visual-baseline-ops.ts` — `acceptStepBaseline` / `acceptRunBaseline`: re-pin from a run's shots AND patch the run's `replay.json` to `match`. Kept separate from both stores to avoid an import cycle.
- `main/services/run-notice-ops.ts` — `dismissRunNotice` / `restoreRunNotice` / `isRunNoticeKind`: mark one run's visual or accessibility findings banner as SEEN. Since 2026-08-19 the only surface that writes it is the test's Accessibility tab — the Visual view's findings banners were retired in favour of header chips and tool-band accepts — but both kinds stay valid: the flag is data on the run, and existing replays carry it. Deliberately not part of the two accept modules beside it, because it is the opposite operation: accepting resolves a finding and changes what every later run reports, dismissing changes one banner and nothing else. Without it, signing off on findings you hadn't looked at was the only way to clear a nagging screen. The flag lives in the run's `replay.json` (`RunReplay.dismissedNotices`) rather than in renderer state — a dismissal that returns when you select another run is not a dismissal — and pinning it to the run is sound because a finished run's findings are frozen. Guarded in the `check:visual-pipeline` dismiss section, which asserts the diffs, the pinned baseline and the unaccepted violations all survive a dismissal. Two surfaces share it — the Visual view's two banners and the Accessibility tab's — which is why the flag lives on the replay rather than in either component.
- `renderer/lib/run-verdict.ts` — the one mapping from a test's run history to its sidebar dot, in the same shape as `ai-debug-status.ts` and for the same reason (the colour IS the signal, and a wrong one is silent). `verdictsByTest(runs)` picks the newest run per test, widens it to its **batch cohort** when it has a `batchId` — the other browsers of the same batch — and grades the cohort: all passed → green; all passed with more than `HEAL_TOLERANCE` (3) `healedSteps` across the cohort → green-yellow; a third or less failed → yellow-orange; more than a third but not all → orange-red; all failed → red. At the three-browser size that is exactly 1-of-3 and 2-of-3, but the rule is a RATIO because the browser set is the user's to choose. Nothing is sticky: the verdict is recomputed from the newest cohort every time, which is how "a passing re-run turns it green again from anywhere in the app" holds without a remembered state that can get stuck. The three blend colours are `--support-green-yellow` / `--support-yellow-orange` / `--support-orange-red` in `renderer/ui/tokens.css`, keyed for Tailwind in `renderer/styles.css` — **and that keying was missing until 2026-08-14**, so those three states drew a TRANSPARENT dot for as long as the five-way scale existed: "2 of 3 runs passed" was indistinguishable from a test that had never run. `check:renderer-classes` could not see it because a literal holding exactly one class, which is missing, has nothing emitted beside it to corroborate that it is a class list; it now recognises this repo's own `support-*` vocabulary unaided. `testVerdicts` is the fuller reading — verdict, tally and tone — and `verdictsByTest` is its tone-only projection, one traversal rather than two. `groupVerdictTone` applies the same five-way scale a level up for a library FOLDER, counting TESTS rather than runs (a folder of five where one failed on three browsers is "4 of 5 tests passed"), treating a healed member as passed, and returning null when no member has ever run. Covered by `run-verdict.test.ts` + the dot cases in `library-sidebar.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/library-sidebar.tsx` — **a `Rail` since A4 (2026-08-10)**, not the SDK `Sidebar`: `--gl-rail` fill, hairline edges, mono uppercase labels, the neutral selection treatment, and rows that activate on `click`. The views nav moved OUT of the scroll area into the rail's `nav` slot — it used to be pushed down by `mt-auto`, which pins it to the bottom only while the library is short. The tests list is the scrolling body; the AI connection footer is a `RailRow` in the `footer` slot. Historically, the tests list was the sole content at the top of the scroll area (front and center); below it, a dedicated "Views" section held app-level nav items "Stats" (BarChart3 icon → `/stats`) and "Visual" (Images icon, subtitle "Screenshot replay" → `/visual`) under a "Views" label. The `Sidebar` `footer` prop contains only `AiConnectionFooter` (ML connection status), keeping the gradient-blur footer exclusive to the model status. Selected state via `useRouterState` pathname === the route. A test row carries favicon + name + host and **nothing about its run engine** — a `BrowserIcon` accessory was tried there on 2026-08-07 and removed the same day (see DECISIONS): the engine is a fact you need when you are about to run a test, which is the toolbar, not when you are looking for one in a list. It does carry two STATE indicators in the `accessory` slot (`RowIndicators`, added 2026-08-08): the AI-debug sparkle for that test's sessions (`aggregateStatus` + `toneFor`, the same tone contract as the run panel, trainer rows and global chip) and a run-verdict dot on the **five-way green→red scale** in `renderer/lib/run-verdict.ts` (`verdictsByTest` over the shared `["runs"]` query; 2026-08-09). Both answer questions you ask WHILE scanning the list — "is that job done?", "which of these is broken?" — which is what separates them from the engine. **Folders since 2026-08-14 (REDESIGN §7.2).** `libraryRows` (renderer/lib/library-groups.ts) turns the flat list into folder rows plus loose tests; a folder row is the same `.gl-rail-row` with a caret, a `SiteIcon` monogram of its OWN name, a count and the aggregate verdict in the same accessory cell — so folders and tests stack into one column with one edge. It is deliberately NOT selectable (there is no group screen, and a second `data-selected` row would make the rail's selection mean two things), and its members are not rendered at all while collapsed rather than hidden with CSS, or the row count would stop matching the count on the header. Collapsed state is `RecorderSettings.collapsedTestGroups`, written optimistically. A test's context menu gains "Move to Group", where creating and joining are the same gesture; the folder's own menu carries Rename and "Ungroup Tests", which is the only deletion there is.

### Settings

Redesigned 2026-08-07 from one flat scroll into a sidebar of eight panes with search. See DECISIONS.md for why this shape.

- `renderer/lib/settings-schema.ts` — the window's shape **as data**, pure and DOM-free so it runs in the node project. `PANES` (id/title/subtitle/group) and `paneSegments()`, which returns RUNS of consecutive panes sharing a group rather than one bucket per group — two separate stretches are ungrouped (Appearance at the top, Diagnostics + Experiments at the bottom), and bucketing by value would render the developer panes directly under Appearance. `SETTING_INDEX` maps every row to its pane, its label, its search keywords and the `RecorderSettings` key it writes (absent for credential-backed and action rows, which is what keeps "reset section" away from the API key and the webhook URL). `searchSettings(query)` is case-insensitive substring over label + keywords + the owning pane's title, every term required — substring rather than fuzzy, because a settings search that returns near-misses is worse than one that returns nothing. `SETTINGS_DEFAULTS` mirrors `recorder-settings-store.ts` (which stays the authority) so the window can say "3 settings differ from the default" without asking the backend; `modifiedKeys`/`resetPatch` build on it, with a `sameValue` that compares arrays and `{width,height}` structurally — reference equality would report an untouched `disabledAestheticEnhancements` as modified on every load. Also the five **clamps** (`clampTestTimeoutSec`, `clampRetainedRuns`, `clampRetentionDays`, `clampHealRetries`, `clampHealTimeoutMs`), lifted out of the old async handlers where the only way to test "9999 runs is refused" was to render a window and fire a change event, plus `formatBytes`. Covered by `settings-schema.test.ts` (118 tests), whose load-bearing assertion is that **every persisted default is surfaced in exactly one pane** — a key in the store with no row is unreachable, a key claimed by two panes shows two different modified-counts for one value, and both are invisible on screen.
- `renderer/settings/settings-controller.tsx` — every piece of state and every handler, lifted wholesale out of the old view. One controller rather than per-pane state because two loads have side effects that must fire when SETTINGS is opened, not when a section is looked at: the LLM auto-probe rewrites the configured model when the stored one is no longer installed, and the settings load feeds every pane's modified-count. The ~20 `useState`s for `RecorderSettings` fields collapse into one object plus `save(patch)`; the patch shape is unchanged (one key per `recorder:setSettings` call, merged backend-side), so nothing crossing IPC moved. `save` is **optimistic and does not roll back** on failure — the toast reports it, and a rollback would fight the user's next keystroke. Each load goes through a local `load()` helper whose `try` matters as much as its `.catch`: the calls are made synchronously inside the effect, so a surface that THROWS (a preload that didn't expose it) would abort the effect and skip every load after it. A **resolved** `null` from `recorder:getSettings` is coerced to `{}` for the same reason — it doesn't reach the catch, and the first pane to read a key off null took the whole screen down. `SettingsControllerProvider` is the test seam. Mounted by `SettingsScope` (below), which `RootShell` puts above the whole `SplitView` while a settings route is open — so entering Settings pays for these loads and no other screen does.
- `renderer/settings/settings-view.tsx` — **two routed screens since 2026-08-24** (docs/plans/settings-view.md): `SettingsView`, the board at `/settings`, and `SettingsPaneView` at `/settings/$pane` (with `/settings/$pane/$topic` for the Documentation pane's topic). It was one component inside its own `BrowserWindow` with its own `SplitView`, a pane held in `useState` and seeded from a URL fragment, a `Toolbar` naming the pane, and Escape-to-close. All four are the router's now: the pane is a param (so it is in the trail, in back and forward, and in the rail), the crumb names the screen (a second heading inside the pane would be the same word twice), and Escape belongs to the palette, the AI panel and every dialog — Back is what closes a view. **The board is a screen and not a redirect to the first pane**, because an address that renders nothing of its own leaves the "Settings" crumb above a pane pointing at the pane you are on, and because a card can carry `PaneDef.subtitle` — the line saying what is inside a section, which the 190px rail has never had room for. `$pane` is a string out of history and is checked against `paneById`: an unknown one gets an explained empty state, never a silent fall back to Appearance. `RoutedDocumentationPane` is the one place that knows the third level exists; the pane itself takes `topic`/`onSelectTopic` as props and stays router-free, which is what keeps `panes/*.test.tsx` able to render it against a hand-built controller.
- `renderer/settings/settings-nav.tsx` + `settings-rail.tsx` + `settings-scope.tsx` — the nav, **drawn in the app's own `Rail`** since 2026-08-24, which is REDESIGN §B4's sentence literally: "the rail becomes the settings nav while in settings (the panes *are* the navigation), and the library list hides." B4 could only buy recognition — Settings was its own window, so there was no library list to hide — and it bought it by building this from `Rail`/`RailGroup`/`RailRow`, the components the main window's library uses. Now `library-sidebar.tsx` renders `SettingsRailRows` where the library would be and `SettingsRailSearch` in the rail's pinned `search` slot, exactly as it renders `RoutinesRail` on `/batch`. `settings-nav.tsx` is presentational; `settings-rail.tsx` is the half that knows what a route is; `settings-scope.tsx` holds the controller AND the search query above the whole `SplitView`, because the rail and the content are its `sidebar` and its `children` and neither can hold what the other reads. The scope also owns the rule that spans both: a search that empties the pane you are on returns the pane to move to, and the rail navigates there with `replace: true` — a history entry per keystroke would make Back walk out of Settings one letter at a time. The row accessory does double duty: match count while searching, modified count otherwise. Panes with no match **leave the list** rather than greying, because greying keeps the list the same length and hides that search narrowed anything. Rows activate on **click** (`SidebarListItem` fired on `mouseDown` — the documented trap where `fireEvent.click` reports "0 calls"), and selection is announced by `RailRow`'s own `aria-current="true"`. The modified-count accessory is a neutral `.gl-chip`, not a blue `Badge` — colour means outcome in this palette, and a coloured count beside a pane name claims something about a result.
- `renderer/lib/settings-route.ts` — `isSettingsPath` / `parseSettingsPath`: the ONE spelling of "is this a settings screen". Three components ask — `RootShell` (which mounts the scope), `LibrarySidebar` (which swaps the rail's body) and `AppStrip` (which builds the trail) — and a rail that swapped one screen wider than the scope throws out of `useSettingsController`, which is a blank window and a clean log. It answers with raw segments and no opinion about whether they name anything; deciding that is `paneById`'s job, the same split `stats-categories.ts` uses.
- `main/services/settings-target.ts` — the application menu's half. ⌘, and the six Help items are built in the main process before any window exists, and what they open is a route: `settingsTarget("documentation/setup")` validates the segments and `main/index.ts` pushes `settings:open`, which `RootView` re-checks against `paneById` before navigating. Two questions, not one rule spelled twice — "is this a legal segment" and "is this a pane that exists" — which is why they are not shared. A menu click with no window open is HELD and replayed from `ready-to-show`, the same pattern `takePendingDeepLink` uses, because on macOS the menu outlives every window. It replaced `paneFragment`, which built a URL fragment for a `loadURL`; the judgement and its test came across unchanged.
- `renderer/settings/setting-row.tsx` — one row. `summary` is always visible; `details` goes behind a disclosure (descriptions were ~2/3 of the old window's vertical space). **`danger` split into three props in B4**, because one prop was doing three jobs: `flag` is a short red uppercase badge beside the label (WHAT this row touches), `risk` is a block of copy with a red inset rail (WHAT IT COSTS you), and `doc` is a separate link (HOW IT WORKS — a different question, and answering both behind one link means the second gets skipped). **`flag` still suppresses `details` outright** rather than trusting callers to leave it unset, and `risk` needs no such guard because it has no closed state to be in — that is the rule made structural. A flagged row draws the row-level rail only when it has NO `risk` block: the block draws the same rail one level in, and two at two indents read as a rendering glitch rather than as emphasis. `nested` indents a dependent row and draws the dependency rule. `stacked` moves the control to its own full-width line below the label, for rows whose control is a cluster rather than one switch — in the horizontal layout the two columns compete for the same width and the wide one wins. Search filtering happens HERE via `RowFilterProvider`, so a pane's JSX is the same whether or not a search is running — there is no second, filtered rendering path to drift.
- `renderer/settings/pane-section.tsx` — a titled group that removes itself when a search filters away every row it holds. Reads the `id` prop off its children rather than taking a second list of ids, which would drift. Carries the B4 reskin as two class names (`gl-setting-set`, `gl-setting-group`): a square hairline box on `--gl-panel` instead of the SDK's rounded translucent card, and a mono uppercase legend instead of a bold sentence. Applied as classes on our own components rather than by restyling the SDK's emitted Tailwind, so `check:renderer-classes` can prove both resolve.
- `renderer/settings/panes/*.tsx` — Appearance (theme, flourishes, the site-icon opt-in), Recording (trainer window), Test defaults, Auto-Heal, Storage, **Stats**, **Failure reasons**, Cost, AI, Alerts, **Proxy** (its own bullet below), **Diagnostics**, **Experiments**. **Failure reasons** (added 2026-08-19) holds the `autoFailureReasons` switch (on by default — the categorizer is the deterministic triage mapping, local, no egress, unlike every AI toggle in Settings) and the custom-reason editor: built-ins listed read-only, customs created/renamed/disabled inline (never deleted — the store refuses; see `failure-reason-store.ts`), with the shared field limits on the inputs. Covered by `failure-reasons-pane.test.tsx`. **Recording gained the "AI step suggestions" row (2026-09-01, PR 4)** — the `aiSuggestionsEnabled` switch (off by default; enabling it is the consent) with an always-visible `risk` disclosure in the alerts pane's `riskFor` shape: what a send carries (the step list's descriptions plus the bounded inventory of visible controls), what never leaves (what the user types into a field, page HTML, logs, scripts, headers, secret values), and the destination the configured provider implies — `check:agent-egress` is what keeps the first two sentences true. The last two are B4's renames: "Advanced" is a promise about difficulty and it attracts everything nobody could place, so it becomes the pane settings go to be lost in — what is actually there is a screenshot shortcut and a capture button, which is *diagnostics*. The two flags that were an "Experimental" SECTION inside the AI pane get their own pane instead: both change how a RUN behaves, and nobody asking "why did my script change?" would look for the answer under which model answers questions. A section heading is also invisible from the sidebar, so its caveat only reached someone already reading the AI pane top to bottom; as a pane the caveat is in the subtitle and both rows carry `risk`. **Appearance owns the site-icon opt-in** (REDESIGN §3.5, outstanding since the favicon egress fix): off by default, `flag` + `risk`, naming icons.duckduckgo.com and what it learns. It lives here rather than in a credentials pane because what the user is choosing between is two ways the sidebar can LOOK. **Test defaults is the pane the redesign is built around**: a third of the app's settings answer "what should a NEW test do", they map one-to-one onto `test-detail-view.tsx`'s six run controls, and they are ordered to match it — so the two read as the same panel at two scopes. The pane subtitle now carries the "each test can override this" fact that used to be repeated in half the row descriptions. Dependent rows are **unmounted, not greyed** (`record-all-headers` under `default-record-logs`; the Auto-Heal parameters under the Auto-Heal switch) — the old greyed-out siblings had nothing on screen saying which setting controlled them, and the Auto-Heal parameters were editable while writing settings nothing would read. **Cost** (added 2026-08-12) is the pane the Stats → Cost panel's two assumptions moved into, plus the currency they are denominated in and a GitHub-runner dropdown that pre-fills the price; its runner row is the one control in the window that writes a setting it does not own (`costPerCiMinute`) and stores nothing of its own, so it has a `SETTING_INDEX` entry with **no `key`** and stays out of both the modified count and "reset section". **Stats** (added 2026-08-17) is the run-history pane, and it is separate from Storage on purpose: Storage is about SCREENSHOTS (how many runs' worth per test, how old, and the bytes they cost), this is about run RECORDS, and the two failures are not alike — screenshots going early costs you pictures, records going early costs you the history every number on the Stats screen is computed from. It holds the readout that states the lifetime run total against how many records are still stored (the one place in the app that difference is named), the `runLogRetainedRuns` dial, and Reset stats / Delete stats and logs. Those two destructive controls ALSO remain in the Stats view's Manage-data native menu, deliberately: a native menu cannot be searched, so Settings could not answer "how do I clear my run history" at all, and that is a question a settings screen gets asked. Both call the same IPC handlers. How many RECORDS are kept is not a setting here — it is bounded by what rewriting the index costs per run, which is a fact about the machine rather than a preference. Storage promotes the usage figure out of the middle of a description string into the pane's first element; it is the only live number in Settings, and both its retention controls are `NumberInput` with the unit (`days`) rendered INSIDE the control — a sibling `<span>` displaced the right-aligned field by its own width and knocked the two inputs out of line with each other. Auto-Heal's two parameter controls are the same shape for the same reason (`ms`), and keep an explicit `aria-label` because `NumberInput`'s unit is `aria-hidden`. **Integrations** is the one place a toggle **confirms before turning on** (`AlertDialog`, ON only), because the webhook starts sending data off the Mac on a schedule the user no longer intervenes in; its Webhook URL row is `stacked` so the field-plus-three-buttons cluster doesn't crush the label column. That pane holds the outbound connections — the **issue tracker** (Linear or GitHub, chosen here), the webhook (moved out of Alerts), and the branch switcher's GitHub token (which had **no settings UI at all** before, reachable only from inside the branch switcher) — so "what does this app talk to?" has one answer in one place. **The tracker is chosen here and nowhere else**: it is a configuration decision made once, and the compose dialog is the wrong moment to ask. The two GitHub token fields are deliberately separate and labelled as such — see `github-issue-token-store.ts`. Every row's wording comes from the SELECTED provider's `ProviderVocabulary`, including the plural (`containerPlural`), which exists because appending an "s" was invisibly fine while "Team" was the only word this could hold and produced "3 repositorys" the moment GitHub existed. The three local notification rows deliberately did NOT move with the webhook: they send nothing anywhere, and a pane whose subject is outbound connections is weaker for listing three things that aren't; **Alerts** keeps them and now claims, per row, that everything on it is local. The connection row renders `hasKey` and `account` as separate claims, so a stored-but-failing key reads "Not connected" with the reason rather than "Connected". Its "only when you click it" sentence is in `summary`, not `details`, because `SettingRow` **drops the disclosure on a flagged row** — a credential warning must never be one click away, and copy written as `details` there compiles, type-checks and renders nowhere. Every button carries an `aria-label` naming its credential ("Save webhook URL"), since three rows otherwise announce "Save, Save, Save".

- `renderer/settings/panes/proxy-pane.tsx` — **Settings → Proxy, 2026-08-19**, modelled on the mabl Desktop App's proxy settings (see DECISIONS): **Traffic to proxy** (None / App / Test / Both — App is the app's own traffic, Test is the training browser and every test run, None ignores the configuration without erasing it), **Proxy settings source** (Automatic = the OS decides, Manual = the rows below), the manual URL/Username/Password/SSL Verify rows, and a **validate dialog** with one check per traffic class plus mabl's own caveat that a validation can disagree with a real run. In the Connections group: that group is "what does this app talk to", and this pane is HOW it talks to everything. The manual rows are **unmounted in automatic mode** (the dependent-row rule); the URL field commits on blur through `normalizeProxyUrl` and keeps a refused draft rather than wiping it; the password row is flagged (`stores credentials`), write-only, and its always-visible summary carries the MCP limitation — runs started from the MCP server can't decrypt the password and say so in their run report. SSL Verify off adds a `risk` block naming what is being given up, and only relaxes connections that actually go through the configured proxy. mabl's Save/Cancel is this window's immediate-save; its Reset is the standard Reset-section footer (the password, key-less in `SETTING_INDEX`, is excluded like every credential); its "Apply to app view" is dropped because this app's windows are served over `app://` from disk — there is no app-view traffic to proxy.
- `renderer/settings/panes/documentation-pane.tsx` + `renderer/lib/doc-blocks.ts` + `renderer/lib/docs.ts` — **the app's manual, 2026-08-13.** Before this the app had no documentation surface at all: every doc lived in the repo, and the only in-app mention of the MCP server was one clause inside the Debug screenshots row — so searching Settings for "mcp" returned a screenshot toggle. The pane renders `docs/MCP-GUIDE.md`, since 2026-08-26 `docs/CI-GUIDE.md`, and since 2026-09-01 `docs/POPUPS-GUIDE.md` itself, because two copies of a document is two documents. **`APP_DOCS` is a list and the pane is grouped by it**: one `PaneSection` per document titled with that document's label, and each topic's eyebrow is its OWN document's title — it was `APP_DOCS[0].page.title` while there was one document, which is correct exactly until there are two and then labels every CI topic as the MCP guide. **Topic slugs are unique ACROSS documents**, not within one: a slug is simultaneously the `docRowId` the search indexes under, the list's React key and the `/settings/documentation/$topic` segment, none of them scoped by document — so two files both ending in "See also" collide in all three and a Help item opens the wrong document. `check:docs-blocks` asserts uniqueness, and checks `REQUIRED_TOPIC_SLUGS` and the Help menu's links over the UNION of shipped docs rather than per file, which is the form that fails the moment a second document exists. `doc-blocks.ts` is a **subset markdown parser** that THROWS on anything it cannot draw (ordered lists, nested bullets, images, raw HTML, an undivided table); `docs.ts` imports the markdown with Vite's `?raw`, which inlines it into the renderer bundle — that is also what makes it work in a packaged app, since `build.files` ships `build/**` and not `docs/`. Parsed at module scope, so a malformed document fails loudly rather than rendering as blank space, and `check:docs-blocks` turns that into a red gate first. Topics are the `##` sections, slugged with any leading "N. " dropped so section numbering can change without breaking a link. Three things a rendered file would not give: the topics are in `SETTING_INDEX` (**derived**, not listed by hand) and therefore reachable by the settings search, indexed on **full text** — the one place in this window where a near-miss is the right answer, since "where is flake dealt with" is a question a paragraph can answer — and listed **last**, because a search that empties the open pane moves to the first pane with a hit and docs would otherwise swallow every query; they are reachable by deep link from the Help menu; and the setup topic ends with the resolved MCP server path from `mcp-install.ts`. Only https-on-github.com hrefs render as links, because that is all `shell.openExternal` accepts — an underlined thing that does nothing is worse than plain text. **Every command block is copyable, 2026-08-24.** Most of what this pane renders is commands, and none of them could leave the app: `renderer/styles.css` suppresses selection app-wide (`body { user-select: none }` — right for a step list, wrong for the one screen made of text meant to be run elsewhere), and only the machine-specific card had a button. Both halves are fixed locally: the code surfaces opt back into `user-select: text` with `cursor: text` beside it (the I-beam is the only signal a selection is possible, since `body` also sets `cursor: default`), and every fenced block is a `DocCodeBlock` — one always-present copy icon, opaque and in `--gl-panel` because the `<pre>` scrolls sideways underneath it. The card's own labelled button was removed rather than kept next to the icon: two controls copying one string reads as a fault, and having it on only that block is what taught the reader a block without one is a block to retype. `check:docs-copyable` holds the stylesheet half, which no test can — the dom project runs with `css: false`, so a computed-style assertion passes against the bug — plus the source shape (no bare `<pre>` outside the component); `documentation-pane.test.tsx` presses the buttons.
- `docs/POPUPS-GUIDE.md` — **the third in-app document, "Pop-ups, banners and dialogs" (2026-09-01)**, written for a user of the app rather than a developer, and the answer to a field report in which every mechanism reached for was the wrong one: a *Handle next dialog* step inside an `if` with Continue on Failure, against a Klaviyo form (DECISIONS 2026-09-01). Its first topic is the sorting question — a browser dialog the *Handle next dialog* step answers, or a page overlay that only *Handle pop-ups* can touch — and the rest follows: the switch at its two scopes, the built-in handlers and what each clicks, teaching a rule for any other site, the exact system lines a run prints, what travels to an unattended run (the per-test option, in the export bundle) and what stays on the Mac (the rules, the default, the handler switches), when to turn it off, and a symptom table. Registered as `APP_DOCS[2]` (`id: "popups"`), in `SHIPPED_DOCS` for `check:docs-blocks`, in `check:renderer-egress`'s shipped-docs scan (no absolute URL — the vendors are named in words), and deep-linked from the Help menu at `which-kind-of-pop-up-is-it`, `handle-pop-ups` and `pop-up-troubleshooting`; the Overlay rules pane's *Handle pop-ups* row links `handle-pop-ups` through `SettingRow`'s `doc` prop. Its closing topic is "Related reading" rather than "See also" or "Where to read more", because slugs are unique across the three documents, not within one.

- `main/services/origin-variable.ts` — **turning a test's baked-in site address into a variable (2026-08-22, R5).** `originsIn` (which origins the steps still name, the test's OWN site first), `originOf`, `isOnOrigin`, and `parameteriseOrigin`, behind `tests:originsIn` / `tests:parameteriseOrigin` and a button on the Variables tab. **Why it exists:** Playwright's `use.baseURL` resolves RELATIVE navigations only, and a recorded `goto` is the absolute URL the recorder watched — measured against real Playwright, and against this machine's library, where all 48 `goto` steps are absolute and no record carries a `baseUrl`. So the base-URL machinery cannot re-point a recorded test at staging or a preview, and R5 as the plan specifies it would change nothing. `${name}` interpolation can, and was already proven end to end; what was missing was the edit. The rewrite goes through `mapInterpolatable` — the single definition of which fields interpolate — so the fields it rewrites and the fields the generator substitutes cannot drift, and a URL assertion cannot be left pinned to production while the navigation moves. "On this origin" needs a boundary as well as a prefix (`https://shop.example.commerce.test` is a different site). Idempotent, reuses a same-named variable rather than declaring a second, and refused on a hand-edited script whose steps no longer drive the run. The IPC handler is atomic over steps AND variables: two calls would leave a window where the steps reference `${SITE_URL}` and nothing declares it.
- `main/services/mcp-install.ts` — where this install's `mcp/server.mjs` is, answered **from disk**, plus the `claude mcp add` line that registers it. **A packaged app carries the server as of R15 (2026-08-22)**, so the present case is now the normal one: `PROJECT_ROOT` resolves to `Contents/Resources/app`, which is exactly where `build.files` puts `mcp/`, and the pane's copy-command branch started firing with no change to the resolution here. Two things the packaged path changes, both invisible until pasted: the command names the app's OWN Electron binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` rather than `node` (someone who installed a `.app` has no reason to have Node), and it is SINGLE-quoted — the product is called "Good Looks!", and a `!` inside double quotes is history expansion in every interactive zsh and bash, so the old double-quoted form worked only while the path it named was a repo checkout. The absent branch is kept for a stripped dev tree and so a `build.files` regression degrades to an honest message rather than a command naming nothing.
- Descriptions use the SDK's `FieldDescription`. The old view's `<p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">` was a **dead class** — no `muted-foreground` token exists in the design system, so every description rendered at full-strength primary text, which is a large part of why the window read as an undifferentiated wall.
- **The settings window is gone** (2026-08-24). It was 760×560, min 620×420, sized so the pane beside the sidebar was wide enough for a label, its description and a control on one row. Settings is a route in the main window now, whose floor is 960×456 — see docs/plans/settings-view.md, and `e2e/ui-scale.spec.ts` for the layout question that survived the window.

### Build, test and preview tooling
- `scripts/bootstrap-worktree.mjs` — `npm run bootstrap`. Symlinks a git worktree's `node_modules` at the main checkout's, so a fresh worktree can run lint/type-check/`test:all` immediately instead of paying a multi-minute install (or, as was the case before this, running none of them: a fresh worktree has no dependency tree at all). **Refuses when the two `package-lock.json` files differ** — a shared tree across branches with different dependencies means an install on one silently rewrites the other's, which surfaces days later as an unexplained version error on a branch nobody touched. `.gitignore` spells `node_modules` without a trailing slash so the symlinked form is still ignored. **The link is not sufficient for `npm run package`**, even when the lockfiles match — see `scripts/verify-package.mjs` for what electron-builder does with it and why that is a silent failure rather than a loud one.
- `scripts/verify-package.mjs` — the two guards around `npm run package`, wired into the script itself (`--preflight` before the build, `--verify` after electron-builder) and re-run by CI on the bundle it packages. **The preflight refuses when `node_modules` is a symlink**, which is what `npm run bootstrap` leaves behind: electron-builder collects dependencies by reading `node_modules` directly, and through a link it finds the direct dependencies and nothing below them — it prints `cannot find path for dependency` naming ~80 of them and then **exits 0**, leaving 175 packages out of the bundle. The `--verify` half is the load-bearing one: it computes the runtime dependency closure from `package.json` + the installed tree and asks the finished `.app` whether each package is *resolvable* from the directory that needs it, using Node's own walk-up rule. Resolving rather than comparing directory listings is deliberate — npm hoists some packages and nests others, both layouts are correct, and a listing comparison calls the nested case a failure. It follows `dependencies` only: `devDependencies` are not shipped and `optionalDependencies` may legitimately be pruned. A short `RUNTIME_CRITICAL` list (`@playwright/test`, `playwright`, `playwright-core`, `axe-core`) is asserted on top, so the error names the consequence — these are resolved *by path* by `playwright-runner.ts`, which is exactly why no bundler can vouch for them. Guarded by `check:package-integrity`, whose fixture bundles every direct dependency and no transitive one, because that is the shape of the real bug and a weaker check goes green on it.
- `scripts/dev-app-bundle.mjs` (+ hand-written `.d.mts`) — what makes `npm run dev` **look like this app**. In development the app is not its own macOS bundle: `electron .` runs `node_modules/electron/dist/Electron.app`, and macOS takes the Dock icon and the first menu bar title from the bundle that is running — so dev showed the Electron atom under an "Electron" menu however the app named itself at runtime. `app.setName()` is not the fix, and calling it would be actively harmful here: it renames `app.getPath("userData")`, which is precisely the redirection `main/shell/user-data.ts` exists to undo. So this clones Electron.app once into `node_modules/.cache/dev-app`, rewrites `CFBundleName`, `CFBundleDisplayName`, `CFBundleExecutable` and the identifier, drops `build-icon.icns` over the bundle's icon, and re-signs; `scripts/dev.mjs` launches that binary instead of `npx electron`. **Renaming the executable is not cosmetic** — macOS falls back to `CFBundleExecutable` for the process name, so a bundle with the right `CFBundleName` and the stock executable still says "Electron" (measured, not assumed). **Re-signing is not optional**: editing `Info.plist` invalidates Electron's ad-hoc signature and Apple Silicon refuses to run the result. It costs ~0.15s and ~0 bytes because `cp -Rc` is an APFS clone, and a stamp file (Electron version + the icon's size and mtime) means it is rebuilt only when one of those changes. **Every failure returns null** and dev falls back to plain Electron — a wrong-looking icon must never be the reason the app won't start. Runs standalone (`node scripts/dev-app-bundle.mjs --force`). A packaged build needs none of this: electron-builder writes both fields from `build.productName` / `build.mac.icon`. Guarded by `check:app-identity`, which builds the real bundle and reads the plist back.
- `scripts/switch-branch.mjs` — the build half of the in-app **branch switcher**. `--repo <primary checkout> --branch <name> --out <builds root>` fetches the ref, checks it out **detached** into its own git worktree under the app's data directory, gives it a `node_modules` (symlink when the lockfiles match, `npm ci` when they don't — bootstrap-worktree *refuses* at that point, but there is no human at a prompt here), runs **the branch's own `npm run build`**, and prints `{ok, appPath}`. `--in-place` builds a directory that is already checked out, which is how "return to my checkout" recovers a checkout that has never been built. Structured events on stdout behind a `##gl-switch##` sentinel; child output on stderr, which the app renders as a live build log. Runs standalone on purpose — a build reachable only by clicking a button in the app is one nobody can debug. **A worktree, never `git checkout` in place**: switching the user's own checkout would discard or block on uncommitted work, rewrite the files of the running app, and leave no way back if the branch fails to build. It refuses to report success unless `build/main/index.js` and `build/main-window.html` both exist — an `npm run build` that exits 0 without them launches a blank window and a clean log.
- **`shell/electron` branch** — the de-Glazed tree: stock Electron + Vite + esbuild, nothing outside `node_modules`, so `npm ci && lint && type-check && test:all && build` all run on a hosted CI runner, which this branch structurally cannot do. Branched from `a61598c` (the port's real merge base, established by comparing all 199 shared source files against each candidate), so `git diff main..shell/electron` is the port itself and the 12 commits of drift arrive by cherry-pick. It carries `.github/workflows/gate.yml` (the real gate on ubuntu; end-to-end + `electron-builder` on macOS) and an `e2e/` suite that drives the actual app through Playwright's `_electron` support. Its browser preview (`renderer/dev/`) has since been ported to `main` — see the next bullet. See `docs/plans/testing-pipeline-and-preview-environments.md` for the phased plan and `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-08-07) for why.
- **The browser preview** — `npm run dev:web` runs the whole renderer in an ordinary browser tab against a fake backend, with no native shell and no build slot to take turns over. Four pieces: `preview.html` (the entry — its name is load-bearing, since the renderer build globs `*-window.html` and would otherwise ship it as a fourth window), `renderer/dev/preview-boot.ts` (installs the bridge BEFORE the app entry is imported, mounts the "fixture data, no backend" banner, and honours `?view=` / `?test=` — plus one view mounted INSTEAD OF the app, `?view=specimen`, for the primitives. **`?view=settings` used to be a second** — Settings was a separate `BrowserWindow` reachable only through `window:openSettings`, which does nothing in a tab, so it had no browser answer at all before B4. It is a route since 2026-08-24, so it is an ordinary navigation like `?view=stats`, with `&pane=<id>` for one section. Since 2026-08-12, **`?view=trainer-panel`** and **`?view=chrome`** mount the docked trainer panel and the training browser's URL strip the same way, for the same reason and one step further: the panel is a `BrowserWindow` the backend opens when a session starts and the strip is a `WebContentsView` inside the recorder window, so neither was reachable from a tab at all — which is how both stayed on the component library's stock classes long after every screen they mirror had moved to the theme. The panel preview is boxed to 360px on purpose: a tool row that wraps at 360 and not at 1400 is exactly what it is opened to check. **`?view=bar-lab`** (2026-09-01) mounts `renderer/dev/bar-mockups.tsx` the same way: three CANDIDATE action-bar directions for the trainer reorganisation, each rendered at main-pane width and in a 360px box across the states that historically reflowed the row, so a direction can be picked before any production view changes — its classes wear a `mock-` prefix, never `gl-`, because a `gl-` rule that exists only in a dev stylesheet is exactly the ambiguity `check:renderer-classes` exists to kill. Deep-link one shot with `?dir=b&state=armed`. Each re-creates the providers from its own entry point rather than importing it — those modules `createRoot` and would mount a second copy — and pulls in `styles.css`, without which the window renders as an unstyled column of buttons), `renderer/dev/preview-bridge.ts` (a `window.glazeAPI` over ~50 typed handlers, **plus `?view=recorder` since B6, and `?view=recorder-editing` since 2026-08-10 for a session CONTINUING an existing test — the only state in which the insert cursor is mid-list and therefore the only one that shows the labelled cursor,** — `RootShell` swaps the outlet for `RecordingView` only while `state.recording`, and nothing in a browser tab can make that true, so the trainer had no address at all; the flag reports a live session over a fixture test's steps, with `pageReady: true` because the view renders a "Loading page…" chip and disables every control until it is. **A push bus and a scripted run since B5a** — `on` was a no-op, which quietly bounded the preview to whatever a view renders before anything happens to it. Everything in this app with a *result* arrives by push, so "Run test" started a run that could never finish and the run panel was only ever reachable in its Running state: the failed console path could not be reviewed at all. `startFakeRun` emits `runner:output` / `runner:step` / `runner:done` on a timer so steps land one at a time, and takes its OUTCOME FROM THE FIXTURE rather than a coin flip — `t-login`'s stored run failed, so `?test=t-login` is a stable address for "show me the failed path". Steps after the failing one are left unreported, as Playwright would leave them. **`runner:stop` since 2026-08-22** cancels the scripted run's pending ticks and reports it done with a non-zero code — and deliberately no `end` for the step in flight, because that is what the real runner's SIGKILL leaves behind; a preview that closed the step tidily could never show the spinner-after-stop bug the store settles. The 260ms tick is an `installPreviewBridge` option (`runTickMs`) since 2026-08-19, because a test awaiting `runner:done` at the preview's pace inherits the fixture's step count as wall-clock — t-checkout's twelve steps meant 6.5s against vitest's 5s default, so growing the fixture grew a test toward its timeout; the bridge's tests pass 1ms and the preview keeps the pace a person can watch. Two signature details are load-bearing and both were bugs first: listeners are called `(event, payload)` because `api.on` reads `args[1]`, and the payload key is `id`, not `testId`. **`startFakeChat` since 2026-08-10** answers `llm:chat` with a canned diagnosis streamed in chunks, and that reply ENDS IN A `glaze-request` BLOCK on purpose: the panel's most intricate surface is everything that happens after the model asks for more data — the card, the fetch, the review, the two-step send — and none of it is reachable without a reply that actually contains the block. Every other way to see it needs a local model installed and cooperating, which no agent and no non-Mac reviewer has) and `renderer/dev/preview-fixtures.ts` — which since B8 carries a **captured run** (`REPLAY`), because `artifacts:list` returning `[]` meant the Visual screen, the largest file in the renderer, only ever rendered its empty state. It has one step of each diff state (match, changed, new-baseline, unable, uncaptured), since a fixture where everything matches exercises one branch of the viewer and hides four; the frames are generated SVG data URIs, because a real PNG is a binary blob nobody can diff in review and a remote image is an egress path `check:renderer-egress` bans outright. Since §6.6's drift strip it also carries `REPLAY_HISTORY` — eight earlier runs of the same test, cloned from `REPLAY` so a step added above cannot go missing from its own history — because drift is a statement about a SERIES and a fixture with one run in it renders nothing at all, the same trap as `artifacts:list` returning `[]`, one level up. It is built by `vite.config.ts` under `--mode preview`, which swaps the window entries for `preview.html`, sends the output to `build-preview/` so it can never overwrite the packaged renderer, switches `base` from `./` (file://) to `/` (served over http, deep paths), and adds an SPA fallback so any path serves `preview.html`. Under Glaze this was a second standalone config, because the SDK owned the app's build and this project had none of its own; the port gave the app a real Vite config, and a mode is all the difference that was left. **The router uses `createMemoryHistory()`**, so a URL path can never select a view; `?view=stats` / `?view=settings` / `?test=<id>` ask the router directly instead. Two things the native WebView provides that a tab does not are declared in `preview.html`: `color-scheme` (the app's `Text` resolves to `text-inherit`, so without it every label renders black on black) and an opaque backstop on `html` — behind body, never by overriding `--color-window-bg`, because every `bg-panel` surface depends on that compositing. Guarded by `preview-bridge.test.ts`. `npm run build:preview` emits a static bundle to `build-preview/`.

### Theme layer — `renderer/theme/` (the indie redesign, Phase A)

The bespoke design layer the redesign is built on. See `docs/REDESIGN.md` for the
plan it implements. A2 (tokens, fonts, atmosphere) and A3 (the primitives) landed
2026-08-08 with **nothing consuming them**, deliberately — the app was unchanged
on screen, so a regression in Phase B had exactly one candidate cause. **A4
(2026-08-10) is where that stops being true**: the shell mounts `Atmosphere`,
draws the top strip and the rail out of this layer, and retires the light theme.
The nine screens inside the shell are still the old chrome until Phase B.

- `renderer/theme/tokens.css` — the `--gl-*` custom properties, declared on
  `:root`. Real declarations, not Tailwind theme keys or borrowed SDK names, and
  that is the whole point: this repo has shipped `bg-muted` and the entire
  `border-token-*` family emitting **no CSS at all** for months (DECISIONS
  2026-08-06), because a class that does not exist and one that does are
  indistinguishable in a `.tsx`. A name we declare ourselves is a name
  `check:theme-tokens` can check. Dark only, and the absence of a `.light` block
  is the decision rather than an omission. Carries two rules that are load-bearing
  rather than cosmetic: **colour means outcome** (so selection is neutral white at
  low alpha — `--gl-sel-bg`/`--gl-sel-ring` — never a status hue it would compete
  with), and **AI is a treatment, not a colour** (`--gl-holo`, on borders and
  small marks only, never a text fill). `--gl-status-w: 78px` is a layout
  contract, not styling: it is why a column of status chips has one edge.
- `renderer/theme/fonts.css` + `fonts/` — self-hosted Space Mono and Space
  Grotesk, latin + latin-ext woff2, 92KB for all six files, SIL OFL 1.1 text
  beside them. **No network `src` fallback**: the mockup pulls from
  fonts.googleapis.com, which in a packaged app is an outbound request on every
  launch against an egress posture of one opt-in webhook (DECISIONS 2026-08-04),
  plus a blank-until-loaded flash and a hard failure offline. A missing file
  falls through to the system font instead — legible, obviously wrong to a
  designer, and sends nothing. **The 500 → 400 / 600 → 700 weight mapping lives
  in the `font-weight` RANGE descriptors**, not at the call sites: Space Mono
  ships only 400 and 700, the design leans on 500 and 600, and leaving the
  browser to synthesise means the Glaze WebView and a browser preview disagree
  about what the app looks like. Declaring the 400 file as `100 500` and the 700
  file as `600 900` makes every request match a real face, so no synthesis
  happens at all. Space Grotesk is a genuine variable font (`fvar`/`gvar`/`avar`
  verified in the woff2) and needs none of that. `@font-face` is a declaration,
  not a fetch — nothing loads until a screen actually uses it.
- `renderer/theme/atmosphere.css` + `atmosphere.tsx` — the three global fixed
  layers (grain, vignette, scanlines) and the motion floor. **Motion that reports
  something is not decoration**: a running step must still pulse under `calm`,
  so the two kinds are separated by `data-gl-motion="ambient"` rather than by
  animation name (CSS cannot select on animation-name, and threading a flag
  through every animation string gets missed on the one element nobody
  re-tested). `!important` throughout because the redesign applies animation
  shorthand inline, and a motion setting that loses to the thing it turns off is
  worse than none. **`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` forces `calm` as a FLOOR** —
  the rule the mockup omits — clamped one-directionally so `still` survives it.
  `resolveAtmo()` is the mechanism (via `useSyncExternalStore`, so the *first*
  paint is already right — with an effect, the one frame a motion-sensitive user
  sees is the animated one); the `@media` rule in the CSS is the backstop for the
  window before React mounts. The layers are **portalled to `document.body`**,
  because a `position: fixed` element inside an ancestor carrying `transform` or
  `filter` positions against that ancestor — not hypothetical in a design built
  on both. Grain is an inline `feTurbulence` data URI (~400 bytes) standing in
  for the mockup's 4.2MB PNG plate; it was always going to look like noise at 3%
  opacity, and this *is* noise.
- The three stylesheets come in through `renderer/styles.css`, which all three
  windows import — **above the `@source` lines**, because `@import` must precede
  every other at-rule and the SDK's build prepends its own two framework imports
  to that file. Below `@source` they would be dropped by any pipeline that does
  not inline them first, which is a stylesheet that silently loses the theme.
- `renderer/theme/tokens.ts` — the narrow set of tokens that a `var()` genuinely
  cannot express, and deliberately small because every value in it is a second
  copy. Three reasons a value qualifies: it gets **concatenated** (`StatusChip`
  draws `tone + "55"` over `tone + "12"`, and CSS cannot append to the result of
  `var()`), it gets **interpolated** (`Temp` mixes along a ramp, which is
  arithmetic on a colour and belongs in a function a test can call), or it is a
  **layout contract a check has to name** (`STATUS_W`). `toneSurface()` and
  `insetRail()` are the only places those two derivations happen — `Btn tone="go"`
  and `StatusChip` share them rather than each writing the hex out.
  `check:theme-tokens` pins every value against its `tokens.css` declaration in
  both directions, and separately pins that **no status hex is ever written into
  a stylesheet**, which is what keeps that single route honest.
- `renderer/theme/primitives/` + `primitives.css` — the seventeen presentational
  components (`Atmosphere` is the eighteenth, from A2), each with its own test
  file. **`ShotHighlight`** is the newest (2026-09-01, propagation's evidence
  figure: a step screenshot with the healed element boxed in the trainer's
  refine-box idiom — border, spotlight, a label pill riding the top edge —
  redrawn in `--gl-*` vocabulary. The rect is NORMALIZED 0-1 of the image, the
  one convention `ElementFingerprint.rect`, the capture manifest and the heal
  journal share, so the overlay scales with the figure and never the viewport;
  `shotHighlightBox` is exported because jsdom cannot measure the figure but
  can prove a rect becomes four percentage strings. Two visual claims kept
  deliberately distinct — a MEASURED box is solid and spotlit (a 100vmax
  box-shadow the stage clips), an APPROXIMATE one is dashed with no spotlight,
  a visibly weaker statement — and motion is decoration only: one 700ms settle
  pulse on reveal, static under `prefers-reduced-motion`, and position/size
  never animate because a box easing toward its rect briefly draws a geometry
  claim that is not true. No rect renders the bare shot — "no screenshot" is
  an acceptable state, an empty frame is not. The lit states exist only in
  `?view=specimen`, whose box sits over the frame's own drawn button so a
  geometry mistake reads as a box around nothing.)
  **`ToolTile`** came just before it (2026-09-01, the trainer action bar's tile
  — mark, name, and what it does; folded is the strip posture and the 360px
  panel's only one, and the fold is the CONTAINER's choice, because a strip
  where one tile is tall reflows mid-reach; `flex: 1 1 0` unfolded /
  `flex-basis: auto` folded, both `min-width: 0`, is the shrink-not-wrap
  contract `check:narrow-layout` §5 pins; the caret is an inline SVG so the
  primitive stays dependency-free, and the mark is the CALLER's glyph because
  which icon means "assert" is the view's vocabulary, not the primitive's).
  **`Calendar`** came before it (2026-08-17), and the only one that exists
  because a native control could not do the job: `<input type="date">` needs no
  code at all, but its popup cannot be styled, cannot show the one-off horizon
  as a visible disabled edge rather than an absence, and is not in the document
  — so nothing about the bound, the month rollover or the keyboard could be
  asserted outside an e2e run. It implements the two-dimensional keyboard
  pattern `role="grid"` promises (arrows by day and week, PageUp/PageDown by
  month, Home/End, one roving tab stop), and keeps focus and selection
  separate, or paging to next month would change what the Routine does. The
  time inputs beside it stay native precisely because they have none of those
  problems. The split between the two is not taste: everything identical on every
  instance is a named class, and only per-instance derived values are inline,
  because **an inline style is invisible to the source-level checks** and
  anything they police needs a rule with a name to point at. The ones carrying a
  real argument rather than a shape: `Temp` (the deviation ramp, §3.4 — dead
  inside ±10%, and it falls to `off` rather than fabricating a median);
  `StatusChip` (fixed width **plus `flex-shrink: 0`**, added in B7 — a width a
