  flex row can take back is not a fixed width, and the run-history table's
  status cell proved it by squeezing the chip to nothing beside a "healed" chip,
  so the row reported the heal and silently dropped the outcome; `running` takes
  the holo treatment because running is the *absence* of an outcome, not one of
  them); `StepRow` (status as
  an inset `box-shadow` rail that COMPOSES with the selection ring, so a row can
  be selected and failing at once); `MenuItem` (the consequence line renders
  unconditionally — the honest description of concurrency 8 is a failure mode
  that looks like a flaky suite); `Menu` (the box it goes in: Escape with focus
  return, outside `pointerdown`, `aria-expanded`, choose-closes — **and the
  keyboard pattern `role="menu"` promises**, added 2026-08-10 after shipping
  those roles with no key handler at all. Opens on the item in force, arrows
  wrap, Tab closes rather than walking the list); `SiteIcon` (monogram by default, favicon
  opt-in, because the mockup's per-row icon lookup is an egress path);
  `CRT` (content never treated, z-610 above the overlays).
- `renderer/main/variables-panel.tsx` — the test detail view's **Variables** tab: three `Panel`s (Variables, Reuse & identity, Datasets), each with its own control in its own header. **Redesigned 2026-09-01** (DECISIONS): the four kinds share ONE namespace and one stored list, but are DRAWN as one hairline group per kind — `Values`, `Secrets`, `Captured`, `Generated` — each stating its own storage rule and offering exactly one value affordance, so inside a group there is only one thing a control can mean. `grouped` maps each kind to its rows carrying the INDEX INTO THE STORED LIST; every edit writes back through that index, so grouping changes what is drawn and never what is saved, and `duplicateAt` stays a whole-list computation (a plain `email` and a secret `email` collide). A kind is CHOSEN on the header's Add menu and CHANGED from the row's `⋯` menu — a rare, consequential action that used to be a dropdown on every row, one mis-click from the value field; `changeKind` drops the fields the new kind may not carry, so a plaintext value cannot survive on screen under a legend promising encryption (`normalizeVariables` already refuses to store it). Only kinds in use draw a group. Renders from a local working copy rather than the record, because `normalizeVariables` drops invalid names and the record-as-draft version deleted a row the moment its name was half-typed (DECISIONS 2026-08-08). **The plaintext notice is body copy, not a callout** (2026-09-01, superseding 2026-08-17's amber `Callout`): same words, same unconditional render — "Value" is still the default kind and the decision is still made before the first row exists — but colour on this tab is now reserved for the two tiers a `Flag` carries, `blocking` (an invalid or duplicated name; the list is held back from the backend until it is fixed) and `action` (it saved and the next run goes ahead wrong: a secret with no stored value that steps already read, or basic auth naming a secret that is gone). Everything merely true — a stale session, an orphan flow parameter — is dim prose. Each group also restates its own kind's rule in its legend, where the choice is made. **Reuse & identity** gathers the three things that are not variables (Reusable flow, Login session, HTTP basic auth) as partitioned subgroups; basic auth stays beside the list because its password IS a Secret variable by construction. **Datasets is a table** — one column per non-secret variable, one row per dataset row — because the question the section answers ("what is `email` on each row") is a column, and the card-per-row layout could only answer it by reading every card. Guarded by `check:variables`, `check:basic-auth`, `check:renderer-classes` and `check:theme-tokens`; covered by `variables-panel.test.tsx`, whose native-menu harness answers `glazeAPI.Menu.popup` so the Add and row menus run their REAL handlers.
- `renderer/components/variable-picker.tsx` — **picking a variable, and declaring one, from wherever a step is being built (2026-08-17).** `varRef(name)` is the ONE place the renderer spells `${name}` — the generator's `VAR_REF_RE` is the authority on reading it, and two spellings of one reference is how a step comes to interpolate nothing. `MASKED_VALUE`/`hasMaskedValue` are the trainer's rule that a variable's value is never displayed — chips carry `****` for any variable that has one (a secret always, since the renderer cannot read the encrypted store and "no value" would be the wrong claim), and the absence of the marker means there genuinely is none. `insertAtCaret` splices a reference into a draft (clamped, because the caret is read before a native menu opens and the draft can be shorter by the time the pick returns). `VariableChips` renders one button per declared variable, with `selected` making it a choice (the fill composer, where one variable IS the value) or omitting it for an insert (the inline editor, where nothing stays chosen). `NewVariableForm` declares one without leaving the step: name, kind (**Value or Secret only** — "captured" is created BY a capture step, and offering it here would declare a name nothing ever writes to), value, and the **plaintext warning**, which appears the instant the kind is Value because that is where a password gets typed for the first time. It validates the name shape locally so the common typo is caught before the round-trip, and otherwise shows what the backend said rather than swallowing it — a rejected create that looks successful leaves the user picking a variable that does not exist. Three hosts: `step-composer.tsx` (the `fill` kind), `edit-steps-view.tsx` (toolbar), and `step-row.tsx` (`varRef`/`insertAtCaret` only — the row is a single input on a 360pt line, so it uses a native menu instead of chips). Covered by `variable-picker.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/step-composer.tsx` — **the inline step composer, C §6.2, 2026-08-12.** Was `add-step-dialog.tsx`. Composing a step by hand now happens IN THE LIST, at the insert cursor, in both the main window (`recording-view.tsx`) and the trainer panel (`trainer-panel-view.tsx`). The cursor was added so a step could be placed somewhere other than the end, and the control for placing it then covered up the answer to "where is it going?" — composing in place makes the position part of what you are looking at while you fill the fields in. Both call sites render it through a `composerAt(index)` helper rather than hoisting one element out of the tree, because "between step 3 and step 4" is a position in the list's map, not a place in the component tree; it renders at most once, since `state.cursor` is a single index, and it has a home in the EMPTY list too (the only way to put a step into a session that has captured nothing). **Eleven kinds since 2026-08-17** — `fill` joined them, and it is the only one whose whole purpose is what it *doesn't* store: a target element plus a chosen variable, emitted as `{type:"fill", locator, value:"${name}"}`, so a password reaches the browser from the encrypted store at run time and never enters the spec. It is what the training browser's right-click **"Use variable…"** opens (a `fill` `ContextAction`, element already resolved), and it carries the inline **New variable** form, because the trainer cannot reach the Variables tab and a new recording has no record for that tab to write to. Both come from `renderer/components/variable-picker.tsx`; the variables themselves arrive as a prop, since the three hosts read them from different places (the trainer from `RecorderState.variables`, Edit Steps from the record). **The other ten kind forms are unchanged and so are their tests** — the plan's "removes 1,170 lines" does not survive contact and should not: those lines are ten step kinds times their fields, each with behaviour behind it, and deleting them to hit a number would be deleting the feature. What changed is the frame, which had to grow back three things Radix was providing: **Escape** bound on the panel (without it the only way out of a half-filled composer is the mouse); **Add disabled until `build()` returns steps** (a modal can afford an always-enabled confirm that does nothing, because it stays open and "nothing happened" reads as "I have not finished" — a panel in the list cannot, and a button that silently declines is indistinguishable from a broken one); and a **max-width on the body**, since the panel inherits the step list's width where the dialog was `size="large"`. It also exposed a latent bug: the reset effect cleared `locator`, which `TargetElementPicker` — a child — had just seeded from the picked element, and a child's effects run before its parent's. The reset is gone; both call sites `key` the composer on the kind and the picked element, so every open and re-target is a fresh mount and `useState(null)` is the reset. Covered by `step-composer.test.tsx` (the ten forms, plus a `describe` for the panel frame) and by the context-action tests in `trainer-panel-view.test.tsx`, which now query `[data-gl="step-composer"]` rather than `role="dialog"`. **A download kind (2026-08-19):** "Expect a download" emits Playwright's blessed two-half idiom — the `waitForEvent` promise ARMED before the triggering step (an arming pre-pass in the generator places it; structural neighbours refuse and fall back to in-place), awaited-and-asserted after; filename contains/exact, optional save-to-`V` with the `repeatVar`-style identifier gate. Recorded automatically: the recorder listens for the session's `will-download` (filtered to the training webContents, unhooked via `stopPolling`), CANCELS the transfer, and inserts the step with the reported filename through the `insertStep` normalize funnel. The preview replays it as a narrated no-op — verification happens on runs. Covered by `download-emission.test.ts` (ordering, scoping, numbering, round-trip incl. the try-recursion's braceless spelling, fixed point), `step-composer-download.test.tsx`, download rows in `step-row.test.tsx` and `step-replayer-parity.dom.test.ts`, and boundary rows in `check:step-ingest`; rationale in the DECISIONS entry. **A twelfth kind, `loop` (2026-08-19):** "Repeat steps" inserts a `loop`/`endLoop` pair the way the condition kind inserts `if`/`endif` — count clamped [1, 500] here, at the boundary, and in the generator (three independent guards on a numeral that lands in the spec bare). The generator emits a real `for` with depth-derived variable names (`i`, `i2`, …), repairs stray or unclosed halves so the spec can never become a SyntaxError, and the parser reads blocks back through a KIND STACK (endif for `if`, endLoop for `for` — one counter cannot tell the interleavings apart). Loop rows: count inline-editable, halves excluded from disable/wrap/lone-replay, preview narrates that the body runs once. Covered by `loop-emission.test.ts`, `step-composer-loop.test.tsx`, the loop rows in `step-row.test.tsx`, depth cases in `describe-step-depths.test.ts`, and replayer rows in `step-replayer-parity.dom.test.ts`; see the DECISIONS entry for why balance is the design center. **The Run-flow kind binds arguments as of 2026-08-19:** selecting a flow with declared parameters renders one field per parameter (`FlowArgsFields`), the flow's own default shown as placeholder text, and only filled-in fields land in `flowArgs` — the blank-means-use-the-default rule lives in `collectFlowArgs` and is what keeps an empty field from overriding a default with an empty string. Drafted values reset when the flow changes. Covered by `step-composer-flows.test.tsx`, which drives the native-menu Select through a `Menu.popup` stub. **The condition kind carries an ELSE checkbox (2026-08-19):** ticked, one submit inserts `if`/`else`/`endif` together — there is no lone insertable else, pairing by construction. The `else` step itself is empty; its meaning is positional. The generator's `blockKinds` stack refuses a stray, second, or loop-innermost else as a `commentSafe` comment; the parser accepts `} else {` only atop an un-elsed if; live replay lands ON the else when the condition was false and jumps else→endif when it held (`matchingElseIndex`/`matchingBlockIndex` in `recorder-service.ts`). Covered by `else-emission.test.ts`, `block-index.test.ts`, `step-composer-else.test.tsx`, else rows in `step-row.test.tsx`/`describe-step-depths.test.ts`/`step-replayer-parity.dom.test.ts`; rationale in DECISIONS. **The match-count gate (2026-08-22):** the composer asks the page how many elements the locator it is about to submit matches (`api.recorder.countMatches`, re-asked on every emitted locator, skipped when it carries `nth`) and refuses Add — with the count, `ambiguousTargetHint` — when it is more than one. Only MORE than one blocks: zero may be a hand-typed locator for an element the page shows later, -1 is the page failing to answer, and neither is a verdict. Distinct from the context picker's readout, which prices the semantic base plus context; this prices the locator the step will hold, whichever candidate is selected. Covered by `step-composer-count-gate.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/flow-args-fields.tsx` — the flow-argument form shared by the composer (at insert) and the step row's **"Edit Flow Arguments…"** kebab dialog (afterwards), plus `collectFlowArgs`, the one place the blank-means-absent rule is spelled. The dialog fetches the flow list itself because the parameter list may have changed since the step was inserted; saving prunes arguments for parameters the flow no longer declares, and a flow that is gone (deleted, or unmarked as a flow) gets a plain explanation with Save disabled. The kebab patch flows through `recorder:updateStep`, which re-normalizes `flowArgs` (the one map-valued field a step patch can carry) rather than allowlist-copying it. Covered by `flow-args-fields.test.tsx` and the flow-arguments describe in `step-row.test.tsx`; the generator side of the same blank-vs-absent contract is pinned by `main/services/flow-binding.test.ts`.
- `renderer/main/edit-steps-view.tsx` — **Edit Test → Edit Steps: the step list editor for a saved test, with no browser open.** A local DRAFT of the `Step[]` (drag-to-reorder, inline edit, delete, the locator-free "+ Add step" menu — Go to URL, Wait, Set viewport, Scroll to position, Press key, Assert page URL/title — and the inline New variable form), committed through `tests:updateSteps` on Save and discarded on Cancel. **New steps land at the INSERT CURSOR since 2026-09-02** — the same `CursorGap` between rows the two trainers render, with the same `INSERT_HERE` label everywhere except the end of the list — not at the end: this is the editor for a test recorded months ago, where the missing step is almost never the last one, and until then every add appended and the user dragged the row up into place. The cursor is a gap index into the draft and follows the trainer's rules: it opens at the end (a saved test is not being executed, so there is no "where the browser is" for it to open at, as there is when a test is continued in the trainer); an add splices there and advances it past what landed, so two adds keep their order; a deletion above it pulls it back so it still names the same gap; a reorder leaves it alone, as the backend's `reorderStep` does. The arriving step gets `justAdded`, so StepRow scrolls it into view. Both mutations read the draft from the render closure rather than inside a `setDraft` updater, because the cursor has to move in the same event as the list and an updater must stay pure. Pinned by `edit-steps-view.test.tsx` ("placing a new step") and by `check:insert-cursor` §6.
- **Baseline provenance — C §6.6, 2026-08-12.** `renderer/lib/baseline-provenance.ts`, rendered into `CRT`'s `caption` by `useBaselineCaption` in `visual-view.tsx`. The screen asked the user to judge a frame against a baseline and said nothing about the baseline; "these two differ" means something entirely different depending on whether it was pinned yesterday from the same engine or months ago from another. **Three of the plan's six fields do not exist in this app and were not invented**: no COMMIT (nothing reads the user's repository), no WHO ACCEPTED (single-user, no identity), no VIEWPORT (not on `RunRecord`, and a test can resize mid-run so there is no single one to report). What ships is the run, the age, the engine, headed/headless, element-scoped, and **whether the run still exists** — retention prunes run history while a baseline outlives it, so a baseline can be valid and untraceable, which bounds how far the comparison can be trusted. An absent `runBrowser` reads as chromium for a run that EXISTS (history, not a default) and as `null` for one that is gone; the line says "run since pruned" rather than dropping a field, since a provenance line with a hole reads as a rendering bug. Shown only where the baseline is on screen — under the current frame or the diff map it would attribute one frame's history to another, and in Blink it appears only on the baseline half. Stale (30 days) marks amber and only the line, not the frame: an old baseline is not a fault, it is a prompt to look. Covered by `baseline-provenance.test.ts` (the sentence itself, not the DOM) and a `describe` in `visual-view.test.tsx`.
- **The weekly read — C §6.5, 2026-08-12.** `renderer/lib/weekly-digest.ts` (pure) + `renderer/main/digest-panel.tsx`, rendered ABOVE the Stats scroller and outside it. **A READ, NOT THE MOCKUP'S DIGEST PREVIEW**: that sat beside the delivery channels §7.3 removed, and with delivery gone a preview previews nothing. What survived is the question — six panels on that screen are tables and breakdowns answering things the reader already knew they wanted, and none said how the week went. **A quiet week is not a good week**: with no runs it says "Nothing ran this week", never "0 failed" or "all passed", which are true of an empty set, read as good news, and describe the failure mode the app exists against. The week-on-week comparison is what makes it weekly and is OMITTED for a first week rather than compared against zero (which reads as explosive growth and is really a statement about the app being new). Failures are counted per TEST — one test failing three times is one problem, three tests failing once is three — with two named and the rest counted, since a list of five names is a table written in prose and the real table is further down. Flake reuses §6.4's `flakeRuns` rather than adding a second definition; **that function wants ONE test's history, OLDEST FIRST**, and sorted the other way it silently finds nothing (`cost-model`'s own `realRuns` re-sorts for the same reason). The panel renders nothing with no history at all, but a suite whose history is all older than a week still gets it — "nothing ran this week" is the most useful thing it can say about a suite that has gone quiet. Covered by `weekly-digest.test.ts` and `digest-panel.test.tsx`.
- **Stats → Report, the Export panel — C §6.5, 2026-08-12.** `main/services/report-emitter.ts` (gather, redact, save), the `report:emit` IPC channel, and `renderer/main/report-panel.tsx` under Cost in Stats. The panel renders `shared/emitters.mjs`'s own `EMITTERS` array rather than a copy, so an emitter added to the module cannot be forgotten in the UI. **THE RENDERER NEVER HOLDS THE EMITTED TEXT, and that is the shape of the feature rather than an implementation detail**: `redactWithSnapshot` reads an encrypted store that cannot leave the main process, so a channel returning bytes would move the un-redacted payload across the IPC boundary first and leave the redaction applied to a copy. The channel is therefore a VERB — it answers with a path, a byte count and a row count. `check:emit-redaction` pins all three halves: every emitter call in the service passes `redact: redactWithSnapshot`, none reaches for `NO_REDACTION` (the escape hatch that exists for the MCP and for tests, and which would satisfy the first check while defeating it), and no `report:` channel returns text; it strips comments before that second scan, because the first version went red on the service's own comment explaining the rule — a guard that fires on the sentence documenting it teaches people to delete the documentation. `listLive` rather than `list`, because a run whose test was deleted has its name dropped and a report row reading "undefined" is worse than one fewer row. A cancelled save is an ANSWER and raises no toast; "last written" is keyed per emitter, since one shared slot would make a second export look like it replaced the first. The mockup's PDF and "public link" chips and its channel toggles are not built — see §6.5. **Scoped and unattended as of 2026-08-21 (R1).** `buildReport(emitterId, scope)` is the gather, and it takes an `EmitScope` — `{testId?, batchId?, since?, until?, runIds?}`, ANDed, with `{}` still meaning the whole live history so existing callers are unchanged. The scope reaches the SQL in `stepDurations` rather than filtering its output, because those rows come back aggregated per step and a post-filter would leave the surviving medians computed over runs outside the scope; an EMPTY `runIds` selects NOTHING rather than widening. Two destinations share `writeEmitted`: the save dialog, and `emitReportTo(emitterId, absolutePath, scope)` — main-process-only and deliberately NOT an IPC channel, because a channel taking a path would let the renderer choose where bytes land, which surrenders the same property the verb shape protects. `check:emit-redaction` grew with it: it now scans every `.ts` under `main/` for a VALUE import of the emitters (a type-only `EmitterId` import is not runtime reach) and asserts `emitReportTo` takes no redactor from its caller. Behaviour covered by `report-emitter.test.ts`, the SQL by `check:metrics-db` §5c. **A failure now says which step (2026-08-21, R13):** `RunRecord.failedStepLabel` is persisted (previously a local in `playwright-runner`, used by the notification and the alert and then discarded), `report-emitter` resolves `failureReasonId` to a NAME because the pure emitters cannot reach the custom-reason store, and all three run-shaped emitters replace the old `exit N · log <absolute path>` body — a path naming nothing on any other machine — with the step and the reason. The LOG is still not inlined, and `firstErrorLine` was left out for the same reason: it is log content. The label is redacted in all three, because a `fill()` step's label contains its value. Covered by `report-panel.test.tsx` and `check:emit-redaction`; the preview bridge answers `report:emit` with the CANCEL the real dialog reports, because a browser tab has no filesystem and a fake success would put a path on screen naming a file nobody can open.
- **The emitters — C §6.5 / §7.3 (MCP Phase 5), 2026-08-12.** `shared/emitters.mjs` + hand-written `.d.mts`, tested from `main/services/emitters.test.ts`. Five formats, six `EMITTERS` rows (NDJSON and CSV are separate rows over the same `step_metrics` data): JUnit XML, GitHub Actions annotations, ticket markdown, OTLP JSON trace, and NDJSON/CSV of `step_metrics`. **EMIT, NOT SEND, AND THE MODULE IS WHERE THAT IS ENFORCED** — every function returns a STRING, so there is nowhere in it to open a socket, store a credential or know a hostname; that is REDESIGN §10's open question 3 answered by construction rather than by policy. Redaction is passed IN as a parameter because `redactWithSnapshot` reads an encrypted store and must stay app-side; importing it would make the module impure and untestable at once. In `shared/` because the app and the standalone MCP both emit and §7.3 says to build them once — hence `.mjs` + `.d.mts`, since the app is bundled TypeScript and the MCP is plain ESM with no build step. The test lives under `main/` for the reason `branch-paths.test.ts` gives: vitest's node project globs `main/**/*.test.ts`, so a test beside the module would match NEITHER project and pass by never running. `EMITTERS` is exported as DATA and the panel will render it, so an emitter cannot be added to the module and forgotten in the UI; `risk` is set on the three that carry more than outcomes (OTLP's span attributes include the URL under test; NDJSON/CSV are unaggregated per-step rows) and stated per-emitter rather than once at the top, since a general warning about "exports" is not read as being about the row under the cursor. The mockup's PDF and "public link" chips and its channel toggles are NOT built — see §6.5. ~~**Not yet wired to any surface**: the Export panel and the weekly digest are the remaining halves of §6.5.~~ **Corrected 2026-08-21 — the Export panel shipped the same day this entry was written** (2026-08-12, the entry directly above), so "not yet wired to any surface" was stale within hours and stayed that way for nine days; `docs/QA-KNOWN-GAPS.md` carried the same claim. The weekly digest is the remaining half of §6.5. ~~**The gap that IS real, and is what the stale line was hiding:** the emitters have no NON-INTERACTIVE caller.~~ **Closed 2026-08-21 by R1** — `emitReportTo` writes to a path without a dialog (see the Export-panel entry above). Until then `emitReport` opened `dialog.showSaveDialog` before writing anything, so producing a file needed a running app, an open window and a person choosing a path, and no scheduled routine, headless run or CI job could obtain one. What is still missing is a CALLER: nothing in the app writes a report on its own yet, which is R64. See [plans/test-runner-improvements.md](plans/test-runner-improvements.md).
- **The job ticker — C §6.8, 2026-08-12.** `renderer/lib/job-ticker.ts` (the reading, pure) + `renderer/main/job-ticker.tsx` (the subscription and markup), filling the second of the two slots `top-strip.tsx` shipped empty in A4. **Its data source is `recorder-store`** — REDESIGN §10's open question 2, answered from the codebase: that store already owns the live run map and is the one provider mounted for the whole session, and its own `runs:changed` comment argues that a subscription on a ROUTE component is not listening on any other route. **Which made the batch half a bug fix:** `batch:progress` had exactly one subscriber, `batch-view`, so a batch you started and walked away from was invisible everywhere else, and the `batch:done` cache invalidation only fired if you happened to be looking at that screen. Both moved onto the store; the view keeps its own `batch`, which answers a different question ("the record I am displaying", possibly a historical one). Five readings: one run (named — with one job the name IS the information), several (counted — five names do not fit and a truncated list lies about which five), batch (which outranks its own member runs, since they are in the run map too), a held failure, and idle, which renders **nothing** — an "idle" chip is a permanent word in the chrome that is true and useless, and the ticker's PRESENCE is the signal. Every label leads with its state because the strip truncates: the first version put the verb last and FAILED was the first thing cut. `RunInfo` gained `finishedAt` for the hold — measured from `startedAt`, a run longer than the window is expired before it finishes, which kills the one notice the feature exists to give. The component owns a 1s interval solely so the hold can expire on an otherwise idle app. The pulsing dot is deliberately NOT `data-gl-motion="ambient"`: `atmosphere.css` already ruled that motion which reports something is not decoration, naming this exact case. Covered by `job-ticker.test.ts`, `job-ticker.test.tsx` and new `describe`s in `recorder-store.test.tsx`.
- **The region breakdown ("what moved") — C §6.6, 2026-08-12.** Backend clustering in `main/services/visual-diff.ts`, wording in `renderer/lib/diff-regions.ts`, boxes and the ranked list in `visual-view.tsx`. A diff map lights every changed pixel with equal weight, so a paragraph of font smoothing and a button that moved 40px look identical; this turns the same pixels into measured boxes, ranked by how much of the change each holds. **A COARSE GRID, NOT PER-PIXEL CONNECTED COMPONENTS** — per-pixel components on a 1280×3000 screenshot produce hundreds of antialiasing specks, which is the noise the feature exists to see past, reproduced in a new shape and ranked above real change; snapping to a grid first merges a paragraph's smoothing into one region and keeps a moved button its own, at 40×95 cells rather than 3.8M pixels. The changed-pixel mask is read back off the overlay pixelmatch already drew — it writes unchanged pixels as GREYSCALE and changed ones in a marker colour, so "r, g and b are not all equal" identifies them exactly; both marker colours are passed explicitly rather than defaulted, because that invariant is the only thing making it safe. The flood fill is iterative (a full-page change is one component over every cell, which is a stack overflow as recursion) and four-neighbour (diagonal contact reads as two things to a person). Capped at `MAX_REGIONS`, with the remainder COUNTED rather than dropped. `diff-regions.ts` supplies the place ("top left", "centre") from each box's CENTRE — a corner reading calls a change through the middle of the page "top" and sends the reader to the wrong place — plus the summary sentence and `dominantRegion`, which names a lead only when one box holds most of the change. Boxes render on the DIFF map only, since Current and Baseline are the frames being judged; picking a list row switches mode, highlights, and scrolls the box into the CRT's own scroll container (not `scrollIntoView`, which walks every ancestor and slid the page out from under the pointer). Covered by `visual-diff.test.ts`, `diff-regions.test.ts` and a `describe` in `visual-view.test.tsx`.
- **`CRT`'s plate — the frame-overlay alignment fix, 2026-08-12.** `renderer/theme/primitives/crt.tsx` now wraps the image and its `children` in `.gl-crt-plate`, a box that shrinks to the image. Everything callers put in `children` — masks, the element-scope outline, §6.6's region boxes — is stored in normalized (0–1) coordinates, so "50% down" has to mean half way down the PICTURE. They were previously laid beside the bezel and positioned against the pane, which is a scroll viewport: a full-page screenshot is routinely three times its height, and every overlay drifted by however much the frame overflowed. `StepScreenshot`'s own comment had asserted "the wrapper shrinks to the image" since B8; this makes that true. Found by looking at §6.6's region boxes in the browser preview, not by any test — jsdom has no layout engine, so nothing in the gate can see it.
- **Baseline drift — C §6.6, 2026-08-12.** `renderer/lib/baseline-drift.ts`, rendered by `StepDrift` in `visual-view.tsx` as a 20px strip under the step row. Every other mode on this screen answers "did this frame change?" for ONE run; nothing in the app could answer the one that follows — is it changing *repeatedly*? Those have different fixes: a frame that changed once is a change to look at, a frame over threshold in six of the last ten runs is a baseline nobody re-pinned, and reading it one run at a time makes one standing problem look like six small ones. **A run with no reading is a gap, never a zero** — a skipped step, capture off, a run predating the step or an `unable` comparison measured nothing, and a zero-height bar says the frame was identical, which is a claim nobody made. That distinction is pure geometry inside a 20px strip and **it shipped collapsed once already**, with `MIN_BAR` flooring a bar at one pixel — indistinguishable from the dash meaning no reading; `check:drift-gap` now pins both halves of the fix (a floor thick enough to read as a bar, and a gap marker lifted clear of the baseline bars stand on), because jsdom has no layout engine and the `dom` project runs with `css: false`. **A single change is never drift**, which falls out of `MIN_RUNS_FOR_DRIFT` and `DRIFT_SHARE` together rather than out of a branch — one edit, one moved frame, one re-pin is the ordinary case and the thing this most easily cries wolf about — so a test pins it across the whole window range. Bars scale to the window's own peak, not to 100%: diff ratios here are small enough that a full axis draws every bar as the same flat line. Only `drifting` takes a colour, in the amber the changed bars already use. Costs one replay read per run in the window, sharing the `["replay", …]` cache the viewer already fills and keyed per RUN, so moving between steps is free — which is what separates it from the region breakdown, the remaining §6.6 piece, which needs pixel analysis that does not exist. Covered by `baseline-drift.test.ts`, `check:drift-gap` and a `describe` in `visual-view.test.tsx`; `renderer/dev/preview-fixtures.ts` grew `REPLAY_HISTORY` so the strip is visible in the preview at all.
- **Carried frames — a gap in the filmstrip, 2026-08-24.** `renderer/lib/carried-frame.ts` (pure: which frame, and why), `CarriedBar` + the fallback branch in `StepScreenshot`, `.gl-visual-carried*` in `screens.css`. Only page actions capture a screenshot — `replay-builder.ts`'s `captureMethod` is the authority, and it returns null for scrolls, assertions, waits, `page.keyboard.press` and `if`/`endif` — so a recorded test routinely has runs of steps with no picture, and the viewer answered every one of them with an empty box: scrubbing 4 → 5 → 6 → 7 blanked the screen twice between two frames that differ by almost nothing. A gap now shows the nearest EARLIER captured frame, which is the last known visual state. **The label is the load-bearing half, and it is why this is a module rather than four lines inline.** Everything in a `CRT` here is evidence and the question being asked of it is "does this look right?", so an unqualified frame from another step is the app answering with the wrong picture — and the four reasons a step has none are not interchangeable: "captures nothing" (a scroll), "the capture failed", "the run died here", "never ran" support different readings of the same image. The failing step needs its own wording and cannot be inferred from anything else: capture happens only AFTER an action resolves, so a click that threw has no manifest entry and no `actionIndex` — identical, field for field, to a scroll — which is why status is asked before `actionIndex`, and `if`/`endif` before both (`buildReplay` records control flow as "skipped", which would report "this step didn't run" about a branch that ran). **And every note says the step's own effect is NOT in the picture**: screenshots are viewport-only (nothing in this app passes `fullPage`), so a `scroll to (0, 670)` step carries the PRE-scroll viewport, and a carried frame someone misread would be worse than the empty box it replaced. The marker is chrome under the bezel, never on the frame (`check:crt-untreated`), and NEUTRAL — colour on this screen means outcome, and amber here would read as the visual-change warning it means everywhere else. Carried frames carry NO overlays: masks, the element-scope box and diff regions are all normalized against THIS step's capture and would land wherever two frames happen to line up. `current` mode only — baseline and diff are comparisons pinned to a step, and both are already gated on `step.screenshot`. Covered by `carried-frame.test.ts` and a `describe` in `visual-view.test.tsx`; the preview fixture grew a two-step gap bracketed by captures, and `artifacts:readShot` now stamps each frame with its FILENAME — every run frame rendered the same placeholder, so a step showing another step's picture looked identical to one showing its own, which is exactly what this does on purpose.
- **Wipe and Blink — C §6.6 first slice, 2026-08-12.** `CompareShot` in `visual-view.tsx`, over the pure `renderer/lib/visual-compare.ts`. Two compare modes that need BOTH frames at once, which is why they get their own component rather than a branch inside `StepScreenshot` — that one resolves a single `src` from the mode, and threading a second query through it would make every single-image mode pay for a fetch it does not use. The overlays that ride on the frame (element-scope box, mask layer) are built once and handed to whichever component the mode selects, since duplicating them into both branches is how the two would drift. **Neither frame is treated**: wipe uses `clip-path`, never opacity, and blink swaps a whole frame rather than cross-dissolving — the premise of both modes is that any difference on screen is a difference in the page, and a partly-transparent layer invents one. **`blinkIntervalMs` returns null under reduced motion, which makes Blink a MANUAL toggle rather than a stopped one**: the alternation is not decoration on top of the information, it IS the information, so the capability stays and only the involuntariness goes. `clampWipe` keeps the divider off both edges (`WIPE_MARGIN_PCT`) because flush to one there is no handle left in the frame to drag it back with; `wipeFromPointer` guards a zero-width box, which is the frame's real state for one paint before its image loads and would otherwise make the first drag of every session jump; `wipeAfterKey` drives it from the keyboard. Both modes are offered only when a current frame exists — a mode whose empty state is "both have to exist" is one that should not have been offered. Covered by `visual-compare.test.ts` and a `describe` in `visual-view.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/cost-panel.tsx` + `renderer/lib/cost-model.ts` + `renderer/settings/panes/cost-pane.tsx` — **Stats → Cost, C §6.4, 2026-08-12.** CI cost savings, manual testing avoided, debugging avoided (the AI Debug savings — `summariseSavings` with a second reader, never a re-derivation — in time until the user states an hourly rate, then money), failures caught and what flake cost, over a spend-by-test table with an earning/review verdict, **each figure revealing its arithmetic with live operands on hover/focus** (the stat-card derivation convention: note and math share one grid cell and swap by `visibility`, so the slot is pre-sized and nothing moves; both spans are always in the DOM, which is how jsdom tests pin the strings), **paged at `DENSE_PAGE_SIZE` (25)** the same way Step health is. Everything is derived from the run history and **two stated assumptions** — price per CI minute, minutes per manual run — which are rendered ON THE PANEL in prose under the figures they produce, and **set in Settings → Cost**, where they persist along with a currency. The panel's first design edited them in place and threw them away on reload, on the argument that a Settings row makes a reader of the figures hunt for what produced them; what that missed is that an assumption you retype on every visit is one nobody sets twice, so the panel was in practice always read at the shipped guess. The sentence stayed, the editing moved, and the button beside the sentence deep-links to the pane — `navigate({ to: "/settings/$pane", params: { pane: "cost" } })` since Settings became a view, where it was `window:openSettings("cost")` applied as a URL fragment on a fresh window. The Cost pane offers **GitHub's published runner rates as a pre-fill**: the spread between a Linux 1-core runner and macOS is 31×, so a default nobody can correct is wrong by an order of magnitude for anyone on the wrong hardware. **The chosen runner is DERIVED from the price** (`runnerForRate`), never stored beside it — storing both is how a pane comes to read "Linux 2-core" over a price that is nothing of the sort. Two refusals survive from the first design and one was lifted: **time is still never converted to money uninvited** (the one gate is the hourly rate in Settings → Cost, where zero means "don't say"; the Debugging avoided tile prices its net time only through it), and it still says **"failures caught"** rather than "regressions caught"; but **the no-currency-symbol rule is gone**, because it existed only while the app had never been told a currency — `none` is on the picker and still prints bare numbers. `formatSpend` takes the currency and puts the symbol inside the `<` (`<$0.01` is "less than a cent"; `$<0.01` reads as a typo); `formatRate` is separate because two decimals turn the shipped 0.008 into `<$0.01` and gut the sentence that exists to make the figures checkable. Spend counts **every** run including failures (CI bills for those, and excluding them would be the flattering kind of figure); manual time is credited only for **passes** (a failed run verified nothing). Flake reuses §6.1's rule rather than defining it a second time. **Nothing else notices a settings change on its own** — react-query's focus refetch listens to `visibilitychange`, which never fired when focus moved between two BrowserWindows of one app, and now the settings controller simply holds its own copy in `useState` while every other view reads `["recorder-settings"]` through react-query — so `recorder:setSettings` pushes `settings:changed` and `useSettingsFreshness` (in `root-view.tsx`) invalidates that key; Stats is the first consumer where that is visible rather than cosmetic, because it is the view the user is standing on while they correct the price. Both Stats tables carry an `aria-label` (`Run history`, `Spend by test`). Covered by `cost-model.test.ts` (arithmetic, currency, the runner round-trip), `cost-panel.test.tsx` (the sentence, the symbols, the paging, the way out), `cost-pane.test.tsx` (the pre-fill and the clamps), `settings-freshness.test.tsx` and `recorder-settings-cost.test.ts`.
- **Change temp against real medians — C §6.3, 2026-08-12.** `Temp` reads `metrics-store` now, per test and per step, which is what turns §3.4's decoration into a measurement. `testDurationTrend` (`shared/metrics-query.mjs`) is new: a test's recent p50 against the p50 before it, **passed runs only** — a run that died on step two is fast and one that timed out is as slow as the budget, and either poisons a median being used to say whether a pass was unusual. It rides the existing `metrics:slowness` channel when a `testId` is given rather than taking its own, because the step list and the run summary are one screen asking one question and two channels would let them answer it from two different reads of a database being written to while they look. **On a step row, BOTH numbers are medians** (`recentP50Ms` against `previousP50Ms`) and neither is the current run: a single run's duration for a single step is noise — a GC pause, a slow DNS answer — and colouring it would light half the list on every run for reasons that are not about the test. `mode="rule"` there, because a coloured numeral among the row's icons reads as a status badge. **For the run summary the metrics median is PREFERRED over the one `run-summary.ts` derives from run history, and the reason is retention**: `run-history.json` is pruned and the metrics DB is rolled up *before* retention runs, so after a prune it holds strictly more of a test's past — and a median is exactly the statistic that degrades silently when its sample is truncated, staying plausible while ceasing to be true. `summariseRun`'s `medianMs` falls back to the history-derived one rather than to no median at all, since the metrics DB is a derived shadow allowed to be unavailable. Covered by the `testDurationTrend` block in `check:metrics-db` (real SQL against a real database — `all()` swallows a throw by contract, so a statement SQLite rejects returns `[]` and reads exactly like "this test has never passed"), plus `run-summary.test.ts` and `step-row.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/command-palette.tsx` + `renderer/lib/command-palette.ts` — **⌘K, C §6.7, 2026-08-12.** Run a test, run a tag, open a view, record, generate, reach the last failure. The component is a provider: it wraps `RootShell` in `root-view.tsx` so the strip's key cap can read its opener from context, and so its own two dialogs outlive a navigation the palette itself triggered. **A COMBOBOX, NOT A DIALOG** — one focusable element (the input) over a `role="listbox"` with `aria-activedescendant`, which is both the pattern a screen reader reads correctly (rows that take focus announce themselves and leave the query behind) and the more testable one, since the selection is an attribute rather than `document.activeElement`. **The ranking is pure and lives in the lib**: title prefix → word prefix → subsequence, with every keyword match below every title match, and a shortness term that tie-breaks INSIDE a tier and can never cross between them. A palette's only real failure mode is a wrong FIRST row — nobody reads the list, they type three letters and press Enter — which is why the scoring is three tiers rather than a fuzzy library: a matcher that guesses is one where the top row moves for reasons the user cannot see. Groups (Actions / Tests / Tags / Views) render only while the query is EMPTY; a searched list is flat, because its order is the answer and re-grouping destroys what the search produced. `clampSelection` guards a background refetch, not the keyboard — typing resets the selection, so no keystroke can leave it past the end, but a test deleted in another window shortens the live `["tests"]` query with no input at all. **⌘K is the one shortcut in the app that does not exempt text fields** (`HistoryNav`'s ⌘[ does, because `[` is a character); ⌘K produces none, and a palette you cannot open from the log search is one you learn not to trust. The plan says "debug the last failure" and the row says "open" it: an AI debug session needs the script and run output `test-detail-view` assembles, and faking that context from here would open a session about the wrong run. DOM ids are `cmdrow-*` / `cmd-listbox` and deliberately NOT `gl-`-prefixed — `gl-*` is the theme's CLASS namespace and `check:renderer-classes` audits every `gl-` string against the emitted stylesheet, so an id borrowing it reads to the guard as a class that styles nothing. Covered by `command-palette.test.ts` (ranking) and `command-palette.test.tsx` (wiring).
- `renderer/theme/shell/` + `shell.css` — the frame the screens sit inside (A4).
  `TopStrip` (wordmark, breadcrumb, two empty slots, actions), the `Rail`
  family (`Rail`, `RailGroup`, `RailRow`, `RailEmpty`), and — since C §6.9,
  2026-08-12 — `BootPlate`. Separate from
  `primitives/` because there is exactly one instance of each of these in a
  window, and the split is what lets a reader answer "screen thing or frame
  thing?" without opening a component. Three things in here are decisions rather
  than markup. **The ⌘K and job-ticker slots render nothing** — both features are
  Phase C (§6.7, §6.8), and a key hint that opens no palette teaches a shortcut
  that answers with silence, while a disabled one ships a permanently greyed
  control for a feature nobody asked for; the props exist so those PRs are a
  drop-in. **The views nav is its own slot below the scrolling body**, which is a
  fix and not a restyle: it used to be an `mt-auto` block at the end of the
  library list, so it was at the bottom only while the library was short — with
  more tests than fit, Stats/Visual/Batch/Heals scrolled away with them.
  **`RailRow` activates on `click`**, where the SDK's `SidebarListItem` fired on
  `mouseDown` (the AppKit idiom, and a documented trap here: `fireEvent.click`
  did nothing to it and the assertion reported "0 calls"). That is a real
  behaviour change — a press dragged off a row no longer navigates — and
  REDESIGN §8.2 predicted it. `--gl-strip-h` is written once and shared by the
  strip and the rail header, which is what makes them read as one band.

  **`BootPlate` (C §6.9)** is the 2.6s glitch plate, mounted once in
  `root-view.tsx` immediately after `Atmosphere` — the plate wears the same
  `echo` treatment as the Home wordmark and that treatment is keyed on the
  `data-glitch` attribute `Atmosphere` sets. Four things about it are decisions.
  **It covers the app rather than delaying it**: everything below is mounted and
  interactive the whole time, and the plate is `pointer-events: none`, so it is
  a curtain over a running show rather than a loading screen holding one up.
  **It is skippable on any key or click**, unadvertised — 2.6s is right the first
  time and wrong the two-hundredth, and an on-screen "Skip" would make the plate
  look like something being endured. **Reduced motion gets a different DURATION**
  (900ms), not merely a stiller plate: a motionless black rectangle held for 2.6
  seconds reads as a hang, not as a splash, and the fill rule is drawn full and
  still rather than left at zero width, which reads as stalled. **It plays once
  per window, and the flag is set when it FINISHES** — set on mount, StrictMode's
  mount/unmount/remount would make it never appear in development. The hold and
  the fade are TWO effects on purpose: one effect with `phase` in its
  dependencies clears the fade timer it just set the moment it sets `phase`, and
  the plate then sits at zero opacity over the app forever, having looked
  perfectly correct for the first 2.6 seconds. Covered by `boot-plate.test.tsx`.
- `renderer/main/app-strip.tsx` — **also owns Back/Forward since the Stats drill (2026-08-10).** `HistoryNav` renders two `ChromeButton`s over `router.history` and binds ⌘[ / ⌘]. The app had neither before, and genuinely did not need them: every screen was one level deep and the router runs on `createMemoryHistory()`, so there was no browser history behind it and nowhere to go back TO. **Back is not the breadcrumb** — the trail goes UP, back returns to where you came FROM, and they stop coinciding the moment you drill out to a test. Forward is offered because it can be answered honestly: the history API has `canGoBack()` and no `canGoForward()`, but memory history stamps `__TSR_index` into each entry, so `index < length - 1` disables it truthfully rather than shipping a control that sometimes does nothing.
  Its original job stands: the location becomes a breadcrumb and the rail
  handle gets its behaviour. **The breadcrumb
  names what is on screen, not what the URL says**, and the two differ in exactly
  one place that matters: while recording, `RootShell` swaps the whole outlet for
  the trainer *without navigating*, so a trail derived purely from the router
  would confidently name the wrong screen. The Home crumb is also the only way
  back to home — the rail lists tests and views and has never had a Home row.
  The handle is not `SplitView.SidebarToggle`: that renders an SDK `Button`,
  rounded by `cva` default with no "no radius" variant to ask for, which is the
  reason the redesign is a rewrite of the chrome rather than a restyle. It reads
  the same context directly.
- `renderer/theme/screens.css` — what one screen IS, one section per screen as
  Phase B lands (B1: Home, B2: Heals). The fourth and last of the theme's stylesheets, and
  the split is the same one by scope: primitives are what a screen is built out
  of, `shell.css` is what it sits inside, `shared.css` is what it embeds, this
  is the screen itself. A rule wanted by a SECOND screen belongs in `shared.css`
  instead — that move is the signal a component is being born, and making it
  late is much cheaper than inventing a component early. Carries the `echo`
  glitch, which A2 declared (`data-glitch` on the root) and nothing consumed
  until now: two pseudo-element ghosts of the wordmark in the SAME ink, because
  red-and-cyan fringing would spend two status hues on decoration at the largest
  type size in the product.
- `renderer/theme/shared.css` — the chrome for the five components every screen
  EMBEDS (A5, 2026-08-10): the pager, the log inspector's console, the tag
  cluster's chips, the heal row, and the step row's furniture. **`.gl-step-list`
  (2026-08-13) is the column all four step lists are drawn in** — the detail
  view's Steps tab, its Edit Steps editor, and both trainers. It owns the
  padding and the tail below the last row; the width belongs to
  `.gl-step-list-row`, which takes `width: max-content` (a step too long for the
  pane becomes a horizontal scroll rather than an ellipsis) with
  `min-width: 100%` so a short row still fills the column and hover, selection
  and the status rail span the list. **`max-content` on the COLUMN is the
  mistake that looks identical**: percentage widths inside resolve against the
  stretched column, so the trainer's step composer — a form belonging to a 360px
  panel — laid out at the width of the longest step with half its controls off
  the edge. `e2e/panel-overflow.spec.ts` is what catches that; the local gate
  cannot. `.gl-step-list--tight` is
  both trainers (their rows are separated by their own insert cursors, so the
  column's gap would double one, and their list ends in a cursor gap plus the
  composer, which is the drop target the tail provides elsewhere).
  `.gl-step-list .gl-step-row-actions` is sticky to the right edge — without it
  every row's ✕ sits past the viewport as soon as one step is long. Guarded by
  `check:scroll-layout`; see DECISIONS 2026-08-13. A third file
  rather than more of `primitives.css` because the split is by scope —
  primitives are what a screen is built out of, `shell.css` is what a screen
  sits inside, and these are composed in `renderer/main/` where they can be
  wired to queries and IPC the theme layer must not know about. Two rules in
  here are decisions: `.gl-chip` (neutral) and `.gl-chip-tone` (via
  `toneSurface()`) are separated by whether the label reports an OUTCOME, and
  neither is a `StatusChip` — that one is fixed at `--gl-status-w` so a column
  has one edge, and these are inline labels of deliberately different lengths.
  The tag delete no longer turns red on hover: an outcome hue on a hover state
  reads as a result, so the destructive fact is stated in the confirm dialog
  instead of implied by the affordance.
- The **Stats section of `renderer/theme/screens.css`** (B7, 2026-08-10) holds
  the KPI tile, the pass/fail chart, the table treatment (`.gl-table`, plus
  `.gl-table-runs`' `min-width` — see the `StatusChip` note below), the
  log-search field and result rows, the two-line `.gl-rowline`, and the
  Stability / suite-cost / divergence row furniture. **The page's LAYOUT stays
  in Tailwind** — the `ScrollArea`'s `min-h-0 flex-1` and the `mx-auto … pb-10`
  content container are read at source level by `check:scroll-layout`, which
  would have nothing to read if they moved into CSS.
- `renderer/dev/specimen.tsx` — every primitive in every state at
  `/?view=specimen`, mounted INSTEAD OF the app. Part of the preview, never
  shipped. It exists because nothing in the suite has ever *seen* one of these
  rendered — jsdom has no layout engine and the dom project runs with
  `css: false` — so without it the first look at a `StatusChip` would be inside a
  Phase B screen, where a spacing mistake is indistinguishable from a mistake in
  the screen. Measured there in a real engine: all seven chips exactly 78.00px
  with one shared right edge, the ramp's dead band identical across three
  samples, and the CRT at 610 with `filter: none` under a mounted scanline layer.
- **Six guards**, all for the same reason `check:text-color`
  exists: the dom suite runs with `css: false`, so there is no cascade to ask,
  and jsdom returns zeros from `getBoundingClientRect()` so a width assertion
  would prove nothing while passing.
  - `check:renderer-classes` — the only one that is not source-level, because it
    is the one whose question source cannot answer: does this class *paint*?
    It builds the renderer and reads the emitted stylesheet, asserting that
    every class used in a class-list context produces a rule and every `var()`
    read names a declared property. **Since B4 it audits the `gl-*` layer's own
    class names too**, in a second pass: the first pass only looks at Tailwind's
    colour- and type-bearing prefixes, which was the right scope for the bug it
    was written for and no scope at all for the redesign's own vocabulary — a
    `className="gl-setting-groupp"` compiles, lints, type-checks and renders as
    an unstyled div, which is `bg-muted` again in our namespace. Both passes use
    the same oracle (`.foo` OR `\:foo`, since a class may legitimately be used
    only behind a variant), and the second carries a liveness assertion so a
    renamed prefix cannot leave it auditing nothing and reporting a clean pass.
    **A third pass, source-level, asserts no theme stylesheet nests a style
    rule inside another style rule.** Both passes above ask whether a name is
    PRESENT in the emitted sheet, and presence is not effect: a bad merge
    nested the whole Stats section inside `.gl-detail-tabs [role="tab"]`,
    which is valid CSS, built clean, emitted every `.gl-stats-*` selector —
    and applied none of them (DECISIONS 2026-08-10). At-rule nesting
    (`@media`, `@supports`, `@keyframes`) is legitimate and used, so the walk
    tracks the kind of block it is inside rather than banning depth. `check:theme-tokens` below covers `--gl-*`
    only — the redesign's own layer — which is why it did not see the twenty-
    eight SDK class names and fourteen custom properties the port left dangling
    (DECISIONS 2026-08-09). It also pins, by value, that `text-secondary` is the
    text ramp and not the secondary *surface*: Tailwind derives a whole utility
    family from one colour key, and the two halves of that family need different
    colours here. Matches `.foo` and `\:foo` so a class used only behind a
    variant is not reported as missing, and builds into a fresh temp directory
    because Vite hashes CSS filenames without clearing stale ones.
  - `check:theme-tokens` — every `var(--gl-*)` read names a declared token; no
    token declared twice or empty; all four sheets imported and ordered above
    `@source`; every woff2 present *and really woff2* (a proxied download leaves
    an HTML error page with the right extension); the overlay layers never take
    the pointer (a full-viewport fixed layer that does makes the entire app
    unclickable with nothing on screen to say why); and — when `build-preview/`
    exists — that all of it survives into the **emitted** stylesheet.
  - `check:status-width` — the chip reads `--gl-status-w`, nothing re-sizes it,
    no call site passes an inline width, and `78px` appears in exactly one file.
    The failure is one row at a time and invisible in isolation.
  - `check:batch-row-cells` — a checklist row is the same size whether or not it
    is in the job. Three of its cells exist only on a ticked row and each leaves
    a SPACER behind; the check asserts every spacer still exists in the view and
    matches its control on **both axes**. Two of them matched on neither: the
    marks render 24px (from a `width={24}` prop, since `.gl-menu-root`'s own
    `flex: 0 0 auto` beats any basis the stylesheet states for them — so the
    number in a mark's CSS rule is INERT and only the spacer obeys it) against
    20px spacers with no height at all, so ticking a box slid every later cell
    8px and collapsed the row from 33px to 27px. It also pins the `min-width`
    floor under Run/Stop, the same one `.gl-detail-run` carries. Reported from
    the running app; jsdom has no layout engine and the dom project runs with
    `css: false`, so source is the only place the answer exists.
  - `check:selection-neutral` — two tiers, from the palette's own token list: the
    outcome hues and the AI accent may never appear on a selection, hover or
    active state; `--gl-cyan` is declared "running / live / **focus**" so it is
    allowed on a caret or focus ring but still never on a selection.
  - `check:crt-untreated` — no filter, blend mode, opacity, shadow or background
    image anywhere inside the bezel (the caption is exempt — it is chrome, not
    evidence); the bezel's z-index is above `--gl-z-atmo` AND below
    `--gl-z-modal`, both asserted as RELATIONSHIPS so that raising one number
    without the other fails; and `crt.tsx` renders no overlay of its own. The
    modal half was added on 2026-08-10 against a real bug: Radix dialogs ship at
    Tailwind's `z-50`, so every dialog opened on the Visual screen was drawn
    BEHIND the bezel — clipped copy, confirm button under a screenshot — and
    nothing catches that, since the dialog mounts and its buttons are in the
    accessibility tree. `.gl-z-modal` (primitives.css) is on the Dialog and
    AlertDialog overlay and content in `renderer/ui/overlays.tsx`.
  - `check:sdk-retired` (A5) — the seven surfaces already moved onto the theme
    layer import only structural or native-backed pieces from `@ui` (Dialog,
    ScrollArea, the native-menu families, toast). The premise it protects erodes
    silently: a Phase B PR touching `heals-panel.tsx` needs a button, `Button` is
    one import away and is what 80 other files still use, and the result
    compiles, renders, passes every test and looks *almost* right — a rounded
    control among square ones, which jsdom cannot see (`css: false`) and no
    reviewer spots on one import line in a 400-line reskin diff. It asserts the
    mirror too, since `pager.tsx` now imports nothing from `@ui` at all and
    would otherwise pass vacuously: every retired surface must actually read the
    theme. Adding a Phase B screen to its list is how that screen's reskin gets
    the same protection.

### Components
- `SplitView` (sidebar + primary; `storageKey="recorder"`), `Sidebar`/`SidebarList`/`SidebarListItem` (manual `selected`/`onClick` for route-based nav).
- `Toolbar`/`ToolbarContent`/`ToolbarActions`, `Dialog` (props mode: New recording, Rename), `AlertDialog` (delete), `Field`+`Input`.
- `SegmentedControl` (assert mode: visible/text), `Status` (recording/paused, run pass/fail), `Badge` (step type), `Tabs` (Steps/Script), `ScrollArea` (`autoScrollToBottom` for live steps + run output), `EmptyState`.
- Icons from lucide-react. Toolbar action buttons with text are text-only (no icon) per patterns.
- **The three metrics panels** (Phase 4), rendered by `stats-view.tsx` below Stability, each from its own `metrics:*` handler and its own react-query key so they fail and refresh independently. `step-health-panel.tsx` — the join table, one row per step across all history, client-sortable over rows the query already ordered worst-first and paged 25 at a time (sort first, then page — the other order ranks only what's on screen); every count travels with its denominator ("4 heals" means different things across 5 runs and across 400), and a null duration renders as a dash, never a 0 that would sort to the top of "fastest". `suite-cost-panel.tsx` — what the instrumentation costs (capture and a11y, both MEASURED; the speed setting is reported separately because it is derivable but not recorded as a total, and an estimate beside two measurements reads as a third measurement) plus the steps whose median grew; when no step has two full windows it says *why* the trend is empty rather than reporting "no slowdowns", which on a young history reads as a clean bill of health. `divergence-panel.tsx` — only the steps that disagree across engines, with `insufficient` summarised as a count; the "no step disagrees" reassurance is withheld entirely unless at least one step was actually compared. Covered by `metrics-panels.test.tsx`.
- `RunTriage` (`run-triage.tsx`) — one line under the Output panel's header, for a finished FAILED run with a `recordId`, over `runs:triage` → `shared/triage.mjs`. Shows the verdict, its confidence, and **the strongest piece of evidence unconditionally** — a verdict word on its own is an assertion people either believe or ignore. The rest of the evidence and all of `limits` sit behind one disclosure, together: the limits are why a verdict is soft, and separating them invites the verdict to be read without them. An `unknown` verdict **renders** rather than hiding, because an uncaptured failure is exactly the case where the user can act (turn capture on, run again); only a null result — metrics unavailable, or no rows for this run — renders nothing. Not a `Tooltip`: Radix tooltips can't be opened under jsdom, and this is the part of the feature where being wrong is quiet. Stale responses are dropped by a `current` flag, or a slow verdict for the previous run paints onto the next one. **On the `Verdict` primitive since B5a**, and TWO OF THE FOUR VERDICTS CARRY NO TONE: `site` is amber (caution, go and look there) and `runner` is red (the failure is in the test you wrote), but `mixed` and `unknown` are the classifier declining to call it, and colouring either would have the component assert what the reasoning behind it refused to — so `Verdict`'s `tone` became optional with a neutral dot, the same contract `StatusChip` already had. Never phos: the run has already failed, and a green dot anywhere on this panel reads as a pass whatever the sentence beside it says. Covered by `run-triage.test.tsx`, plus a pair in `ai-debug-icons.test.tsx` pinning that the line cannot disturb the sparkle beside it.
- `RunFailureReason` (`run-failure-reason.tsx`) — the failure-reason row above the triage line in the run Output panel, for the same finished-failed-with-`recordId` runs. The two answer different questions: triage argues site-vs-test from evidence, this is the label the failure is FILED under — assigned automatically at run end (shown with an `auto` provenance tag, toneless because provenance is not an outcome) and correctable here, where a manual pick is never overwritten by the automatic path. Native-menu `Select` offering Uncategorized, the built-ins, and ENABLED customs — a disabled custom that is the current value keeps its item so the trigger can render its name, and an id the catalog no longer knows shows raw (facetLabel's honesty rule). Reads `["runs"]` and `["failure-reasons"]` (the latter invalidated by the `failureReasons:changed` push subscribed in `recorder-store.tsx`, so a rename made in Settings reaches a label already on screen); writes over `runs:setFailureReason`, patching the cached run in place on success. Covered by `run-failure-reason.test.tsx`, driving the native menu through the Menu.popup stub and asserting what the menu was HANDED (the Select swallows popup errors, so "picking a withheld item fails" would pass vacuously).
- `RunSummaryPanel` (`run-summary-panel.tsx`) + `summariseRun` (`renderer/lib/run-summary.ts`) — **C §6.1, 2026-08-12.** The five panels a run that did NOT fail deserves. The detail view used to be shaped around failure — a verdict chip, a triage line and a log — which is right for the run you arrived at because something broke and wrong for every other one: a passed run's panel was a green word over eight lines of Playwright's own chatter, answering nothing anybody came to ask. Each state now answers its own question: `never` what pressing Run would do, `running` how far in (a determinate bar over `stepStatus`, plus an elapsed clock the view ticks itself — a run WAITING streams no log, and those are exactly the runs somebody is watching the clock on), `passed` what held (duration as a `Temp` against the median of this test's previous PASSED runs, steps, capture, and any unaccepted a11y violations), `healed` what Auto-Heal substituted and how much of it nobody has reviewed, `retry` what was different between the failure and the pass. `failed` renders nothing here — `RunTriage` is that state's panel, and a summary above it would put a description of the failure over the explanation of it. **The decision and the arithmetic are pure and separate from the markup**, which is what lets the claims that would be silent if wrong be tested directly: healed beats retry, an absent `healFailedSteps` is null and never 0, an absent `speed` is Unknown and never fast, one run is not a median. Two states carry AMBER despite the run passing (`healed`, and a `retry` where nothing differed) — a mis-heal usually succeeds, so a healed pass is the run most worth distrusting and the one that looks most trustworthy. Fed from `["runs"]` and `["heals", id]`, both of which `test-detail-view.tsx` already holds for its tab badges, so the summary costs nothing extra. Covered by `run-summary.test.ts` (the state table) and `run-summary-panel.test.tsx` (the rendering and the tones); all six states are visible at `?view=specimen`, because the preview's fixtures only ever reach one of them.
- `RunOutput` (`run-output.tsx`) — **reskinned in B5a**. The verdict is a `StatusChip` (one shape, one width, the palette's tones) rather than an SDK `Status` badge, and `running` takes the holo treatment instead of a hue: running is the ABSENCE of an outcome, and a colour there makes a run still in flight look like one that finished and reported. The log is `.gl-console` on `--gl-black` — the only true #000 in the palette, reserved for this and the backing behind a captured frame, because Playwright's output is evidence and should not look like our chrome. **The log expands.** The panel is a 224px strip, about eight lines: enough to see that something failed and never enough to read the stack, so anyone diagnosing a failure was scrolling a viewport the size of a business card while the step list above sat idle. Expanding hands it the whole pane (`flex: 1 1 auto`, so it takes what the window has rather than a number written in the stylesheet); it is a drawer rather than a modal because the verdict, the triage line and the log are one thought. Not persisted — a panel that stayed expanded would hide the step list on the next test opened, for a run nobody had looked at. Icon-only "Copy output" (Copy/Check toggle) copies via `window.glazeAPI.clipboard.writeText`; when the run failed (`code !== 0`) a "Debug with AI" (`Sparkles`) button opens `AiDebugDialog` (owned by `test-detail-view.tsx`, which has the test/script context `RunOutput` doesn't). **Extended in C §6.1:** the panel is rendered ALWAYS, not only once something has run in this session — opening a test cold said nothing about it at all, not even that it had never run — and it drops the log (there is none) rather than the panel. The chip reports the six states via `chipFor`, not pass/fail. The summary's own cap is `40vh` and NOT a percentage — a rule kept from when the no-log panel was `flex: 0 0 auto` and its height indefinite, against which a percentage `max-height` resolves to `none` and silently does nothing; viewport units are definite everywhere, so the spelling cannot regress if the sizing story changes again. **Tabbed like the trainer's console since 2026-09-01**: the head carries a `.gl-tabs` strip — Console / Step details / History — with the verdict chip, the failure actions, an Auto-scroll toggle (Console tab only, same control as the trainer's) and the expand drawer clustered on the trailing edge, and the Console tab gains the trainer's status bar over the log (`Running… n/m` / `Done — p/r passed` / `Stopped at step n`, with the hit-rate pill; `hitRateTone` moved to `renderer/lib/hit-rate.ts` so the two consoles share the thresholds). Step details lists each step's outcome from the session's run beside its `locatorExpr` locator — session-scoped on purpose, run history stores no per-step results. Cookies is deliberately NOT carried over from the trainer: it edits the live training browser over recorder IPC, and outside a recording session there is no browser to edit. **One height for all three tabs, and the user's to set**: the old no-live-run shrink (first `:not(:has(.gl-run-log))`, then a `data-compact` attribute) made the Console tab shorter than its siblings, so it is retired — the Console tab keeps its bar-plus-log surface even before a run, with the bar saying why the log is empty. The panel's top edge is a drag handle (`.gl-run-resize`, SplitView's pointer idiom plus arrow keys and a double-click reset), clamped between 140px and the window minus ~220px, writing the dragged height as an inline `flex-basis` and remembering it in localStorage (`runpanel:height`) the way SplitView remembers pane widths; the expand toggle stays unpersisted, since a panel that stayed expanded would hide the step list on the next test opened.
- `RunHistoryPanel` (`run-history-panel.tsx`) — the console's History tab: this test's past runs (via `runsForTest`, newest first, capped at 30 with a pointer to Stats) with the amber facts chipped per row (healed / retry / a11y / shot count — a green row that the record knows better about must say so), and a "Recent screenshots" strip from the most recent captured run. The run list arrives as a prop (the detail view already holds `["runs"]`); the pictures are queried inside the tab, which Radix unmounts when inactive, so a user who never opens History never pays for them. The replay index and frames use the Visual view's own query keys (`["replays"]`, `["replay", testId, runId]`, `["shot", …]`) so the two screens share a cache; the latest CAPTURED run comes from the replay index rather than run history, because retention can prune the JSON history while the replay is still on disk. Thumbnails and the "Visual view →" link open the Visual view — the tab shows, it does not compare. Covered by `run-history-panel.test.tsx`, including that `readShot` is handed the LATEST replay's run id (a thumbnail read from the wrong run's directory looks identical to the right one).
- `AiDebugDialog` uses `Dialog` in props mode with no `onConfirm` (so no auto footer) — dismissible only via Esc/outside-click, per the documented Dialog pitfall; body renders its own Stop/Regenerate/Apply-to-script/Copy action row plus a `ScrollArea` that renders parsed segments (prose `<p>` + `CodeBlock` cards) via `renderer/lib/parse-llm-response.ts`. `AlertDialog` confirms the script overwrite before applying.
- `CustomContextMenu`/`...Sub`/`...SubContent` (sidebar row right-click) — used instead of native `ContextMenu` specifically because the "Adjust Test Speed" submenu embeds a `Slider` (arbitrary React content); native `ContextMenu` is NSMenu-backed and can't host custom widgets. `CustomContextMenuTrigger asChild` wraps a plain (non-forwardRef) `SidebarListItem` fine — Radix's `asChild`/`cloneElement` only needs the child to spread `...props` onto its DOM node, no ref required.
- `Callout` (`color="yellow"`, `icon={<TriangleAlert/>}`, `<Callout.Text>`) in `test-detail-view.tsx` — shown above the Steps/Script tabs when `test.stepsDiverged` is true (script has statements the parser couldn't map back to steps after an edit) AND `stepsDivergedDismissed` is not set. **Dismissible** (`tests:dismissDiverged`), and the dismissal is persisted on the record: `stepsDiverged` stays true, so everything else reading it — the run comparison, the MCP — keeps saying so, and only the banner is silenced. The backend re-arms it whenever divergence is established AFRESH (a `tests:updateScript` re-parse that skips statements, or a `tests:updateSteps` save left out of the script), so an old acknowledgement can never hide a new problem.

### Data & storage
- `userData/recorder/tests.json` — array of `TestRecord`.
- `userData/recorder/scripts/<id>.spec.ts` — generated specs. A `node_modules` symlink → app node_modules is created here so specs resolve `@playwright/test`. **`ensureModuleResolution` REPAIRS that link, it does not merely create a missing one.** The scripts dir outlives the build that made the link: adopting a legacy Glaze store (`main/shell/user-data.ts`) inherits the old install's link, still pointing into the SDK tree, and the spec then resolves a *second* copy of `@playwright/test` while the CLI runs from ours — which fails every run at collection with "Playwright Test did not expect test() to be called here". It `lstat`s rather than `existsSync`, because `existsSync` follows symlinks and so answers `false` for a dangling one, which is exactly the case that has to be repaired. A real directory is left alone. Covered by `main/services/module-resolution.test.ts`.
- `userData/recorder/browsers/` — Playwright browser install (`PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`).
- `userData/recorder/run-history.json` — array of `RunRecord` (run stats index; capped 1000). `userData/recorder/logs/<runId>.log` — raw console output per run. These two are deleted independently (see the Stats feature): reset-stats keeps the `.log` files. `RunRecord.id` is now minted at run START by the runner (passed into `append`) so it equals the artifacts `runId`.
- `userData/recorder/artifacts/<testId>/<runId>/` — **visual-testing screenshots (Phase 1)**: `<stepIndex>.png` (0-based page-action order) + `manifest.json` (`{testId,runId,title,status,startedAt,finishedAt,steps:[{index,action,target,value,ok,ts}]}`). `runId` === `RunRecord.id`. Written by the `glaze-capture.mjs` fixture inside the Playwright worker; NOT inlined into tests.json (binary blobs kept out of the JSON store). Retention: newest 10 run dirs per test (`artifact-store.ts`). Deleted with the test via `tests:delete`. **Phase 2:** the run dir also holds `replay.json` — the canonical per-step replay model (`{testId,runId,testName,url,status,startedAt,finishedAt,failedIndex,visualThreshold?,steps:[{index,stepId,label,type,status,screenshot,diff?}]}`, aligned to `Step[]` index, `screenshot` a filename or null). Written by the runner (`buildReplay`), read by the Visual tab. Runs captured before Phase 2 lack `replay.json` so they don't appear in the replay list. **Phase 3 (visual diffing):** each `ReplayStep` also carries `diff?: {state:"new-baseline"|"match"|"changed"|"unable", ratio?, threshold?, reason?, diffFile?}` and the run dir holds `<stepIndex>.diff.png` overlays for `changed` steps. **Pinned baselines** live at `userData/recorder/artifacts/<testId>/baseline/<stepId>.png` + `baseline.json` (`{testId,updatedAt,steps:{[stepId]:{stepId,runId,at,label}}}`) — keyed by `Step.id` (NOT URL: a test is a deterministic script and Step.id survives reorder/insert; only screenshot-producing steps get a baseline). Managed by `baseline-store.ts`; deleted with the test via `tests:delete`. The first captured run seeds baselines; later runs diff against the PINNED baseline (never the previous run). Diffing is pure-JS (`visual-diff.ts` → pixelmatch + pngjs, no native bindings); size mismatch (viewport/responsive change) or a decode error degrades to `state:"unable"`, never a false flag. Per-test threshold on `TestRecord.visualThreshold` (percent of pixels, default `DEFAULT_VISUAL_THRESHOLD` = 0.1 in `recorder/types.ts`).
- `userData/recorder/shopify-signatures.bin` + `userData/recorder/shopify-signatures.json` — registered Shopify crawler signatures, **deliberately split across two files** (`shopify-signature-store.ts`). The `.bin` holds the three header values, encrypted via `createEncryptedSecretStore`; the `.json` is a PLAINTEXT register of host, expiry and id, and **carries no header value**. The split exists for the standalone MCP server, which has no Electron and therefore no safeStorage: without the register it cannot tell "no signature configured" from "one is, and I can't read it", so its only options are warning on every run or never. Note this contradicts `test-secrets-store.ts`'s one-blob argument on purpose — that store answers ONE question two ways, this one answers two questions — and the llm-service failure that argument names is answered head-on by reporting THREE states (`none`/`registered`/`unreadable`), never collapsing `unreadable` into `none`. Write order is asymmetric so a partial failure always leaves the VISIBLE state: register first when adding, blob first when removing, and `signatureFor` requires both halves so a value stranded in the blob is never sent.
- `userData/recorder/mailbox.json` + `userData/recorder/mailbox-token.bin` — the test mailbox, split for the reason above (`mailbox-store.ts`).
