The `.json` carries the endpoint and when it was saved and **no token**; the `.bin` holds the token through `createEncryptedSecretStore`. The token joins the redaction snapshot the moment it is saved, not at the next run start — it is the THIRD path a credential reaches run output by (attached by the generated SPEC, so it lands in a Playwright error on a failed fetch and in a run that records network traffic).
- `userData/recorder/alert-webhook.bin` — the outgoing-alert webhook URL, **encrypted via `safeStorage`** (atomic `.tmp` rename), managed by `webhook-url-store.ts` — since 2026-08-18 a `createWebhookUrlStore` FACTORY instantiated per credential file, because two features now hold a webhook each. Same treatment as the Anthropic key: backend-only, never returned to the renderer — only `{hasUrl, host}` crosses IPC.
- `userData/recorder/insights-slack-webhook.bin` — the Slack channel that receives new insights reports (`insights-slack-url-store.ts`, the same factory). Deliberately its own credential, not the alert webhook: the incident channel and the report channel are different subscriptions. Configured on the Integrations pane; the send itself goes through `alert-service.sendInsightReportAlert`, gated on `insightsSlackEnabled`.
- `userData/recorder/batch-history.json` — array of `BatchRecord` (batch id, per-test results, summary; capped 50, newest first). Written **write-through** by the batch runner after every per-test transition, and by the MCP server's `run_batch` in the same shape — so a batch driven from an MCP client appears in the app's Batch view. Joined to individual runs by `RunRecord.batchId`.
- `userData/recorder/annotations.json` — **Phase 4 step notes**: flat array of `Annotation` (`{id,testId,runId,stepId,text,createdAt,updatedAt}`), one per `(runId, stepId)`. Deliberately its own index file (mirrors `tests.json`'s pattern) rather than embedded in `replay.json`, since `replay.json` is a runner-owned model fully regenerated every run — annotations must survive that regeneration and outlive artifact retention pruning. Managed by `annotation-store.ts` (`list/upsert/deleteTest`); blank text in `upsert` deletes the note. Deleted with the test via `tests:delete`.
- `userData/recorder/failure-reasons.json` — flat array of `CustomFailureReason`, the user-defined half of the failure-reason vocabulary (built-ins live in `shared/failure-reasons.mjs`). Primary data, never in metrics.db: run records carry the reason ID and this file is what makes the ID mean something, on every surface and after every rename. NOT deleted with any test — a reason is library-wide — and reasons are disabled rather than deleted, so no run is ever left holding an id with no name. Managed by `failure-reason-store.ts`; read directly by `mcp/server.mjs` to resolve names in `list_runs`.
- `userData/recorder/heal-journal.json` — flat array of `HealEntry`, every locator Auto-Heal has changed (`heal-journal-store.ts`), capped 200 per test with settled entries pruned first. **Normalized on READ as of 2026-09-01**, where it used to be a bare `as HealEntry[]`. The cast was defensible while the app was its only writer and stopped being so when `good-looks ingest` began carrying heals back from CI runners: an entry's `appliedLocator` is one accepted click from being generated source Playwright executes in Node, so the journal joined the capture boundary's path and gets its rule — rebuilt from named keys, locators (candidates included) through `normalizeLocator`, page URL and rect through `heal-evidence.mjs`, and an entry that cannot be narrowed DROPPED rather than repaired. Guarding the writer alone was never enough: entries written before any guard are already on disk, and the file is hand-editable. An ingested entry carries `ingested: true`, which is how a surface can say where a heal came from rather than inferring it from a runId whose run may not have travelled.
- `userData/recorder/script-changes.json` — flat array of `ScriptChangeEntry` (`{id,testId,origin,model?,reviewed,before,after,addedLines,removedLines,truncated?,status,at}`), one per rewrite of a test's whole spec. Its own index file rather than rows in `heal-journal.json`, and that separation is load-bearing: five consumers read the heal journal and key its entries on `stepId`/`runId` (`metrics-store.ts`'s `healed` column, `shared/rollup.mjs`, `flake-source.ts`, and `mcp/server.mjs` twice), so a script-level entry there would be counted by every one of them as a healed step — and `mcp/server.mjs` is plain `.mjs` reading the raw JSON, outside `type-check`, where a missed filter is silent forever. Managed by `script-change-store.ts`. Deleted with the test via `tests:delete`.
- `userData/recorder/llm-config.json` — `{ provider, model, baseUrls }` for the LLM integration. `provider` is now `"ollama" | "lmstudio" | "anthropic"`.
- `userData/recorder/insight-reports.json` — the AI insights reports plus the scheduler's state, one file (`{version, state: {lastGeneratedAt, lastAttemptAt, lastError, lastSeenAppVersion}, reports}`) because they change together and two tmp+rename cycles can disagree after a crash. PRIMARY data, deliberately not `metrics.db` (which drops and replays on schema bumps — an LLM answer cannot be replayed). Capped at 24 newest-first; normalized field-by-field on read AND write (`normalizeInsightReport` in `main/recorder/types.ts`); a stored action for a since-deleted test SURVIVES the read and renders disabled — reports are period history, and history doesn't rewrite. `insights:clearAll` removes the state too, so deleting everything leaves no shadow memory of when the deleted things were made. Managed by `insight-report-store.ts`.
- `userData/recorder/lmstudio-token.bin` — LM Studio's API token, encrypted the same way by `lm-studio-token-store.ts` (both go through `encrypted-secret-store.ts`). Only `hasToken` booleans cross IPC.
- `userData/recorder/github-token.bin` — the branch switcher's GitHub token, encrypted the same way. Only `hasToken` booleans cross IPC.
- `userData/branch-builds/<slug>-<hash>/` — one git worktree per switched-to branch, plus its build. Under `userData` and never beside the repository, because the repository is the user's working tree and this feature must put nothing in it. The directory name comes from `worktreeDirFor`, which is where containment is decided.
- `userData/recorder/anthropic-key.bin` — the user's Anthropic API key, **encrypted via `safeStorage`** (written atomically via a `.tmp` rename). Managed by `anthropic-key-store.ts`; the key stays backend-only (decrypted in-process cache, never returned to the renderer). Only `hasKey` booleans cross IPC.
- `userData/recorder/proxy-password.bin` — the manual proxy's password, encrypted the same way by `proxy-password-store.ts`. Only `hasPassword` booleans cross IPC. The REST of the proxy configuration deliberately lives in plain `recorder-settings.json` (so the MCP server can apply it to the runs it spawns); this file is the one part it cannot read, and `describeRun` reports that on every affected MCP run.
- `userData/recorder/recorder-settings.json` — global preferences: `{ showUrlBar: boolean (default true), defaultRunSpeed, defaultWindowSize: {width,height} | null (default null = the trainer's own window size; normalized on read/write via `normalizeViewport`), autoHealEnabled: boolean (default true), autoHealRetries: number (default 3, clamp 1–10), autoHealAttemptTimeoutMs: number (default 4000, clamp 1000–30000), defaultRunBrowser: "chromium"|"firefox"|"webkit" (default "chromium", validated on read/write via `isRunBrowser` — an unknown engine name would otherwise be handed straight to the Playwright CLI and fail the run), defaultCaptureArtifacts: boolean (default false), defaultRunHeadless: boolean (default false), disabledAestheticEnhancements: string[], aiInsightsEnabled: boolean (default false — the consent switch for the scheduled AI report), aiInsightsCadence: "daily"|"weekly"|"monthly" (default "weekly", validated on read/write via `isInsightsCadence`), notifyOnInsightsReady: boolean (default true) }`. Backed by `recorder-settings-store.ts` (validates/clamps on load and set). Snapshotted into the session at `recorder:start`. `defaultRunHeadless` is the fallback for the per-test "Run headless" toggle (test runs only — does NOT affect the trainer). Per-test override persisted on `TestRecord.runHeadless` via `tests:setHeadless`, mirroring `captureArtifacts`.

### IPC channels
- Invoke: `recorder:start {url,name,testId?,viewport?}` (testId seeds the session from an existing `TestRecord` — its steps/url/name/createdAt win over the passed url/name, and so does its recorded viewport; `viewport` is the New Recording dialog's window-size preset, normalized by `normalizeViewport` and recorded as the session's first `viewport` step) / `recorder:pause` / `recorder:resume` / `recorder:setAssert {mode,soft?}` / `recorder:deleteStep {stepId}` / `recorder:insertStep {step,index?}` / `recorder:reorderStep {stepId,toIndex}` / `recorder:updateStep {stepId,patch}` / `recorder:setCursor {index}` / `recorder:replayStep {stepId}` → `{ok,error?}` / `recorder:startRefine` / `recorder:endRefine` (Refine Selector picker: startRefine pauses + enters pick mode, endRefine resumes; both take no args — the target step is tracked renderer-side in `refiningStepId`, and a pick updates that step's locator rather than inserting a new step) / `recorder:stop` / `recorder:getState` → `RecorderState` (now includes `editing`, `assertSoft`, `cursor`, `refineMode`). Push: `recorder:steps` (full `Step[]`, after every mutation — replaces the old per-step `recorder:step`) and `recorder:picked` (`PickedElement` — an element captured via Refine Selector).
- Invoke: `trainerPanel:dock` / `trainerPanel:undock` → `{docked}`. Push: `trainerPanel:docked {width}` / `trainerPanel:undocked {reason}` / `trainerPanel:viewportNarrowed {width}`. Dock state lives in the BACKEND (it moves real windows) and the panel's button follows the push rather than flipping optimistically — a dock can legitimately be refused on a display too small to hold both windows, and an inverted label makes the control look dead. `viewportNarrowed` fires once per session because docking shrinks the training browser, so a responsive site may render a layout the user did not choose.
- Invoke: `recorder:getSteps` → `Step[]` — the session's current steps, for a window that opened AFTER the initial `recorder:steps` push (that push fires inside `recorder:start`, so the docked panel never sees it). The store calls this on mount alongside `getState` and sets `stepsLoaded`; both trainers gate `controlsDisabled` on it, because acting on a step list you have not received yet inserts at the wrong position. The insert cursor opens at `initialCursor(editing, stepCount)` — index 1 when continuing an existing test, since opening a session executes only the initial navigation. **A replay moves it too** (`cursorPastReplayed`, 2026-08-10): the cursor marks where the BROWSER is, and a replay executes more than the navigation. Without that half, replaying to reach a state and then recording wrote the new step in BEFORE the steps that reach it — the same silent wrong-order failure the initial cursor was added to stop. Pinned by `check:insert-cursor`.
- Invoke: `recorder:getSettings` → `RecorderSettings` (includes `defaultTestTimeoutMs`, default 60000) / `recorder:setSettings {…}` → `RecorderSettings`, backed by `recorder-settings-store.ts`. Snapshotted into the session at `recorder:start` (a mid-session toggle doesn't affect an already-open trainer window; it takes effect next time a trainer window opens). Per-test Playwright timeout override: `tests:setTestTimeout {id, testTimeoutMs: number|null}` (null clears); runner resolves override → Settings default → 60s and passes `--timeout=` + `PW_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS`.
- Invoke (Auto-Heal): `recorder:applyHeal {stepId, locator}` → applies a chosen heal candidate's `Locator` to the step (thin wrapper over `updateStep`, then broadcasts `recorder:steps`). Heals reach the renderer on the `recorder:replayLog` `step` event's `heal` field, which the Console renders as candidates/badge. **There is no `recorder:healSuggestion` push** — it existed until 2026-08-09 and nothing ever subscribed to it. Note the consequence, because it is a real gap rather than a tidy-up: only `replayFromCurrent` forwards its `HealResult` onto `replayLog`; the other three callers of `tryHeal` (single-step preview, `replayAll`, `replayFromStart`) keep `okWithHeal` and drop the candidates, so for those paths the candidates exist only in the journal (the Heals view) and nowhere on screen. **Auto-Heal replay flow (`tryHeal` + `healAndRetry` in `recorder-service.ts`):** Auto-Heal runs in **all four** replay paths — `replayStep` (single-step preview), `replayFromStart`, `replayAll` (Edit-in-Trainer auto-run), and `replayFromCurrent`. It was originally wired into `replayFromCurrent` only; the shared re-run wiring now lives in `healAndRetry(wc, step, index, error)`, which every path calls, and structural `if` steps are excluded in the loop paths (no healable target). Guarded by `check:auto-heal-wiring`. When a step fails with an element-not-found error AND `autoHealEnabled`, it runs `healStep`, and if candidates are found it re-runs the step with the best candidate; on success it sets `heal.ok=true`, `heal.autoApplied=true`, `heal.appliedLocator`, auto-applies the locator to the step, and treats the step as passed. Otherwise the (un-applied) candidates stream to the Console for the user to pick manually.
- Invoke: `tests:list` / `tests:get {id}` / `tests:getScript {id}` / `tests:delete {id}` / `tests:rename {id,name}` (skips script regen if `scriptEdited`) / `tests:updateScript {id,source}` (writes the spec file, sets `scriptEdited: true`; for non-imported tests also resyncs `steps` via `parseSpecDetailed` and sets `stepsDiverged` when any statement couldn't be classified — surfaced as a `Callout` in `test-detail-view.tsx`) / `tests:setSpeed {id,speed}` → `TestRecord` (`speed` is `"slow"|"medium"|"fast"`, validated in the handler) / `tests:setHidden {id,hidden}` → `TestRecord | null` (toggles sidebar visibility; no file deletion).
- Invoke: `tests:importFiles` (opens native folder picker) / `tests:importGit {url, ref?}` → `{ imported: number; names: string[]; ids: string[]; needsBaseUrl: string[]; unsupported: string[] }` (ids for post-import navigation; the last two are what the import has to say out loud — see `import-warnings.ts`). Both handled by `import-service.ts`. `tests:repairImports {id}` → `{ copied: string[] }` re-copies an imported test's sibling modules from its `sourceDir`.
- Invoke (LLM): `llm:getConfig` → `LlmConfig` / `llm:setConfig {provider?,model?,baseUrls?}` → `LlmConfig` / `llm:status {provider}` → `LlmProviderStatus` (for `anthropic`, `reachable` = valid key that lists models; adds `hasKey`) / `llm:detect` → `LlmProviderStatus[]` (local providers only; Claude probed on demand via `status`) / `llm:listModels {provider}` → `LlmModel[]` / `llm:chat {messages,provider?,model?,temperature?}` → `{requestId}` (streams) / `llm:cancel {requestId}`. **Anthropic key channels:** `llm:setApiKey {key}` → `{hasKey:true}` / `llm:clearApiKey` → `{hasKey:false}` / `llm:hasApiKey` → `{hasKey}` (never returns the key). Renderer wrappers under `api.llm.*` in `renderer/lib/api.ts`; renderer type mirror in `renderer/lib/llm-types.ts`. No new preload wiring — reuses the existing `glaze.ipc` bridge.
- Push (LLM, via `sendToMain`): `llm:chunk {requestId,delta}`, `llm:done {requestId,cancelled?}`, `llm:error {requestId,message}`. Consumed by `renderer/lib/use-llm-chat.ts` (phase 2's `AiDebugDialog`).
- Invoke: `runner:run {id,headed,captureArtifacts?}` → `{runId}` (runId === testId); `runner:stop {runId}`. **`captureArtifacts` (default off)** is the per-run visual-testing gate (Phase 0 of the visual-testing roadmap) — NOT on `TestRecord` (that's a permanent property; this is a per-run choice) and distinct from the DOM-action training recorder. `playwrightRunner.start` threads it to the Playwright subprocess as env `GLAZE_CAPTURE_ARTIFACTS` ("1"/"0", alongside `PW_SLOWMO_MS`) and records it on the `RunRecord`. (That gate is now fully implemented — capture/storage/diffing all shipped in later phases; earlier notes saying it "carries no capture logic yet" are stale.) UI: a "Capture screenshots" `Checkbox` in `test-detail-view.tsx`'s `ToolbarActions` next to Run test (local state, default off, disabled while running) → `run(id, captureArtifacts, headless)`. **`headed`** is now driven by a per-test **"Run headless"** `Checkbox` (also in `ToolbarActions`, next to Capture screenshots; init from `test.runHeadless ?? defaultRunHeadless`, persisted via `tests:setHeadless`): `run()` in `recorder-store.tsx` invokes `api.runner.run(id, !headless, captureArtifacts)` — headed = not headless. `playwrightRunner.start` appends `--headed` only when `headed` is true, so omitting it runs Playwright headless. Runs only — trainer (`recorder:*`) is unaffected. Invoke: `tests:setHeadless {id,runHeadless}` → `TestRecord`.
- Invoke (visual replay, Phase 2): `artifacts:list` → `RunReplaySummary[]` (all captured runs with a `replay.json`, newest first; each carries `changedSteps` count for the run list's visual-change marker) / `artifacts:getReplay {testId,runId}` → `RunReplay | null` / `artifacts:readShot {testId,runId,file}` → data-URL string | null (base64 PNG; `file` validated to `<n>.png` or `<n>.diff.png`). Under `api.artifacts.*` in `renderer/lib/api.ts`; type mirrors in `renderer/lib/recorder-types.ts`; reuses the `glaze.ipc` bridge (no preload change).
- Invoke (visual diffing, Phase 3): `visual:getThreshold {testId}` → number / `visual:setThreshold {testId,threshold}` → number (clamped 0–100, saved on `TestRecord`) / `visual:acceptRun {testId,runId}` → `RunReplay | null` (pin every captured step of a run as new baselines) / `visual:acceptStep {testId,runId,stepId}` → `RunReplay | null` (pin one step) / `visual:baselineShot {testId,stepId}` → data-URL | null / `artifacts:dismissNotice {testId,runId,kind}` and `artifacts:restoreNotice` → `RunReplay | null` (`kind` is `"visual"|"a11y"`, validated by `isRunNoticeKind` — it is written into `replay.json` and read back forever after). Accept ops (`visual-baseline-ops.ts`) re-pin the baseline AND patch the run's `replay.json` so the accepted step flips to `match` (ratio 0) immediately. Under `api.visual.*` in `renderer/lib/api.ts`.
- Invoke (batch runs): `batch:run {testIds, captureArtifacts?, runHeadless?, browser?}` → `{batchId, alreadyRunning}` (throws if `testIds` is empty; an unknown `browser` falls back rather than failing the run) / `batch:stop` / `batch:status` → `BatchState | null` (includes a finished batch's results, so the view can restore them). Push: `batch:progress` and `batch:done`, both carrying the whole `BatchState` + `summary`. Batch state is **persisted** (see `batch-history-store.ts`) and survives restarts; `RunRecord.batchId` joins runs back to their batch.
- Invoke (Routines): `routines:list` → `Routine[]` (oldest first) / `routines:get {id}` / `routines:save {routine}` → the STORED Routine or `null` / `routines:delete {id}` → `{removed}`. `save` takes `unknown` and returns what was actually stored: the store rebuilds the payload, so a step it dropped or two steps it collapsed differ from what the editor sent, and rendering the return value is what keeps the screen showing the job that will run. The channels are `routines:*` because they are new — ROUTINES.md's rename table says NOT to rename `batch:*`, `batch-history.json` or `RunRecord.batchId`, which are internal and invisible and cost a migration to buy a word.
- Invoke (Routines, running): `routines:run {id}` → `{batchId, alreadyRunning, skipped, plannedRuns}`. Reads the Routine, builds `routineRunPlan` against the live library, and hands the result to the SAME `batchRunner.start` `batch:run` uses. Throws `routineBlockedReason`'s sentence when nothing can run; returns `skipped` otherwise, because a batch quietly one test shorter than the job it came from is the failure Routines exists to prevent. `concurrency` is clamped against DISTINCT tests, as in `batch:run` — lanes are per-`testId`, so a multi-engine step still runs one window at a time.
- Invoke: `tests:setBrowser {id, runBrowser}` → `TestRecord` (per-test browser engine; validated backend-side).
- Invoke: `tests:setBaseUrl {id, baseUrl}` → `TestRecord`. The address an IMPORTED spec's relative navigations resolve against; `null` or an empty string clears it. Validated through `normalizeBaseUrl` — the same gate the config parser's findings pass — so a value typed by the user and one read off disk are held to one standard, which matters because it ends up in the environment of a Playwright process. UI: a "Base URL" `Input` on `test-detail-view.tsx`'s toolbar, rendered only when `test.sourceDir` (a recorded test navigates absolutely and would get a box that does nothing), persisting on BLUR rather than per keystroke — a URL is invalid for most of the time it is being typed — and invalidating `["test",id]`/`["tests"]` because the run guard reads `baseUrl` off the record.
- Invoke: `tests:duplicate {id}` → `TestRecord` (the new copy). Runs `duplicateTest` (record + spec + imported sandbox), then `testSecretsStore.copyTest` + `refreshSecretSnapshot()`. Secrets are copied HERE rather than in the service because the store is async and every write to it must be followed by refreshing the snapshot. Throws on an unknown id, and on a hand-edited/imported test whose spec file has gone missing — there is nothing to rebuild it from.
- Invoke: `tests:setTags {id, tags}` → `TestRecord`. `tags` is typed `unknown` and passed through `normalizeTags` (trim, collapse inner whitespace, dedupe case-insensitively with first spelling winning, sort, cap 20 tags × 32 chars) — it sits directly behind IPC, so a non-array or non-string entries normalize to `[]` rather than throwing or persisting junk into `tests.json`. Idempotent, so repeated saves don't churn the record.
- Invoke: `tests:deleteTag {tag}` → `{tag, removed}`. Removes the tag from every record carrying it (case-insensitive, hidden tests included). `tag` is typed `unknown` and goes through `normalizeTags` too, so canonical form has exactly one definition; anything that normalizes away throws "A tag is required" rather than touching the library. `removed` is the true count and can exceed what the renderer previewed, since `tests:list` excludes hidden tests.
- Invoke (trainer cookies): `recorder:listCookies` → `LiveCookie[]` / `recorder:setCookie {cookie}` / `recorder:deleteCookie {cookie}` / `recorder:clearCookies` — each mutation returns the FRESH list so the panel can't drift from the session. These act on the training browser immediately; recording a change as a step is separate (`recorder:insertStep` with a `cookie` step).
- Invoke (alerts): `alerts:setWebhookUrl {url}` → `{hasUrl, host}` (validates http(s), stores encrypted) / `alerts:clearWebhookUrl` / `alerts:status` → `{hasUrl, host}` / `alerts:test` → `{ok}` (posts a canned payload and **rethrows on failure**, unlike `sendAlert` — the point of a test button is to find out that it doesn't work). The URL only ever travels renderer→backend.
- Invoke (proxy): `proxy:setPassword {password}` → `{hasPassword:true}` / `proxy:clearPassword` → `{hasPassword:false}` / `proxy:hasPassword` → `{hasPassword}` (same write-only contract as the API key; set/clear also refresh the live proxy state, because the cached dispatchers embed the password) / `proxy:verifyApp` → `ProxyVerifyResult` (one request to the ACTIVE AI provider's endpoint through the app-traffic path — any HTTP status is reachability) / `proxy:verifyTest {url}` → `ProxyVerifyResult` (one `net.request` through a throwaway session configured exactly as the training browser's would be). Neither validator throws — a failed check is the answer, and the dialog renders it with `via` naming the actual path. The settings themselves ride `recorder:getSettings`/`setSettings` like every other row; `recorder:setSettings` calls `refreshProxy()` when a proxy key changed.
- Invoke (AI insights): `insights:list` → `InsightReportSummary[]` (newest first) / `insights:get {id}` → `InsightReport | null` / `insights:status` → `InsightsStatus` (the persisted state + `generating`) / `insights:generateNow` → `{started} | {started:false, reason:"disabled"|"alreadyRunning"}` (skips the schedule and the retry backoff — the user asked — but never the overlap guard or the enabled gate) / `insights:markRead {id}` → `{changed}` / `insights:delete {id}` → `{removed}` / `insights:clearAll` → `{removed}` / `insights:exportPdf {id}` → `{path, bytes} | null` (a VERB in the `report:emit` shape — the renderer never holds the produced bytes; null = the save dialog was cancelled). Slack destination: `insightsSlack:setUrl {url}` / `insightsSlack:clearUrl` / `insightsSlack:status` → `{hasUrl, host}` / `insightsSlack:test` → `{ok}` (rethrows on failure, like `alerts:test`). Push: `insights:changed` (payload `null`; a report generated/read/deleted, or a generation started/failed). The one subscriber is `RecorderProvider` — the rail's unread dot reads `["insight-reports"]` from every route, and a view-level subscription would be the stale-dot bug `run-derived-cache.ts` documents. NOT in `RUN_DERIVED_KEYS`: nothing a run does writes this store.
- Invoke (branch switcher): `branches:status` → `BranchStatus` (`{available, reason?, checkout?, appPath?, repo?, current?, switched, hasToken}` — **never throws**; the library sidebar gates its "Branches" row on `available`, and the browser preview's fake bridge answers it `available: false` explicitly rather than letting `defaultFor` return null) / `branches:listPulls` → `PullRequestSummary[]` / `branches:listBranches {refresh}` → `BranchSummary[]` / `branches:switch {branch}` → `{appPath}` (resolves as the relaunch is scheduled — the process is gone a moment later, so the caller should expect the window to disappear rather than render a success state) / `branches:home` → `{appPath}` / `branches:setToken {token}` → `{hasToken:true}` / `branches:clearToken` → `{hasToken:false}`. Push: `branches:progress` (`{kind: "step"|"log"|"done", phase?, message?, line?}`) while a build runs — progress is a push rather than the invoke's result because the invoke does not settle until the build has finished, which is the whole span the user needs to see something during.
- Invoke (batch history): `batch:list` → `BatchRecord[]` (newest first) / `batch:get {batchId}` / `batch:delete {batchId}` → `{removed}` / `batch:clearHistory` → `{removed}`.
- Invoke (run history): `runs:list` → `RunRecord[]` / `runs:getLog {id}` → raw log string / `runs:searchLogs {query}` → `LogSearchResult[]` / `runs:resetStats` → `{removed}` (keeps logs) / `runs:deleteAll` → `{removed}` / `runs:deleteRange {fromMs,toMs}` → `{removed}` / `runs:logsDir` → absolute logs dir. Push: `runs:changed` (fired after a run is persisted; StatsView invalidates `["runs"]`). All under `api.runs.*` in `renderer/lib/api.ts`; reuses the `glaze.ipc` bridge (no new preload).
- Push (backend→renderer via `webContents.send`, received via `glaze.ipc.on`): `recorder:steps` (Step[]), `recorder:state` (RecorderState), `recorder:picked` (PickedElement, from Refine Selector), `recorder:finished` ({testId}), `runner:output` ({runId,stream,chunk}), `runner:done` ({runId,code}).
- `window.glazeAPI.clipboard` is now wired in `renderer/preload.ts` (`createClipboardAPI`) — used by the run-output copy button.
- `window.glazeAPI.shell.showItemInFolder` is wired in `renderer/preload.ts` (uncommented SDK stub) — used by the sidebar row context menu's "Reveal in Finder".

### Integrations
- **MCP server** (`mcp/server.mjs`, standalone stdio, name `good-looks`) — lets Claude Code/Codex/other MCP clients work with the test library without the app open. Reads/writes the same `userData/recorder/*` files as the backend (via `mcp/glaze-data.mjs`'s `resolveDataDir()`). **Twenty-one tools.** Reading: `list_tests` (also exposes `tags` + `runBrowser`, which is what makes `run_batch`'s tag selector discoverable), `get_test` (steps + spec source + variables/datasets/timeout — the variables are what make a secret-bearing test identifiable *before* a run is attempted, and the datasets are what make `run_test`'s `datasetId` discoverable at all), `list_runs`, `get_run_log`. Running: `run_test`, `run_batch`. Routines (added 2026-08-13): `list_routines`, `run_routine`. Evidence (added 2026-08-07): `get_visual_report`, `get_a11y_report`, `get_run_logs`, `list_heals`, `list_batches`, `compare_runs`. Diagnosis (added 2026-08-08): `triage_run` — site or runner for one failed run, over `shared/triage.mjs`; since 2026-08-19 it also answers `suggestedFailureReason` (the same `shared/failure-reasons.mjs` mapping the app's auto-categorization applies — advisory, since this server never writes app data), and `list_runs` carries each run's `failureReason` with the name resolved against the CURRENT vocabulary (built-ins + `failure-reasons.json`), so a rename in the app reads back renamed here. Analytics (Phase 4, also 2026-08-08): `get_step_health`, `get_suite_cost`, `get_browser_matrix` — the three metrics views, over `shared/step-insights.mjs` — and `get_flake_report`, which reads run-history.json rather than the metrics DB so it answers before the app has ever been opened on this machine. These five are the only tools that touch the metrics DB (`get_flake_report` excepted); all of them open it read-only via `readHandle` and never create it. Seeing the app itself: `capture_app`, `get_screenshot`.

`run_test`/`run_batch` share one `executeTest()` helper — in `mcp/run-tests.mjs` since 2026-08-25, so a CLI can call it too — which spawns the bundled Playwright CLI directly, builds its environment and arguments through `mcp/run-plan.mjs`, always runs HEADLESS (no user is watching an MCP-driven run), and appends a `RunRecord` so it shows up in the app's Stats. Settings → Proxy applies to these runs through the same shared rule the app's runner uses (`playwrightProxyEnv` in `shared/proxy-config.mjs`, fed from `recorder-settings.json`) — minus the proxy password, which is encrypted to the app; the run still goes THROUGH the proxy unauthenticated rather than silently direct, and `describeRun` says so up front, the same contract as secret variables.

**Parity with `playwright-runner.ts`, and where it stops.** By 2026-08-07 the two had drifted in four silent ways; three are closed. Runs now inject `GLAZE_VARS` (so dataset sweeps work: `run_test` takes `datasetId`, `run_batch` takes `allDatasets`/`datasetIds`, expanded through the shared `buildQueue`), honour `testTimeoutMs` via the shared `resolveTestTimeoutMs` and an authoritative `--timeout`, and strip ANSI at one choke point before writing the log — and on read too, since every log written before that is still on disk full of cursor-control bytes. **Secrets cannot be injected at all**: their values are encrypted through `safeStorage`, a native app-only API, so `run_test` refuses a secret-declaring test with an explanation and `run_batch` skips it with a note rather than running it with empty strings. **Capture parity is still open** — no fixture loads here, so no screenshots, a11y, console/network, Auto-Heal or crawl settling; every run response instead carries a `fixtures` field naming what it skipped, measured against what the test itself asks for. Pinned by `check:mcp-parity`, which runs the generator, reads the `process.env.X` references out of the spec it produces, and checks the run env against that set.

**`get_run_logs` withholds rather than serves whenever ANY test in the library declares a secret variable.** `console.json`/`network.json` are stored raw and the app redacts on read (`artifact-store.readLogs`); this process has no secret values to redact with. Keyed library-wide rather than per-test for the same reason the app's redaction snapshot holds every secret it knows — any run's log can carry any test's secret. Errors clearly if the chosen browser isn't installed rather than auto-installing. **`run_batch`** selects by explicit `testIds` (run in the order given), by `tag` (case-insensitive; `"__untagged__"` for untagged), or everything visible; runs sequentially; a failing test doesn't abort it. Missing ids come back in `missingTestIds` rather than being silently dropped — "run these 5" must not quietly run 4 and report success — and the response is flagged `isError` when any test failed so an agent can't read a red suite as green. It writes a `BatchRecord` to `batch-history.json` in the app's own shape (write-through), so an MCP-driven batch appears in the app's Batch view. **`run_routine`** (2026-08-13) is the rename table's one addition — the table forbids renaming `run_batch`, because an MCP client meeting a renamed tool gets "unknown tool" rather than a redirect, so the two coexist. It resolves a Routine by id, or by exact name as a convenience: an ambiguous name is an ERROR listing the candidate ids rather than a guess, since nothing enforces unique names and this tool spawns browsers. It plans through `shared/routine-plan.mjs` and queues through `shared/batch-queue.mjs` — the same two functions the app uses — so "what does this routine run" has one answer. Three things it has to stamp, each silent when missed: `routineId` on the `BatchRecord` (or the batch lands under the migrated "Batch" that owns unattributed ones and the routine's history reads empty), `batchId` on every `RunRecord` (the join key — without it the batch's rows link nowhere), and `runRecordId` rather than `executeTest`'s own `runId` on each result (the field the Batch view actually reads). All three are pinned by `check:mcp-parity`, scoped to the tool's own body — the first draft scanned the whole file and stayed green when the `routineId` stamp was deleted, because the tool's JSON response contains the same text. **`run_group`** (2026-08-14, REDESIGN §7.2) runs one library FOLDER. Registered alongside `run_batch` rather than left as a parameter on it — an MCP client discovers tools, not parameters — but both names are handed the SAME `runBatchTool` function, so there is one selection, one queue expansion, one pool and one BatchRecord instead of a second batch runner that starts identical and drifts; `check:mcp-parity` §12 pins that both registrations reference the one handler and that it exists exactly once. `run_batch` gains `group` as a selector for the same reason. Selection is in `mcp/select-tests.mjs` beside the tag rule, and folder names match EXACTLY where tags fold case — a tag is matched, a folder is displayed, the same asymmetry `testStore.renameGroup` draws. Precedence is ids > group > tag: a folder is where a test lives, a tag is a label it carries. `"__ungrouped__"` selects the top level, spelled like `"__untagged__"`. An empty folder is reported as that folder rather than as "the library". **`list_routines`** reports each Routine's `plannedRuns` from the same planner, so the count it gives and the number of processes it would spawn cannot disagree. **Note:** the server had the same `startsWith("chromium")` install-detection bug as the app (a `chromium_headless_shell-*` dir satisfies it without providing a usable browser); both now match `<engine>-`. Deps: `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, `zod@^3`, both already in `dependencies` and so already inside the bundle. Registered for this project in `.mcp.json` (`node mcp/server.mjs`); **a packaged app carries the server as of R15**, and external clients point at `Contents/Resources/app/mcp/server.mjs` run by the app's own binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` — see `mcp/README.md` and docs/MCP-GUIDE.md §2 for the exact commands. The setup lines here and in both of those documents named a Glaze-era path (`app.glaze.macos.main/…/.glaze-sources/`) that the SDK port deleted on 2026-08-08, so for two weeks every setup instruction the project shipped, including the in-app manual, pointed at a path that did not exist.
- `@playwright/test@1.62.1` (pinned; 1.53.0 until 2026-08-22) — bundled test runner + browser installer.
- Local LLM runtimes — **Ollama** (`http://127.0.0.1:11434`) and **LM Studio** (`http://127.0.0.1:1234`), both via the backend's global `fetch` (Node 24). No npm dep, no `glaze.capabilities` entry needed (backend already does network via git-clone import + Playwright browser download). Chat is unified on the OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions` endpoint both expose; model listing differs (Ollama `/api/tags` vs LM Studio `/v1/models`). Ollama needs no credential; **LM Studio optionally does** — its server has an authentication setting that makes every route demand `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, so Settings → AI carries an **API token** field for LM Studio (encrypted via `lmstudio-token.bin`, write-only from the renderer, `hasToken` boolean back).
- **Claude (Anthropic)** — hosted provider added alongside the local ones, via the backend's `fetch` (no npm dep; not the Glaze AI capability). Auth is a user-supplied **Console API key** (from `console.anthropic.com`), stored encrypted (see `anthropic-key.bin`). Chat: `POST https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` with `x-api-key` + `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, `max_tokens: 4096`, system prompt hoisted to top-level `system`, streaming SSE parsed via `content_block_delta` → `text_delta` (mapped onto the same `llm:chunk/done/error` events the local path uses). Models via `GET /v1/models`, stable-sorted **Sonnet → Opus → Haiku** (newest-first within family) so the auto-selected default (`models[0]`) is a balanced general-purpose model. All prompt builders / `useLlmChat` / `ModelPicker` are provider-agnostic and inherit Claude with no change. `runChat` in `llm-service.ts` branches on provider. The Settings → Claude section lets the user delete the key (**Clear** button → `llm:clearApiKey`) and pick the default model (shared **Model** radio → `setConfig({model})`).

- `TestRecord.scriptEdited?: boolean` marks a hand-edited script; `tests:rename` leaves the file alone when set, and continuing via "Edit in Trainer" always regenerates from steps and clears the flag (the UI warns before doing so via an `AlertDialog`).
- `TestRecord.sourceDir?: string` records the folder an imported test came from, so its sibling modules (`./helpers.js`, etc.) can be re-copied into the scripts dir via `tests:repairImports`.
- `TestRecord.hidden?: boolean` marks a test removed from the sidebar view; `testStore.list()` filters these out, but `testStore.get()` still returns them (detail page still works if navigated to). `tests:setHidden {id,hidden}` toggles it without touching the file system.
- `TestRecord.stepsDivergedDismissed?: boolean` — the user waved the divergence banner off. Cleared by every handler that (re)establishes or resolves divergence, which is what stops a months-old click from silencing the next real one. Covered by `tests:dismissDiverged` in `handlers.test.ts`.
- `TestRecord.stepsDiverged?: boolean` set by the `tests:updateScript` handler when `parseSpecDetailed` couldn't classify every statement in a script edit (e.g. an LLM-applied fix using a construct `spec-parser.ts` doesn't round-trip) — the resync still writes whatever it could parse, but the flag tells the UI the step count may be an undercount rather than showing a silently-wrong number.
- **The Script tab is a CodeMirror 6 editor since 2026-08-22 (Script IDE View, Phase 1)** — `renderer/main/script-view.tsx` is the synchronously importable face, `renderer/main/script-editor-cm.tsx` the host it loads with `import()` (the renderer's one lazy chunk; 153 KB gz, budget 260 KB in `check:script-ide-layout`), `renderer/main/script-editor-theme.ts` the `EditorView.theme` + `HighlightStyle` built on `--gl-*`/`--gl-code-*` tokens, and `renderer/theme/editor.css` the host stylesheet (flat rules; the gutter marks' classes). One host serves both the read view (`readOnly`) and the edit view, so the gutters show whether or not the user is editing: a **run gutter** painted from `RunInfo.stepLines` (the line each step index last ran from, carried on `runner:step` since the same day) with the parser's `stepRanges` as the fallback, and a **parse-coverage gutter** marking the `skippedRanges` of `tests:previewScript` — statements the Steps tab will not track. Diagnostics are one `@codemirror/lint` set fed by two sources: Lezer error nodes on every keystroke (the live "cannot load yet" mark) and the CLI's pre-save verdict, held in a `StateField` and refreshed through `needsRefresh` (`forceLinting` alone only runs a lint that is already pending). The action bar reads out the step under the caret (`describeStep`), and clicking a reported problem calls the editor's `focusLine`. Tests drive the view through `EditorView.findFromDOM(content)` and a transaction — a contenteditable takes no `fireEvent.change` — and `renderer/__tests__/setup.ts` polyfills `Range.getClientRects`, which jsdom lacks and CodeMirror's first measure throws on. Settings → Editor (`renderer/settings/panes/editor-pane.tsx`; `editorFontSize`/`editorLineWrap`/`editorLineNumbers`/`editorTabSize`/`editorCheckOnSave` on `RecorderSettings`, validated identically in `read()` and `set()`) reaches the host through compartments, and the font size through the two `--gl-code-*` tokens written on the document element.
- **Configuration and structure in the Script tab (Phase 5, 2026-08-23).** `renderer/lib/editor-keymap-table.ts` + `renderer/main/editor-keymaps.ts` — the three keymap presets as (command, key) tables, SPLIT IN TWO as of 2026-08-24: the table, the labels, `rowsFor` and `prettyKey` are pure data in `lib/`, and the map from a command to CodeMirror's handler plus `keymapFor(preset)` is the `main/` half. Settings → Editor renders a static list of labels and key caps and used to import the CodeMirror half to get them — invisible while Settings was its own entry chunk, and the moment it became a route in the main window it put the whole editor in the app's startup bundle and turned `check:script-ide-layout` red. ⌘K and ⌘I are never bound here. `renderer/main/editor-snippets.ts` — the live templates (every one a `test.step` wrapper), offered at a line start by first word, sharing the completion popup with the type service. Fold by step: `stepRangesField` + `foldService` in `script-editor-cm.tsx` fold a wrapper from its first line to its last, from the parser's `stepRanges`. Format on save: `tsService.format` (TypeScript's `getFormattingEditsForDocument`) applied to the draft in `saveScript` before the check and the write (`renderer/lib/text-edits.ts` applies offset edits). `renderer/main/script-outline-panel.tsx` — the Outline: parsed steps with lines, filter + Enter, and the caret locator's usages across the library from `renderer/lib/find-usages.ts` (equality by `locatorExpr`). The fenced code step: `type: "code"` with `code` (verbatim statements) and `label` — `normalizeRawStep` refuses it, `normalizeStep` accepts it capped at `MAX_CODE_CHARS`; the generator emits it under a wrapper (`dedent` + re-indent; disabled → commented line by line); the parser turns a wrapper with no modelled steps and something skipped into one; `describeStep` names it by label or first line; the trainer's `runStep` reports it as not replayed. `shared/user-page-fixture-source.mjs` — the page stylesheet / init script run fixture (`glaze-user-page.mjs`; env `GLAZE_USER_CSS_B64`/`GLAZE_USER_INIT_B64`, one base64 value each), installed by the capture fixture under `USER_PAGE_ON` and written by the runner beside the other fixtures; the trainer's half is `applyUserPage` in recorder-service.ts on every dom-ready. Settings: `editorFormatOnSave`, `editorKeymap`, `userStylesheet`, `userInitScript` (capped at `USER_PAGE_TEXT_MAX`).
- **Type intelligence in the Script tab (Phase 4, 2026-08-23).** `renderer/main/ts-intelligence.ts` — the editor extension over a `TsIntelligence` the host hands in (null when the service is unavailable): a `StateField` of CodeMirror diagnostics fed 300 ms after the last edit (the service's document is synced first; an answer for a document that moved on is dropped), merged into the editor's one `linter` beside the syntax and CLI sources and refreshed through `needsRefresh`; an inspection's fix becomes a diagnostic `action` applying its edits as one transaction (the lint panel's button is the lightbulb); `autocompletion` with one override source that syncs then asks (unprompted only after a dot or inside a word); `hoverTooltip` showing TypeScript's quick info; and the popups' theme as an `EditorView.theme` (editor.css may not address `.cm-` elements). The detail view asks `ts:ensure` once per mount (`["ts","status"]`), builds the intelligence over `api.ts.*` keyed by the test id, closes the document on unmount, and shows "Types ready" / "Types unavailable" (reason in the title) in the script bar. Settings → Inspections (`renderer/settings/panes/inspections-pane.tsx`) is one switch per rule from `shared/inspections.mjs`.
- **AI in the Script tab (Phase 3, 2026-08-23).** `renderer/main/ghost-text.ts` — the ghost-text CodeMirror extension: a `StateField` holding `{ text, at }`, a `Decoration.widget` after the caret (`.gl-ghost`, styled in `editor.css`), a `ViewPlugin` that asks the host's `GhostSource` once per pause in typing (400 ms) with the document split at the caret, one request in flight and aborted by the next keystroke, a late answer dropped unless the doc object and the caret are what they were; Tab accepts (highest precedence, falls through to `indentWithTab` when nothing is shown), Escape dismisses. `renderer/lib/ghost-source.ts` — the host side over `api.llm.fim`: bounds the window sent and backs off 15 s from a failing server. The detail view turns it on while editing with an autocomplete slot assigned (`["llm","config"]` query, short staleTime — Settings is another window and cannot invalidate this cache). `renderer/main/script-ai-panel.tsx` — ⌘K rewrite and Explain, rendered between the script bar and the editor: a rewrite's instruction goes to the chat slot with `buildRewriteMessages` (whole file, or the editor's selection — `ScriptEditorHandle.view()` gives the host the selection), the answer's one fenced block becomes `diffLines` + `DiffView`, and Apply hands the new text to the host, which puts it in the EDIT BUFFER and remembers an `ai-inline` origin (`affordance`/`provider`/`model`/`promptVersion`, `reviewed: true`) for the next Save. Explain asks the instant slot straight away with `buildExplainMessages` (caret statement, failed step from `RunInfo.stepStatus`, run output) and shows prose. The header is the budget line (what is sent, ≈tokens at four chars each, which slot); hosted requests from the editor are capped at six a minute (`hostedCapAllows`, module state); after a rewrite `tests:previewScript` says how many new statements the step list cannot show. ⌘K is bound INSIDE the editor (`onAiRequest`, `Mod-k` in the host keymap) so it wins over the command palette only while the editor has focus. `renderer/lib/ai-instructions.ts` resolves the standing instructions (`aiInstructions` + `aiInstructionsByHost[host]`, exact host) both prompts carry. Settings → AI: `ai-role-rows.tsx` (Instant helpers: Same as chat / Ollama / LM Studio / Claude; Autocomplete: Off / Ollama / LM Studio — never Claude, pinned by `check:editor-egress`) and `ai-instructions-rows.tsx` (global textarea, per-site table saved whole).
- **A script save is a transaction (2026-08-22).** `tests:updateScript` takes `base` — the text the draft started from — and throws `SCRIPT_CHANGED_ON_DISK` (`shared/script-save.mjs`, spelled once for both processes) when the file no longer matches it; the Script tab answers with **Reload (discard draft)** or **Overwrite with my draft**. Text, not a revision counter: the writers that bypass the record (`regenerateCallers` rewrites other tests' spec files and never saves their records) are exactly the ones a counter misses. `tests:updateScript` and `tests:updateSteps` refuse outright while `recorderService.sessionTestId()` is the test — the detail view is unmounted during a session, so the refusal cannot live in the renderer, and `finalize` would discard the write minutes later. `tests:previewScript` reports the parse a save WOULD do (`parseSpecDetailed` with positions: `stepRanges`, `skippedRanges`) plus `newlySkipped`, the unmapped statements the stored script did not already have — the Script tab asks once over those ("N statements won't become steps … Save anyway") and never over an import's or hand logic's standing misses. `renderer/lib/script-buffer.ts` is the dirty registry: the editor marks a test while its draft differs from its base, and `ai-debug-store.tsx` holds an unattended auto-apply for review while it is marked. `testStore.writeScript` is write-then-rename.
- **Generated specs wrap every statement in `await test.step("<title>", async () => { … });` since 2026-08-22** (`stepOpen`/`stepClose` in `script-generator.ts`), so Playwright's report and trace viewer show steps by name and the editor folds per step. The rules that keep the wrapper from changing what runs: only a statement is wrapped — never a brace half (`if`/`else`/`endif`, a loop's `for`/`}`), never a comment (disabled, UNGENERATABLE, group markers, refusals), never a download's arming line (it must stay in the scope its `await downloadN` reads from); the continue-on-failure `try` goes OUTSIDE the wrapper; `record1` attributes the inner statement, so the line map and the reporter's `pw:api` markers land on the statement, not the header. The title is `stepTitle(step)` — a phrase (`click testid "go"`, `expect page urlContains "/cart"`), never `describeStep`'s fall-through to the emitted statement, which would double the file and put `page.getByTestId("a")` inside a string. `spec-parser.ts` consumes a wrapper whole (before the brace closer, so its `});` cannot pop an open `if`/`for`; the title is never scanned) and `buildStepLineMapFromSource` in the runner sees through wrapper headers and closers for hand-edited specs. The AI prompts (`llm-prompts.ts`) ask for one statement per wrapper rather than none.
- `parseSpecDetailed` returns positions since 2026-08-22: `stepRanges[k]` is where `steps[k]` was read from and `skippedRanges` where each unclassifiable statement sits, both file-relative because `stripComments` is offset-preserving (a stripped comment becomes the same number of spaces). Derived from the scan's own progress in `parseBody` (`record`), so no branch had to learn about positions; recursive branches (a `try` wrapper, a disabled line, a `test.step`) pass their slice's offset. **Since 2026-08-22 the textarea is `wrap="off"` + `white-space: pre`, and it is the editor's ONE scrolling element**: the highlight `<pre>` behind it is `overflow: hidden` and follows it programmatically, the gutter follows by transform. Before that the textarea soft-wrapped while the pre did not, so the first line wider than the pane offset every row below it by one in one layer only, and the pre scrolled on its own (the "sync" wrote `scrollTop` to the gutter, which does not scroll). `script-view.test.tsx` pins the structure; jsdom cannot see the rest.
- **Save checks the draft before it writes (2026-08-22).** `tests:checkScript` (`main/services/script-check.ts`) writes the draft BESIDE the real spec — same directory, so `./glaze-runtime.mjs` and an imported test's siblings resolve as they will at run time — and runs the real Playwright CLI over it in `--list` mode with a reporter (`glaze-list-reporter.mjs`, emitted like the step reporter) that writes the collection outcome to a file named by `GLAZE_LIST_OUT`. A file, not stdout: the draft's own module-scope `console.log` shares stdout with `--reporter=json`. About half a second; no browser. It catches a syntax error (line + column + code frame), an import that does not resolve (on the import's line), a duplicate title, a module-scope throw and a file with no `test()`; it does NOT catch type errors, because Playwright strips types rather than checking them — pinned as a row in `check:script-check` so the limitation is stated rather than assumed. `--list` EXECUTES the draft's top-level code, which is the same trust a run extends to the same file, and why it runs in a child process under a run's env and never in the main process. The draft is removed whatever happens; every spelling of its path the CLI uses (absolute, realpath'd — macOS keeps `/var` behind `/private/var` — and relative) is renamed to the spec's own name in what the user sees. In the view: a failed check keeps the editor open, lists each problem as a row (click → caret on that line, via `lineStartOffset`), marks the line in the gutter and tints it in the highlight layer, and adds **Save anyway** — the file is the user's, and a check that could not RUN is reported the same way rather than passed. The `saveAndEditInTrainer` path writes unchecked on purpose: it exists so the edit survives the regeneration that follows. Guarded by `check:script-check` (real CLI, nine rows), `script-check.test.ts` (the pure half + a fake process), `tests:checkScript` in `handlers.test.ts` (real CLI through the handler, read-only on the spec), and the "saving a script edit" block in `test-detail-view.test.tsx`.
- The Script tab **read view** renders via `renderer/main/script-view.tsx` (`ScriptView`) — an IDE-style code viewer: a line-numbered gutter (right-aligned, `--color-token-tertiary`, `aria-hidden`) + lightweight regex syntax highlighting (keywords → `--color-token-primary` blue, strings → `--color-token-string` red, numbers/decorators → `--color-token-highlight`, literals → `--color-token-secondary`, comments → `--color-token-tertiary` italic, punctuation → `--color-token-tertiary`). Uses the design system's dedicated `--color-token-*` vars (NOT the status text tokens like `text-success`, which collapse to white in dark mode). Tokenizer is dependency-free, threads multiline block-comment/template-literal state across lines. The Edit view is still the raw `<textarea>`.

### Conventions & constraints
- **External Claude Code collaboration:** `CLAUDE.md` (repo root, tracked in git) is a handoff doc for a Claude Code CLI session run directly in this folder — architecture, directory map, hard constraints (forbidden imports, protected dirs, npmrc rules), SDK reference paths, and npm commands. External sessions can edit + `npm run lint`/`type-check`/`build`, but can't launch or live-preview the native app — that still requires the Glaze app. Both share the same local-only git repo (no remote); commits from either show up interleaved in `git log`.
- **Sidebar `actions` cannot host a React dropdown menu.** `Sidebar` runs `applyButtonDefaults` over its `actions`, which breaks any `*DropdownMenuTrigger asChild` wrapper — both the native `DropdownMenu` and `CustomDropdownMenu` render an empty actions wrapper (no trigger button, no error thrown). Verified: only the plain `<Button>` renders there. For a header action menu, keep a plain `<Button>` in `actions` and open a native menu from its `onClick` via `window.glazeAPI.Menu.popup({ items, x, y, coordinateSpace: "view" })` (already exposed in `preload.ts`), switching on the returned `commandId`. Anchor with the button's `getBoundingClientRect()` (left/bottom); leave `positioningItem` unset (a harmless "ignoring invalid positioningItem" debug warning is logged). **`coordinateSpace: "view"` is required** — `Menu.popup`'s `x`/`y` are documented as absolute screen-space coordinates, but `getBoundingClientRect()` gives coordinates relative to the window's content view. Without `coordinateSpace: "view"`, those small view-local numbers get treated as virtual-desktop coordinates and the menu always opens near the primary display's origin (i.e. on the laptop screen) even when the app window is on a different display. All four renderer popup call sites (`recording-view.tsx` ×2, `library-sidebar.tsx`, `stats-view.tsx`) now pass it. The backend context menu in `recorder-service.ts` (`menu.popup({ window: recWindow, ... })`) is unaffected — it passes an explicit `window`, which anchors it correctly regardless of coordinate space.
- **Glaze `webContents.executeJavaScript` runs each call in a fresh ephemeral content world** — JS globals do NOT persist between calls, and the backend `console-message` event does NOT fire for page logs. The recorder therefore stores all state + the step queue on shared `<html>` DOM attributes (which persist for the document lifetime and are readable from any world) and the backend polls to drain them. Do not switch back to console/global-based capture.
- Recording window loads the external URL directly with `titleBarStyle: "default"` and NO app preload (external/untrusted page). Popups are denied and loaded in-place via `setWindowOpenHandler`.
- **No custom in-window address bar is possible:** the recorder window's content IS the externally-loaded page (no app-owned HTML wrapper, no `<webview>`/`WebContentsView` embedding in this SDK), so a real editable URL bar can't be overlaid on the page. The "URL bar" is the native title bar instead — `recorder-service.ts`'s `updateTitle()` sets `recWindow.setTitle("Recording/Editing — <current URL>")` on every `did-navigate` when `session.showUrlBar` is true (the global default), so it tracks navigation live; when the setting is off, the title is left at its static initial value (set once at window creation) and never updated.
- **All navigation stays in the trainer window (3 layers):** Glaze routes navigations out to the system browser in ways that broke recording. Fixes, in `recorder-service.ts` `start()` + capture script:
  1. **Plain cross-origin `<a href>` links (the main culprit):** Glaze hands cross-origin main-frame navigations to the default browser. `wc.on("will-navigate")` calls `details.preventDefault()` then `loadNavInWindow(url)` (re-issues via `loadURL`) for main-frame, non-same-document http(s) navigations. A `selfLoad` guard prevents the programmatic `loadURL` from being re-intercepted (loop). `did-navigate` no longer needs to be prevented.
  2. **`target="_blank"` anchors:** capture script `keepInWindow()` rewrites the clicked anchor/form `target` to `_self` in the capture-phase click/keydown, plus injects `<base target="_self">`.
  3. **`window.open`:** `setWindowOpenHandler` returns `{action:"deny"}` and calls `loadNavInWindow`; `did-create-window` closes any stray child as a backstop.
  - NOTE: an external browser window is NOT a Glaze window, so it never appears in `LiveAppInspectionStatus`. Verify in-window navigation by reading the recorder window's own `location.href`, not by counting app windows.
- The navigating click is caught by draining on `will-navigate`/`did-navigate` in addition to the 250ms poll.
- Runner spawns with `process.execPath` + `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, `cwd` = scripts dir, `NODE_PATH` = app node_modules.
- Built backend runs from `.glaze/build/main`, so runtime deps must be resolved against `.glaze-sources/node_modules` (see `resolvePlaywright` candidate scan).
- **`Slider`'s `onValueCommit` is unreliable for a plain click (no drag) — commit on `onValueChange` instead.** `TestSpeedSlider` in `library-sidebar.tsx` originally persisted on `onValueCommit`; the thumb's local value visibly updated on click but the backend write never happened (no `tests:setSpeed` call, no `testStore.save` log line) because a single click never produces the drag gesture Radix's slide-end/pointerup handling expects. Fixed by calling the commit logic directly from `onValueChange`, guarded by a `useRef` so repeated same-value events don't re-fire — fine for a low-cardinality (3-stop) slider; a continuous-value slider would need a debounce instead.
- App logs (`~/Library/Logs/app.glaze.macos.<appId>/glaze-*.log`, tail the most-recently-modified one) are the fastest way to confirm an IPC handler actually ran — `testStore.save()` logs `"Saved test record"` (not the channel name), so grep the log message text, not the IPC channel/handler name.


---

## The Electron shell (`main/shell/`) — added by the port off Glaze

The only directory permitted to import `electron`. Everything else in `main/`
goes through `@shell/backend`; `eslint.config.js` enforces it. See
[../PORTING.md](../PORTING.md) for the migration record.

### `main/shell/backend.ts`
The adapter that replaced `@glaze/core/backend`. Re-exports Electron's `app`,
`BrowserWindow`, `Menu`, `Notification`, `dialog`, `ipcMain`, `safeStorage`,
`screen`, `globalShortcut`, and supplies the four things Electron has no direct
equivalent for: the `logger`, a `BrowserWindow` wrapper that strips Glaze's
`windowKey` option (so window-creation code was not rewritten), a no-op
`initDevToolsButtonState`, and the `WebContentsNavigationEvent` type the
recorder's navigation guards are written against. The wrapper is a function
returning an instance rather than a subclass — Electron's `BrowserWindow` is
native-backed and cannot be `extend`ed.

### `main/shell/stdio-guard.ts`
Makes writing to stdout/stderr unable to kill the app, and is imported for
effect by `logger.ts` (`guardStdio()` at module load, `writeSafely` around the
console write). **The bug: the logger could crash the process it was logging
for.** `log()` wrapped its file write in a best-effort `try/catch` — the file's
header promises "a full disk must not take the recorder down with it" — and left
the console write bare beside it. stdout is a **socket** whenever the app was
launched by another process (a terminal, a script, an agent's shell), so when
that parent exits and the app does not, every later log line writes to a dead
pipe and Electron shows "Uncaught Exception: write EPIPE". Once that happens
every `logger.*` call throws, which turns the logger into a landmine on exactly
the error paths that exist to report failures.

**Two guards, because Node delivers the error two ways** and the obvious
one-line fix only covers one. An EPIPE noticed while the write is being
dispatched comes straight back out of `console.log` (the reported stack) and a
`try/catch` catches it; one noticed afterwards arrives as an `error` EVENT on
the stream, where no `try/catch` can see it and an unhandled `error` throws from
the event loop. Reproducing the orphaned-parent case produces the second kind.
Separate from `logger.ts` because it is a process-level policy rather than a
logging detail — and because `logger.ts` imports `electron`, which nothing in
`vitest.config.ts` aliases, so a test cannot load it; this module imports
nothing. `guardStdio` is idempotent (a listener per call would trip Node's
max-listeners warning, which prints to the broken stream) and tolerates a
`null` stream, which is what a detached-stdio launch produces.

Covered by `main/shell/stdio-guard.test.ts` for what a unit test can settle, and
by `check:logger-epipe` for what it cannot: the async half arrives from the
event loop of a process whose parent has exited, so the check spawns a real
writer, orphans it behind a short-lived launcher, and watches it survive.
**It carries a control row** — the unguarded shape must actually die — because
destroying the read end from the test process does NOT break the pipe (Node
keeps buffering), which is how a first draft passed while proving nothing. Two
source assertions tie the proven mechanism to the code that runs, since the bug
was a bare console call sitting next to a guarded one.

### `main/shell/user-data.ts`
Decides where the app's data lives, and must run as the **first statement** in
`main/index.ts` — before the module-scope reconciliation and migration passes
(batch history, routines, AI debug), which would otherwise repair the wrong
store. (Imports are hoisted, so "first" means first in the body.)

Under Glaze the host supplied `userData`; stock Electron derives it from
`productName`, so the port silently began reading an empty `Good Looks!/`
directory while the real 1.4 GB store sat beside it under
`app.glaze.macos.<id>-local/`. The app looked wiped rather than misdirected.

Resolution order: the `GOOD_LOOKS_USERDATA` override; then the current
directory if it already holds a recorder store; then the newest legacy Glaze
store, **adopted in place** (no copy, so a Glaze build keeps working and there
is no half-migrated state); then Electron's default. Adoption fires only when
the current directory has no store of its own, which makes it one-shot and
means it can never redirect an install that has real data.

`metrics.db` deliberately does **not** count as "has a store" — it is a derived
shadow and the port writes an empty one on first launch, so counting it would
stop adoption from ever firing. Covered by `user-data.test.ts` (18 cases),
including that one.

### `main/shell/logger.ts`
`logger.info(scope, message, meta?)`, matching the SDK's shape so ~40 call
sites were untouched. Console plus an append-only file under
`userData/logs/main.log`, because a packaged app has no terminal attached.
File writes are best-effort and never throw.

### `main/shell/host-handlers.ts`
The IPC handlers the Glaze runtime used to register automatically, backing
`window.glazeAPI.{dialog,shell,clipboard,nativeTheme,Menu}`. Under Electron
nobody registers them unless we do, and a missing one surfaces as "No handler
registered" the first time a menu or copy button is used — so the set is the
full surface the renderer actually calls, enumerated by grep rather than guessed.
`Menu:popup` is the important one: it converts the renderer's plain-data menu
template into a real macOS menu and answers with the picked `commandId`, which
is what keeps `Select` and `DropdownMenu` native. Also exports
`forwardRendererConsole`, which pipes renderer errors into the main log — the
port's first failure was a blank window with a completely clean log.
`shell:openExternal` is the one handler with a validator in front of it; see
below.

### `main/shell/external-url.ts`
The allowlist deciding which URLs may be handed to the OS, and the whole of the
`shell:openExternal` boundary. `shell.openExternal` launches whatever Launch
Services has registered for a scheme — `file://` opens a document, a custom
scheme starts another application — and the URL this app passes is a PR's
`html_url` out of a GitHub API response, so it is network input. `checkExternalUrl`
accepts https on `github.com` (or a subdomain) and **returns the parsed href**,
which the handler opens instead of its argument: validating a parsed URL and
then opening the caller's raw string means two values that merely usually agree.
Guarded by `check:open-external` (hostile hosts, schemes, userinfo, normalisation)
and by `check:recorder-navigation`, whose blanket ban on `openExternal` in `main/`
is now a one-file allowlist — that check also pins the fact the exception rests
on, that the **training window has no preload** and so an untrusted page has no
`glazeAPI` with which to invoke any host channel.

### `main/shell/app-protocol.ts`
Serves the built renderer over a custom `app://` scheme. Vite emits module
scripts, which under `file://` get a null origin and are blocked by CORS; the
symptom is a blank window. Registering a real scheme also gives the pages'
CSP a meaningful `'self'`. Path containment is judged on the **real** path
against the **real** build root, the same rule the import sandbox applies, so
neither `..` in a URL nor a symlink inside the bundle can read the rest of
the disk.

## `renderer/ui/` — the app's component library

Replaced `@glaze/core/components` (73 symbols across 36 files). Same symbol
names and prop contracts, read off the SDK's `.d.ts` files, so consuming views
only changed their import specifier to `@ui`.

- `primitives.tsx` — Button, Badge, Input, Textarea, Label, Status, Text, Table family
- `controls.tsx` — Radix form controls: Checkbox, Switch, RadioGroup, Slider, Tabs, SegmentedControl, Tooltip
- `layout.tsx` — Toolbar family, the composite ScrollArea (auto-follow-bottom), Field family, Callout, EmptyState, Sidebar family, SplitView, ErrorBoundaryView. **`SplitView` gained a `header` slot in A4** — a band across the top of every pane, rendered INSIDE the context. The redesign's top strip spans the whole window (wordmark over the rail, breadcrumb over the content), so it cannot be a child of the primary pane, and as a sibling above the SplitView it would sit outside `SplitViewContext`, where its rail handle throws; hoisting the collapse state into the caller to avoid that would move `storageKey` persistence and ⌃⌘S out of the one component that owns them. **`SidebarToggle`'s `pinned` prop is real now** rather than accepted and ignored: `pinned={false}` drops the absolute positioning *and* the `registerPinnedToggle` call, because `hasPinnedToggle` is what makes a Toolbar indent its title by 44px and a toggle that is not floating over the pane needs no space reserved
- `overlays.tsx` — Dialog, AlertDialog, the Radix DOM context menu, Toaster/`toast`, and the DOM date picker. **`DialogFooter` is a WRAPPING row and the composed dialog has NO Cancel button**, both since 2026-08-09. It was `flex justify-end` with no wrap over `whitespace-nowrap` buttons that cannot shrink, so a row too wide for its container was laid out with the surplus hanging off the *left* — in the trainer panel (360 DIP → a 296px dialog → 264px of content) "Discard Edits · Cancel · Save & Exit" is ~282px, and the destructive button rendered outside the dialog. Wrapping is what makes that structurally impossible; dropping Cancel (the close "X", Escape and the overlay all already dismiss) is what puts the exit dialog back on one row. The composed footer is its own exported component, **`DialogActions`**, so the layout test lays out the real buttons rather than a copy — as is `dialogPanelClass`, the panel geometry that test reconstructs. Guarded by `dialog-actions.test.tsx` (what is in the row), `check:dialog-footer` (source-level: wrap, no Cancel, and the 296px arithmetic re-derived from `PANEL_WIDTH`) and `e2e/dialog-footer.spec.ts` (the real measurement). **Visible** in the browser preview's specimen (`?view=specimen`), at 296px with the content edge drawn — the only place the narrow case can be looked at, since the main window's 928px floor makes every dialog roomy there. **`headerActions`** (2026-08-17) is the panel's top-right corner slot, rendered in one flex group with the close "X" and to its left, for WINDOW-level controls only — the AI debug dialog's minimize, which used to sit among the response's own icons. That dialog pairs it with `showCloseButton={false}`: dismissal there routes to `minimize` anyway, so the "X" was a second button doing one thing under the harsher name, and minimize is now its only corner control. The composed `Dialog` widens the header's right padding when it is filled, since the extra icon eats into the title's line. Anything acting on the CONTENT belongs in the description row or the footer. `dialogHeaderActionClass` is the shared styling so a corner control reads as one; guarded by `dialog-header-actions.test.tsx` (containment + order + padding, none of which a by-role query would catch). **The confirm button does NOT close the dialog** (2026-08-18): it calls `onConfirm()` and reads nothing back, and CALLERS close themselves. Restoring a global auto-close is unsafe — `IssueComposeDialog` swallows its own errors and must stay open on a resolved confirm — so the decision is distributed, and `check:dialog-close` is what keeps it honest: it enumerates the dialogs that start something outliving themselves from OUTSIDE the outlet `RootShell` swaps (the rail's `+` and the ⌘K palette mount theirs there; Home's copy is unmounted by the swap, which is why the bug kept looking fixed) and asserts each contains a close, plus the `root-view.tsx` swap shape that enumeration is derived from. The contract itself is stated in `dialog-actions.test.tsx`; the real overlay is checked by `e2e/dialog-lifecycle.spec.ts`, which drives the ⌘K path because the rail's `+` is a native menu Playwright cannot click
- `native-menu.tsx` — **Select and DropdownMenu, still backed by real macOS menus.** Items render to `null`; the tree is walked into a plain-data template and handed to `Menu.popup`, which answers with a `commandId`. Options never enter the DOM, so assert the displayed value and cover persistence at the IPC layer — unchanged from the original.
- `tokens.css` — design tokens (light/dark), bridged to Tailwind in `renderer/styles.css`. Carries a second block of **SDK-compatibility names** — the text ramp (`--text-primary/secondary/tertiary`), the `--support-*` status family, named surfaces (`--panel`, `--well`, `--control-subtle`), and the Radix numeric steps (`--blue-9`, `--red-9`). The 36 ported views were deliberately not rewritten, so they still address that vocabulary; none of it came across with the component library, and every one of those names styled nothing until 2026-08-09. Defined in terms of the semantic tokens above them rather than as fresh literals, so light/dark flips in one place. Guarded by `check:renderer-classes`
- `use-theme.ts` — **gone (2026-08-10, A4).** It kept `.dark` on `<html>` in sync with `nativeTheme` and returned whether dark was active. The app is dark only now (REDESIGN §0): each window's entry HTML applies `.dark` unconditionally, `main/shell/host-handlers.ts` pins Electron's own `themeSource` to `"dark"` so the native menus this app pops up for every Select agree, and the `nativeTheme:*` handlers, the preload bridge, the `NativeThemeInfo` type and the `nativeTheme:updated` push went with it — an IPC surface nobody calls is indistinguishable from one about to be needed again
